# Decisions Log

Record architectural decisions here. Do not silently change an approved rule.

## Approved

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| ADR-001 | Modular monolith | Accepted |
| ADR-002 | Next.js frontend, NestJS backend, tRPC, Prisma, PostgreSQL | Accepted |
| ADR-003 | Proper double-entry and accrual accounting | Accepted |
| ADR-004 | One organization now; explicit organization scope for future isolation | Accepted |
| ADR-005 | Simple Legal Entity/GST UI; multi-entity schema | Accepted |
| ADR-006 | Lead and Tender separate; Project may reference either/both | Accepted |
| ADR-007 | Project stage and operational status are separate | Accepted |
| ADR-008 | CAPEX event billing and OPEX schedule billing | Accepted |
| ADR-009 | OPEX schedule rows do not automatically create invoices | Accepted |
| ADR-010 | One milestone may have multiple invoices | Accepted |
| ADR-011 | One receipt may settle multiple invoices; partial receipts supported | Accepted |
| ADR-012 | Typed deductions with full lifecycle tracking | Accepted |
| ADR-013 | Optional Purchase Order | Accepted |
| ADR-014 | Accountant/assigned PM approval; no self-approval; no amount slabs | Accepted |
| ADR-015 | Subscription cycle is reminder/forecast until actual expense is recorded | Accepted |
| ADR-016 | Employee Effort and Contribution merge; gross salary costing | Accepted |
| ADR-017 | Single inventory location | Accepted |
| ADR-018 | No fixed-asset/depreciation module | Accepted |
| ADR-019 | Introducer advances and overpayments supported | Accepted |
| ADR-020 | EMD/SD may relate to Tender, Project, or both | Accepted |
| ADR-021 | Posted financial records immutable; correction by reversal/note | Accepted |
| ADR-022 | Payment Alert replaces a separate mandatory Billing Queue | Accepted |
| ADR-023 | Node.js 24 LTS baseline; exact dependency versions pinned at bootstrap | Accepted |
| ADR-024 | npm workspaces; local development starts from root with `npm run dev` | Accepted |
| ADR-025 | Production applications run as direct Node.js processes managed by PM2 through `ecosystem.config.cjs` | Accepted |
| ADR-026 | Docker, Docker Compose and Caddy are excluded from project runtime and deployment | Accepted |
| ADR-027 | No Pino dependency; use an internal console-based structured logger with PM2 stdout/stderr capture | Accepted |
| ADR-028 | Phase 0 design pack accepted; Phase 1 approved to begin | Accepted |
| ADR-029 | Vitest as the test runner for every workspace, including apps/api via unplugin-swc | Accepted |
| ADR-030 | GitHub Actions adopted as CI provider (promotes TDR-002) | Accepted |
| ADR-031 | Tailwind CSS v4 (CSS-first `@theme` config) for apps/web and packages/ui | Accepted |
| ADR-032 | Env var/port naming convention fixed (WEB_PORT, API_PORT, WORKER_HEALTH_PORT, etc.) | Accepted |
| ADR-033 | `.nvmrc` (24) plus root `engines`/`.npmrc engine-strict=true` machine-enforce Node 24 LTS | Accepted |
| ADR-034 | All shared packages (`packages/*`) compile to CommonJS; apps/api uses Nest's SWC builder | Accepted |
| ADR-035 | Phase 2 identity/auth/RBAC engineering defaults (argon2id, token TTLs, lockout, cookie/CORS/CSRF posture, `UNAUTHENTICATED` error code, outbox lease/retry tiers, `zitaware_crm_app` DB role name) | Accepted |
| ADR-036 | Geography (states/districts/cities) is global statutory reference data, system-seeded with a composite-FK-enforced state/district relationship; district/city seed data is representative, not nationwide-exhaustive, in Phase 2 | Accepted |
| ADR-037 | One generic MasterPort/MastersService (keyed by MasterTypeKey) serves every organization-scoped flat lookup master sharing the common baseline shape, instead of a bespoke service per table | Accepted |
| ADR-038 | MastersService uses a single delegate-registry cast (`as unknown as FlatMasterDelegate`) instead of a switch-per-operation, once the registry grew past 3 types; `Prisma` is re-exported from `@zitaware/database` so apps/api can reference `Prisma.TransactionClient` | Accepted |
| ADR-039 | Post-2.10 schema hardening: Document.documentCategoryId (org-consistent composite FK, entity-type validated in-service), Employee.userId unique + org-consistent composite FK, Employee.status is the sole lifecycle field (isActive removed), Notification is an outbound delivery log only (no read_at) | Accepted |
| ADR-040 | Session-rotation race fixed with a guarded compare-and-swap plus a unique `rotatedFromSessionId`; complete organization-consistency composite-FK audit across all Phase 2 models (17 relations); disposable `zitaware_crm_shadow` database + safeguard script for all future migration authoring | Accepted |
| ADR-041 | Auth cookies' `secure` flag is conditional on `NODE_ENV === 'production'` (was unconditionally `true`, invisible to curl-only testing, but real browsers refuse to store/send a Secure cookie over plain http://localhost); apps/web's tRPC client now sends `credentials: 'include'`; apps/web's global typography was still the raw create-next-app Geist scaffold and is now corrected to docs/15's actual font tokens | Accepted |
| ADR-042 | `npm audit` findings triaged: postcss forced to a patched version via a root `overrides` entry (fixes the only production-reachable CVEs, in next's bundled build-time copy); `@ant-design/nextjs-registry`/`eslint-plugin-import` resolved for free by a lockfile regen; vitest/eslint/@nestjs/cli/@swc/cli/sharp CVEs deliberately deferred — all are dev-tooling-only or require a major bump with no upstream non-breaking fix available yet | Accepted |
| ADR-043 | Phase 2.11 AppShell: shared component names follow docs/16 §7.1 (SidebarNavigation/KpiCard/FilterCard/EntityTable/StatusTag), superseding docs/15 §21's differently-named catalogue for the same responsibilities; server-side session guard via a plain-fetch `getServerSession()` in the `(platform)` layout; four real bugs found and fixed (Tailwind v4 `@source` gap, AntD Menu's icon-className-clobbering, wrong 768/640px breakpoints instead of the mandated 980px, missing brass focus-visible styling); `@ant-design/icons` dropped for hand-written inline SVGs after it broke Turbopack's page-data-collection; one pre-existing, upstream Next.js 16.2.x/16.3.0-preview bug in `apps/web`'s production build left open and disclosed, not fixed | Accepted |
| ADR-044 | Phase 2.11 Masters catalogue: geography (states/districts/cities) becomes editable via `master.manage`-gated create/update/setActive — superseding **only** ADR-036's read-only stance (the composite FK stays) — so admins can add the districts/cities the partial seed omits; geography audit events are attributed to the acting admin's organization (audit_events is org-scoped, geography rows are not); a row's parent (state/district) is fixed at creation (update changes only the name) to keep cities hierarchy-consistent; master-list usage-count column omitted (dependents don't exist until Phase 3); add/edit uses `MasterFormDrawer`; `payment_modes` + `document_categories` entity-type junction deferred | Accepted |
| ADR-045 | Phase 2.11 Roles & Permissions (part 1 of Users/Roles/Permissions): roles become admin-manageable — `isSystem=true` roles (Admin/Accountant/Project Manager) are locked baselines (no edit/delete, guaranteeing `user.manage` always exists on Admin); three new `isSystem=false` starter roles seeded (CEO, Admin Staff, Employee) get full create/edit/delete/permission-matrix CRUD; Super Admin is not a separate tier — it is the existing all-permissions Admin role; deletion is blocked while any user still holds the role | Accepted |
| ADR-046 | Phase 2.11 Users (part 2 of Users/Roles/Permissions): `UsersService` gains `getDetail`/`enable`/`revokeSessions`/`linkEmployee`/`unlinkEmployee`; `invite` gains an optional `employeeId` to link a new user to an existing, unlinked, active employee in the same transaction; `disable` now rejects `INVITED` users (a real pre-existing gap — the DB's own `chk_users_status_password_consistency` check forbids a DISABLED row with no password); `RolesService` gains `clearPermissionOverride` so a per-user override can be reverted back to plain role-inheritance, not just flipped between GRANT/REVOKE | Accepted |
| ADR-047 | Phase 2.11 Employees: `EmployeesService.update()`'s audit event fixed to capture a full old/new diff for every changed field (was silently logging only the new `fullName`, dropping salary/department/designation changes entirely) — this is the resolution to "compensation history," deferred to the future Salary Import screen per docs/16 §7.6; `getDetail` composes the linked user's email/status/roles; the Employees screen deliberately drops the HTML prototype's inline Login-email/Role/Export fields (docs/04's own `FUNCTIONAL_CHANGE` disposition) in favor of a separate "Enable System Access"/"Revoke Access"/"Unlink" flow reusing the existing Users backend (`users.invite/disable/enable/unlinkEmployee`), per ADR-046 | Accepted |
| ADR-048 | Phase 2.11 Legal Entities & GST Registrations: the entire backend already existed (schema/service/router/wiring) from earlier work — only `updateGstRegistration` (trade-name-only edit) was added to close a real field-coverage gap, plus integration tests for the previously-untested `LegalEntity.update`/`setActive` and `GstRegistration.setGstRegistrationActive` paths; the screen restructures the HTML prototype's flattened Company row (GSTIN/PAN/bank-account all on one row) into the correct Legal Entity → GST Registrations 1-to-many the schema already models, per docs/04's own `FUNCTIONAL_CHANGE` disposition; GSTIN-state cross-validation (leading 2 digits vs. the selected state's `gst_state_code`) was considered and explicitly declined by the user — left unvalidated | Accepted |
| ADR-049 | Soft delete for Legal Entities & GST Registrations: `uq_legal_entities_org_pan`/`uq_gst_registrations_gstin` converted from plain to partial unique indexes (`WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`, migration `20260731085614_...`) so a deleted row's PAN/GSTIN can be reused — the exact bug the user reported (deactivating didn't free the identifier, and nothing ever wrote to the already-existing but unused `deletedAt` columns); `delete()`/`deleteGstRegistration()` added, with `delete()` blocked while any GST registrations or financial years still reference the entity (mirrors `RolesService.delete`'s dependent-count guard); no restore action — delete is final by explicit user decision | Accepted |
| ADR-050 | Phase 2.11 Financial Years & Accounting Periods: entire backend (schema/service/router/12-period generation/maker-checker lock-reopen lifecycle) already existed from earlier work — added `setCurrentFinancialYear` to close a real gap (`isCurrent` had no toggle path after creation, only at create time); corrected `docs/09-security-audit.md`'s "Period reopen requires Admin permission" to describe the actual, tested mechanism (shared `accounting_period.lock` permission + a different-actor maker-checker check, not an Admin-only gate) — user's explicit choice over adding a new stricter permission; delete explicitly out of scope (`FinancialYear` has no `deletedAt` column at all, not requested) | Accepted |
| ADR-051 | Phase 2.11 Audit Log (last family in the sequence): built entirely from scratch — no backend existed at all, unlike every prior family. New `audit` port/service/router does server-side paginated, filtered reads (a deliberate first-time departure from every other screen's client-side-fetch-everything convention, since `audit_events` is written to by every mutation in the app and will grow unbounded); actor filter is free-text against the `actor_email` snapshot, not a Users-list dropdown. Found and fixed a real, systemic bug while verifying: `recordAuditEvent` only ever received an explicit `actorEmail` from `IdentityService` (login flow) — all 10 other services calling it across the app never populated it, despite docs/09 requiring an actor-email snapshot on every audit event. Fixed centrally inside `recordAuditEvent` itself (auto-resolves `actorEmail` from `actorId` when not supplied), not by threading a new parameter through every router/service | Accepted |

## Technical decisions to finalize during Phase 1

| ID | Question | Default recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| TDR-003 | Error monitoring | Sentry |
| TDR-004 | Production server and upstream TLS/routing environment | Select based on existing Zitaware infrastructure; Caddy is excluded |
| TDR-005 | Backup retention/RPO/RTO | Define before production |
| TDR-006 | Money rounding policy | Per-line tax rounding with deterministic document rounding line |
| TDR-007 | Inventory costing | Weighted average — **promoted to Accepted in Phase 7.2, see ADR-092** |
| TDR-008 | Manual journal approval | Maker-checker for production |

These defaults may be implemented only when the phase begins and must then be moved to Approved with rationale.

## Business details that can be configured later

These do not block schema design:

- Actual legal entity names and future GST registrations
- Chart of Accounts codes
- Invoice prefixes/number formats
- GST/HSN/SAC defaults
- TDS sections/rates
- Alert lead times
- Retention due-date policies
- Email templates

## Phase 1 decisions

```text
ID: ADR-028
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 0 design pack review completed; all 103 business screens plus 3 shared HTML files verified present and mapped, screen contracts and module-reference entries complete, design system matches HTML baseline, no missing-HTML blocker remains.
Decision: Phase 0 is marked COMPLETE. Phase 1 — Repository and Engineering Foundation is approved to begin.
Consequences: Repository bootstrap, monorepo scaffolding, and engineering foundation work may proceed under the constraints already recorded in ADR-001–ADR-027.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-029
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: docs/10 and docs/14 require test/test:integration turbo pipelines but pin no framework. NestJS's ecosystem conventionally assumes Jest; Next.js and plain-TS packages benefit from Vitest's speed and native ESM/TS support. Confirmed with the user during Phase 1 planning.
Decision: Use Vitest for every workspace, including apps/api. apps/api uses unplugin-swc (with explicit swc options, not the shared .swcrc) so @nestjs/testing's decorator-based DI resolves correctly under Vitest instead of Jest.
Consequences: One test runner across the whole monorepo; apps/api's vitest.config.ts and vitest.integration.config.ts must keep their swc options in sync with any future decorator/target changes.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-030
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: TDR-002 listed GitHub Actions as the default CI provider, pending Phase 1 finalization. docs/11 Phase 1 exit criteria requires CI running lint, typecheck, unit tests, and migration validation. Confirmed with the user during Phase 1 planning.
Decision: Adopt GitHub Actions as the CI provider. .github/workflows/ci.yml runs npm ci, prisma generate, lint, typecheck, test, build, and prisma migrate deploy against a GitHub-hosted Postgres service container.
Consequences: CI configuration lives in .github/workflows/. The ephemeral Postgres service container is a hosted-runner primitive, not a local Docker/Compose runtime dependency, and does not modify ADR-026's exclusion of Docker from local dev/production.
Supersedes: none (promotes TDR-002 from Proposed to Accepted)
```

```text
ID: ADR-031
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: Tailwind CSS major version was unpinned. v4 uses CSS-first @theme configuration instead of a JS config file. Confirmed with the user during Phase 1 planning.
Decision: Use Tailwind CSS v4 for apps/web and packages/ui. Canonical tokens from docs/15-ui-design-system.md section 5.3 are centralized in packages/ui/src/theme/tailwind-theme.css as CSS custom properties plus a Tailwind v4 @theme block exposing semantic utility names (bg-app, bg-surface, text-ink, text-muted, border-ui, bg-brass-soft, etc.).
Consequences: No tailwind.config.js/ts exists; all future screens extend the shared @theme block in packages/ui rather than a JS config. The Ant Design ConfigProvider theme (packages/ui/src/theme/antd-theme.ts) is the exact object from docs/15 section 7.3 and must be kept in sync if design tokens change.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-032
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: docs/14 mandates env var categories (app env, public URLs, per-app ports, DB connection, session secret, object storage, email, allowed origins, log level) but no literal names or port numbers.
Decision: Fix the following names/defaults in .env.example: NODE_ENV, LOG_LEVEL, WEB_PUBLIC_URL, API_PUBLIC_URL, WEB_PORT=3000, API_PORT=4000, WORKER_HEALTH_PORT=4100, DATABASE_URL, SESSION_SECRET, ALLOWED_ORIGINS, S3_ENDPOINT/S3_REGION/S3_BUCKET/S3_ACCESS_KEY_ID/S3_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY, POSTMARK_SERVER_TOKEN/POSTMARK_FROM_EMAIL, PGBOSS_SCHEMA. All validated fail-fast via packages/config's Zod schema at boot.
Consequences: Future code must use these exact names; renaming any of them requires updating packages/config/src/env.ts, .env.example, and .github/workflows/ci.yml together.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-033
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: ADR-023 pinned Node.js 24 LTS but nothing enforced it at install time.
Decision: Add .nvmrc (24) for nvm/fnm users, plus root package.json engines ("node": ">=24 <25", "npm": ">=10") and .npmrc (engine-strict=true) so npm refuses to install on the wrong Node major.
Consequences: Contributors without Node 24 available must install it (this session used Homebrew's node@24 keg-only formula) before npm install succeeds.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-034
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: Packages under packages/* are consumed as raw TypeScript by three different runtimes with incompatible module-resolution rules: Next.js/Turbopack (bundler resolution), Node's native TypeScript execution used by Nest's dev/prod runtime, and tsx (used by apps/worker). Emitting ESM from these packages caused Node's module auto-detection to require exact file extensions on every relative import, breaking apps/api at runtime; emitting extensionless ESM broke under Node's native loader regardless of package.json "type". Also, NestJS CLI's default "nodenext" tsconfig conflicted with the monorepo's shared TypeScript source packages, and its native watch/build path failed against Node 24's native TypeScript stripping.
Decision: All packages/* compile to CommonJS (module: commonjs, moduleResolution: node10) with plain extensionless relative imports, and expose "main"/"exports" pointing at compiled ./dist output (not raw ./src) so every consumer — Turbopack, Nest's runtime, tsx — resolves the same artifact the same way. apps/api uses Nest CLI's official SWC builder (nest-cli.json "builder": "swc", plus apps/api/.swcrc) instead of the default tsc/ts-node path, which resolves CommonJS output correctly under Node 24 without the native-TypeScript-execution extension requirement. turbo.json's "dev" task depends on "^build" so packages are compiled once before any app's dev server starts; each package additionally exposes its own "dev" script (tsc --watch) for live rebuilds during active development.
Consequences: Editing a package's source requires its tsc --watch (or a rebuild) to be running for consuming apps to see the change — turbo dev handles this automatically. packages/ui's CSS export (./theme/tailwind-theme.css) is the one exception that still points at ./src, since Tailwind consumes it directly and it is never compiled by tsc.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-035
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: The approved Phase 2 plan (docs/12 §K "Proposed ADRs") flagged a set of engineering/security defaults as new proposals, not business blockers, to be recorded here rather than treated as approval-blocking questions.
Decision: Phase 2 identity/auth/RBAC uses: argon2id password hashing; access tokens (JWT, 15 min TTL) plus stateful refresh tokens (30 day TTL) with rotation-on-use and family-wide revocation on reuse detection; invitation tokens (72h) and password-reset tokens (1h), both random+hashed (SHA-256), single-use; login lockout after 5 failed attempts for 15 minutes; a refresh-token cookie that is HttpOnly/Secure/SameSite=Lax, with explicit server-side Origin re-validation on auth.login/refresh/acceptInvite/completePasswordReset as the CSRF-equivalent control; a new `UNAUTHENTICATED` tRPC-mapped AppErrorCode (distinct from `FORBIDDEN`) for "no valid session"; outbox lease-based claiming with a 5-minute lease timeout, tiered (not exponential) backoff [0.5, 2, 10, 30, 60] minutes, and dead-lettering after 5 attempts; and `zitaware_crm_app` as the proposed runtime DB role name (its UPDATE/DELETE grants on audit_events are revoked). All values are adjustable without schema impact if the user prefers different numbers.
Consequences: apps/api/src/identity implements this lifecycle end-to-end (identity.service.ts, password.util.ts, token.util.ts, access-token.util.ts), verified by apps/api/src/identity/identity.integration-spec.ts (6 tests: login rejection/lockout, token issuance+verification, refresh rotation+reuse-detection, logout revocation). Changing any TTL/threshold/backoff tier only requires updating packages/config/src/env.ts defaults, not code.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-036
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: The approved Phase 2 plan classifies states/districts/cities as global, statutory, system-seeded reference data (no organization_id), with a corrected composite-FK design (round-2 correction item #2) so a city's denormalized state_id is DB-enforced against its district's actual state, not merely checked in the application layer. The plan required this composite FK but did not mandate full nationwide district/city data population as Phase 2 scope.
Decision: `districts` carries a supporting `uq_districts_id_state_id UNIQUE (id, state_id)` constraint; `cities` enforces `chk_cities_state_matches_district FOREIGN KEY (district_id, state_id) REFERENCES districts(id, state_id)`. Both districts and cities also get normalized (case/whitespace-insensitive) per-parent-scope unique name indexes. The seed script loads all 36 official GST state/UT codes in full, but only a representative subset of districts/cities (Maharashtra: Mumbai City, Pune, Nagpur; Gujarat: Ahmedabad, Surat; Delhi: New Delhi; Karnataka: Bengaluru) — enough to exercise the constraints and support development/testing. Full nationwide district/city population is treated as a follow-up data-loading task, not Phase 2 schema/API work.
Consequences: Any environment needing complete India-wide geography coverage (e.g. a GST registration in a district not yet seeded) must load additional district/city rows before that workflow can be used — this is a data gap, not a schema or constraint gap. apps/api/src/geography exposes read-only geography.listStates/listDistricts/listCities (gated by `master.read`); verified by apps/api/src/geography/geography.integration-spec.ts (4 tests, including a composite-FK rejection test).
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-037
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: The approved Phase 2 plan classifies internal_departments/designations/employment_types (2.4), government_body_types/lead_types/lead_sources/tender_portals/project_types/security_instrument_purposes/security_instrument_types (2.5), and document_categories/expense_categories/penalty_categories/payment_modes (2.8) as all sharing one identical common-baseline shape (id/organization_id/code/name/description/sort_order/is_active/audit-stamps/version), and the plan's own §B item 4 describes a lookup master as riding "in the generic masters API only" — implying a genuinely generic API, not table-specific services repeated 7+ times.
Decision: packages/contracts/src/ports/master.port.ts defines one MasterPort keyed by a `MasterTypeKey` string union (extended as each sub-phase adds its tables); apps/api/src/masters/masters.service.ts implements it with a single switch-per-operation dispatch to the correct Prisma delegate, sharing one row-to-MasterRecord mapping function. This avoids both a fragile `any`-cast generic-delegate abstraction (Prisma's per-model types are too different to unify safely) and 7+ near-duplicate service files.
Consequences: Adding a new flat lookup master (2.5, 2.8) means adding one case to each switch in masters.service.ts and extending MasterTypeKey/the router's Zod enum — not a new file. Geography (states/districts/cities) is intentionally NOT part of this port since it is global/statutory/system-seeded/hierarchical, not organization-scoped-flat.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-038
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: Sub-phase 2.5 added 7 more tables to MasterTypeKey (10 total), which would have grown ADR-037's switch-per-operation dispatch to 40 duplicated cases across list/create/update/setActive, with 2.8 still to add 4 more. TypeScript cannot call a union of Prisma's per-model delegate types with a single generic argument object — the per-model Where/Data input types are structurally distinct even though the underlying columns are identical — so a fully type-safe generic dispatcher isn't achievable without codegen.
Decision: Replace the switch-per-operation with one `getDelegate(client, type)` function containing a single `Record<MasterTypeKey, unknown>` lookup and one `as unknown as FlatMasterDelegate` cast, where FlatMasterDelegate is a small hand-written structural interface (findMany/findFirstOrThrow/create/updateMany) covering only the operations MastersService actually calls. `Prisma` is re-exported from `@zitaware/database`'s client.ts/index.ts so apps/api can type `MastersDbClient = PrismaClient | Prisma.TransactionClient` without adding `@prisma/client` as a direct apps/api dependency.
Consequences: Adding a new flat lookup master now means adding one line to the registry object and one line to MasterTypeKey/the router's Zod enum — not touching any method body. The one cast is contained to a single function, documented with the rationale above; every other line in MastersService remains fully type-checked. Verified by apps/api/src/masters/masters.integration-spec.ts's "dispatches correctly for a different master type (lead_types) via the same registry" test, which also confirms no cross-table leakage.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-039
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: A user-conducted review of the sub-phase 2.0–2.10 schema surfaced four points needing explicit business/data-model decisions rather than silent inference: (1) how a Document is classified vs. which business entity it's attached to; (2) User↔Employee cardinality; (3) whether Notification is an end-user-facing inbox or an outbound delivery log; (4) whether Employee needed both `status` and `is_active`. The same review also corrected an inaccurate claim repeated in this project's own migration comments: composite unique constraints and composite foreign keys (e.g. `(id, state_id)`) ARE expressible natively in Prisma's schema DSL — only functional (`lower(trim(...))`) and partial (`WHERE`) indexes genuinely require raw SQL. The Phase 2.3–2.9 geography/other hand-written composite constraints were written as raw SQL under the mistaken belief that Prisma couldn't express them at all; that belief is now corrected and the new constraints below are modeled natively.
Decision:
(1) Document gets `documentCategoryId` (nullable — classifies the document itself, e.g. "Tender Notice"), enforced via a native Prisma composite FK `(document_category_id, organization_id) -> document_categories(id, organization_id)` (trivially satisfied when null). `DocumentLink.entityType` remains the separate concern of which business entity the document is attached to; `DocumentsService.linkToEntity` validates entityType against `document_category_entity_types` for the document's category before creating a link (no rows for that category = applies to every entity type, per the established design; a document with no category skips this check entirely).
(2) `Employee.userId` stays nullable (an employee may exist without application access; a user — e.g. a platform administrator — may exist without an employee record) but is now `@unique`: at most one employee record per user. When populated, a native Prisma composite FK `(user_id, organization_id) -> users(id, organization_id)` enforces that the linked user belongs to the same organization as the employee.
(3) Notification stays an outbound delivery log only — no `read_at` added. The "Notifications" platform-module nav item (docs/02-system-architecture.md) is renamed "Notification Delivery Log" to remove any ambiguity with an end-user inbox. A real in-app notification inbox, if ever needed, is a separate future model, not an overload of this delivery-tracking table.
(4) `Employee.is_active` is removed entirely (migration `DROP COLUMN`, no compatibility alias — there is no production data). `status` (`ACTIVE`/`INACTIVE`, `chk_employees_status`) is the sole lifecycle source of truth; `EmployeesService.list`'s default filter and `setStatus` both read/write `status` only.
Consequences: Both new composite FKs are declared as ordinary Prisma `@relation`/`@@unique` fields (not hand-written migration SQL), and correctly do NOT trigger Prisma's recurring "propose to drop the hand-added constraint" problem that has affected every migration since phase2_03_geography — because Prisma now has full knowledge of these constraints. The existing geography composite FK is NOT retroactively converted (out of scope for this change); a future cleanup could migrate it to the same native-Prisma approach to eliminate that recurring manual-strip step. Verified by new integration tests: cross-organization document-category rejection, category-restricted vs. unrestricted entity-type linking, duplicate/cross-org employee-user linking rejection.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-040
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: A follow-up user-conducted hardening pass identified a real, security-relevant TOCTOU race in `IdentityService.refresh()` (two concurrent refresh calls on the same token could both mint a successor session, since the revoked-check happened at read time, not at write time), plus asked for a complete organization-consistency composite-FK audit across every Phase 2 model — not just the four tables already converted in ADR-039 — wherever a child and its parent are both organization-scoped. It also asked for a safe migration-development procedure with a disposable shadow database, following the incident recorded in ADR-039's own commit history where `prisma migrate diff --shadow-database-url` was mistakenly pointed at the real local dev database.
Decision:
(1) `IdentityService.refresh()` now performs the session-revocation write as `tx.session.updateMany({ where: { id: session.id, revokedAt: null }, data: {...} })` and checks `count === 1` before proceeding to mint a successor — the loser of a concurrent race sees `count === 0` and is rejected with `UNAUTHENTICATED`, never silently creating a second valid successor. `Session.rotatedFromSessionId` is now `@unique` (nullable-unique — Postgres allows unlimited NULLs, enforces uniqueness only among non-null values) as DB-level defense-in-depth on top of the application-layer fix.
(2) 17 relations across 11 child tables were converted from plain single-column FKs (or, in three cases — `AccountingPeriodTransition.accountingPeriodId`, `UserRole.userId`, `UserPermissionOverride.userId` — no FK/relation at all) to native-Prisma composite FKs enforcing organization consistency: `UserInvitation`/`PasswordResetToken`/`Session`/`UserRole`/`UserPermissionOverride`.userId → `users(id, organization_id)`; `UserRole.roleId` → `roles(id, organization_id)`; `GstRegistration`/`FinancialYear`.legalEntityId → `legal_entities(id, organization_id)`; `AccountingPeriod.financialYearId` → `financial_years(id, organization_id)`; `AccountingPeriodTransition.accountingPeriodId` → `accounting_periods(id, organization_id)`; `Employee`.internalDepartmentId/designationId/employmentTypeId → their respective master tables; `DocumentLink.documentId` → `documents(id, organization_id)`; `Notification.sourceOutboxEventId` → `outbox_events(id, organization_id)`; `Notification.recipientUserId` → `users(id, organization_id)`; `AuditEvent.legalEntityId` → `legal_entities(id, organization_id)`. Every parent model involved gained a supporting `@@unique([id, organizationId])`. `RolePermission` and `DocumentCategoryEntityType` were confirmed NOT to need conversion — neither carries its own `organization_id` column, so there is no second organization value to cross-check (org-scoping is entirely inherited through their parent). The existing `districts`/`cities` composite FK (ADR-036) remains the one NOT-yet-converted case, by explicit scope decision, not oversight.
(3) `schema.prisma`'s `datasource db` block now declares `shadowDatabaseUrl = env("SHADOW_DATABASE_URL")`, pointing at a genuinely separate, disposable `zitaware_crm_shadow` database (created alongside `zitaware_crm_dev` on the same local Postgres instance — no new service, consistent with ADR-026's Docker exclusion). `packages/database/scripts/assert-shadow-database-safe.mjs` refuses to proceed if `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` is unset or identical to `DATABASE_URL`, and is wired as a precondition into new `prisma:migrate:dev` and `prisma:migrate:diff` npm scripts — the latter supplies `--shadow-database-url` from the verified-safe env var so a human is never in a position to type the wrong value in. `prisma migrate deploy`/`generate`/`status` (what CI actually runs) were confirmed to work unmodified with `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` unset, so no CI changes were needed.
Consequences: Verified by a new `identity.integration-spec.ts` test that fires two concurrent `refresh()` calls on the same token and asserts exactly one succeeds and exactly one session claims the predecessor's `rotatedFromSessionId`. All 17 composite FKs were verified directly against Postgres (manual cross-organization insert attempts, each confirmed rejected with a foreign-key violation) in addition to the full existing test suite (36 apps/api + 3 apps/worker integration tests) passing unchanged — the conversions required zero application-code changes beyond the schema itself, since every existing service already always supplies both the parent id and the organization id together. Future migration authoring must use `npm run prisma:migrate:dev`/`prisma:migrate:diff` from `packages/database`, never a hand-typed `--shadow-database-url` flag (see docs/07-database-conventions.md "Migration development procedure").
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-041
Date: 2026-07-28
Status: Accepted
Context: Building the first Phase 2B frontend screens (auth: login/accept-invite/forgot-password/reset-password) surfaced two bugs invisible to all prior curl-only backend testing across sub-phases 2.1-2.10, plus one unrelated foundation gap. curl does not enforce the browser rule that a `Secure` cookie is refused over plain HTTP, so ADR-035's `secure: true` cookie setting was never actually exercised against a real browser until now. Separately, apps/web's `layout.tsx`/`globals.css` still carried the raw `create-next-app` scaffold (Geist font, generic light/dark background variables) from Phase 1, never reconciled against docs/15-ui-design-system.md's actual typography tokens.
Decision: `packages/contracts/src/routers/auth.ts`'s `cookieOptions.secure` is now `process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production'` instead of unconditionally `true` — real browsers on `http://localhost` (dev) need a non-Secure cookie; production (served over HTTPS) still requires Secure. `apps/web/src/server/provider.tsx`'s tRPC `httpBatchLink` now passes a custom `fetch` with `credentials: 'include'`, since apps/web (`:3000`) and apps/api (`:4000`) are different origins and the browser would otherwise never send the session cookie cross-origin even once set correctly. `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx` no longer loads Geist; `globals.css` now sets `body`'s font-family to docs/15 §6.1's body stack (`-apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont, "Segoe UI", Roboto, "Helvetica Neue", Arial, sans-serif`) and exposes a `--font-display` token (`'Calibri', 'Segoe UI', Candara, 'Trebuchet MS', sans-serif`) for page/record titles, replacing the unrelated light/dark background scaffold with the actual `--color-app`/`--color-text` design tokens.
Consequences: Every future Phase 2B screen inherits the corrected font foundation automatically — this had to be fixed before, not after, building more screens on top of it. Verified via a real curl-with-cookie-jar functional test against the running dev servers (login → Set-Cookie → auth.me succeeds → logout → auth.me correctly fails UNAUTHENTICATED) and a full `next build` production build. No browser-automation tool is available in this environment, so actual JS-driven form submission and pixel-level visual verification in a rendered browser were NOT performed — this is disclosed as a real gap against CLAUDE.md's browser-testing requirement, not silently skipped.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-042
Date: 2026-07-29
Status: Accepted
Context: Adding `@playwright/test` as a local devDependency surfaced `npm install`'s standing count of 17-21 dependency vulnerabilities, which had never been triaged. A per-package review found the findings fall into three groups: (a) `postcss` (moderate/high — XSS via unescaped `</style>`, arbitrary file read and path traversal via `sourceMappingURL`) reachable through both `next`'s own bundled copy and `vite`/`@tailwindcss/postcss`, fixable by a patch-level bump within the same 8.x line; (b) `@ant-design/nextjs-registry` and `eslint-plugin-import`, each with a non-major fix already permitted by their existing `^range`; (c) a cluster requiring a major version bump with no narrower fix available — `vitest` 2.x→4.x (the one CRITICAL finding, GHSA-5xrq-8626-4rwp, but only exploitable if `vitest --ui` is started and exposed to an untrusted network, which this project's scripts never do), `eslint` 9.x→10.x (dev-only lint tool; `eslint-config-next`'s own peer range already permits it, but this project deliberately did not attempt an unreviewed major bump of the linter), and `@nestjs/cli`/`@swc/cli` (dev-only build tooling; npm's own suggested "fix" is a downgrade to an ancient release, meaning no real upstream fix exists yet). `sharp`'s CVE (GHSA-f88m-g3jw-g9cj, libvips) was investigated separately: `next@16.2.12` declares `sharp: "^0.34.5"` as an optional dependency — a caret range on a pre-1.0 package only permits patch releases, so forcing an override to the patched `0.35.3` would fall outside next's own declared compatibility and risk silently breaking `next/image`'s optimization path; deferred until `next` itself bumps its bundled range.
Decision: Root `package.json` gains `"overrides": { "postcss": "8.5.24" }`, forcing the patched version everywhere in the tree including inside `next`'s own `node_modules/next/node_modules/postcss` nested copy (confirmed via `npm ls postcss` after a full lockfile regen — applying the override required deleting both `node_modules` and `package-lock.json` together and reinstalling from scratch; a partial reinstall left a stale, override-violating resolution in place, and a bare `npm audit fix` afterwards was found to reintroduce the same stale resolution by re-triggering install without changing any declared version, so `npm audit fix`/`--force` were deliberately not run again after establishing the clean state). No override was added for `sharp` (see Context). `vitest`/`eslint`/`@nestjs/cli`/`@swc/cli` are left at their current versions, to be revisited as a dedicated, individually-tested major-version-bump task rather than folded into this pass.
Consequences: `npm audit` findings went from 21 (4 moderate, 16 high, 1 critical) to 19 (3 moderate, 15 high, 1 critical) with zero application-code behavior change — confirmed by a full lint + typecheck + build + unit-test + integration-test pass across every workspace after the dependency changes. Two unrelated latent issues surfaced during that verification and were fixed in the same pass, not deferred: (1) a stale `.next` build cache produced a `/_global-error` prerendering crash on `apps/web`'s production build — cleared and rebuilt clean, confirmed by a version-diff of the old vs. new lockfile that no React/Next/AntD package actually changed version, so this was pre-existing cache staleness, not a dependency regression; (2) `apps/api/src/rbac/users.integration-spec.ts`'s `afterAll` deleted `outboxEvent` rows before `notification` rows, violating the `fk_notifications_outbox_event_org_consistent` composite FK added in ADR-040 — this test file was simply missed when that FK was introduced; fixed by deleting `notification` first, matching the pattern already correct elsewhere. The remaining 19 findings are tracked here as a deliberate, reasoned deferral, not an oversight — revisit `sharp` when `next` bumps its own bundled range, and treat the `vitest`/`eslint` major bumps as their own tested change.

**Correction (ADR-043):** this ADR's item (1) above — "cleared and rebuilt clean... pre-existing cache staleness" — is now known to be wrong. The 2.11 AppShell increment reproduced the identical `/_global-error` crash reliably (not intermittently) across a clean-checkout baseline, a bare root layout, both Turbopack and webpack, `next@16.2.0` through the latest `16.3.0-preview.10`. It is a genuine unfixed upstream Next.js bug, not cache staleness — see ADR-043.
Supersedes: none
```

```text
ID: ADR-043
Date: 2026-07-29
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 2.11's AppShell — the authenticated frame every remaining Phase 2B screen (masters catalogue, Users/Roles, Employees, Legal Entities, Financial Years, Audit Log) will render inside — required deriving `AppShell`/`SidebarNavigation`/`Topbar`/`PageHeader`/`KpiCard`/`FilterCard`/`EntityTable`/`MasterFormDrawer`/`StatusTag` from `references/ui/html/01-platform/shell.html` per docs/16 §7.1, plus a real server-side session guard per docs/04 §5.2. Building and verifying it (real login, real 1440px/mobile screenshots, keyboard pass) surfaced four genuine bugs, none visible from lint/typecheck/unit tests alone, and confirmed one pre-existing upstream bug is not fixable from this repo.
Decision:
(1) **Naming**: component names follow docs/16 §7.1 verbatim (`SidebarNavigation`, `KpiCard`, `FilterCard`, `EntityTable`, `StatusTag`) rather than docs/15 §21's differently-named catalogue for the same responsibilities (`PermissionMenu`, `StatTile`, `FilterBar`, `DataTable`, `StatusPill`) — one component set, two docs disagreeing on names; docs/15 §21 needs a follow-up edit to reconcile, out of scope here.
(2) **Session guard**: `apps/web/src/server/session.ts`'s `getServerSession()` reads the `zitaware_crm_access_token` cookie via `next/headers` `cookies()` and calls the API's `auth.me` directly (manually forwarding the cookie — apps/web and apps/api are different origins) from the `(platform)/layout.tsx` Server Component, `redirect('/login')`-ing before any protected markup renders. `auth.me` was extended with a new `AuthPort.getProfile()` method returning `displayName`/`organizationName`, since the shell's brand area and user menu need real data docs/04 §5.1 requires ("replace prototype branding... with configurable Zitaware organization data"), which the prior `{ userId, organizationId, permissions }` shape didn't carry.
(3) **`@ant-design/icons` dropped**: importing it directly (not through antd's own internal usage) broke Turbopack's page-data-collection step across unrelated `(auth)` routes with `TypeError: (0, b.createContext) is not a function` — confirmed by removing the import and switching to hand-written inline SVG icons (`packages/ui/src/components/icons.tsx`), matching `shell.html`'s own approach (raw inline `<svg>`, no icon library) rather than fighting the bundler.
(4) **Tailwind v4 `@source` gap**: Tailwind's automatic content detection only walks `apps/web`'s own directory tree — every class used exclusively inside the sibling `packages/ui/src` workspace (responsive variants, arbitrary grid/height values) silently generated no CSS at all (`grid-template-columns: none`, `display: block` where `md:hidden` should apply), while the page still partially "looks right" because AntD's own component-level theme tokens (Menu/Layout dark background) independently cover part of the same area — masking the gap until a real screenshot was inspected closely and cross-checked against `document.styleSheets`. Fixed with `@source "../../../../packages/ui/src";` in `apps/web/src/app/globals.css`.
(5) **AntD `Menu`'s icon-className-clobbering**: AntD's `Menu` clones each `icon:` element and replaces its `className` entirely with `"ant-menu-item-icon"`, silently discarding a Tailwind size class passed on the icon — the icon then renders at its unconstrained natural size (measured at 1392×1392px, filling the viewport). Fixed by sizing icons via `width`/`height` SVG attributes in the shared `Icon` wrapper (survives the clone) instead of `className`.
(6) **Wrong breakpoint**: the shell was built against Tailwind's default `md:`/`sm:` breakpoints (768px/640px) instead of docs/15 §19's mandated `980px` — fixed with `min-[980px]:` arbitrary variants throughout `AppShell`/`Topbar`.
(7) **Missing brass focus-visible styling** (docs/15 §20: "Focus is visibly brass"): the mobile-nav/alerts/user-menu icon buttons relied on the browser's default blue outline; added an explicit `focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-brass` treatment. The Topbar search field was a bare native `<input>` with `outline-none` and no replacement focus style at all — replaced with AntD's own `Input` (already theme-configured for a brass border + brass-soft glow on focus in `antd-theme.ts`), consistent with docs/15 §7.1 ("Ant Design owns... inputs").
(8) **Pre-existing upstream Next.js bug, left open**: `apps/web`'s production build fails prerendering the auto-generated `/_global-error` page (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'useContext')`). Confirmed via exhaustive bisection: reproduces on a clean checkout with zero AppShell code, on a completely bare root layout, with both `--webpack` and Turbopack, on `next@16.2.0` (first patch of the pinned minor) through `16.3.0-preview.10` (newest available release). `next build --debug-prerender` (which disables the `experimental.prerenderEarlyExit` optimization along with forcing `NODE_ENV=development`) does not crash, but is explicitly unsuitable for a real production build; setting `experimental.prerenderEarlyExit: false` alone in production mode made it worse (broke every route, not just the two auto-generated ones). Per explicit user decision, left open and disclosed rather than chased further — `npm run build` is red for `apps/web` specifically until Next.js ships a fix or the pinned version is deliberately changed.
Consequences: `packages/ui` gained `react`/`react-dom`/`@testing-library/*`/`jsdom` devDependencies and its own `vitest.config.ts` (jsdom environment) for the 20 new component tests. Every other workspace's `build` passes clean; `apps/web`'s own `lint`/`typecheck`/`test`/`test:integration` all pass — only its production `next build` is affected, and only by item (8). Verified end-to-end with a real Playwright login against the running dev stack: desktop (1440px) and mobile (900px, below 980px) layouts, the off-canvas drawer, permission-filtered navigation (bootstrap admin's 17 permissions correctly show all 7 nav items across two groups), and keyboard-focus brass rings on every interactive control. `(platform)/page.tsx` replaces the old temporary root `page.tsx` (moved, not duplicated — a route group's `page.tsx` maps to the same URL, so both could not coexist).
Supersedes: ADR-042's `/_global-error` "pre-existing cache staleness" diagnosis (see the correction appended to that ADR above).

**Correction (ADR-052, 2026-08-02):** item (8) above is now known to be resolved. During Phase 2.12 final verification, `npm --workspace apps/web run build` succeeded cleanly (exit 0, all 20 routes generated, including the two auto-generated `/_global-error`/`/_not-found` pages) against the same pinned `next@16.2.12` that previously crashed — reproduced three times across this session, including twice after a full `.next`/`.turbo` cache removal. The likely cause: commit `1959386` (the dependency security audit triage, landed the day after this ADR) regenerated `package-lock.json` to pick up a patched `postcss` and free-riding fixes for `@ant-design/nextjs-registry`/`eslint-plugin-import` — the resulting transitive-dependency shuffle plausibly carries whatever internal React-context/prerendering path `/_global-error` was tripping over, even though no `next` version bump itself was involved. Not re-bisected in isolation to confirm the exact transitive package, since the fix is already in place and working — `apps/web`'s production build is no longer a known issue. See ADR-052.
```

```text
ID: ADR-044
Date: 2026-07-30
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 2.11's Masters catalogue slice needed the states/districts/cities screens. ADR-036 made geography read-only (statutory, system-seeded) and the seed loads only a partial India dataset, so an administrator cannot currently add a district or city the seed omits — a real gap once a GST registration or address needs an unseeded location. The user chose (over keeping it read-only) to make geography editable this increment. Separately, the master-all prototype and the per-master list prototype both show counts/columns that Phase 2 cannot populate as drawn.
Decision:
(1) Geography gains `master.manage`-gated `create`/`update`/`setActive` for all three levels (`GeographyService`, `geographyRouter`). This supersedes ONLY ADR-036's read-only aspect — the `cities(district_id, state_id) -> districts(id, state_id)` composite FK and the normalized-name unique indexes from ADR-036 stay and are relied on. Hierarchy is validated in-service (a district requires an active state; a city requires an active district and derives its `state_id` from that district, never accepting a caller-supplied one) on top of the DB composite FK. A row's parent is fixed at creation — `update` changes only the name (and, for states, code/gst_state_code) — so a city can never drift out of hierarchy consistency by re-parenting. `gst_state_code` is editable in Phase 2 (no tax documents exist yet to reference it; the "immutable once referenced" rule is deferred to the phase that adds them).
(2) Audit attribution: geography rows carry no `organization_id`, but `audit_events` requires one. Geography mutations record the audit event under the **acting admin's** organization. This is unambiguous in single-org Phase 2 (ADR-004). Multi-org governance — who may edit global statutory data, and whose audit trail it belongs to — is deferred to whichever phase introduces a second organization / super-admin scope.
(3) Catalogue and list presentation: the masters-all tile count is the number of records in each master (`masters.getCatalog` for the 12 generic types; `geography.getCounts` for states/districts/cities). The per-master list's prototype "usage count" column (dependent leads/projects/employees) is **omitted** — those dependents don't exist until Phase 3. Add/edit uses `MasterFormDrawer` (docs/16 §7.1's permitted "equivalent page section") rather than the prototype's inline form. `payment_modes` (global, behavior-flagged, no API) and `document_categories`' entity-type applicability junction (a relation, not a scalar column — ADR-039) are explicitly deferred to their own follow-ups, called out so they are known rather than silent omissions.
Consequences: Every editable schema column on the shipped master screens is present in its create/update form — verified against schema.prisma per the field-coverage rule: the 12 generic types (code[create-only]/name/description/sort_order/status), states (name/code/**gst_state_code**/status — the previously-missed field), districts (name/state-context/status), cities (name/district-context/derived-state/status). Verified via Playwright against the running stack: catalogue tiles with real counts, a district created under Maharashtra with hierarchy validation, states showing correct Active status and the GST State Code column, zero console errors. Geography CRUD covered by nine `geography.integration-spec.ts` tests (create district+city with derived state and audit attribution, orphan-parent rejection, duplicate rejection, version conflict, deactivate/list-filtering). A stale compiled `apps/api/dist` (the lightweight-run trade-off) briefly showed all states "Inactive" because the old build lacked the new `isActive` mapping — resolved by a forced rebuild, a reminder that backend source edits require `npm run build:backend` in that run mode.
Supersedes: ADR-036's read-only geography stance only (the composite FK and unique indexes from ADR-036 remain in force).
```

```text
ID: ADR-045
Date: 2026-07-30
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 2.11's next required screen (docs/04 §18) is Users, Roles & Permissions. The seed shipped only 3 roles (Admin/Accountant/Project Manager), all `isSystem=true` and uneditable, but the business names six personas (Employee, Admin Staff, Accountant, CEO, Project Manager, Super Admin) that don't map 1:1. The user asked for a scalable, admin-defined role model rather than more hard-coded roles, and asked to clarify whether "Super Admin" is a distinct tier from the existing all-permissions "Admin".
Decision:
(1) Roles gain full CRUD (`create`/`update`/`setPermissions`/`delete`) alongside the existing `list`/`getPermissions`/`assignRole`/`removeRole`/`setPermissionOverride`, gated `user.manage` for mutations and `role.read` for reads (matching the existing assignRole/override gating). A `roles.listPermissionCatalog` endpoint exposes the 17-entry seeded `Permission` table (key/resource/action/description) to drive a permission-matrix UI grouped by resource.
(2) `isSystem=true` roles (Admin, Accountant, Project Manager) are **locked**: `update`/`setPermissions`/`delete` reject them with `VALIDATION_FAILED`. This guarantees Admin always retains `user.manage` — no code path can lock every admin out of RBAC control. To adapt Accountant/Project Manager's permission set, an admin clones it into a new custom role rather than editing the baseline in place.
(3) Three new `isSystem=false` starter roles are seeded, tunable via the same CRUD as any admin-created role: **CEO** (every `*.read` permission — cross-functional oversight, no `*.manage`), **Admin Staff** (`master.read`, `document.read`/`upload`, `employee.read`, `user.read` — clerical data entry), **Employee** (`organization.read`, `document.read`/`upload` — minimal self-service login). Seeding is idempotent (`role.upsert` keyed on `organizationId_name`, matching the existing 3 roles' pattern).
(4) **Super Admin is not a separate tier.** The existing all-permissions `Admin` role (already holding `user.manage` and, now, role-CRUD) *is* the super-admin role in single-org Phase 2 (ADR-004). A true cross-organization super-admin scope is deferred to the future multi-org phase.
(5) Role deletion is refused while `userRole` rows still reference it (`memberCount > 0`) — an admin must reassign/remove those grants first; this mirrors the existing pattern of never leaving a dangling FK-referenced row implicitly cleaned up.
Consequences: `RoleSummary`/`RoleDetail` gained `memberCount` (and `RoleDetail` gained `permissionKeys`/`version` for optimistic-concurrency updates). The Roles screen (`(platform)/roles/page.tsx`) is a `NEW_REQUIRED` screen with no HTML prototype (docs/04 §18) — built from the shared shell/card/table/drawer language (docs/15), not copied from a prototype file. Its permission-matrix drawer body is a keyed, freshly-mounted subcomponent (`RoleFormBody`) that derives its initial form/checkbox state directly from already-loaded query data at mount time — an `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` `set-state-in-effect` rule (new in this codebase) forbids syncing async query data into local state via `useEffect`, so the drawer gates rendering on `!detail.isLoading` and remounts (via `key`) per role/mode instead. Verified with 6 new `roles.integration-spec.ts` tests (catalog count, create+getDetail round-trip, unknown-permission-key rejection, setPermissions replacement, system-role-mutation rejection, delete-with-no-members vs. delete-with-members) and a live Playwright pass against the running lightweight stack: all 6 seeded roles show correct System/Custom tags and member counts, a custom role was created with a real permission subset and confirmed in the database, a system role opened fully read-only (disabled fields, zero submit buttons), zero console errors.
Supersedes: none — additive to the existing RBAC backend (Phase 2.1/2.2).
```

```text
ID: ADR-046
Date: 2026-07-30
Status: Accepted
Context: Second and final commit of the Users, Roles & Permissions family. The screen contract (docs/04 §18) calls for invite/disable, role grants, per-user overrides, project scope and session revoke, with an optional Employee link. The user's explicit clarification set firm rules: all internal staff must have an Employee record; employees may exist without logins; login users may exist without an Employee record (CEO example); the link is nullable/unique and manageable from either side; an employee's designation/project assignment must never auto-create or imply login access; revoking access disables the User without touching the Employee record or its history.
Decision:
(1) `UsersService.invite` gains an optional `employeeId`. When present, the same transaction that creates the User also sets `Employee.userId` — but only if that employee is active, in-org, and currently unlinked; otherwise the whole invite is rejected (`VALIDATION_FAILED`), never silently dropping the link. `linkEmployee`/`unlinkEmployee` provide the same link management for an *existing* user (from the Users screen), independent of invite. This is deliberately the same backend the still-pending Employees screen's future "Enable system access" button will call — no duplicate code path.
(2) `getDetail` composes a user's roles, per-user overrides, resolved effective permissions (reusing `RolesService.getEffectivePermissions`, not reimplemented), and linked employee into one read — the Users screen's detail drawer is one query, not four.
(3) `enable` only reverses a `DISABLED` user who already has a `passwordHash` (i.e., who had actually accepted an invitation before being disabled) — a user disabled while still `INVITED` (no password) cannot be "enabled" the same way; `reinvite` is the correct next step for them. This surfaced a **real pre-existing gap**: `disable()` (built in Phase 2.1) had no status guard at all, and would crash the whole transaction on Postgres's own `chk_users_status_password_consistency` check constraint if ever called on an `INVITED` user (`DISABLED` status paired with a null `password_hash` violates it) — instead of a clean `VALIDATION_FAILED`. `disable()` now explicitly rejects `INVITED` users with a clear message pointing at revoking the invitation instead.
(4) `revokeSessions` is a standalone admin action (distinct from `disable`, which already revoked sessions as a side effect) — reuses the identical `session.updateMany({ revokedAt: null })` pattern already used by `disable` and the password-reset flow, just under its own `USER_SESSIONS_REVOKED` audit action and without touching `status`.
(5) `RolesService.clearPermissionOverride` is added alongside the existing `setPermissionOverride`. Without it, the per-user override UI would be a one-way ratchet — an admin could GRANT or REVOKE a permission but never return it to plain role-inheritance. It is a thin complement (delete the override row, audit `USER_PERMISSION_OVERRIDE_CLEARED`, no-op if none exists), not a new capability class.
(6) `UserSummary` gains `roleNames`/`employeeLinked` so the Users list can show both without a second round-trip per row.
Consequences: Both the Users list and detail drawer are `NEW_REQUIRED` screens (docs/04 §18) built from the shared shell/table/drawer language, no HTML prototype. The detail drawer follows the same keyed-fresh-mount pattern as ADR-045's role editor (`UserDetailBody`, gated on `!detail.isLoading`) for the same `set-state-in-effect` reason. Verified with 7 new `users.integration-spec.ts` tests (getDetail composition, invite-with-employeeId linking + already-linked rejection + disable-keeps-the-link, enable/disable status guards including the new INVITED-disable rejection, revokeSessions, link/unlinkEmployee) and 1 new `roles.integration-spec.ts` test (clearPermissionOverride round-trip) — 57 integration tests pass workspace-wide. Verified live via Playwright against the running lightweight stack: invited a user with a role and a linked employee (picker correctly showed only the unlinked, active employee), list showed the role tag and "Linked" indicator, detail drawer's Grant→Revoke→Inherit cycle on a permission override was confirmed to actually change the rendered "(not granted)" state and the Inherit button's presence at each step, zero console errors throughout.
Supersedes: none — additive to the existing RBAC backend; the `disable()` INVITED-status guard is a bug fix, not a behavior change to any documented/intended prior contract.
```

```text
ID: ADR-047
Date: 2026-07-30
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 2.11 (part 5) — Employees. Most of the backend (`EmployeesService.list/listLinkable/create/update/setStatus`) already existed from earlier work; the frontend was entirely unbuilt. Planning surfaced two things needing an explicit decision: the user's earlier instruction that salary revisions go to "compensation history" rather than overwriting the salary (no such table exists; docs/16 §7.6 already earmarks a separate future "Salary Import" screen as the authoritative monthly-salary source, with `displayed_salary_amount` explicitly non-authoritative/reference-only), and the canonical HTML prototype (`references/ui/html/06-hr-payroll/employee-master.html`) embedding Login-email/Role fields and an Export button directly into employee create/list, which conflicts with the already-established Employee/User separation (ADR-039/045/046).
Decision:
(1) **Compensation history, resolved as: fix the audit trail, defer real history.** `EmployeesService.update()`'s `recordAuditEvent` call was a real, independently-found bug — it only ever logged `{ fullName: input.fullName }` as `newValues`, with no `oldValues` at all, silently dropping every other changed field including `displayedSalaryAmount` from the audit trail. It now computes a full old/new diff for every field actually touched by the call (department, designation, employment type, full name, reimbursable, salary, user link), giving a complete, queryable revision history via the existing `audit_events` table (visible later on the Audit Log screen) with zero new schema. A dedicated compensation-history table remains deferred to when Salary Import is built, per docs/16 §7.6 — user's explicit choice over adding that model now.
(2) **HTML prototype reconciliation.** The prototype's inline "Login email"/"Role" fields on employee create, its table's "Role" column, and its "Export" button are dropped — sanctioned by docs/04-screen-contracts.md's own disposition for this screen (`FUNCTIONAL_CHANGE`: *"Login email/user access is optional and managed through Users/RBAC"*), not an unreviewed deviation. Login/role assignment happens only via a separate "Enable System Access" action (email + roles, after the employee already exists) — reusing `users.invite({ employeeId })`, `users.disable`, `users.enable`, and `users.unlinkEmployee` directly from the Employees screen, exactly the reuse ADR-046 anticipated ("the still-pending Employees screen's future 'Enable system access' button"). The prototype's "Role" table column becomes a "System Access" column (Linked/Not enabled), preserving its at-a-glance intent without conflating RBAC Role (User-side) with Designation (Employee-side job title, a separate master). Export is deferred — no export infrastructure exists anywhere in Phase 2 yet.
(3) `EmployeesService.getDetail` (new) composes an employee with its linked user's email/status/role names in one query, mirroring `UsersService.getDetail`'s composition style — the Employees detail drawer's single data source.
(4) Field-coverage: `status` and `userId` are deliberately **not** raw create/update form fields — `status` changes only via the pre-existing `setStatus` action (new employees always start `ACTIVE`, matching the service's own create signature, which has no status parameter); `userId` is managed only via the System Access actions above, never a raw ID picker, mirroring how the Users screen already manages the identical relationship from its side. `deletedAt`/`deletedBy` stay unused — no hard-delete action, consistent with every other master/entity screen in Phase 2.
Consequences: The Employees screen (`(platform)/employees/page.tsx`) is a `FUNCTIONAL_CHANGE` screen per docs/04, built from the shared shell/table/drawer language, with the same keyed-fresh-mount pattern as Roles/Users (`EmployeeFormBody`, gated on `!detail.isLoading`) for the same `set-state-in-effect` lint reason. `dayjs` added as an explicit `apps/web` dependency (was only a transitive antd dependency before) for the joining-date `DatePicker`. Verified with 2 new `employees.integration-spec.ts` tests (audit old/new diff on salary+designation change; `getDetail` composition with and without a linked user) — 61 integration tests pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green. Verified live via Playwright against the running lightweight stack: created an employee with department/designation/salary, confirmed the audit event captured `50000.0000 → 55000.0000` in the database directly, used "Enable System Access" (email+role) and confirmed the invited user appears with the chosen role tag, confirmed only "Unlink" is offered for an `INVITED`-status link (not "Revoke Access", consistent with ADR-046's `disable()` guard) and that unlinking correctly returns the drawer to "Enable System Access", deactivated/reactivated the employee — zero console errors throughout.
Supersedes: none — additive; the `update()` audit-event fix corrects a latent bug rather than changing any documented contract.
```

```text
ID: ADR-048
Date: 2026-07-31
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 2.11 (part 6) — Legal Entities & GST Registrations. Unlike every other Phase 2.11 family so far, this one's entire backend already existed and worked end-to-end (`LegalEntity`/`GstRegistration` Prisma models, migration `20260728110258_phase2_06_legal_entity_gst_fy_period`, `LegalEntityPort`, `LegalEntityService`, `legalEntitiesRouter`, full context/router/module/main.ts wiring, and integration tests covering the core create + duplicate-PAN + valid/invalid-GSTIN paths) — only the frontend was missing. Planning surfaced one real field-coverage gap and one deliberate scope question.
Decision:
(1) **`updateGstRegistration` added.** `tradeName` was a real editable column with no update path at all — only `create`/`setGstRegistrationActive` existed for a GST registration. Added `updateGstRegistration({ id, tradeName, expectedVersion })`, matching the existing `update`/`setActive` optimistic-concurrency + audit pattern exactly. `gstin`/`stateId`/`legalEntityId` remain permanently fixed at creation — a registration is tied to one state/GSTIN for its lifetime, matching real GST rules, so no update path is added for those.
(2) **GSTIN-state cross-validation explicitly declined.** A GSTIN's leading 2 digits are supposed to equal the selected state's `gst_state_code`; nothing enforces this anywhere in the codebase. The user's explicit decision, after being asked directly: don't validate it — `createGstRegistration` accepts any state/GSTIN pairing exactly as it already did. Recorded here as a considered, deliberate deferral, not a silent gap discovered and then ignored.
(3) **HTML prototype reconciliation.** The canonical prototype (`references/ui/html/02-crm/master-company.html`) flattens Company name/GSTIN/PAN/State/Bank-account/Status onto one row — one GSTIN and one bank account directly on the company. This is explicitly corrected by `docs/04-screen-contracts.md`'s own disposition for this exact screen (`FUNCTIONAL_CHANGE`: *"A legal entity has registrations and bank accounts; do not store one GSTIN/bank account directly on the company row"*), matching the schema exactly (`GstRegistration` is its own table, 1-to-many off `LegalEntity`). The Legal Entities list shows Legal Name/PAN/Registered Address/Status only; GST Registrations are a per-entity sub-list reached from the entity's edit drawer (GSTIN/State/Trade Name/Status), not flattened into the top-level table. Bank accounts are out of scope entirely — no bank-account master/model exists yet anywhere in the schema, future Finance-phase scope. Export is deferred, consistent with every Phase 2.11 screen so far.
(4) Field-coverage: `panNumber`/`gstin`/`stateId` are create-only (immutable, matching the existing service signatures which never accept them in `update`); `isActive` is managed via the existing `setActive`/`setGstRegistrationActive` actions, not raw fields, consistent with every other Phase 2 module's convention; `deletedAt`/`deletedBy` stay unused (no hard-delete).
Consequences: The Legal Entities screen (`(platform)/legal-entities/page.tsx`) is a `FUNCTIONAL_CHANGE` screen per docs/04, built from the shared shell/table/drawer language. Because `LegalEntitySummary`'s fields are already flat and complete (no linked-record composition needed, unlike Employees/Users), the edit drawer's initial values come directly from the already-loaded list row — no `getDetail` call or keyed-fresh-mount-on-async-load pattern was needed for the core form; only the nested GST Registrations sub-list is its own async query, rendered directly (no state-sync footgun since it's not fed into local component state). Verified with 2 new `organization.integration-spec.ts` tests (`LegalEntity.update`/`setActive` round-trip with a stale-version rejection; `updateGstRegistration`/`setGstRegistrationActive` round-trip with a stale-version rejection) — 61 integration tests pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green. Verified live via Playwright against the running lightweight stack: created a legal entity, added a GST registration under it with a real Maharashtra GSTIN, renamed its trade name and confirmed the change rendered, deactivated/reactivated both the registration and the entity itself — zero console errors throughout.
Supersedes: none — additive; `updateGstRegistration` closes a gap without changing any existing behavior.
```

```text
ID: ADR-049
Date: 2026-07-31
Status: Accepted
Context: User reported that deactivating a Legal Entity or GST Registration doesn't let its PAN/GSTIN be reused. Root cause: `legal_entities.pan_number` and `gst_registrations.gstin` had plain (non-partial) unique indexes, and neither `LegalEntityService` nor its GST-registration methods ever wrote to the already-existing `deletedAt`/`deletedBy` columns — there was no delete path at all, only `create`/`update`/`setActive`. Even a soft-delete write alone would not have freed the identifier without also converting the indexes to partial ones filtered on `deleted_at IS NULL` — this repo has done that exact migration shape twice before (`uq_user_permission_overrides_*` in `phase2_02_rbac`; `uq_financial_years_one_current` in this same `..._legal_entity_gst_fy_period` migration), so the same convention was followed exactly rather than invented fresh.
Decision:
(1) **Migration** `20260731085614_legal_entity_gst_soft_delete_partial_unique`: dropped and recreated both `uq_legal_entities_org_pan` (`organization_id, pan_number`) and `uq_gst_registrations_gstin` (`gstin`) as partial unique indexes `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`. Authored via the mandated safe procedure (`npm run prisma:migrate:dev` from `packages/database`, shadow-database-verified, never a hand-typed `--shadow-database-url`, per ADR-040). The auto-generated diff also proposed dropping `chk_cities_state_matches_district`/`uq_districts_id_state_id` — the same recurring spurious-drop artifact every migration since `phase2_04_hr_masters` has had to leave un-applied (hand-added constraints with no Prisma-DSL representation); not applied here either, consistent with every prior migration.
(2) **`LegalEntityService.delete()`** (new): rejects with `VALIDATION_FAILED` ("This legal entity still has GST registrations or financial years. Remove those first.") if any GST registration (active or inactive — anything not itself soft-deleted) or any financial year still references the entity; otherwise soft-deletes with the same optimistic-concurrency (`expectedVersion`) pattern every other mutator in this service already uses. This mirrors the one existing delete precedent in the codebase, `RolesService.delete`'s member-count guard — a considered choice over `DocumentsService.softDelete`, the codebase's other delete-shaped method, which skips `expectedVersion` entirely (an inconsistency in that method, not copied here). Financial years have no delete path at all today, so a legal entity with any financial year can only ever be deactivated, never deleted — an accepted, understood consequence, not a gap being fixed in this change.
(3) **`LegalEntityService.deleteGstRegistration()`** (new): no dependent guard — confirmed nothing in the schema holds a FK to `GstRegistration.id` — same optimistic-concurrency soft-delete pattern.
(4) **No restore action.** Explicit user decision: delete is final. No restore convention exists anywhere in this codebase (confirmed, zero precedent) — introducing one now would be new, unbounded design surface (e.g. what happens if the PAN/GSTIN was already reused by a different row by the time someone tries to restore) with no established pattern to lean on. Recovering from a wrong delete means re-creating the row with the same, now-freed, identifier.
Consequences: Frontend gained a "Delete" button (danger Popconfirm, mirroring the Roles screen's only existing Delete precedent) on both the Legal Entities list and the GST Registrations sub-list. Verified with 2 new `organization.integration-spec.ts` tests: deleting a GST registration frees its GSTIN for reuse on the *same* legal entity (the user's exact reported scenario) and rejects a stale-version re-delete; deleting a legal entity is blocked while a GST registration exists, blocked while a financial year exists, and succeeds once both are gone, freeing its PAN for a brand-new entity — 63 integration tests pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green. Verified live via Playwright against the running lightweight stack, reproducing the user's exact bug report end-to-end: created an entity + GST registration, confirmed deleting the entity was blocked with a clear message, deleted the GST registration, re-added the *identical* GSTIN to the *same* entity and confirmed it succeeded, then deleted the (new) registration and the entity itself and confirmed a brand-new entity could reuse the freed PAN — zero console errors from the application itself (one benign network-log entry for the intentionally-tested 400 rejection response, not a product defect).
Supersedes: none — additive; closes a real, user-reported gap without changing any documented contract.
```

```text
ID: ADR-050
Date: 2026-07-31
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 2.11 (part 7) — Financial Years & Accounting Periods. Like Legal Entities before it, the entire backend already existed and worked end-to-end (`FinancialYearService.create` builds a financial year and its 12 accounting periods atomically; `transitionAccountingPeriod` implements the full soft-close/lock/reopen state machine with a maker-checker actor check) — only the frontend was missing. This screen is `NEW_REQUIRED` with genuinely no HTML prototype anywhere (confirmed: zero matches in `docs/16`, zero files under `references/ui/html`/`references/ui/screenshots`) — full design freedom, built from the shared shell/card/table/drawer language. Planning surfaced one real gap and one doc/code discrepancy.
Decision:
(1) **`setCurrentFinancialYear` added.** `isCurrent` could only be set at `create` time (which already correctly un-sets whichever financial year was previously current for that legal entity) — there was no way to revert to an older financial year as current afterward. The new method reuses the identical "unset old current, set new current" transaction shape `create` already has, gated `financial_year.manage`, with its own `FINANCIAL_YEAR_SET_CURRENT` audit event.
(2) **`docs/09-security-audit.md` corrected, not the code.** Its "Period reopen requires Admin permission" sentence didn't match the tested, working implementation: all three transitions share one permission (`accounting_period.lock`, held by Admin *and* Accountant), and the actual safeguard is the maker-checker actor check (`SELF_APPROVAL_NOT_ALLOWED` — whoever locked a period cannot be the one to reopen it, backed by the DB's own `chk_accounting_periods_reopener_differs`). User's explicit choice: correct the doc to describe this real, already-enforced behavior rather than add a new, stricter Admin-only permission that would take reopen ability away from Accountant. The doc's "Maker-checker" section also gained accounting-period reopen as an explicit listed example — it was omitted there despite the schema/service already treating it as one.
(3) **Reason-collection UX.** Every transition requires a mandatory 1–1000 char `reason`, which no existing Popconfirm-only pattern in this app can collect — a small `Modal` with an `Input.TextArea` (first use of this shape in the app) gates every Soft Close/Lock/Reopen action, with the confirm button disabled until a reason is typed.
(4) **Delete explicitly out of scope.** `FinancialYear` has no `deletedAt`/`deletedBy` columns at all (confirmed against the full schema) and the original request only asks for open/soft-close/lock/reopen — adding delete would need its own schema migration and wasn't asked for.
Consequences: The Financial Years screen (`(platform)/financial-years/page.tsx`) starts with a Legal Entity picker (financial years are always scoped to one entity, matching the port's own `list({legalEntityId})` shape) — the first screen in this app where a top-level page requires picking a parent before showing any list. `AccountingPeriodsDrawer` resolves `lockedBy`/`reopenedBy` actor ids to display names via the already-loaded `users.list` query (same pattern as Employees' department/designation name resolution), and pre-emptively disables the Reopen button client-side when the signed-in admin is the same actor who locked the period — a courtesy on top of the server's authoritative check, mirroring the same "explain why an action is blocked" ethos as Legal Entities' delete guard.
```
