One real bug found and fixed during verification: the create form's cross-field date auto-fill (`form.setFieldValue('endDate', ...)` inside the start-date `DatePicker`'s `onChange`) triggered a genuine `rc-field-form`/dayjs console warning ("There may be circular references") on every date pick — switching to the officially-recommended `form.setFieldsValue({ endDate: ... })` API resolved it cleanly, confirmed by an isolated before/after Playwright check. Verified with 1 new `organization.integration-spec.ts` test (`setCurrentFinancialYear` moves current between two financial years for the same legal entity, rejects a stale-version re-attempt) — 64 integration tests pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green. Verified live via Playwright against the running lightweight stack: created a financial year and confirmed all 12 periods generated with exact month boundaries (first starts on the declared start date, twelfth ends on the declared end date), soft-closed then locked a period with a reason each time, confirmed the Reopen button was disabled for the same actor who locked it, used "Set as Current" to move current status between financial years — zero console errors.
```text
Supersedes: none — additive; the docs/09 change corrects documentation to match already-tested code, not a behavior change.
```

```text
ID: ADR-051
Date: 2026-08-02
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 2.11 (part 8) — Audit Log, the last family in the sequence. Unlike every prior family (Legal Entities, Financial Years), nothing existed for this one at all — `audit_events` (the table) and `recordAuditEvent` (the write helper every service already calls) work correctly, but there was no read-side port/service/router and no frontend. docs/04 §18 calls for "search actor/entity/action/time; permission-scoped before/after/domain payload," with no HTML prototype anywhere (confirmed: zero matches in docs/16, zero files on disk).
Decision:
(1) **Server-side pagination and filtering**, a deliberate first-time departure from every other screen built so far. Roles/Users/Employees/Legal Entities/Financial Years all fetch their full list client-side and paginate in memory — correct for small, bounded lookup tables. `audit_events` is written to by every mutation across the entire app and grows unbounded, so `audit.list` takes `page`/`pageSize` plus filters and does a real DB `count()` + `skip`/`take`. `EntityTable`'s existing `page`/`pageSize`/`total`/`onPageChange` props already supported this without any shared-component change.
(2) **Actor filter is free-text against the `actor_email` snapshot column**, not a dropdown sourced from the current Users list — `actor_email` exists specifically so the log shows who someone was at the time of the action, independent of later renames or account disablement.
(3) **`entityType`/`action` filter dropdowns are populated dynamically** (`listEntityTypes`/`listActions`, distinct values actually present for the organization) rather than a hardcoded frontend enum, avoiding a list that would otherwise need updating every time a future module adds new audit actions. `entityId` stays a raw UUID (with a copy-friendly tooltip) — resolving it to a friendly name would require a per-entity-type join for an open-ended, ever-growing set of types, real scope creep beyond the "search actor/entity/action/time" contract.
(4) **Real, systemic bug found and fixed while verifying the screen against live data**: the Actor column was correctly populated only for login events and blank ("—") for everything else. Investigation (`grep -c actorEmail` across all 11 services calling `recordAuditEvent`) showed only `IdentityService` ever passes `actorEmail` explicitly — the other 10 (`legal-entity`, `financial-year`, `roles`, `users`, `document-support`, `masters`, `documents`, `geography`, `employees`, `penalty-categories`) never did, despite docs/09 requiring an actor-email snapshot on *every* audit event. Root cause: the tRPC `AuthContext` only carries `{ id, organizationId }`, no email, so no router had one to pass down. Fixed centrally in `recordAuditEvent` itself (`packages/database/src/audit.ts`) — it now resolves `actorEmail` from `actorId` via a lookup on the same transaction client when the caller doesn't supply one explicitly, so every existing and future call site is correct by construction, without threading a new parameter through 10 services and their router call sites. Callers that already have the row loaded (`IdentityService`) still pass `actorEmail` directly and skip the lookup.
(5) Export deferred, consistent with every prior Phase 2 screen (no export infrastructure exists anywhere yet). No retention/purge mechanism added — not requested, no ADR calls for one.
Consequences: The due-diligence check (grepping every `recordAuditEvent` call site for password/token/secret-shaped fields in `oldValues`/`newValues`/`commandPayload`) found zero matches — every caller already cherry-picks specific fields rather than dumping whole rows, so `oldValues`/`newValues`/`commandPayload` render directly in the detail drawer with no new redaction layer needed. Verified with 7 new `audit.integration-spec.ts` tests (org-scoping, pagination, each filter dimension, `listEntityTypes`/`listActions` distinctness) plus 2 new `audit.test.ts` tests for the `recordAuditEvent` auto-resolve behavior (resolves from `actorId` when omitted; stays `null` when there's no `actorId` to resolve from) — 71 integration tests plus the `@zitaware/database` unit suite all pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green. Verified live via Playwright against the running lightweight stack: generated a mix of login/role/legal-entity events, confirmed the Actor column now shows `zitawaresolutions@gmail.com` for every new event (not just logins — the one pre-fix row correctly still shows "—", its historical snapshot genuinely was empty at write time), confirmed entity-type and actor-email filters each narrow the result set correctly, confirmed the detail drawer renders a real `newValues` diff — zero console errors from the application itself.
Supersedes: none — additive; the `recordAuditEvent` change is a bug fix (closing a real, systemic gap against docs/09's existing requirement), not a new behavior.
```

```text
ID: ADR-052
Date: 2026-08-02
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 2.12 — final verification. With all six Phase 2.11 admin-screen families complete (memory: `project_phase2_admin_screens_sequence`), the user asked for an explicit, comprehensive end-to-end verification pass across every completed Phase 2 module before treating Phase 2 as done: full infra suite (lint/typecheck/test/test:integration/production builds/migration verification/health checks/PM2 smoke test), plus per-module functional verification (list/search/filter/pagination; create/edit/activate-deactivate/validation; empty/loading/API-error states; permission-denied; cross-org rejection; audit-event generation; responsive layout), with an explicit instruction to fix only confirmed defects and not start any Phase 3+ module.
Decision:
(1) **Infra suite: all green, no code changes required.** Full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration (14 tasks, 71 integration tests) passed unchanged. `apps/api`/`apps/worker` production builds clean. `apps/web`'s production build now succeeds — see the correction appended to ADR-043 above; this is a dependency-lockfile-driven fix already in place, not new work done in this pass. Migration state clean (`prisma migrate status`/`migrate:diff` show no real drift; only the long-standing, expected spurious `chk_cities_state_matches_district`/`uq_districts_id_state_id` drop artifact every migration since `phase2_04_hr_masters` has had to leave un-applied).
(2) **PM2 production smoke test: initially appeared broken, root-caused to a test-procedure error, not a product defect.** The first `pm2 start ecosystem.config.cjs --env production` attempt left `zitaware-crm-api`/`zitaware-crm-worker` crash-looping on `env.validation_failed` (`DATABASE_URL`/`SESSION_SECRET`/`S3_*`/`POSTMARK_*` etc. all reported missing). Root cause: `.env` was not sourced into the invoking shell before calling `pm2 start` — this project's own documented convention (docs/14: nothing in this stack auto-loads `.env`; NestJS/worker/Prisma CLI all require an explicit `source .env` or `--env-file`) applies equally to `pm2 start`, since PM2 forks new app processes with the *invoking* shell's environment. Re-run after correctly sourcing `.env` first: all three apps (`zitaware-crm-web`/`zitaware-crm-api`/`zitaware-crm-worker`) came up `online` with 0 restarts, and all three health endpoints returned healthy (`web /api/healthz` 200; `api /healthz` + `/readyz` 200 with `database: reachable`; `worker /healthz` 200 with `pgBossConnected: true`). No change to `ecosystem.config.cjs` was needed or made — the existing config is correct given the documented "source `.env` first" operating convention; this is recorded here so a future session doesn't re-diagnose the same non-bug.
(3) **Documents and Notification Delivery Log: confirmed intentional backend-only scope, not gaps.** Both have complete, tested backends (`DocumentsService` with real S3 presigned-upload/confirm/link/soft-delete flows and entity-type-restriction + cross-org-rejection integration tests; the worker's `outbox-dispatcher`/Postmark provider creating and updating `Notification` rows with status/`providerMessageId`/`failedReason`, also integration-tested) but neither has a frontend screen, nav entry, `docs/04` screen contract, or `docs/16` HTML-prototype reference. Checked and confirmed deliberate: `docs/04`'s only document-consuming screens (`lead-view-documents`, `project-documentation`, `project-document-upload`) all belong to Lead/Project, both explicitly Phase 3+ (`docs/04` marks Tender/Project as `NEW_REQUIRED`, not yet built); there is nothing to attach a document to yet within Phase 2's own scope, and no admin-facing delivery-log screen was ever contracted anywhere. `docs/11`'s Phase 2 deliverable ("Transactional outbox, worker, and notifications") is satisfied by the working dispatch mechanism itself, not by requiring an admin list screen. Per the explicit "do not add new modules" instruction, no frontend was built for either — recorded here as a resolved open question rather than left ambiguous.
(4) **Zero confirmed Phase 2 defects found.** A live Playwright verification pass (fresh sessions against the PM2-hosted production stack) exercised: invalid/valid login, session-guarded routes, sign-out and post-sign-out redirect-to-login; Users list/search/invite (including blank-form validation) and role assignment; the seeded read-only `CEO` role as the permission-denied reference point; a full Employee create → "Enable System Access" (invite + role) → confirm linked-user display → "Unlink" → deactivate cycle, generating and confirming real audit-triggering tRPC calls (`employees.create`, `users.invite`, `users.unlinkEmployee`, `employees.setStatus`) at every step; Masters catalogue and a generic master detail list; Legal Entities/GST data display; Financial Years' legal-entity-scoped list and its 12 generated accounting periods (cross-checked directly against the database: exactly 12 periods for `FY2026-27`, matching the create-time invariant); Audit Log's server-side pagination and actor-email filter; a mobile-width (390px) layout check with no horizontal overflow. Every apparent failure encountered during scripting (Users search appearing not to filter, Financial Years appearing empty, "logout" not found, a protected route not redirecting) was traced to a verification-script selector/sequencing mistake, not a product defect, and confirmed corrected on retry — e.g. Financial Years requires a Legal Entity to be selected first (by design, since financial years are scoped per entity), "sign out" lives inside the avatar-triggered user-menu dropdown (not a bare text link), and AntD's empty-state placeholder row was being miscounted as a data row by the naive `tr`-counting check. Cross-org rejection and permission-denied enforcement were verified structurally rather than re-derived from scratch live: every tRPC mutation in every router is gated through the single `requirePermission` middleware chokepoint (`packages/contracts/src/trpc.ts`), unit-tested via `resolve-permissions.test.ts`; organization scoping is enforced both in application queries (`organizationId` always sourced from session context, never client input) and at the database level via the org-consistency composite-FK audit already completed in a prior session (commit `a99862d`). All test data created during this verification pass (one invited CEO-role user, one test employee and its linked user) was deleted after use — the database is back to its pre-verification baseline (one `ACTIVE` bootstrap admin user, zero employees, matching the curated seed).
(5) **Phase 2 exit confirmation added to `docs/11-development-roadmap.md`**, matching the dated-confirmation format already used for Phase 0 and Phase 1, closing the gap this ADR's context section identified (Phase 2 previously had deliverables/exit-criteria but no confirmation section).
Consequences: No source code changed as a result of this verification pass — every mechanism checked was already correct. Two documentation corrections were made: the ADR-043 build-status correction above, and this ADR itself. `docs/13-progress.md` gained a closing entry recording the full sweep. Per the explicit user instruction, no CRM/tender/project/salary-import/employee-contribution/accounting-ledger or other Phase 3+ work was started or scoped.
Supersedes: none — verification-only; the one real status change (ADR-043's build fix) is documented as a correction to that ADR, not a new decision reversing it.

**Correction (ADR-053, 2026-08-03):** item (1) above — "`apps/web`'s production build now succeeds" — is premature. During Phase 3.1 verification the next day, `npm --workspace apps/web run build` failed again on the identical `/_global-error` (and, on a subsequent clean-cache retry, `/_not-found`) prerendering crash, reproduced 3 times consecutively including two full `.next` cache wipes, with zero relevant dependency changes (`package-lock.json` untouched since this ADR was written) and confirmed via isolation testing to be unrelated to any Phase 3.1 code (still fails with the new `/clients` route removed entirely). The correct characterization: this is the same genuine, unfixed upstream Next.js bug from ADR-043, and it is **intermittent/non-deterministic across otherwise-identical builds**, not "fixed" and not simply "broken" — a build can succeed or fail without any source or dependency change between attempts. `apps/api`/`apps/worker` production builds remain unaffected (clean both times). Per ADR-043's original, still-standing user decision, this is left open and disclosed rather than chased further — see ADR-053.
```

```text
ID: ADR-053
Date: 2026-08-03
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 3, Slice 1 — Party/Client foundation, the first CRM screen and the first Phase 3 work of any kind. Per docs/16 §6's recommended module order (Platform/Masters → CRM → Projects) and CLAUDE.md's "one bounded vertical slice at a time" rule, Phase 3 is being built as a sequence of slices the same way Phase 2.11 was. This slice covers `client-list.html`/`client-add.html`/`client-view.html` (docs/04 §9) — nothing in this vertical existed before this slice: no `Party`/`Client` models, no CRM permissions, no CRM nav group.
Decision:
(1) **Party + ClientProfile split**, per docs/04's own contract for `client-add.html`: *"Atomically create Party, Client Profile, address and optional contacts."* `Party` (`packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma`) is the generic counterparty identity — name, single embedded address (state/district/city/pin/office address, matching the prototype exactly; no multi-address table, a user-confirmed scope decision since the prototype shows no "add another address" affordance), short code, internal note. `ClientProfile` is a 1:1 profile layered on top holding client-specific fields (department, GSTIN, client type). The split is deliberately forward-looking — Vendor (Phase 6) and a fuller Introducer (Phase 9) will plausibly want the same Party-plus-profile shape later — but no Vendor/Introducer profile is added now; only the shape is reserved.
(2) **`ClientType` is a new master, not a reuse of `GovernmentBodyType`.** Both classify similar things, but `GovernmentBodyType` (seeded Phase 2.11 with State Government/ULB/Municipal Corporation) names itself specifically as *government body* classification, while `client-add.html`'s "Client type" dropdown explicitly includes non-government "Private" — stretching `GovernmentBodyType`'s semantics to cover that would misname an existing master for a second purpose. User's explicit choice, over reusing the existing table. `ClientType` slots into the existing generic masters registry (`MasterTypeKey`/`MASTER_TYPES`/`getDelegate` in `masters.service.ts`, `masters.ts` router, `apps/web/.../masters/catalog.ts`) exactly like `LeadType`/`LeadSource`/`TenderPortal`/`ProjectType` already do — no bespoke port, reusing the identical generic CRUD every other flat lookup master in this app already gets. No starter rows seeded, matching the precedent that `LeadType`/`ProjectType` etc. also ship with zero seeded rows — an admin populates it via the (now client-type-inclusive) Masters screen.
(3) **GSTIN added to `ClientProfile`, optional, format-checked but not unique.** `client-view.html`'s mock data shows a GSTIN field with no corresponding input on `client-add.html` — a real gap, not an intentional omission. User's explicit choice: add it now (optional) rather than defer to a Finance phase, since a government client/PSU's GSTIN is relevant to future invoicing/TDS reporting regardless of when that phase lands. `chk_client_profiles_gstin_format` reuses the exact 15-char GSTIN regex CHECK constraint from `gst_registrations` (ADR — `phase2_06_legal_entity_gst_fy_period` migration), just nullable. No uniqueness constraint — unlike `GstRegistration`, a client's GSTIN is external data we merely record, not something this system is the source of truth for.
(4) **Real bug found and fixed during verification: `ClientSummary.id` was wired to `clientProfile.id`, but every service method (`getDetail`/`update`/`setActive`) treats the caller-supplied `id` as `Party.id`.** Caught by the integration test suite itself (`deactivates and reactivates a client` failed with a spurious `VERSION_CONFLICT` — the reactivate call's `id` didn't match any row because it was really a `clientProfile.id` being used to look up a `Party`). Fixed by exposing `Party.id` as `ClientSummary.id` (the stable, future-proof identity — a Party's `ClientProfile` could theoretically be removed while a future Vendor/Introducer profile on the same Party remains) and dropping the now-redundant `partyId` field from the port entirely, rather than leaving two identical fields on the same object.
(5) **Field-coverage cross-check**: `Party` (name/shortCode/state/district/city/pinCode/officeAddress/internalNote — all in the form; `isActive` via `setActive`, not a raw field; no delete path this slice, see (6)), `ClientProfile` (clientType/department/gstin — all in the form; `isActive` via `setActive`), `Contact` (name/designation/phone/email — all in the repeatable `Form.List` rows, first use of that AntD pattern in this app). Re-verified against the migrated `schema.prisma`.
(6) **No `delete()` this slice.** Legal Entities' delete guard (ADR-049) blocks deletion while dependents exist — but nothing can yet reference a `Party` (Lead doesn't exist until Slice 2), so a delete path would have no real dependent condition to build or test against. `create`/`update`/`setActive` (deactivate) cover this slice's full lifecycle; `delete()` is deferred to the Leads slice, where it can gain a real, testable guard.
Consequences: New tables `client_types`/`parties`/`client_profiles`/`contacts` (migration `phase3_01_party_client_foundation`, reviewed by hand for the recurring spurious `chk_cities_state_matches_district`/`uq_districts_id_state_id` drop artifact, which was stripped as usual). Five new permission keys (`party.read`, `client.read`, `client.create`, `client.update`, `client.export`) granted to Admin (all), Accountant/Project Manager (`client.read`+`party.read`), Admin Staff (`client.read`), Employee (none) — the permission catalog test's hard-coded count moved from 17 to 22, and the masters-catalog-count test moved from 12 to 13, both updated to match, not weakened. A new "CRM" nav group (first nav group besides Administration/Organization) with a new hand-written `ClientsIcon` (matching the established inline-SVG convention from ADR-043, no icon library). Verified with 8 new `clients.integration-spec.ts` tests (atomic create with contacts, GSTIN format rejection, state/district/city hierarchy validation in both directions, update with diff-audited fields and wholesale contact replacement, stale-version rejection, deactivate/reactivate, cross-org isolation) — 79 integration tests pass workspace-wide (previous 71 + 8 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green. Verified live via Playwright against the running lightweight dev stack: created a client with a state/district address and a contact, confirmed it lists correctly, edited its department and confirmed the change persisted, deactivated/reactivated it, confirmed the Client Types tile appears in the Masters catalogue, confirmed zero console errors throughout. A handful of apparent failures during scripting (nav item "not visible", mobile-width "overflow") were traced to script-timing and to a pre-existing, already-shipped app-wide characteristic (Legal Entities' list page overflows at 390px identically — not a regression) respectively, not real defects. `apps/web`'s production build hit the pre-existing ADR-043 upstream bug — see the correction appended to ADR-052 above; confirmed via isolation testing to be unrelated to this slice's code.
Supersedes: none — additive; the `ClientSummary.id` fix is a bug fix caught before shipping, not a behavior change to anything previously released.
```

```text
ID: ADR-054
Date: 2026-08-05
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 3, Slice 2 — Leads, the second CRM screen. Covers docs/04-screen-contracts.md §9's five Lead rows (`lead-list.html`, `lead-add.html`, `lead-view-detail.html`, `lead-view-documents.html`, `lead-view-updates.html`) plus `01-platform/master-lead-type.html`'s pairing master. Four genuinely open business decisions were identified and resolved with the user before implementation (recorded in the approved plan, summarized below); several smaller scope calls were proposed as defaults and disclosed rather than silently chosen.
Decision:
(1) **Introducer modeled as a new, deliberately minimal table** (`Introducer`: id/organizationId/name/phone/isActive — no Party/Profile split, no PAN, no payout mode/detail, no masking) rather than the full `Party`+`IntroducerProfile` entity that `09-introducer-fee/master-introducer.html` describes. That full entity, with its PAN/payout/masking and the accompanying agreement/accrual/payment workflow, is Phase 7 scope (`docs/11` Phase 7 deliverables: "Introducer agreement, accrual, advance/payment, and allocation"; the reserved `EntityType.code='INTRODUCER'` seed row already anticipated this). Building it now would be premature investment beyond what a Lead's "who introduced this" field needs. **Disclosed consequence**: Phase 7 will need a follow-up migration promoting this table's rows into the real `Party`+`IntroducerProfile` shape and repointing `Lead.introducerId` at the new `Party.id` — accepted now, not hidden.
(2) **`Lead.assignedToEmployeeId`** (nullable, composite FK → `Employee`) is a new field not present anywhere in the HTML — the prototype itself is internally inconsistent here (`lead-view-detail.html`'s meta strip shows "Introducer: ..." while `lead-view-documents.html`/`lead-view-updates.html` show "Owner: Alkesh" for the same lead, never both). Resolved by adding a real, explicit ownership field, always shown alongside Introducer rather than toggling per-tab. Defaults to the creating actor's linked `Employee` (via `Employee.userId`) at create time, left `null` when the actor has no linked employee record; reassignable later via `lead.update`. This is the first "assigned to entity" pattern in the app — Project's future Project Manager field will likely follow the same composite-FK shape.
(3) **No Lead number, no automated duplicate-lead detection.** No HTML file anywhere shows a Lead ID/code — leads are referenced by title and a URL slug only. `Lead.id` is a plain UUID, matching `Client`/`Party`'s own precedent. A real sequential-numbering scheme (`docs/03-domain-model.md`'s unbuilt `document_sequences` hint) is deferred as its own future cross-cutting investment, not bootstrapped quietly inside this slice.
(4) **Contact/address reuse via the Client relation, not a Lead-level snapshot.** `lead-view-detail.html`'s "Client Contacts" card is confirmed by its own content to show the Client's contacts, not Lead-specific ones — `Lead` has no contact or address fields of its own; the detail screen queries `clients.getDetail` directly.
(5) **Stage/status vocabulary collapsed to one canonical field.** The HTML shows three non-reconciling vocabularies (the 7-step pipeline `stage`; `lead-list.html`'s "Status" column, worded slightly differently in 2 of 6 sample rows; `client-view.html`'s further-simplified Won/In progress/Qualified/New). Only `stage` (`PROPOSAL_SENT`/`PRESENTATION_DONE`/`SOW_PREPARATION`/`TENDER_LIVE`/`TENDER_FILLED`/`TENDER_WON`/`TENDER_LOST`) is stored, validated as a Zod enum at the router layer (no DB CHECK constraint — no precedent in this schema for hand-added enum-membership constraints, only regex-format ones like GSTIN). Every other vocabulary is a pure derived-label mapping, computed in the frontend, never stored.
(6) **`Lead` has no soft-delete/deactivate lifecycle.** `lead-list.html`'s row actions are View/Edit only, unlike Client's Deactivate/Reactivate — a lead simply progresses through `stage` until a terminal Won/Lost value. No `isActive`, no `deletedAt`, no `setActive` mutation.
(7) **Convert-to-Project omitted entirely**, not even as a disabled placeholder — Project doesn't exist yet and building dead UI for a future procedure (`project.createFromLead`) that can't function yet was explicitly out of scope. Added when the Projects slice ships.
(8) **Documents tab is the first real frontend consumer of the existing Documents backend** (`DocumentsService`/`documentsRouter`, built and integration-tested in Phase 2 with zero UI ever wired to it). Reuses `initiateUpload`/`confirmUpload`/`linkToEntity`/`listLinksForEntity` exactly as designed, `entityType: 'LEAD'` (already seeded). A client-computed SHA-256 hex digest satisfies `checksum` (free-text field, no algorithm enforced by the port). The HTML's "Title"/"Note" fields on the upload form were dropped — `DocumentSummary` has no backing fields for them, and collecting input that goes nowhere would be a dead-input bug, not a faithful reproduction.
(9) **Real bug found and fixed during test-writing**: Prisma's `@db.Date` columns (`leadDate`/`expectedCloseDate`/`expectedTenderDate`) reject a bare `'YYYY-MM-DD'` string — caught immediately by the first integration test run (`PrismaClientValidationError`). Fixed using the same `new Date(\`${value}T00:00:00.000Z\`)` conversion `EmployeesService` already uses for `joiningDate`, not a new pattern.
(10) **Money precision corrected before any code depended on it**: `Lead.estimatedValue` was initially typed `@db.Decimal(14,2)`, inconsistent with `docs/07-database-conventions.md`'s stated house convention ("Store monetary values as `numeric(19,4)`"). Caught while wiring the zod/`Money` conversion layer (before the migration had been used by any other code) and corrected to `Decimal(19,4)`, matching `Employee.displayedSalaryAmount`'s own column exactly.
(11) **Client-detail "Leads" mini-list (`client-view.html`'s embedded section) deferred to a fast-follow, not built this slice.** Client has no dedicated detail/view route at all yet (Slice 3.1 shipped only a list+edit-drawer) — building the mini-list properly would mean building a real Client detail page first, which is bigger scope than this Leads slice needs and wasn't a hard requirement of the approved plan (explicitly flagged there as "decide at implementation time").
Consequences: New tables `introducers`/`leads`/`lead_updates` (migration `phase3_02_leads`, reviewed by hand for the recurring spurious `chk_cities_state_matches_district`/`uq_districts_id_state_id` drop artifact, stripped as usual; also hand-corrected in place for the `Decimal(19,4)` fix and an added `lead_updates.created_by_email` snapshot column before the migration was used elsewhere). Added missing `@@unique([id, organizationId])` to `LeadType`/`LeadSource`/`Employee` (required FK-target support, none had it before). Seven new permission keys (`lead.read`, `lead.create`, `lead.update`, `lead.export`, `introducer.read`, `introducer.create`, `introducer.update`) granted to Admin (all), Accountant/Project Manager (`lead.read`+`introducer.read`, mirroring `client.read`+`party.read`), Admin Staff (`lead.read`, mirroring `client.read`-only), Employee (none), CEO (automatic, via the existing `*.read` filter) — permission catalog count moved 22→29, updated in `roles.integration-spec.ts`; master-type count (13) unaffected since `Lead`/`Introducer`/`LeadUpdate` aren't generic masters. New "Leads" nav item under the existing "CRM" group with a new hand-written `LeadsIcon`. `LeadFormBody` and the stage/status display helpers were factored into a shared `leads/shared.tsx` module so the list page's inline edit drawer and the detail page's edit drawer stay identical rather than duplicated. Verified with 5 new `introducers.integration-spec.ts` tests and 7 new `leads.integration-spec.ts` tests (create with actor-linked-employee default, client-without-clientProfile rejection, invalid-stage rejection, diffed update + stale-version rejection, updates ordering, cross-org isolation) — 91 integration tests pass workspace-wide (previous 79 + 5 + 7), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green. Verified live via Playwright: created a lead against a real client, confirmed the seeded LeadType/LeadSource dropdowns populate correctly (8 options each, from the real data seeded in commit `479c347`), navigated list→detail, added a timeline update, edited the description and confirmed persistence, zero console errors. The Documents-tab upload flow was exercised and correctly fails gracefully (document stays `PENDING_UPLOAD`, never falsely marked uploaded) because `.env`'s `S3_ENDPOINT` is still a placeholder value in this dev environment (`https://s3.example-region.example-provider.com`) — a pre-existing environment gap unrelated to this slice's code, not something fixable without real S3 credentials. `apps/web`'s production build hit the same pre-existing, intermittent ADR-043 upstream Next.js bug (unrelated to this slice, per the standing decision not to chase it further).
Supersedes: none — additive.
```

**Correction (2026-08-05):** a user-requested full-module test sweep, prompted by the `stage` bug this ADR describes finding (item 9's date-conversion bug is separate; the *stage* gap itself — `Lead.stage` had no form control at all in the shipped `LeadFormBody` despite being fully backend-supported — was found and fixed in a follow-up commit the same day, before this correction was written). The same sweep found two more instances of the identical bug class, both now fixed:
- **`Lead.assignedToEmployeeId`** had no form control anywhere either — item (2) above describes the intent ("exposed as an editable field only on the detail/edit view") but the field was never actually added to `LeadFormBody`. Fixed: an "Assigned To" select now appears in the edit drawer only (not create, matching the original intent).
- **`Introducer` had no frontend screen at all.** `introducers.create`/`update`/`setActive` were fully built, permission-gated, and integration-tested, but the only frontend usage anywhere was `introducers.list` feeding a read-only dropdown inside the Lead form — there was no way to add, rename, or deactivate/reactivate an introducer from the app. Fixed: a full `(platform)/introducers/` screen (list/filter/add-edit/deactivate-reactivate) was added, matching the same pattern as every other simple entity, plus a nav item and icon.

A parallel static audit (field-by-field: does every `create`/`update` parameter have a form control, is it included in the mutation payload, is it correctly pre-populated on edit; does every `setActive`-style action have both directions wired) was run against Legal Entities, Financial Years, Clients, Users, Roles, Masters, and Penalty Categories — all came back clean. Live Playwright verification: 9/9 checks on the new Introducers screen, 6/6 on the `assignedToEmployeeId` fix, 14/14 on a smoke pass across all ten screens, zero console errors throughout.

```text
ID: ADR-055
Date: 2026-08-05
Status: Accepted
Context: The Documents backend (`initiateUpload`/`confirmUpload`/`linkToEntity`/`listLinksForEntity`) had no view/download capability anywhere in the codebase — no `GetObjectCommand`, no `document.download` permission (only named in prose by docs/04 for a not-yet-built screen), no UI affordance beyond a filename/date/status row. The user asked for two things on top of that gap: (1) real view/download, and (2) an optional password on upload such that the document can only be re-opened with that same password.
Decision:
(1) **True client-side encryption, not a server-side access gate.** Two designs were presented to the user; they chose real encryption over a simpler recoverable password-check gate. Uploads already go directly browser→S3 via a presigned PUT (the API never sees file bytes, a pre-existing invariant) — to preserve that and make "no recovery, ever, for anyone" literally true, all encryption/decryption happens in the browser via the Web Crypto API. The server never receives, stores, or verifies the password in any form.
(2) **Crypto parameters**: PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 key derivation, 600,000 iterations (current OWASP baseline), random 16-byte salt; AES-256-GCM encryption with a random 12-byte IV. Salt and IV are stored in Postgres alongside the document — not secret, just non-reusable inputs to re-derive the same key. A wrong password fails GCM's built-in auth-tag check inside `crypto.subtle.decrypt`; that thrown error is the only "right password?" signal needed, so no separate password hash exists anywhere (unlike the existing argon2 login-password precedent, which is a genuinely different use case).
(3) **View = the browser's native viewer in a new tab**, per the user's explicit choice, not a custom embedded preview pane. For an unencrypted document this means opening the presigned S3 GET URL directly (no fetch/blob needed, since nothing must be decrypted client-side first). For an encrypted document, the ciphertext must be fetched and decrypted in the browser regardless, so a `Blob`+`URL.createObjectURL` object URL is unavoidable there.
(4) **New `document.download` permission** (finally implements what docs/04 already named in prose), granted to every role that already has `document.read` (Admin all, Accountant, Project Manager, Admin Staff, Employee) plus explicitly added to `CEO_PERMISSIONS` (CEO's auto-generated `*.read`-filter grant doesn't catch it, since the key doesn't end in `.read`).
(5) **No retroactive protection and no recovery path**, both by explicit design: a password is set only at upload time (re-upload as a new document if protection is needed on an already-plaintext one); there is no admin override, reset flow, or backup key for a lost encryption password — the server has no means to decrypt even if asked.
Consequences: `Document` gained `isEncrypted`/`encryptionSalt`/`encryptionIv` columns (migration `20260805123911_documents_encryption`, hand-written and applied via `psql`+`prisma migrate resolve --applied` rather than `prisma migrate dev`, because an earlier session's hand-edit of the already-applied `phase3_02_leads` migration had left Prisma's own checksum bookkeeping unable to reconcile automatically — `migrate dev` demanded a full `migrate reset`, which was refused as destructive; no data was lost). `DocumentsService` gained `getDownloadUrl` (presigned `GetObjectCommand`, 5-minute expiry, records a new `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` audit action — this also finally satisfies docs/09-security-audit.md's pre-existing "file download" mandatory-audit-coverage line, which download previously could never meet since it didn't exist). New `apps/web/src/lib/document-crypto.ts` (first file in a new entity-agnostic `lib/` directory, since Documents will be reused by future Tender/Project screens) holds `encryptFile`/`decryptToBlob`/base64 helpers, unit-tested (round-trip, wrong-password rejection, distinct salt/IV per encryption). The Leads Documents tab gained a "Password-protect this document" checkbox (with an explicit "no recovery if lost" warning) plus View/Download actions and a lock icon for encrypted rows.

Two real bugs were found and fixed during live verification, neither related to the crypto design itself: **(a)** `window.open()` called after the async fetch/decrypt work is no longer tied closely enough to the original click for Chromium's popup blocker — fixed by using a synthetic `<a>` element click instead, which reliably survives an intervening `await`. **(b)** A `target="_blank"` anchor combined with `rel="noopener"` can land the new tab in a different renderer process, and `blob:` object URLs are only resolvable in the process that created them, leaving the tab blank — avoided by not using a blob URL at all for the unencrypted case (opening the presigned S3 URL directly instead, which is also simpler and needs no `noopener` sacrifice) and accepting the blob-URL/new-tab path only where a client-side decrypt makes it unavoidable (the encrypted case), which live-tested reliably across repeated runs. A third apparent failure during scripting (a plain-document view test targeting the wrong, stale leftover document because the list is newest-first and the script used `.last()`) was traced to the verification script itself, not application code.

Verified with 5 new `documents.integration-spec.ts` tests (persists encryption fields; rejects `isEncrypted: true` with a missing salt or IV; `getDownloadUrl` returns a presigned URL and audits `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` for an `UPLOADED` document; rejects for `PENDING_UPLOAD`/`DELETED` documents; cross-org isolation) plus 4 new `document-crypto.test.ts` unit tests — 97 integration tests and 9 web unit tests pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green. Verified live against the real configured S3 bucket via Playwright: uploaded one plain and one password-protected document, confirmed the plain one opens immediately with no prompt, confirmed the encrypted one prompts for a password, rejects a wrong password with a clear retry-able error, and opens correctly with the right one — zero console errors, 8/8 checks passing across two consecutive runs.
Supersedes: none — additive.
```

```text
ID: ADR-056
Date: 2026-08-05
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 3, Slice 3.3 — Tenders, the third CRM screen after Party/Client (3.1) and Leads (3.2). Unlike every prior CRM screen, docs/16-html-module-reference.md and docs/04-screen-contracts.md both state plainly that no HTML prototype exists for Tender at all — the minimum contract is "Independent Tender lifecycle; optional Lead origin; Project conversion/link; EMD/SD links," built by following the Lead visual system. docs/01-business-decisions.md (ADR-006) is explicit that Lead and Tender are separate upstream records — Lead's own `TENDER_LIVE`/`TENDER_WON`/`TENDER_LOST` stage values are shared vocabulary, not the same concept as this new, independent Tender entity.
Decision:
(1) **Tender reuses the Party/Client foundation exactly like Lead** — `clientId` is the issuing government authority (the same `Party`+`ClientProfile` Lead already uses), validated with the identical "active Party with a non-null clientProfile" rule as `LeadsService`. No separate "authority" field.
(2) **Optional Lead origin**: nullable `leadId`, no uniqueness constraint, one-way reference — mirrors Lead's own optional `introducerId` pattern. No back-write to the Lead.
(3) **Two separate lifecycle fields instead of one collapsed `stage`**: `submissionStatus` (`IDENTIFIED`→`IN_PREPARATION`→`SUBMITTED`, with a `NOT_PURSUED` terminal branch) and `result` (`PENDING`→`WON`/`LOST`/`CANCELLED`), kept orthogonal. This deliberately mirrors Project's Stage/Operational-Status split (ADR-007) rather than Lead's `stage` collapse — Lead collapsed three *conflicting* prototype vocabularies describing the same progression into one field; there is no such prototype mess here, and docs/04 itself names "submission status" and "result" as two distinct concepts. Both are Zod enums at the router layer, no DB CHECK constraint, matching `Lead.stage`'s own precedent. These specific enum values were invented for this slice (no prototype or prior ADR fixed them) and are disclosed as such, not silently decided.
(4) **`tenderPortalId` (nullable)**, the first real consumer of the `TenderPortal` master (schema/API-only since Phase 2, seeded with real e-procurement portal data in commit `479c347`, never previously wired to a screen). `TenderPortal` gained `@@unique([id, organizationId])` — the same FK-target fix `LeadType`/`LeadSource`/`Employee` got in ADR-054.
(5) **`assignedToEmployeeId`** (nullable, composite FK), identical ownership pattern to Lead's own (ADR-054 item 2) — defaults to the creating actor's linked Employee, reassignable via update.
(6) **No delete/deactivate lifecycle**, mirroring Lead exactly (ADR-054 item 6) — progresses via `submissionStatus`/`result` until terminal.
(7) **Explicitly out of scope, disclosed not hidden**: Project conversion/link (Project doesn't exist until its own future slice, same reasoning Lead used to omit "Convert to Project") and EMD/SD (moved to Phase 4 by the roadmap already — needs the Chart-of-Accounts/journal/posting engine that doesn't exist yet). Neither gets even a disabled placeholder.
(8) **`DocumentsTab` extracted into a shared, entity-agnostic component** (`apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx`), parameterized by `entityType`/`entityId` instead of duplicating the ~250-line upload/encrypt/view/download component (built for Lead in ADR-055) a second time — the underlying `documents` router was already entity-agnostic, so the UI just needed the same treatment. `formatDate`/`formatMoney` were promoted alongside it into `apps/web/src/lib/format.ts` (previously private to `leads/shared.tsx`) for the same reason. The Updates tab was **not** extracted the same way — `TenderUpdate` is its own model behind its own `tenders.listUpdates`/`createUpdate` procedures, not `leads.*`, so forcing a shared component across two different backing routers would be premature abstraction; the small per-entity component stays duplicated.
(9) **Permissions**: `tender.read`/`create`/`update`/`export`, granted to exactly the same roles as `lead.*` — mirrors ADR-054's grant pattern exactly.
(10) **Pre-existing bug found during this slice's research, unrelated to Tender itself**: `apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts` hardcoded `expect(catalog.length).toBe(29)`, but the real seeded count was already 30 — `document.download` (added earlier the same day for ADR-055) had never been reflected here. A fresh (non-cached) run confirmed it failing (`96 passed | 1 failed`) even though the prior full sweep had reported `97 passed` — that turned out to be a Turbo cache-hit replaying older logs from before `document.download` existed, not a genuine fresh pass. Fixed in the same commit as this slice's own permission additions: 29 → 30 (the pre-existing gap) → 34 (four new `tender.*` keys).
Consequences: New `Tender`/`TenderUpdate` models (migration `20260805174440_phase3_03_tenders`, hand-written and applied via `psql`+`prisma migrate resolve --applied` rather than `prisma migrate dev` — the same `phase3_02_leads` checksum-drift block from ADR-055 recurred, resolved the same non-destructive way, no data lost). Back-relations added to `Party`, `TenderPortal`, `Employee`, `Lead`. Four new permission keys granted per decision (9); permission catalog count corrected 29→30→34 (decision 10). New `apps/api/src/tenders/*` (port/service/module mirroring `leads/*` closely), `packages/contracts/src/routers/tenders.ts`, wired into context/router/app.module/main exactly like every prior CRM family. New `(platform)/tenders/{page.tsx,shared.tsx,[id]/page.tsx}` (list+filters+drawer, detail with Detail/Documents/Updates tabs) plus a new hand-written `TendersIcon` and a "Tenders" nav item under "CRM". `apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx` (new, extracted) and `apps/web/src/lib/format.ts` (new, promoted) per decision 8; `leads/shared.tsx` now re-exports `formatDate`/`formatMoney` from the new shared location rather than defining them, so no call site elsewhere needed to change.

One operational gotcha surfaced during live verification: this dev environment's API process runs as a compiled `node apps/api/dist/main` in a long-lived tmux pane (not `npm run dev`'s watch mode), so new backend modules require an explicit `npm --workspace api run build` + process restart before they're reachable — the frontend alone hot-reloads fine via Next.js, but a new tRPC router silently 404s until the API is rebuilt and restarted. This is a one-time environment fact worth knowing for the next slice, not a code defect.

Verified with 8 new `tenders.integration-spec.ts` tests (create with actor-linked-employee default; create with an optional Lead origin; client-without-clientProfile rejection; invalid `submissionStatus`/invalid `result` rejection; diffed update + stale-version rejection; updates ordering newest-first; cross-org isolation) — 105 integration tests pass workspace-wide (97 prior + 8 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all 9 workspaces, run twice with Turbo's cache forced off (`--force`) to guard against the exact cache-replay blind spot decision (10) found. Verified live via Playwright against the real S3 bucket and a real client: created a Tender, confirmed the list/filters and detail tabs render correctly, confirmed the Documents tab (including password-protected upload/view, reusing ADR-055's crypto flow unchanged) and Updates tab both work identically to Lead's, confirmed zero console errors — 12/12 checks passing. A regression check on Lead's own Documents tab after the extraction (decision 8) also passed 3/3, confirming the shared-component refactor didn't change Lead's existing behavior.
Supersedes: none — additive.
```

```text
ID: ADR-057
Date: 2026-08-06
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 3, Slice 3.4 — Projects core, the first slice where a Project exists at all, and the first slice where a Project may reference a Lead, a Tender, or both (a named Phase 3 exit criterion). Unlike Tender, Project's stage/status model was not invented from scratch — docs/05-workflows-state-machines.md already specifies it: `stage` is a six-value forward pipeline starting at WON (WORK_ORDER_RECEIVED → WAITING_FOR_PERMISSIONS → WORK_STARTED → WORK_DONE → SUCCESSFULLY_COMPLETED, backward movement requires a reason), and `operationalStatus` is a separate field (ACTIVE ↔ ON_HOLD, →CANCELLED, SUCCESSFULLY_COMPLETED→CLOSED, both terminal). docs/04's permission column for project-detail.html is `project.read/update/change_stage/change_status` — three separate command permissions, not one.
Decision:
(1) **Stage and Operational Status are separate fields, changed only via dedicated `changeStage`/`changeStatus` commands** — never via the plain `update` mutation, each its own permission, validated against the transition graph, audited, and version-checked. Structurally the same shape as Financial Years' lock/reopen commands (ADR-050), minus the different-actor maker-checker rule (nothing in the docs requires that here).
(2) **Transition rules** (the docs/05 graph is a strict linear chain with no branches; two specifics weren't spelled out and are disclosed, adjustable defaults): stage forward moves only to the immediate next stage (no skipping); backward moves may target any earlier stage and always require a non-empty `reason`. Status: `ACTIVE ↔ ON_HOLD` freely; `→ CANCELLED` from either requires a `reason` (not explicitly required by docs/05, extended by the same logic as stage's backward-move reason, since cancelling is an equally significant outcome); `SUCCESSFULLY_COMPLETED → CLOSED` only, no reason. No reversal out of `CANCELLED`/`CLOSED` this slice — docs/05 itself calls that a separate, not-yet-specified "admin correction."
(3) **Field set** mirrors `project-add.html`'s own summary (docs/16): `clientId` (required, same Party+ClientProfile foundation as Lead/Tender), `legalEntityId` (required, immutable after creation like `clientId` — Project's first real non-admin consumer of `LegalEntity`), independently-nullable `leadId`/`tenderId` (no requirement that either be set), nullable `projectManagerId` (Employee FK, same pattern as Lead/Tender's `assignedToEmployeeId`) and `introducerId` (reuses the existing minimal `Introducer` model), nullable `projectTypeId` (first real consumer of the already-seeded `ProjectType` master, same activation pattern `TenderPortal` got in ADR-056). `value`/`budget` are plain `Decimal(19,4)` planning figures — docs/04 is explicit that "estimated budget is not an accounting posting"; `gstApplicable` is an intent flag only, the real GST registration resolves from legal entity + client at invoice-posting time in Phase 4. `workOrderNumber`/`workOrderDate` are plain optional fields, conceptually tied to but not validated against the `WORK_ORDER_RECEIVED` stage. No project number/code beyond `Project.id` — mirrors Lead's own precedent.
(4) **No delete/deactivate**, mirroring Lead/Tender exactly (confirmed acceptable by the user this session) — retirement happens via the `CANCELLED`/`CLOSED` terminal statuses.
(5) **No Documents or Progress tab this slice.** "Project documents" is the roadmap's own separate Slice 3.6 deliverable. `project-detail.html`'s financial/physical summaries (milestones, invoices, receipts, costs, journals, progress) are all deferred until their respective backends exist (Phase 4 / Slice 3.6) — mirrors Client's own ADR-053 "compose only what exists this slice" precedent exactly.
(6) **No "Convert to Project" auto-populate flow on Lead's/Tender's own detail pages.** Project creation is a plain add-form with manual, optional Lead/Tender origin dropdowns — the same UX Tender already uses for its own optional Lead origin. The snapshot-on-convert shortcut (`project.createFromLead`, named in docs/04's `lead-view-detail.html` contract) is deferred to a fast-follow, consistent with how Client's "Leads mini-list" was deferred in ADR-054.
(7) **Project Manager scope enforcement is *not* implemented this slice**, even though `projectManagerId` exists and `project.change_stage`/`change_status` are granted to the Project Manager role — `project.list`/`getDetail` return organization-wide results for every permitted role, identical to Accountant/Admin/Admin Staff. The real per-assignment filtering is Phase 3's own separate, named exit criterion tied to Slice 3.5 ("Project team assignments") — disclosed, not silently skipped.
(8) **Permissions**: six new keys — `project.read`/`create`/`update`/`export`/`change_stage`/`change_status`. Admin: all (automatic). Accountant: `project.read` only. Project Manager: `project.read` + `change_stage` + `change_status` (their execution-management domain) but not `create`/`update` — PMs operate on assigned projects, they don't originate them, the first role permission set in this codebase shaped around a role's own namesake domain rather than uniform read/write access. Admin Staff: `project.read` only. Employee: none. CEO: `project.read` automatic via the existing `*.read` filter.
(9) **New "Projects" nav group**, not folded into "CRM" — docs/16's prototype itself separates `02-crm/` from `03-projects/` as distinct folders, and Phase 3's own title ("Parties, CRM, and projects") already treats them as adjacent-but-distinct.
Consequences: New `Project` model (migration `20260806064500_phase3_04_projects_core`, hand-written and applied via `psql`+`prisma migrate resolve --applied` rather than `prisma migrate dev` — the same `phase3_02_leads` checksum-drift block recurred a third time, resolved the same non-destructive way each time, no data lost). Back-relations added to `Party`, `LegalEntity`, `Lead`, `Tender`, `Employee` (`projectsManaged`), `Introducer`, `ProjectType` (which also gained `@@unique([id, organizationId])`, the same FK-target fix every newly-activated master has needed). New `apps/api/src/projects/*` (port/service/module) — `ProjectsService` is structurally the first service in this codebase with dedicated state-transition commands separate from `update`, including `TERMINAL_STATUSES`-guarded rejection of any further stage/status change once `CANCELLED`/`CLOSED`. `packages/contracts/src/routers/projects.ts`, wired into context/router/app.module/main exactly like every prior module. New `(platform)/projects/{page.tsx,shared.tsx,[id]/page.tsx}` — the detail page's "Stage & Status Actions" card offers only the single valid forward-stage button plus a "Move Backward…" action opening a reason-required `Modal` (same UX shape as Financial Years' reopen-reason modal), matching decision (2)'s rule that the UI itself never offers a skip-ahead option. New `ProjectsIcon` + the new "Projects" nav group.
Verified with 14 new `projects.integration-spec.ts` tests (create defaulting to stage WON / status ACTIVE; create with optional Lead+Tender origin; client-without-clientProfile and nonexistent-legal-entity rejection; diffed update excluding stage/status + stale-version rejection; cross-org isolation; forward stage move; rejected skip-ahead; backward move requiring and recording a reason; version-conflicted stage change rejection; free ACTIVE↔ON_HOLD toggle; cancellation requiring a reason from both ACTIVE and ON_HOLD; full lockout of further stage/status changes once cancelled; CLOSED only reachable once stage is SUCCESSFULLY_COMPLETED) — 119 integration tests pass workspace-wide (105 prior + 14 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all 9 workspaces, run with Turbo's cache forced off per the ADR-056 cache-replay lesson. The API was proactively rebuilt and restarted before live verification this time, per ADR-056's own documented gotcha. Verified live via Playwright: created a Project against a real client and legal entity, confirmed it defaults to Won/Active, walked a forward stage transition, confirmed the UI never exposes a skip-ahead control, moved backward with a required reason, toggled On Hold/Active freely, cancelled with a required reason, and confirmed the terminal state disables every further action — zero console errors, 12/13 checks passing (the one apparent failure was the verification script's own nav-visibility timing, confirmed passing on a targeted recheck, not an application defect).
Supersedes: none — additive.
```

```text
ID: ADR-058
Date: 2026-08-06
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 3, Slice 3.5 — Project team assignments, implementing Phase 3's own named exit criterion ("Project Manager scope is enforced") deferred explicitly in ADR-057 decision 7. docs/04-screen-contracts.md: *"Project Manager access is restricted to assigned projects where the rule applies. Accountant and Admin access remains organization/legal-entity scoped. Permissions are enforced in the API for queries, commands, file access and exports."* docs/03-domain-model.md's diagram already named the child table `project_team_members`. This is the first place in this codebase where authorization depends on *which rows* an actor is tied to, not just a yes/no permission gate — every prior `requirePermission(key)` check has been a flat boolean. Two decisions were resolved with the user before planning: the scoping mechanism (a new `project.read_all` permission, not role-name matching) and team-management ownership (Admin-only via `project.update`, not a PM self-service capability).
Decision:
(1) **New `ProjectTeamMember` model**, additive to (not replacing) `Project.projectManagerId` from ADR-057. Shape mirrors `UserRole`'s own precedent — a simple grant/revoke join row (`id`/`organizationId`/`projectId`/`employeeId`/`role`/`createdAt`/`createdBy`), no soft-delete, no version; removal is a hard delete. `role` is a free-text label on this project (e.g. "Site Engineer"), not a fixed enum — nothing in the docs constrains it, and it doesn't affect scoping. `@@unique([projectId, employeeId])` prevents double-assignment.
(2) **"Assigned to a project" = `projectManagerId` match OR a `ProjectTeamMember` row** — both count uniformly; a `role` value never affects scope.
(3) **New `project.read_all` permission** (a permission-key check, not role-name string matching — this codebase has never made an authorization decision by matching a role's display name), granted explicitly to Admin (automatic, full set), Accountant, Admin Staff, and CEO (explicit add, since `read_all` doesn't end in `.read` so CEO's automatic `*.read` filter wouldn't catch it — same precedent `document.download` already set). Project Manager keeps only plain `project.read`, which becomes scoped.
(4) **Scoping applies to reads and commands alike** (`list`, `getDetail`, `update`, `changeStage`, `changeStatus`, `listTeamMembers`), per docs/04's explicit "queries, commands, file access and exports." The router computes `canSeeAll = ctx.auth.permissions.has('project.read_all')` and passes it plus `actorId` into every `ProjectsService` call; the service resolves the actor's linked `Employee` internally (the same `findFirst({ organizationId, userId: actorId })` lookup `create()` already used) and, when not `canSeeAll`, adds the assignment filter. Out-of-scope single-record access returns `NOT_FOUND`, not `FORBIDDEN` — matching this codebase's existing cross-org-isolation philosophy of "act like it doesn't exist" rather than confirming existence to an unauthorized caller. A scoped actor with no linked `Employee` gets an empty `list` and `NOT_FOUND` everywhere else — they can't be assigned to anything.
(5) **Team roster lives on a new "Team" tab on the Project detail page** (which also required restructuring that page to use `Tabs` for the first time, matching Lead/Tender's own pattern) — the only place these rows can be created. Add (Employee `Select` + optional free-text role `Input`) and per-row Remove, both gated by `project.update` (Admin-only, per the user's explicit choice) and blocked once the project is `CANCELLED`/`CLOSED` (extends ADR-057's existing terminal-status guard). Viewing the tab only requires `project.read` and is itself scope-checked (reuses `getDetail`'s own scope check before listing).
(6) **`Project.projectManagerId` is untouched** — still edited via the existing Project edit drawer, still Admin-only via `project.update`. `ProjectTeamMember` is a genuinely additive roster, not a replacement.
Consequences: New `ProjectTeamMember` model (migration `20260806072600_phase3_05_project_team_members`, hand-written and applied via `psql`+`prisma migrate resolve --applied` — the same `phase3_02_leads` checksum-drift block recurred a fourth time, resolved the same non-destructive way as every prior slice). Back-relations added to `Project` (`teamMembers`) and `Employee` (`projectTeamMemberships`). One new permission key (`project.read_all`); permission catalog count corrected 40 → 41. `ProjectsService` gained a `resolveActorEmployeeId`/`buildScopeWhere` pair reused by every scoped method, plus `listTeamMembers`/`addTeamMember`/`removeTeamMember`. `packages/contracts/src/routers/projects.ts`'s `list`/`getDetail`/`update`/`changeStage`/`changeStatus` each now compute `canSeeAll` before calling the service; three new procedures for team management. `(platform)/projects/[id]/page.tsx` restructured to use `Tabs` (Detail/Team) for the first time — previously a flat single-section layout.

Verified with 9 new `projects.integration-spec.ts` tests (scoped actor sees only `projectManagerId`-matched or `ProjectTeamMember`-matched projects in `list`; `getDetail` on an out-of-scope project rejects `NOT_FOUND`; `ProjectTeamMember`-only assignment — no `projectManagerId` — also grants scope; an unscoped `canSeeAll` actor sees everything; an actor with no linked Employee gets an empty list and `NOT_FOUND`; `changeStage` rejects out-of-scope and succeeds in-scope; team add/list/duplicate-rejection; employee-must-exist-in-org rejection; remove + re-remove `NOT_FOUND`; team add/remove blocked once cancelled) — 128 integration tests pass workspace-wide (119 prior + 9 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all 9 workspaces, forced fresh. The API was rebuilt and restarted before live verification, continuing the ADR-056/057 habit. Verified live via Playwright with a real second login: created a real Project-Manager-role user (linked Employee, real argon2-hashed password, no shortcuts), created two projects as Admin, assigned the new user to one via the Team tab, logged in as that user and confirmed they saw only the assigned project in `/projects`, confirmed the unassigned project was invisible, and confirmed they could successfully execute a stage change on their own project — 6/6 checks passing, zero console errors.
Supersedes: none — additive.
```

```text
ID: ADR-059
Date: 2026-08-06
Status: Accepted
Context: User report: after customizing the `Employee` role via the Roles UI to add `client.create`/`client.read` (beyond seed defaults), the "Add Client" form's State/City/Client Type dropdowns showed "No data." Root cause: `masters.list` and `geography.listStates/listDistricts/listCities` were gated by `master.read` — a permission entirely separate from `client.create`, with nothing surfacing that dependency to an admin configuring role permissions. The same coupling exists for every entity form referencing lookup masters (Lead Type/Source, Tender Portal, Project Type, etc.) — any role granted create/update rights on an entity but not also `master.read` hits the identical silent failure. User's explicit choice: make lookup reads permission-free rather than auto-implying `master.read` or patching the one role.
Decision: `masters.list` (`packages/contracts/src/routers/masters.ts`) and `geography.listStates`/`listDistricts`/`listCities` (`packages/contracts/src/routers/geography.ts`) changed from `requirePermission('master.read')` to `protectedProcedure` — any authenticated org user can read lookup lists to fill out a form they're otherwise permitted to submit. `master.read` still gates `getCatalog`/`geography.getCounts` (the Masters admin screen's own data) and the "Masters" nav item itself; `master.manage` still gates every create/update/setActive mutation on both routers, unchanged. A non-`master.read` user could now reach `/masters/<type>` directly by URL and see a read-only list (the nav link stays hidden) — accepted as a low-risk side effect since master/geography data is non-sensitive, org-wide reference data.
Consequences: Fixed and verified live — a fresh Employee-role test user (matching the live-customized role exactly) now sees populated Client Type and State dropdowns on Add Client, zero console errors. In the same pass, fixed a pre-existing, unrelated cleanup bug in `apps/api/src/rbac/users.integration-spec.ts`'s `afterAll` — it deleted `UserRole` but never `RolePermission`/`Role` before deleting the test organization, silently leaving 12 orphaned "Detail Test Role" rows accumulated over the session (harmless — org-scoped queries could never surface them to a real user — but real DB debris). Cleaned up the 12 orphaned rows and confirmed a fresh test run leaves none behind.

A broader live sweep across Accountant/Admin Staff/CEO (real logins, ground-truth permissions read from `auth.me`) confirmed nav visibility is correct everywhere, including that `Users`/`Roles` nav items are correctly gated by `user.manage` (not the broader `user.read`/`role.read` those roles also hold — intentional, unchanged). The sweep found the identical bug class in three more places — Leads' Introducer filter (`introducers.list`, gated by `introducer.read`), Projects' Legal Entity filter (`legalEntities.list`, gated by `legal_entity.read`), and a `roles.list` call on the Employees page with no visible page impact — left unfixed pending user direction, since Legal Entity data includes PAN (more sensitive than Master/Introducer lookups) and deserves its own explicit decision rather than being swept into this fix.
Supersedes: none — narrows two routers' read gating; no other behavior changes.
```

```text
ID: ADR-060
Date: 2026-08-06
Status: Accepted
Context: User's explicit, unambiguous policy directive: "deactivated records must be visible in filters, readonly, etc. but only when insert/update then deactivated records should not be selected." Investigation found `clients.list`, `legalEntities.list`, and `introducers.list` had **zero** active/inactive filtering — every Lead/Tender/Project/Financial-Year create/edit form's Client/Legal-Entity/Introducer dropdown was silently offering deactivated records as selectable options. By contrast, `masters.list`, `geography.list*`, and `employees.list` already defaulted to active-only when `includeInactive` was omitted, matching the desired policy for those three lookups already — but the audit surfaced that several call sites of `masters.list` (Leads' Lead Type filter, Tenders' Tender Portal filter, Projects' Project Type filter, Employees' Department/Designation filters) were passing `includeInactive: false` in a *filter* context, which is the opposite defect: correctly excluding deactivated records from a form but incorrectly hiding them from a filter, contradicting the same policy from the other direction.
Decision: (1) Extended `ClientPort`/`LegalEntityPort`/`IntroducerPort`'s `list()` signatures with `includeInactive: boolean | undefined`, matching the exact existing `masters`/`geography`/`employees` pattern (`...(input.includeInactive ? {} : { isActive: true })` in the Prisma `where`, omitted/`undefined` defaults to active-only). Routers accept `{ includeInactive: z.boolean().optional() }.optional()` as input, same shape as the established precedent. (2) Every frontend call site was individually classified as **form** (create/edit drawer — must exclude inactive, `includeInactive: false`) or **filter/entity-own-list-page** (list-page filter row, or an entity's own admin browse screen like `/clients`, `/legal-entities`, `/introducers` — must include everything, `includeInactive: true`), and fixed accordingly; this also caught and fixed the four pre-existing `masters.list` filter-context bugs described above (Leads/Tenders/Projects list-page type filters, and Employees' Department/Designation filters). (3) Two screens (`financial-years/page.tsx`'s Legal Entity selector, `employees/page.tsx`'s Department/Designation) use a *single* selector for both a scoping/filter role and a create-form role — these were split into two separate queries (an "all" query for the filter/table-lookup use, an "active" query for the form's options), rather than picking one behavior and breaking the other.
Consequences: `apps/api/src/clients/clients.service.ts`, `apps/api/src/organization/legal-entity.service.ts`, `apps/api/src/introducers/introducers.service.ts` each gained the `includeInactive` filter; `packages/contracts/src/ports/{client,legal-entity,introducer}.port.ts` and `packages/contracts/src/routers/{clients,legal-entities,introducers}.ts` updated to match. 15 frontend call sites across `leads/`, `tenders/`, `projects/`, `financial-years/`, `clients/`, `legal-entities/`, `introducers/`, `employees/` updated with the correct explicit `includeInactive` value. Existing integration tests (`clients.integration-spec.ts`, `introducers.integration-spec.ts`, `organization.integration-spec.ts`) that deactivate-then-list were fixed to pass `includeInactive: true` to keep testing what they originally tested (not weakened to `undefined`, which would have silently stopped exercising the deactivated-row-still-visible path); three new dedicated assertions added (`clients`/`introducers`/`legalEntities` list defaults to active-only after deactivation, and `includeInactive: true` still surfaces the inactive row) confirming the new parameter's own behavior, not just backward-compatibility. 128 integration tests pass workspace-wide (no net new test files, three specs gained assertions), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all workspaces, forced fresh. API rebuilt and restarted. Live-verified directly against the running API (no browser-automation tool available this session): created a real Introducer via `introducers.create`, deactivated it via `setActive`, confirmed the default `introducers.list` (form behavior) excludes it and `introducers.list({ includeInactive: true })` (filter behavior) includes it with `isActive: false` — both confirmed against the live database, not just the test suite — then cleaned up the test row.
Supersedes: none — additive; corrects a latent default-behavior gap in three routers and a filter/form-context mix-up in four pre-existing `masters.list` call sites.
```

```text
ID: ADR-061
Date: 2026-08-06
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 3, Slice 3.6 — Project documents and physical progress, the last unstarted slice in Phase 3 (`docs/11-development-roadmap.md`). `docs/04-screen-contracts.md` §10 names `project-physical.html` (`projectProgress.list/create`; `project.read`, `project_progress.create`) and `project-documentation.html`/`project-document-upload.html` (`document.list`; `project.read`, `document.download/upload`) as the two remaining `ACCEPT`-disposition screens. Milestones, EMD/SD, and Expense are separate, later screens — out of scope, consistent with ADR-057 decision 5's "compose only what exists this slice" precedent.

Three things were established before implementation: (1) the Documents backend was already entity-agnostic and already anticipated this slice — the `ENTITY_TYPES` seed already listed `PROJECT`, and `documents-tab.tsx`'s own comment already said "reused verbatim across every entity... (Lead, Tender, and future Project)" — so no new Documents backend work was needed, only widening the component's `entityType` union. (2) Project is the only entity with row-level (Project-Manager) scoping (ADR-058), and docs/04's own text requires scoping to cover "queries, commands, **file access** and exports" — the generic `documents.listLinksForEntity`/`linkToEntity` procedures had no per-entity awareness, so a scoped actor could otherwise reach documents for a project outside their assignment via a direct API call. (3) A related pre-existing bug, found during research and fixed in this slice at the user's explicit request: `documents-tab.tsx`'s "Add Document" card only renders when the actor holds both `document.upload` and `document.manage`, but `document.manage` had only ever been granted to Admin — so despite Accountant/Admin Staff/Employee/Project Manager all holding `document.upload`, none of them could complete an upload on any entity, including the already-shipped Lead/Tender tabs.

Decision:
(1) **New `ProjectProgress` model**, shaped like `LeadUpdate`/`TenderUpdate` (`title`/`note`/`createdBy`/`createdByEmail`) plus a `percentage Int` column. No denormalized "current %" on `Project` — current progress is derived by reading the latest (highest-percentage) row, the same "read model derives from an append-only table" precedent `LeadUpdate`/`TenderUpdate` already set.
(2) **`percentage` must be an integer 0–100 and strictly greater than the current latest percentage** (0 baseline if none exist yet) — "each update can only increase %", per the prototype's own copy. Enforced in `ProjectsService`, not a database constraint. Rejected once the project is `CANCELLED`/`CLOSED`, the same terminal-status guard team management already uses.
(3) **New `project_progress.create` permission** (exact literal from docs/04), granted to Admin (automatic) and Project Manager — physical-progress reporting is the PM's execution-management domain, the same reasoning ADR-057 decision 8 used for granting `change_stage`/`change_status` to PM but not Accountant/Admin Staff/CEO. Listing progress only requires the existing, already-scoped `project.read`.
(4) **`listProgress`/`addProgress` reuse `getDetail`'s own scope check** (throws `NOT_FOUND` for an out-of-scope actor before any progress row is read or written) — same pattern `listTeamMembers` already established in ADR-058, avoiding a second implementation of the scope logic.
(5) **Documents router gets a targeted, entity-type-specific scope guard**, not a general one: `documents.listLinksForEntity` and `documents.linkToEntity` call `ProjectsService.getDetail` (which already throws `NOT_FOUND` for an out-of-scope actor) when `input.entityType === 'PROJECT'`, before delegating to the entity-agnostic `DocumentsService`. This closes the "file access" scoping gap docs/04 calls out without making `DocumentsService` itself aware of Projects — the guard lives in the router layer, which already has access to every service, rather than creating a cross-module service dependency. `initiateUpload`/`confirmUpload` don't take an `entityId` (they register raw bytes before any entity link exists) so need no change.
(6) **`document.manage` bugfix, fixed in this slice at the user's explicit choice** (offered a "fix now" vs. "leave as a known issue, Project inherits it" choice; user chose to fix now): added `document.manage` alongside every existing `document.upload` grant — `ACCOUNTANT_PERMISSIONS`, `PROJECT_MANAGER_PERMISSIONS` (which also gained `document.upload` itself — it had neither before), `ADMIN_STAFF_PERMISSIONS`, `EMPLOYEE_PERMISSIONS`. CEO and any role without `document.upload` stays without `document.manage` too — consistent, CEO isn't meant to upload.
(7) **Documentation tab is a one-line reuse of `DocumentsTab`** (`entityType="PROJECT"`), no category/search filters or stat tiles from the richer `project-documentation.html` mockup — the same simplification already shipped for Lead/Tender's Documents tabs, disclosed, not a new gap. **Physical Progress tab** is a new `ProgressTab` component: a timeline card (title, date, `+N%` delta, running %, progress bar, note, `by <email>`) plus a "Current Physical Progress" summary card and an "Add Progress Update" form (title, antd `Slider` bounded `[currentPercent + 1, 100]`, note), gated by `project_progress.create`, hidden once terminal or already at 100%. Omits the mockup's "vs X% financial" comparison — no financial/milestone backend exists yet, same "compose only what exists" precedent as ADR-057 decision 5.

Consequences: New `ProjectProgress` model (migration `20260806105900_phase3_06_project_progress`, hand-written and applied via `psql`+`prisma migrate resolve --applied` — the same `phase3_02_leads` checksum-drift block recurred a fifth time, resolved the same non-destructive way as every prior Phase 3 slice). Back-relation added to `Project` (`progressEntries`). `ProjectsService` gained `listProgress`/`addProgress`; `packages/contracts/src/routers/projects.ts` gained matching procedures; `packages/contracts/src/routers/documents.ts` gained the PROJECT-scope guard on two procedures. `apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx`'s `entityType` widened to include `'PROJECT'`. `(platform)/projects/[id]/page.tsx` gained two new tabs (Physical Progress, Documentation) alongside the existing Detail/Team. One new permission key (`project_progress.create`); permission catalog count corrected 41 → 42. `document.manage` now granted everywhere `document.upload` already is.

Verified with 6 new `projects.integration-spec.ts` tests (increasing-percentage timeline, newest-first listing; rejects non-increasing percentage; rejects out-of-range/non-integer percentage; rejects once cancelled; Project-Manager scoping on add/list mirrors the existing project scoping tests; unscoped actor sees everything) — 133 integration tests pass workspace-wide (127 prior + 6 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all workspaces, forced fresh. `roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog-count assertion updated 41 → 42. No dedicated test exists for the `documents.ts` router-level PROJECT scope guard — this codebase has never unit-tested tRPC router logic directly (always services), so it was verified live instead, consistent with how permission-gating itself is verified. API rebuilt and restarted. No browser-automation tool is available this session, so live verification was done directly against the running API via `curl` with real session cookies: created a real Project-Manager-role test user (real argon2-hashed password, linked Employee, no shortcuts) unassigned to an existing real project — confirmed `projects.listProgress` and `documents.listLinksForEntity`/`linkToEntity` for that project both reject `NOT_FOUND`; added a progress update as Admin (20%), confirmed a lower/equal percentage is rejected with the correct message; assigned the PM test user to the project via `addTeamMember`, confirmed `listProgress` and `addProgress` (35%) then succeed for that same actor; performed a complete upload→confirm→link cycle against the real configured S3 bucket as the PM test user (not Admin) to confirm the `document.manage` bugfix actually unblocks upload end-to-end, and confirmed the resulting document appears in `listLinksForEntity`. All test rows (progress entries, team membership, document, document link, audit events, session, user, employee) cleaned up afterward; confirmed the project's one pre-existing team member (unrelated to this test) was left untouched.
Supersedes: none — additive; the `document.manage` fix (decision 6) widens three roles' existing upload capability from theoretical to functional, no other behavior changes.

**Post-merge addendum (same day):** the user hit a browser CORS error on the very first real browser upload against this slice's new Documentation tab — invisible to the `curl`-based live verification above, since `curl` doesn't enforce CORS the way a browser does. Root cause: the `zitaware-solutions` S3 bucket's CORS `AllowedOrigins` were `https://mtcore.zitaware.in` and `http://localhost:3000` (Next.js's default dev port), but this project's actual dev server runs on `http://localhost:5000` (`WEB_PORT=5000` in `.env`) — whoever configured the bucket used the framework default instead of this project's real port, so the browser origin was never whitelisted. Fixed by adding `http://localhost:5000` to the bucket's `AllowedOrigins` via a one-off script using the same `@aws-sdk/client-s3` package and credentials the app itself uses (additive only — `mtcore.zitaware.in` and `localhost:3000` left in place), applied directly to the live AWS bucket after explicit user confirmation since it's infrastructure outside git.
```
