Confirmed via `GetBucketCorsCommand` that the new origin is present. Not a code change — no files in this repo were touched for this fix, and there is nothing to `git commit`. Lesson for future slices: this session's established "verify live via curl since no browser tool is available" method structurally cannot catch CORS issues; a genuine object-upload smoke test still needs an actual browser at some point before calling a Documents-consuming slice fully verified.

```text
ID: ADR-062
Date: 2026-08-07
Status: Accepted
Context: Phase 4, Slice 4.1 — Chart of Accounts, the first slice of Phase 4 ("Milestones and accounting core") and the first Phase 4 slice overall. Phase 3 is complete (ADR-053–061). `docs/03-domain-model.md`'s accounting diagram roots the entire accounting subtree at `chart_of_accounts ├── account_groups └── ledger_accounts` — journal entries and the posting engine reference ledger accounts and cannot exist first. `docs/04-screen-contracts.md` line 565 names it `NEW_REQUIRED`: *"Accountant-managed accounts and read-only journals/source drill-down."* `ExpenseCategory`/`PenaltyCategory`'s own Phase-2 schema comments ("ledger-account linkage deferred to Phase 4") already anticipated this dependency.

A first plan draft was rejected and replaced after detailed user feedback demanding two explicit, non-negotiable objectives: a durable accounting foundation (classification structurally sound, every future journal-line dimension attachable later without schema rework, audit-complete, optimistic-concurrency safe, no physical deletion) and a simple daily UI (one page, two plain tables, one drawer each, no nested routes or trees, derived fields read-only). This ADR reflects the revised, approved design.
Decision:
(1) **One organization-wide Chart of Accounts**, not legal-entity-scoped — future journal lines carry the legal-entity dimension per document (docs/06's journal *header*), same as every other lookup master in this app.
(2) **Two dedicated models** (`AccountGroup`, `LedgerAccount`), not the generic Masters polymorphic system — both carry fields beyond the generic masters' flat code/name/description baseline, the same reasoning that already forced `ExpenseCategory`/`PenaltyCategory` out of that system. A bounded `ChartOfAccountsPort`/`ChartOfAccountsService` inside a new `apps/api/src/accounting/` module — explicitly not a monolithic `AccountingPort`, so later Phase 4/5 accounting slices (posting engine, journals) get their own ports/services in the same module.
(3) **`AccountGroup` classification**: `accountType` (`ASSET|LIABILITY|EQUITY|INCOME|EXPENSE`, the standard double-entry classification) and `normalBalance` (`DEBIT|CREDIT`) are both stored explicitly on every group row (root and custom alike), not derived by a tree-walk on every read — the service enforces the deterministic pairing at write time. A custom group **inherits** both from its parent at creation (the create API doesn't accept them for a non-root group); re-parenting is only permitted to a candidate parent whose stored values match the group's own already-stored values, which is what makes classification never need to cascade to children. The five seeded roots (Assets/Liabilities/Equity/Income/Expenses, `isSystemDefined: true`) have permanently fixed `code`/`accountType`/`normalBalance`/`null` parent — the service rejects any re-parent attempt on a system-defined group, and those three fields are simply never accepted as update-input for any group, making most of the immutability rule structural rather than a runtime branch. `name`/`description` remain editable even on system roots. Every custom group requires an active parent (no second tier of parent-less groups is ever created outside the seed script). Re-parenting rejects direct (`A→A`) and indirect (`A→B→C`, then `A`'s parent set to `C`) cycles by walking up the candidate parent's chain. Deactivating a group is rejected while any active child group or active ledger account still references it, with a specific dependent-count message, and is never permitted at all for a system-defined root.
(4) **`LedgerAccount`** carries no `accountType`/`normalBalance` of its own — always derived via the `accountGroup` relation at read time (a single join, not a tree walk, since an account has exactly one group). `allowManualPosting` (default `true`, disclosed default — neither source document specifies one) flags future postability. Moving an account to a new group is only permitted to an active group whose derived classification matches the account's current group's — same compatible-or-reject rule as group re-parenting. No physical deletion anywhere, only `isActive` toggles.
(5) **Code normalization**: every `code` is trimmed and upper-cased in the service (the sole write path in this codebase) before storage, so the existing `@@unique([organizationId, code])` on the canonical column already prevents `BANK`/`bank` coexisting. As explicit defense-in-depth matching the literal "enforced at the database level" requirement, the hand-written migration additionally adds a `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... (organization_id, upper(btrim(code)))` expression index on both tables, independent of the application write path.
(6) **Permissions** `chart_of_account.read`/`chart_of_account.manage` — Admin automatic, Accountant both explicitly (mirrors the existing `legal_entity.manage` precedent), CEO `read` automatic via the `*.read`-suffix filter, no grant to Project Manager/Admin Staff/Employee (consistent with `legal_entity.read` similarly withheld). New "Finance" nav group (Legal Entities/Financial Years sit under "Organization", which is entity/period administration, not accounting structure) with one "Chart of Accounts" item.
(7) **UI**: one page, two `UiCard` sections (Account Groups, Ledger Accounts), one `Drawer` each. Account Group drawer fields: Code (immutable after creation, disabled in edit), Name, Parent Group (searchable, active groups only, excludes the group itself and its own descendants — computed client-side from the already-fetched flat list, so the picker can never even offer a cycle-causing choice), Description; Account Type/Normal Balance shown read-only, derived from the selected parent, never an editable control. Ledger Account drawer fields: Account Code (immutable, disabled in edit), Account Name, Account Group, Description, and `allowManualPosting` inside a collapsed "Advanced settings" panel with a plain-language explanation. Every create/edit/deactivate control is entirely absent (not disabled) for an actor without `chart_of_account.manage` — a CEO or other read-only viewer sees a clean table with no dead controls. The list queries use `includeInactive: true` (this is the entity's own management screen, per this session's established filter/entity-own-list-page convention from the earlier deactivated-records fix). Frontend performs no accounting calculations — Account Type/Normal Balance display always comes from the server.
(8) **Explicitly out of scope**: journal entries, the posting engine, opening balances, bank reconciliation, invoices/receipts/payments, GST/TDS postings, EMD/SD or retention accounting, control-account mappings (named as a future posting-engine responsibility), project profitability, financial statements, source-document drill-down, seeded leaf ledger accounts, and retrofitting `ExpenseCategory`/`PenaltyCategory` with a `ledgerAccountId` FK (their consuming features don't exist until Phase 5/6 either).
Consequences: New `AccountGroup`/`LedgerAccount` models plus `AccountType`/`NormalBalance` enums (migration `20260807060000_phase4_01_chart_of_accounts`, hand-written from the start — this slice needed the two expression indexes Prisma's schema DSL can't express anyway, so the recurring `phase3_02_leads` checksum-drift block that has forced every prior slice into the same hand-written-SQL + `psql` + `prisma migrate resolve --applied` procedure wasn't even the primary reason this time, though it was hit too when a plain `migrate dev --create-only` was first attempted). New `apps/api/src/accounting/{accounting.module.ts,chart-of-accounts.service.ts}`, `packages/contracts/src/{ports/chart-of-accounts.port.ts,routers/chart-of-accounts.ts}`, wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`app.module.ts`/`main.ts` exactly like every prior module. Two new permission keys; permission catalog count corrected 42 → 44. Five `AccountGroup` root rows seeded directly via Prisma (bypassing the service, as every other seeded baseline already does). New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/chart-of-accounts/page.tsx`, new `ChartOfAccountsIcon`, new "Finance" nav group.

Verified with 18 new `chart-of-accounts.integration-spec.ts` tests (seeded-root correctness; custom-group creation inheriting classification; rejecting create under a missing/inactive parent; rejecting a duplicate code including a normalized duplicate; rejecting system-root re-parenting while allowing name/description edits; rejecting direct and multi-level re-parent cycles; rejecting re-parenting under an inactive or classification-incompatible parent; rejecting deactivation of a system root and of a group with active dependents; deactivate/reactivate lifecycle; stale-version rejection on update and setActive; cross-org isolation; ledger-account creation under a missing/inactive group rejected; ledger-account move rejected to an incompatible or inactive group and accepted to a compatible active one with full audit old/new values; deactivate/reactivate lifecycle; stale-version rejection; cross-org isolation) — 151 integration tests pass workspace-wide (133 prior + 18 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration/production-`build` green across all workspaces, forced fresh. `roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog-count assertion updated 42 → 44. A genuinely fresh database (`zitaware_crm_migration_check`, created and dropped for this check) replayed the entire 22-migration history end-to-end via `prisma migrate deploy` with zero errors, including this slice's hand-written migration; a follow-up `prisma migrate diff` against that freshly-replayed database showed zero drift attributable to this slice — the only remaining diff is a pre-existing, out-of-scope `cities`/`districts` constraint drift already present before this slice started. API rebuilt and restarted. No browser-automation tool is available this session — live-verified directly against the running API via `curl`: confirmed all five roots present with correct type/normalBalance; confirmed deactivate/re-parent rejected on a system root; created a real custom group and ledger account through the router; confirmed a normalized-duplicate code rejection; confirmed a stale-`expectedVersion` update returns HTTP 409/`VERSION_CONFLICT`; created a real Project-Manager-role test user (real argon2-hashed password) and confirmed `chartOfAccounts.listGroups` returns `FORBIDDEN` for a role holding neither `chart_of_account.read` nor `.manage`. All test rows and the test user cleaned up afterward — confirmed exactly the five seeded roots and zero ledger accounts remain. Per the plan's own disclosed gap: the UI-specific requirements (derived fields rendering read-only, controls hidden rather than disabled for a read-only viewer, responsive layout, zero console errors) could only be verified by static code review this session, not a live browser pass, since no browser-automation tool is available — flagged explicitly, not silently claimed as done.
Supersedes: none — additive; the first Phase 4 slice.
```

```text
ID: ADR-063
Date: 2026-08-08
Status: Accepted — complete (UI verification confirmed by the user 2026-08-13; see docs/13-progress.md's "Phase 4 UI verification sweep" entry)
Context: Phase 4, Slice 4.2 — CAPEX/OPEX Milestones, the second Phase 4 slice (ADR-062's Chart of Accounts is the frozen 4.1 baseline). `docs/01-business-decisions.md` states plainly "a schedule row creates an alert; it does not create an invoice or journal" and `docs/05-workflows-state-machines.md` confirms "Schedule generation creates no accounting entry" — the slice boundary is milestones and schedule generation only; Payment Alerts, invoices, receipts, journals, and postings are all explicitly deferred.

A first plan draft was rejected and comprehensively revised after a detailed, 14-point user correction identifying real gaps: a missing `@@unique([id, organizationId])` the draft's own child-table FKs depended on; user-facing, hand-typed technical codes instead of server-generated ones; an unenforced CAPEX allocation invariant (`approvedAmount` treated as reference-only); a referenced-but-missing `updateEvent` operation; an incomplete achievement state machine (`IN_PROGRESS` unreachable — no `start` action); a service-period/invoice-date conflation (assuming advance billing); client-side money arithmetic; and no real idempotency. This ADR reflects the revised, approved design that closes every one of those fourteen points.
Decision:
(1) **Type-safe database-enforced child FKs**: `ProjectMilestone` carries both `@@unique([id, organizationId])` and `@@unique([id, organizationId, type])`. `MilestoneEvent`/`MilestoneInstallment` each carry a fixed `milestoneType` column (CHECK-locked to `'CAPEX'`/`'OPEX'` respectively) and their FK targets `[milestoneId, organizationId, milestoneType]` → the parent's `[id, organizationId, type]` triple — an event can never physically reference an OPEX-typed milestone; this is enforced by the FK/unique-index mechanism itself, not application trust. Twelve hand-added `CHECK` constraints (positive amounts, valid counts/percentages/reminder-offsets, valid date ranges, and CAPEX/OPEX field-nullability) round out the database-level integrity Prisma's schema DSL can't express on its own.
(2) **Fully server-generated, retry-safe codes** (`CAP-001`/`OPEX-001` per pool, `CAP-001-E01` per event) — no code field is ever an input in any drawer. Computed from the current max count inside the create transaction; on a unique-constraint violation the whole transaction retries (up to 5 attempts) with a freshly recomputed number — the smallest correct mechanism for the actual concurrency window, no dedicated sequence-counter table.
(3) **CAPEX event amounts must sum to the pool's `approvedAmount` exactly** — `createCapex` and `updateEvent` both reject with the precise shortfall/excess (`"...short by X.XX"`/`"...over by X.XX"`) unless the sum of all non-cancelled event amounts equals the approved amount to the paisa (`Prisma.Decimal`, never floats). `percentOfPool` is always derived (`amount / approvedAmount × 100`), never independently entered. Additional approved work requires a new, separately-approved pool — never a silent increase to an existing allocation.
(4) **`updateEvent` added** (name/amount/dueDate, optimistic-concurrency-safe via `expectedVersion`), permitted only while `PLANNED`/`IN_PROGRESS`, rejected once terminal; an amount edit re-validates the pool-wide exact-sum invariant against the *proposed* total, the same way a bad create is rejected. (Because the invariant is enforced per-call and there is no bulk-edit endpoint, a single event's amount can in practice only be changed by simultaneously changing another event or replacing the pool — an accepted, disclosed consequence of decision 3's strictness, not a gap.)
(5) **Complete achievement lifecycle, explicit and separate**: `startEvent`/`markEventAchieved`/`cancelEvent` (CAPEX) and `startMilestone`/`markMilestoneAchieved`/`cancelMilestone` (OPEX) — `PLANNED → IN_PROGRESS → ACHIEVED`, or `→ CANCELLED` (reason required) from either non-terminal state. `markAchieved` is only ever valid from `IN_PROGRESS`, matching `Project`'s own no-stage-skipping precedent (ADR-057). `achievedOn` (business date, required)/`achievedBy` and `cancelledAt`/`cancelledBy`/`cancelReason` are stored on the row itself, in addition to audit events. Cancelling an OPEX milestone transactionally cascades every remaining `PLANNED` installment to a new `CANCELLED` status (already-`SKIPPED` installments are left untouched) — the milestone's own audit event lists the cascaded sequence numbers.
(6) **Service period and invoice date are independent sequences**: `scheduleStartDate` (service start) and `firstInvoiceDate` (planned first invoice) are two separately-captured fields; `generateOpexSchedule` derives both date sequences independently from their own start dates, using the same frequency/count. Month-stepping is calendar-safe with end-of-month clamping (`addMonthsClamped` — 31 Jan + 1 month → 28/29 Feb, never overflowing to March), and service periods are consecutive, non-overlapping, and inclusive (period *i*'s end is the day before period *i+1*'s start).
(7) **One pure, backend-authoritative schedule generator** (`generateOpexSchedule` in `milestone-schedule.util.ts`, no I/O) is the single source of truth, called identically by the non-mutating `previewOpexSchedule` query, the real `createOpex` mutation, and the integration/unit tests directly — the first genuine money-arithmetic code in this codebase beyond storing a value, using `Prisma.Decimal` throughout per CLAUDE.md's floating-point rule. Rounding remainder from an inexact division lands entirely on the final installment. All money/date fields cross the wire as strings; the frontend only formats, never computes anything it submits back.
(8) **Backend-authoritative summaries**: CAPEX/OPEX totals are `Prisma.Decimal` sums computed server-side and returned as decimal strings — the frontend never sums fetched rows. Contract Value uses `Project.value` (already available), not a sum of milestone amounts. An "Invoiced to Date" tile is omitted entirely this slice (no invoicing exists yet) rather than showing a misleading hardcoded ₹0.
(9) **New `milestone.skip_installment` permission, separate from `milestone.cancel`.** Grants deliberately depart from `Project`'s own create/update-is-Admin-only precedent: Admin — all six keys automatically. **Accountant — `read`/`create`/`update`/`skip_installment`** (not `mark_achieved`/`cancel` — OPEX billing schedules are finance-maintained records, per the user's explicit reasoning). **Project Manager — `read`/`mark_achieved`/`cancel`** (covers `start*` too, gated by the same pair — PMs execute delivery status but don't originate schedules or skip billing). Admin Staff — `read`. CEO — `read` automatic via the `*.read`-suffix filter.
(10) **Idempotent, atomic creation**: `createCapex`/`createOpex` accept a client-supplied `idempotencyKey`. A minimally-scoped `MilestoneCreationRequest` table (unique on `organizationId`+`idempotencyKey`) is checked at the top of each retry-loop iteration (not just once) so a concurrent identical-key race is also handled correctly — the loser hits the unique constraint and the retry loop returns the winner's result instead of duplicating. Deliberately scoped to milestone creation only, not the eventual cross-cutting Idempotency Registry (a separate, later Phase 4 deliverable). Every create writes the milestone (+ events or + generated installments), the idempotency record, and the audit event in one transaction.
(11) **Simplified frontend**: one "Milestone" project tab, CAPEX/OPEX sections, `Form.List` CAPEX event rows with a live running-allocated/remaining-balance display blocking Save until the remaining balance is exactly zero (form-state-only arithmetic on values just typed, not a persisted figure — the authoritative check is always the backend's exact-sum validation on submit), an OPEX drawer whose schedule preview comes from `previewOpexSchedule`, never computed client-side. Every action is entirely absent (not disabled) when the actor lacks the permission. Achieve requires an `achievedOn` date input (defaulted to today); cancel/skip require a reason.
(12) **Full auditability, no physical deletion**: every state-changing command records actor/organization/correlation-ID/old-new-values/business-effective-date/reason/generated-details atomically via the existing `recordAuditEvent` helper, inside the same transaction as the data change.
(13) **Migration generated normally, no pre-emptive drift assumption** — `prisma migrate dev` was run first per the user's explicit instruction; the recurring, already-understood `phase3_02_leads` stale-checksum drift did reappear (the same single pre-existing artifact hit in every prior slice, proven non-destructive by ADR-062's own fresh-database replay), diagnosed rather than assumed, before falling back to the established hand-write + `psql` + `prisma migrate resolve --applied` procedure for this one new migration.
Consequences: New `ProjectMilestone`/`MilestoneEvent`/`MilestoneInstallment`/`MilestoneCreationRequest` models plus `MilestoneType`/`MilestoneAchievementStatus`/`InstallmentFrequency`/`InstallmentStatus`/`AlertRecipient` enums (migration `20260808070000_phase4_02_milestones`, hand-written to append the twelve `CHECK` constraints, applied via `psql` + `prisma migrate resolve --applied`). New `apps/api/src/milestones/{milestone-schedule.util.ts,milestones.service.ts,milestones.module.ts}`, `packages/contracts/src/{ports/milestone.port.ts,routers/milestones.ts}`, wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`app.module.ts`/`main.ts` exactly like every prior module. Six new permission keys; permission catalog count corrected 44 → 50. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/milestone-tab.tsx`, wired as a new "Milestone" tab on the existing project detail page.

A genuine bug was found and fixed during this slice's own test run, not by the user: Prisma rejects a bare `'YYYY-MM-DD'` string for `@db.Date`-typed columns at runtime (`PrismaClientValidationError: ...premature end of input. Expected ISO-8601 DateTime.`) even though the generated TypeScript types accept `string | Date` — every date-string input (`approvalDate`, event `dueDate`, `achievedOn`) had to be converted to a full ISO-8601 `Date` instance before being passed into a Prisma `create`/`update`/`updateMany` call. Fixed with a small `toDate(s: string): Date` helper applied at every write site; caught by the integration suite (8 of 19 tests initially failed identically), not by lint or typecheck. A second, unrelated issue was a test-authoring mistake, not a service defect: a test expected editing one CAPEX event's amount to a different value to succeed while a sibling event's amount stayed fixed — mathematically impossible under decision 3's exact-sum invariant with only two events (there is no slack to compensate), so the test was corrected to assert the correct rejection and to instead demonstrate a non-amount field edit succeeding.

Verified with 19 new `milestones.integration-spec.ts` tests (CAPEX: exact-sum creation with full audit values, sum-mismatch rejection with the exact shortfall/excess message, empty-events/non-positive-amount rejection, concurrent-safe code generation under `Promise.all`, full event lifecycle start→achieve and separate cancel-from-planned with terminal-edit rejection, `updateEvent` re-validating the pool sum, idempotent-duplicate-returns-original, cross-org/out-of-scope rejection; OPEX: schedule sums exactly and matches `previewOpexSchedule` exactly, lifecycle start→achieve with skip-ahead rejection, `updateMilestone` editable-fields-only, cancellation cascade with a pre-skipped installment left untouched, skip-installment reason-required and already-skipped rejection, stale-version rejection; Project-Manager scoping: scoped actor sees/creates only on an assigned project, cross-org rejection; database CHECK constraints: negative amount, CAPEX-with-OPEX-fields, zero installment count, all rejected via raw SQL) plus 10 `milestone-schedule.util.spec.ts` unit tests (calendar-safe clamping for 31 Jan in leap/non-leap years, quarterly/yearly stepping, exact-sum and rounding-remainder-on-last-row schedule generation, consecutive/non-overlapping/inclusive periods, independent service-period/invoice-date sequences). 170 integration tests pass in `apps/api` alone (151 prior + 19 new; 11 additional unit tests for the schedule generator run separately under `test`, not `test:integration`), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all workspaces, forced fresh. `roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog-count assertion updated 44 → 50. A genuinely fresh database (`zitaware_crm_migration_check`, created and dropped for this check) replayed the entire 23-migration history end-to-end via `prisma migrate deploy` with zero errors, including this slice's hand-written migration. `apps/api`/`apps/worker` production builds are clean; `apps/web`'s production build failed on `/masters/cities` — confirmed via a `git stash` comparison against the exact same failure on the unmodified Phase-4.1 baseline commit that this is the same pre-existing, non-deterministic upstream Next.js prerender bug already disclosed in ADR-043/052/053 (this time manifesting on a different page), not a regression introduced by this slice.

The API was rebuilt and restarted before live verification. No browser-automation tool is available this session — live-verified directly against the running API via `curl` with the seeded Admin session plus three new real test users (Accountant/Project-Manager/CEO roles, real argon2-hashed passwords, cleaned up afterward): created a real CAPEX pool (₹7,00,00,000 across 3 events) and confirmed a mismatched-sum attempt is rejected with the exact shortfall message and an idempotent duplicate `createCapex` call returns the identical milestone id; walked a full CAPEX event lifecycle (start → achieve with `achievedOn` persisted correctly, and separately cancel with a reason) and confirmed `updateEvent` is rejected once an event is achieved; created a real OPEX milestone (₹2,00,00,000 over 36 monthly installments starting 31 Jan, a non-evenly-divisible and end-of-month-clamping case) and confirmed the persisted schedule matches `previewOpexSchedule`'s output with zero mismatches across all 36 rows, sums exactly, and the rounding remainder lands entirely on the last installment; skipped one installment, then cancelled the milestone and confirmed the cascade left the skipped installment untouched while cancelling the other 35; confirmed the Accountant/Project-Manager/CEO permission boundaries from decision 9 exactly (Accountant can create but not start/achieve; PM cannot create or skip but is correctly scoped out of an unassigned project with `NOT_FOUND`; CEO can read but not create). All test milestones/events/installments/audit events/users/sessions were cleaned up afterward.

**This slice's status stays "UI verification pending," not "complete," until a real browser pass confirms**: (1) the CAPEX/OPEX drawer workflows and the live allocation balance (the `Form.List` dynamic event rows, the running-allocated/remaining-balance display, Save correctly blocked until the remaining balance is exactly zero); (2) the OPEX schedule preview panel and the expandable installment rows on the persisted schedule; (3) role-based action visibility (every create/edit/achieve/cancel/skip control genuinely absent, not just disabled, for an actor lacking the relevant permission); (4) drawer validation, responsive layout, and keyboard/focus behavior; (5) zero browser-console errors. All five were only verified by static code review this session (no browser-automation tool is available), not exercised in a real browser — this is a genuine open gap, not a formality, and must be closed with a live click-through before Slice 4.2 is considered done end-to-end.

The `apps/web` production build's `/masters/cities` prerendering failure remains the same pre-existing, disclosed, intermittent upstream Next.js bug documented in ADR-043/052/053 (confirmed non-regression via a `git stash` comparison against the unmodified baseline commit reproducing the identical failure) — a documented upstream baseline issue, not a Slice 4.2 regression, and it does not block or gate this slice's backend completion.
Supersedes: none — additive; the second Phase 4 slice.
```

```text
ID: ADR-064
Date: 2026-08-09
Status: Accepted — complete (UI verification confirmed by the user 2026-08-13; see docs/13-progress.md's "Phase 4 UI verification sweep" entry)
Context: Phase 4, Slice 4.3 — Payment Alerts, the third Phase 4 slice (ADR-063's Milestones is the frozen 4.2 baseline). `docs/04-screen-contracts.md` §11.3 (`project-payment-alert.html`, `MINOR_CHANGE`) and `docs/05-workflows-state-machines.md`'s OPEX-schedule state machine (`PLANNED → ALERT_OPEN → INVOICE_DRAFT → INVOICED`) both describe this screen, but its own contract also lists `invoice.createDraftFromAlert` — Phase 5 territory per `docs/11-development-roadmap.md`'s own split ("Payment alerts" is a Phase 4 deliverable; invoice drafts are Phase 5). This slice's boundary is therefore alerts and snooze only, the same "one bounded vertical slice at a time" split ADR-063 itself already used to defer alerts out of Milestones — the prototype's "Upload Invoice" action (renamed **Raise Invoice** per docs/04) renders as a disabled, tooltipped placeholder, mirroring ADR-063's own "Alert settings (inactive until Payment Alerts ships)" precedent.

This is the first slice driven by a recurring background job rather than only user-initiated commands. `apps/worker` already had exactly one recurring job (`runOutboxDispatchJob`, a `setInterval` tick wrapped in `JobRun` bookkeeping, dispatching `OutboxEvent` rows into `Notification` rows via a pluggable `NotificationProvider`) — this slice's alert-opening job reuses that exact pipeline rather than inventing a second notification mechanism. Two genuine business-rule ambiguities were resolved with the user before implementation (not guessed silently): (1) whether a CAPEX event's alert should require *both* being due *and* achieved, or *either* — resolved as **either** (OR); (2) who "Account Team" (an existing, previously-inert `Milestone.alertRecipient` option) actually resolves to — resolved as **every active user whose effective permissions include `payment_alert.snooze`**, not a hardcoded role name.
Decision:
(1) **Alerts and snooze only — no invoice creation.** `paymentAlerts.listForProject`/`paymentAlerts.snooze` are implemented; `invoice.createDraftFromAlert` is not. No `Invoice` model, no journal, no posting.
(2) **One unified `PaymentAlert` table**, not separate CAPEX/OPEX tables — the screen's own "Open Alerts"/"Snoozed" sections mix both. A `sourceType` discriminator (`CAPEX_EVENT` | `OPEX_INSTALLMENT`) plus two mutually-exclusive nullable FKs (`sourceEventId` → `MilestoneEvent`, `sourceInstallmentId` → `MilestoneInstallment`) are CHECK-enforced so exactly one is set and matches `sourceType` — the same CHECK-constraint-for-type-safety idiom ADR-063 established, reused rather than inventing a third composite-FK scheme.
(3) **At most one `PaymentAlert` row per source, for its lifetime** — `sourceEventId`/`sourceInstallmentId` are each hard-unique. This single invariant is what makes the whole job naturally idempotent: running it any number of times, at any interval, never double-opens or double-notifies a source, since an already-alerted source is excluded by the same `paymentAlert: null` filter that finds new candidates. `projectId` is denormalized onto `PaymentAlert` (a genuine FK) purely for the project-scoped list query, mirroring `ProjectMilestone.projectId`'s own precedent.
(4) **No stored amount/due-date copy** — always read live via the `sourceEvent`/`sourceInstallment` relation at query time, so an in-flight `updateEvent` correction (still legal while `PLANNED`/`IN_PROGRESS`, ADR-063 decision 4) is automatically reflected, never stale. "Overdue" vs. "Due" is a derived display label (`dueDate < today`), never stored state, matching `docs/05`'s "do not store billing state as independently editable data."
(5) **`status` is `OPEN | SNOOZED` only this slice — no `CLOSED`.** Nothing here can ever close an alert (that requires Phase 5's finalized-invoice event); adding an unreachable value now would be schema-ahead-of-need. A deliberate, disclosed scope cut.
(6) **Alert-opening trigger, confirmed with the user**: OPEX — `today >= plannedInvoiceDate - reminderOffsetDays` and the installment is still `PLANNED`. CAPEX — `today >= dueDate - reminderOffsetDays` **or** `achievementStatus === 'ACHIEVED'` (confirmed OR, not AND) — an event achieved ahead of schedule is billable immediately; one left unachieved still alerts on schedule. `CANCELLED` events and `SKIPPED`/`CANCELLED` installments never open. A `SNOOZED` alert whose `snoozedUntil` has passed reopens on the same job tick that would open a new one, with a fresh notification — snoozing only moves the next reminder date, never the underlying due date.
(7) **"Account Team" resolution, confirmed with the user**: every active user in the organization whose effective permissions (role grants, then per-user GRANT/REVOKE overrides applied on top — the same algorithm as `RolesService.getEffectivePermissions`, applied here across all users rather than one) include `payment_alert.snooze` — resilient to custom roles granted that key later, no hardcoded role name. "Project Manager" resolves to the project's assigned manager's linked user email, silently skipped if unassigned or unlinked (never an error). "Both" is the union. A notification is sent exactly once per transition into `OPEN` (new alert or snooze-expiry reopen), reusing `enqueueOutboxEvent`/`OutboxEvent` → `Notification` with a new `PAYMENT_ALERT_OPENED` event type — no new delivery mechanism.
(8) **New `apps/worker/src/payment-alert-opener.ts`**, ticking on its own 15-minute `setInterval` (running more often than the "daily" cadence docs/05 describes for the user-visible reminder is strictly safer and free, given decision 3's idempotency), wrapped in `JobRun` bookkeeping (`jobName: 'payment-alert-opener'`) identically to the existing dispatcher. Reimplements its own recipient-resolution logic (not imported from `apps/api`) since the worker is a separate deployable app with no NestJS DI and intentionally no dependency on `@zitaware/contracts`.
(9) **Cancelling a CAPEX event, cancelling an OPEX milestone (cascading to its installments), and skipping an OPEX installment now also delete any live `OPEN`/`SNOOZED` alert for that source**, in the same transaction as the existing mutation. Physical deletion — the one deliberate exception to this codebase's no-physical-deletion rule, justified because the row represents a reminder for a billing event that provably will never happen, not a financial record. `MilestonesService` (already shipped in 4.2) gains a constructor-injected `PaymentAlertsService` (one-directional, no cycle) and calls a new `closeAlertsForSource` helper from its three cancel/skip paths — the one place this slice touches already-shipped 4.2 code.
(10) **New `payment_alert.read`/`payment_alert.snooze` permissions.** Admin: both automatic. **Accountant: both** — matches the prototype's own copy ("The account team can Snooze...") and mirrors OPEX billing-schedule ownership already established for Accountant in ADR-063. **Project Manager: `read` only** — they can be an alert *recipient* and should see the tab, but snoozing a finance reminder isn't PM's job, the same reasoning that withheld `create`/`skip_installment` from PM on milestones. Admin Staff: `read`. CEO: `read` automatic via the existing `*.read`-suffix filter.
(11) **New "Payment Alert" project tab**, positioned after "Milestone" per the prototype's own tab order. Four summary tiles (Overdue/Due Now/Snoozed/Pending Amount) computed server-side (counts and a `Prisma.Decimal` sum, never client-summed) — "Pending Amount" sums `OPEN` alerts only, matching the prototype's own "across open alerts" copy. Snooze opens a small modal (date + optional note); "Raise Invoice" renders disabled with a tooltip explaining the Phase 5 deferral (shown, not hidden — every viewer should understand the feature exists and why it's inert, unlike a permission-gated control which is hidden entirely). Snooze itself is hidden entirely for an actor lacking `payment_alert.snooze`.
Consequences: New `PaymentAlert` model plus `PaymentAlertSourceType`/`PaymentAlertStatus` enums (migration `20260809080000_phase4_03_payment_alerts`, hand-written to append the two CHECK constraints Prisma's DSL can't express; the recurring, already-understood `phase3_02_leads` checksum drift reappeared and was diagnosed, not assumed, before falling back to the established `psql` + `prisma migrate resolve --applied` procedure). New `apps/api/src/payment-alerts/{payment-alerts.service.ts,payment-alerts.module.ts}`, `packages/contracts/src/{ports/payment-alert.port.ts,routers/payment-alerts.ts}`, wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`app.module.ts`/`main.ts` exactly like every prior module. `apps/api/src/milestones/{milestones.service.ts,milestones.module.ts}` updated for decision 9. New `apps/worker/src/payment-alert-opener.ts`; `apps/worker/src/{outbox-dispatcher.ts,main.ts}` updated for the new event type and tick. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/payment-alert-tab.tsx`, wired as a new tab. Two new permission keys; permission catalog count corrected 50 → 52.

Verified with 6 new `payment-alerts.integration-spec.ts` tests (derived-field/totals correctness, future-date-required snooze validation, full snooze with audit values, stale-version rejection, cross-org rejection, Project-Manager project-scoping), 4 new cross-cutting tests appended to `milestones.integration-spec.ts` (cancelling a CAPEX event/OPEX milestone/skipping an installment each delete their live alert; cancelling a source with no alert yet is a no-op), and 11 new `payment-alert-opener.integration-spec.ts` tests in `apps/worker` (OPEX/CAPEX-due opens, CAPEX-achieved-early opens, `CANCELLED`/`SKIPPED` never open, idempotent re-run, snooze-expiry reopen with exactly one fresh notification, `ACCOUNT_TEAM`/`PROJECT_MANAGER`/`BOTH` recipient resolution including the silently-skipped-unassigned-PM case) plus one new assertion in `outbox-dispatcher.integration-spec.ts` for the `PAYMENT_ALERT_OPENED` render case — 180 integration tests pass in `apps/api` (170 prior + 10 new) and 15 in `apps/worker` (4 prior + 11 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all workspaces, forced fresh. `roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog count updated 50 → 52. A genuinely fresh database replayed the full 24-migration history end-to-end via `prisma migrate deploy` with zero errors. `apps/api`/`apps/worker` production builds are clean; `apps/web`'s production build hit the same pre-existing, disclosed, intermittent upstream Next.js prerender bug already documented in ADR-043/052/053/063 (confirmed non-regression via a `git stash` comparison against the unmodified baseline commit reproducing the identical failure on `/masters/cities`) — not a regression from this slice.

The API and worker were rebuilt and restarted before live verification (the first slice where the worker process itself needed restarting, not just the API). No browser-automation tool is available this session — live-verified directly against the running API and worker: created a real overdue CAPEX event and directly invoked the worker's `openAndReopenAlerts` function (rather than waiting out the 15-minute tick), confirming it opened exactly one alert, recorded the audit event, and enqueued exactly one outbox event which the running worker's own outbox-dispatcher tick picked up and published within seconds; re-ran the function and confirmed no duplicate; snoozed the alert via the real API (rejecting a same-day date first), then forced its `snoozedUntil` into the past directly in the database and confirmed the next run reopened it and enqueued a second, distinct notification (two total, both `PUBLISHED`); cancelled the source CAPEX event via the real `milestones.cancelEvent` and confirmed the alert row was physically deleted; confirmed the Accountant/Project-Manager/CEO permission boundaries from decision 10 exactly, including that Accountant's `project.read_all` grant lets them read and snooze alerts on any project, PM correctly gets scoped `NOT_FOUND` on an unassigned project (the permission check for `read` isn't even reached), and both PM and CEO are cleanly `FORBIDDEN` from `snooze`. All test alerts/outbox events/notifications/audit events/milestones/users/sessions were cleaned up afterward, along with an incidentally-discovered, unrelated leftover `project_milestones` pair (`CAP-001`/`OPEX-001`) from a prior session's live verification that had not been fully cleaned up — removed as part of this pass's own cleanup, not left further.

**This slice's status stays "UI verification pending," not "complete," until a real browser pass confirms**: the Snooze modal workflow; the disabled Raise Invoice button's tooltip and disabled state; role-based control visibility (Snooze absent, not just disabled, for a read-only viewer); the four summary tiles and Overdue/Due/Snoozed badge rendering; responsive layout and keyboard/focus behavior; zero browser-console errors. All were only verified by static code review this session, not exercised in a real browser.

The `apps/web` production build's `/masters/cities` prerendering failure remains the same pre-existing, disclosed, intermittent upstream Next.js bug (ADR-043/052/053/063) — a documented upstream baseline issue, not a Slice 4.3 regression, and it does not block or gate this slice's backend completion.
Supersedes: none — additive; the third Phase 4 slice.
```

```text
ID: ADR-065
Date: 2026-08-10
Status: Accepted — complete (UI verification confirmed by the user 2026-08-13; see docs/13-progress.md's "Phase 4 UI verification sweep" entry)
Context: Phase 4, Slice 4.4 — Journal Entries, Posting Engine, Idempotency Registry, and Period-Lock Enforcement, the fourth Phase 4 slice (ADR-064's Payment Alerts is the frozen 4.3 baseline). Per the user's own direction, this bundles the three together — a posting engine without journal models to write to, without period-lock enforcement, or without duplicate-posting protection isn't a posting engine at all. Reversal and EMD/SD are deliberately left for later slices: both genuinely depend on this slice existing first (EMD/SD's dependency is already recorded in ADR-053), matching the same one-slice-at-a-time discipline every prior Phase 4 slice has followed.

`docs/04-screen-contracts.md` line 565 names the target screen explicitly: "Chart of Accounts and Ledger Viewer" — `NEW_REQUIRED`, "Accountant-managed accounts and read-only journals/source drill-down." Slice 4.1 built only the Chart-of-Accounts half; this slice completes the other half. Like Chart of Accounts, there is no HTML prototype anywhere for this screen (confirmed: zero matches in `docs/16-html-module-reference.md`, zero files under `references/ui/html`) — built from the same shared shell/card/table components already established, per docs/15's design system, per the user's explicit instruction this session to respect the existing UI language even where no specific prototype exists.

`docs/06-accounting-posting-rules.md` is the authoritative, already-frozen specification for journal structure and every mandatory posting control, implemented directly rather than inventing new structure. Nothing in the codebase yet triggers a real posting — Invoices/Receipts/Vendor Bills are Phase 5+, and EMD/SD (Phase 4's own next deliverable) is the first real caller, not built yet. This slice is therefore infrastructure, proven by 16 integration tests and a direct service-level test posting against real production data, matching Chart of Accounts' own "structure with no consumer yet" precedent. The read-only Ledger Viewer half of the UI is fully buildable and testable this slice regardless.

A significant, self-caught research correction happened during implementation: the plan's original design called for a *new* generic idempotency table. Mid-implementation, a generic `IdempotencyKey` model was discovered already in the schema (Phase 1 foundation) with a materially better design (request-hash conflict detection) than what was planned, already in real production use by `UsersService.invite`. The plan's own design was discarded in favor of reusing this existing, proven mechanism — see decision 2.
Decision:
(1) **Scope**: `JournalEntry`/`JournalLine` models, `postJournal` enforcing every `docs/06` "Mandatory posting control," accounting-period resolution and lock enforcement, and a read-only Ledger Viewer UI. Out of scope: reversal, EMD/SD, and any manual/ad-hoc journal entry creation — `postJournal` has no tRPC procedure at all; it is called by other backend services only, once one exists ("posting occurs only at approved business events," docs/06).
(2) **Idempotency reuses the existing `IdempotencyKey` model** (`commandType`/`idempotencyKey`/`requestHash`/`sourceRecordType`/`sourceRecordId`/`resultReference`) exactly as `UsersService.invite` already does — `commandType: 'journal.post'`, a locally-computed `sha256(JSON.stringify(input, sortedKeys))` request hash (the same shape `hashRequest` in `packages/contracts/src/routers/users.ts` uses, reimplemented locally since `postJournal` has no router boundary to share it with). A same-key-different-request call throws `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` — a real behavior gained for free from reusing the mature mechanism instead of a simpler bespoke one. No new idempotency table.
(3) **`JournalEntry.status` is `DRAFT | POSTED | REVERSED`**, all three named because `docs/06` freezes this exact vocabulary as its own spec — but only `POSTED` is reachable this slice; `postJournal` creates rows directly as `POSTED` (no draft-then-post workflow exists), and nothing reaches `REVERSED` until the Reversal slice ships.
(4) **`accountingPeriodId` is a snapshot resolved at post time**, not derived live — a posting's period is a permanent historical fact even if that period is later locked/reopened. No matching period for the accounting date is itself a rejection, independent of the lock check.
(5) **Only `LOCKED` periods reject a posting; `SOFT_CLOSED` still accepts one — confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion before implementation.** `docs/06`'s own mandatory-controls list says only "Reject posting into Locked period"; `docs/05`'s "Soft Closed permits only authorized adjustments" describes a manual-adjustment concept this slice doesn't build. `SOFT_CLOSED` becomes a real gate later once that concept exists to distinguish it from a regular automated posting.
(6) **`JournalLine` carries every dimension `docs/06` names** (ledger account, debit/credit, project, party, employee, GST registration, tax code, narrative) — implementing an already-frozen external spec verbatim, even though no current caller populates most of them. Exactly one of debit/credit is `> 0` and the other exactly `0` — CHECK-enforced. `@@unique([organizationId, sourceType, sourceId])` is a second, belt-and-braces duplicate-posting guard beyond the idempotency registry.
(7) **`postJournal` enforces every `docs/06` mandatory posting control in one atomic transaction**: reject zero lines, negative amounts, a line with both-or-neither debit/credit, an unbalanced journal (`Prisma.Decimal` comparison, never floats), no matching accounting period, a `LOCKED` period, an inactive or `allowManualPosting: false` ledger account, and a duplicate source event — before checking-and-recording the idempotency key and writing the header, lines, and audit event together.
(8) **One new permission, `journal.read`** — Admin automatic, Accountant explicit, CEO automatic via the `*.read`-suffix filter. No Project Manager/Admin Staff grant, matching `chart_of_account.read`'s own precedent.
(9) **Two read-only UI surfaces under the existing "Finance" nav group**: a new `/journal-entries` page (paginated/filtered list reusing the Audit Log's own server-side-pagination precedent, since journal entries grow unbounded exactly like audit events; row click drills into that entry's lines) and a "View Ledger" row action added to the already-shipped Chart of Accounts page's Ledger Accounts table (a drawer showing that account's posted lines with a server-computed running balance, signed per the account's own normal balance) — together satisfying docs/04's two explicit phrases, "read-only journals" and the literal "Ledger Viewer."
(10) **Two purely-additive migration changes to already-shipped 4.1 models**: `LedgerAccount` and `GstRegistration` each gained an `@@unique([id, organizationId])` index (neither had one) so `JournalLine`'s FKs to them could be genuine org-consistent composite FKs, matching the dominant codebase convention rather than leaving them as single-column FKs.
Consequences: New `JournalEntry`/`JournalLine` models plus `JournalEntryStatus` enum (migration `20260810090000_phase4_04_journal_posting_engine`, hand-written to append the two CHECK constraints Prisma's DSL can't express; the recurring, already-understood `phase3_02_leads` checksum drift reappeared and was diagnosed, not assumed, before falling back to the established `psql` + `prisma migrate resolve --applied` procedure). New `apps/api/src/accounting/posting.service.ts` (added to the existing `AccountingModule` alongside `ChartOfAccountsService`), `packages/contracts/src/{ports/journal.port.ts,routers/journal.ts}`, wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`app.module.ts`/`main.ts` exactly like every prior module. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/journal-entries/page.tsx`; `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/chart-of-accounts/page.tsx` gained the "View Ledger" drawer (the one place this slice touches already-shipped 4.1 frontend code). New `JournalEntriesIcon` and "Journal Entries" nav item. One new permission key; permission catalog count corrected 52 → 53.

Verified with 16 new `posting.integration-spec.ts` tests (balanced posting succeeds and is retrievable via `listEntries`/`getEntryDetail` with a full audit event; every mandatory-control rejection — zero lines, negative amounts, both/neither debit-credit, unbalanced, `LOCKED` period, no matching period, inactive account, non-postable account; `SOFT_CLOSED` still accepts a posting per decision 5; idempotent-repeat-returns-original and same-key-different-request-throws-`IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`; duplicate-source-event rejection; cross-org isolation; `getLedgerActivity`'s running balance arithmetically correct across a sequence of debits and credits) — 196 integration tests pass in `apps/api` (180 prior + 16 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all workspaces, forced fresh. `roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog count updated 52 → 53. A genuinely fresh database replayed the full 25-migration history end-to-end via `prisma migrate deploy` with zero errors. `apps/api`/`apps/worker` production builds are clean; `apps/web`'s production build hit the same pre-existing, disclosed, intermittent upstream Next.js prerender bug already documented in ADR-043/052/053/063/064 (confirmed non-regression via a `git stash` comparison against the unmodified baseline commit reproducing the identical failure on `/masters/cities`) — not a regression from this slice.

Live verification this slice was deliberately narrower than prior slices': the user's own long-running dev processes (a tmux-managed session running `apps/api`/`apps/worker` directly, not this session's own PM2 instances) were found actively serving stale, pre-slice code on the usual ports. Two prior slices this week mistakenly killed this same session's processes believing them to be abandoned leftovers — a real mistake, corrected and disclosed to the user at the time. To avoid repeating it, this slice's live verification deliberately did not touch those processes: instead, a direct service-level script posted a real balanced entry against the actual production legal entity, financial year/period, and ledger accounts already in the dev database (not synthetic test fixtures), confirmed it retrieved correctly with the correct total, and cleaned up precisely the rows it created (scoped by exact IDs, never an unscoped `DELETE` — the explicit lesson from this week's earlier cleanup incident). Deliberately **not** exercised live: a period-lock test against a real production period (locking a real period the user may depend on was judged too risky for a verification step — this exact scenario is already covered by 2 of the 16 integration tests against disposable, isolated fixtures) and the HTTP/tRPC router layer specifically (would require restarting the user's own dev processes, not done proactively this pass). The router layer's correctness rests on it being the same `requirePermission(key).query/mutation()` shape already proven correct via live HTTP verification in every prior Phase 4 slice, plus this slice's own full `Services`-interface type-conformance check passing.

**This slice's status stays "UI verification pending," not "complete," until a real browser pass confirms**: the Journal Entries list/filter/drawer workflow; the Ledger drawer's running-balance rendering and date-range filter; responsive layout; keyboard/focus behavior; zero browser-console errors — plus, specific to this slice, an HTTP-level smoke test of `journal.listEntries`/`getEntryDetail`/`getLedgerActivity` once the API process is next restarted with this code.

The `apps/web` production build's `/masters/cities` prerendering failure remains the same pre-existing, disclosed, intermittent upstream Next.js bug (ADR-043/052/053/063/064) — a documented upstream baseline issue, not a Slice 4.4 regression, and it does not block or gate this slice's backend completion.
Supersedes: none — additive; the fourth Phase 4 slice.
```

```text
ID: ADR-066
Date: 2026-08-11
Status: Accepted — complete (UI verification confirmed by the user 2026-08-13; see docs/13-progress.md's "Phase 4 UI verification sweep" entry)
Context: Phase 4, Slice 4.5 — EMD/SD (Security Instruments), the fifth Phase 4 slice. ADR-056 (Tenders, Phase 3.3) explicitly deferred EMD/SD to Phase 4, "needs the Chart-of-Accounts/journal/posting engine that doesn't exist yet" — that dependency was satisfied by Slice 4.4 (ADR-065). This slice gives `PostingService.postJournal` (built call-less in 4.4) its first real caller.

Three HTML prototypes exist under `references/ui/html/03-projects/`: `emdsd-all.html` (global list, `MINOR_CHANGE`), `emdsd-add.html` (lodge form, `FUNCTIONAL_CHANGE`), `project-emdsd.html` (project-scoped tab, `ACCEPT`). `docs/04-screen-contracts.md` names the exact permission keys to implement (`security_instrument.read/export/create/post`) and requires "Cash lodging posts recoverable/bank journal; BG principal is contingent while margin/charges use separate postings." `docs/06-accounting-posting-rules.md`'s EMD/SD section is short and authoritative: cash lodged (Dr EMD/SD Recoverable, Cr Bank), cash returned (reverse), BG principal is contingent/no journal, BG margin/commission/GST "post through their own transactions" (left unspecified — stays unspecified this slice). `docs/03-domain-model.md` gives `security_instruments` its own top-level aggregate with independently-optional `tender_id`/`project_id` (ADR-020). No transactional model existed yet — only the two seeded lookup masters `SecurityInstrumentPurpose` (6 rows) and `SecurityInstrumentType` (6 rows, no `cash` row despite the prototype and docs/04 both naming "cash" as a source type).

Mid-planning, the user asked a direct follow-up question not covered by the plan as drafted: what happens when an EMD/SD is actually funded by a different entity, and how does the eventual return route back to them? This reshaped decision 9 below from the prototype's plain "Funded by Primary Company?" yes/no toggle into a structured, entity-linked capture on both the lodge and return sides — confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (structured entity+bank-account capture, informational only, no new inter-company accounting treatment invented).
Decision:
(1) **Scope**: the `SecurityInstrument` transactional model; lodging (creates the record, and for cash-equivalent types calls `postJournal`); recording a full return (only when the received amount equals the lodged amount exactly); the three UI surfaces; document attachment via the existing entity-agnostic `DocumentsTab`. Out of scope, disclosed: BG margin/commission/GST postings (docs/06 itself doesn't specify these), the funding entity's accounting effect (captured, never posted — decision 9), and penalty-deducted (partial) returns (confirmed with the user — deferred, same "don't invent an accounting treatment docs/06 doesn't define" reasoning).
(2) **`SecurityInstrumentType` gains a `cash` row** (additive seed change) — the prototype and docs/04 both name "cash" as a source type and no existing row covered it.
(3) **`SecurityInstrumentType` gains `requiresCashPosting: Boolean`** — true for cash/demand_draft/fixed_deposit_receipt/banker's_cheque/online_payment (genuinely move bank funds), false for bank_guarantee/insurance_surety_bond (contingent exposure, no journal at lodge or return). Drives `SecurityInstrumentsService`'s branch instead of hardcoding type codes.
(4) **`SecurityInstrumentPurpose` gains `category: SecurityInstrumentCategory?` (`EMD | SD`, nullable) and `defaultRecoverableLedgerAccountId: String?`** (nullable FK to `LedgerAccount`). `category` drives the list screen's EMD/SD tile split; `defaultRecoverableLedgerAccountId` is the per-purpose asset-account mapping the posting engine resolves at lodge time, rejecting a cash-equivalent lodging under an unmapped purpose rather than guessing. Seeded: `earnest_money_deposit`→EMD, mapped to the existing `TENDER_EMD_DEPOSITS` ledger account; `security_deposit`→SD, mapped to a **new** `SECURITY_DEPOSIT_RECOVERABLE` account (same `SECURITY_DEPOSITS` group); the other four purposes categorized but left unmapped. **`category` is nullable, not the originally-planned `NOT NULL`** — caught during implementation, before any migration was finalized: the generic Masters catalogue (`master.manage`, already-shipped Phase 2.11 screen) can create new `security_instrument_purposes` rows through its own generic `create()`/`update()` path, which only ever sets the common code/name/description/sortOrder columns. A `NOT NULL category` would have crashed on the very next admin-created purpose row — a real field-coverage regression to already-shipped functionality, not a hypothetical. Fixed by making the column nullable and having `SecurityInstrumentsService` simply exclude a null-category purpose's amounts from the EMD/SD tile split, rather than forcing every future purpose through this slice's own narrow lodge-form assumptions.
(5) **New `SecurityInstrument` model, immutable after creation except through `recordReturn`** — no update/delete endpoint, same "financial record, correction workflows are a future concern" reasoning as `JournalEntry`'s own no-edit-after-posting rule.
(6) **`projectId`/`tenderId` both nullable, at least one required** (app-level validation, not a DB CHECK) — matches ADR-020 and the domain-model diagram's own independently-optional shape exactly.
(7) **Status is `LODGED | RETURNED`** (two states) — "Return Due" (the prototype's third pill) is derived at query time (`status = LODGED AND returnDueOn < today`), same reasoning Payment Alerts used for computed overdue flags, not a stored third state. `recordReturn` only succeeds when `returnedAmount` equals the lodged `amount` exactly (decision 1's penalty deferral) — a mismatch is rejected with a clear validation error, never silently accepted or partially posted.
(8) **"Our company" reuses `LegalEntity`**; "Issuing bank"/"lodged with (authority)" are free text (no bank-master model exists anywhere in the schema, and building one for data-entry convenience alone is out of scope). **The screen contract's "Validate legal entity and source account" is `bankLedgerAccountId`** — a real FK to `LedgerAccount`, required when the type's `requiresCashPosting` is true, ignored for guarantee types. The picker lists active, postable ledger accounts (the seeded `HDFC_CURRENT_ACCOUNT` is the first real option), the same eligibility rule `postJournal` already enforces.
(9) **Funding-entity capture, reshaped by the user's own follow-up question (see Context)**: `fundedByLegalEntityId`/`fundedByPartyId` (mutually exclusive, app-validated) replace the prototype's plain yes/no toggle, plus `fundedByBankAccountDetails` (free text — no bank-account master, same reasoning as decision 8), `fundedByTransferAmount`/`fundedByTransferMode`/`fundedByNote`. On return, `returnedToLegalEntityId`/`returnedToPartyId`/`returnedToBankAccountDetails` are captured (UI defaults them from the funding fields when present, editable, falls back to "our company" when self-funded). **All of this stays informational only** — confirmed with the user — the lodge/return journal entries are exactly docs/06's Dr/Cr pairs against the lodging legal entity's own books regardless of funding source; no inter-company payable/receivable is posted, since docs/06 defines no such treatment.
(10) **Documents reuse the existing entity-agnostic `documents` feature** — `ENTITY_TYPES` gained `SECURITY_INSTRUMENT`, `DocumentsTab`'s prop union widened. No router/service/schema change beyond the one seed row, since those layers are already string-typed and entity-agnostic.
(11) **`SecurityInstrumentsService.lodge()`** — wrapped in the same generic `IdempotencyKey` registry `postJournal` already uses (`commandType: 'security_instrument.lodge'`, full request-hash conflict detection, not just a presence check). For `requiresCashPosting` types: resolves the purpose's `defaultRecoverableLedgerAccountId` (rejects if unmapped), calls `postJournal` with `sourceType: 'security_instrument_lodge'`. For guarantee types: no `postJournal` call at all. **Not a single shared database transaction spanning both `postJournal`'s own commit and the instrument row's commit** — `postJournal` remains untouched (zero risk to 4.4's 16 passing tests) and is called sequentially, with the journal-level idempotency key deterministically derived from the lodge's own key (`` `security_instrument.lodge:${idempotencyKey}` ``) so a retried `lodge()` call converges on exactly one journal entry instead of an orphaned duplicate, even without shared-transaction atomicity. Disclosed, not silently accepted: a failure between the two steps can leave an orphaned-but-valid, harmless journal entry if the caller abandons that idempotency key entirely rather than retrying.
(12) **`SecurityInstrumentsService.recordReturn()`** — same idempotency pattern (`commandType: 'security_instrument.return'`, `security_instrument.return:${idempotencyKey}` for the journal leg). Always reuses the same `bankLedgerAccountId`/recoverable account the lodge used, mirrored (Dr Bank, Cr Recoverable); the `returnedTo*` fields never affect which ledger account receives the posting (decision 9).
(13) **`security_instrument.post` is not a separate maker-checker gate from `.create`** — both checked together on the one `lodge`/`recordReturn` mutation, matching how 4.4 built `postJournal` with no separate draft/finalize workflow either.
(14) **Four permission keys** (already named by docs/04): `security_instrument.read/create/post/export`. Grants mirror the live `milestone.*`/`payment_alert.*` pattern exactly (confirmed against the real `seed.ts` role arrays, not assumed): Accountant and Admin get all four; CEO gets `.read` automatically via the existing `.read`-suffix filter; Project Manager and Admin Staff each get `.read` only — matching their existing `milestone.read`/`payment_alert.read` grants, so they can see the project-scoped EMD/SD tab without lodging/posting rights.
(15)-(18) **UI**: a global `/emd-sd` list (nav item under "Finance") with server-paginated table, five server-computed summary tiles (EMD Lodged/SD Lodged/Returned/Return Overdue/To Recover), and filters (category/project/status/return-due range); a lodge Drawer (Purpose select replacing the prototype's binary EMD/SD tile-picker — a `FUNCTIONAL_CHANGE`-justified correction since the real master is richer; Instrument type select driving the conditional bank-account field; the funding-entity block from decision 9); a Record Return modal (no prototype — built fresh per docs/15, same freedom already used for Chart of Accounts/Journal Entries) enforcing the exact-amount rule from decision 7 and capturing the returned-to entity; a new "EMD / SD" tab on the existing Project detail page (`security-instrument-tab.tsx`, wired into the same `Tabs items` array `MilestoneTab`/`PaymentAlertTab` already use), reusing the same shared Drawer/Modal/Tiles components as the global list via a shared `emd-sd/shared.tsx` module.
Consequences: New `SecurityInstrument` model plus `SecurityInstrumentCategory`/`SecurityInstrumentStatus` enums, additive `category`/`defaultRecoverableLedgerAccountId` on `SecurityInstrumentPurpose` and `requiresCashPosting` on `SecurityInstrumentType` (migration `20260811100000_phase4_05_security_instruments`, hand-written — backfills `category` per seeded purpose code with a safety-net default rather than assuming `NOT NULL` was ever correct, per decision 4's correction; the recurring `phase3_02_leads` checksum drift reappeared and was diagnosed, not assumed, before falling back to the established `psql` + `prisma migrate resolve --applied` procedure). A second, mid-implementation fix caught before any build ran: a schema doc comment containing a literal `*/` (`` fundedBy*/returnedTo* ``) prematurely closed Prisma's generated JSDoc block and corrupted the entire generated `.d.ts` file, breaking `@zitaware/database`'s build with dozens of unrelated-looking syntax errors — root-caused to the doc comment itself (not a tooling bug) and fixed by rewording it. New `apps/api/src/security-instruments/{security-instruments.service.ts,security-instruments.module.ts}` (first real cross-module caller of `PostingService`, already exported from `AccountingModule` since 4.4), `packages/contracts/src/{ports/security-instrument.port.ts,routers/security-instruments.ts}` (including `listPurposes`/`listTypes` lookup procedures for the lodge form's own selects — richer than the generic Masters catalogue's flat shape, gated `security_instrument.create` alongside the milestones-preview precedent), wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`app.module.ts`/`main.ts` exactly like every prior module. Four new permission keys; permission catalog count corrected 53 → 57. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/emd-sd/{page.tsx,shared.tsx}`, `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/security-instrument-tab.tsx` (wired into the existing tab strip), new `SecurityInstrumentIcon` and "EMD & SD" nav item under "Finance." `@zitaware/ui` and `@zitaware/contracts` were rebuilt explicitly before typechecking `apps/web` — the exact gap that bit twice in 4.4 and was not repeated here.

Verified with 15 new `security-instruments.integration-spec.ts` tests (cash-equivalent lodging posts the correct Dr/Cr journal and is retrievable; guarantee-type lodging posts no journal; neither-project-nor-tender rejection; tender-only lodging accepted; unmapped-purpose rejection; missing-bank-account rejection; funding-entity mutual-exclusivity rejection; idempotent-repeat-returns-original and same-key-different-request-throws-`IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`; full return posts the mirror journal and transitions to `RETURNED` with a full audit trail; mismatched-amount return rejection; already-returned rejection; guarantee-type return with no posting; cross-org isolation; tile-arithmetic sanity) plus the `roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog-count update (53 → 57) — all passing against the real dev database. `@zitaware/database`/`@zitaware/contracts`/`@zitaware/ui` all rebuild cleanly; `apps/api`/`apps/web` both typecheck and lint cleanly.

**This slice's status stays "UI verification pending," not "complete," until a real browser pass confirms**: the lodge-form workflow (including the funding-entity reveal and its Legal-Entity-or-Party mutual exclusivity, and the purpose/type-driven bank-account requirement toggling); the list page's filters/tiles/drawer; the Record Return modal (including the returned-to defaulting from funding info); the project tab's embedding and pre-fill behavior; responsive layout; keyboard/focus behavior; zero browser-console errors. No browser-automation tool was available this session.
Supersedes: none — additive; the fifth Phase 4 slice, and `postJournal`'s first real caller.
```

```text
ID: ADR-067
Date: 2026-08-12
Status: Accepted — complete (UI verification confirmed by the user 2026-08-13; see docs/13-progress.md's "Phase 4 UI verification sweep" entry)
Context: Phase 4, Slice 4.6 — Reversal Mechanism, the sixth and final Phase 4 slice. ADR-065 (Journal Entries/Posting Engine) named reversal explicitly out of scope ("nothing reaches REVERSED until the Reversal slice ships"), and it was the only remaining unstarted item in `docs/11-development-roadmap.md`'s Phase 4 list once EMD/SD (ADR-066) shipped. This slice makes the already-reserved `JournalEntryStatus.REVERSED` value reachable for the first time.

`docs/06-accounting-posting-rules.md`'s core principles are already frozen: "Posted journal entries are immutable." / "Reversal entries reference the original entry." Its Receipt-reversal line states the mechanism plainly: "Create an opposite journal. Do not delete the receipt or original journal." `docs/01`/`docs/04` add: "Posted corrections use reversal, credit note, debit note, or replacement" and "Correction actions must request a reason and create reversal/replacement records." `docs/09-security-audit.md` lists `reverse` in its common-actions vocabulary and requires "Receipt reversal requires a reason and elevated permission." No HTML prototype or screen contract exists for a reversal UI anywhere — the same full-design-freedom situation Chart of Accounts and Journal Entries were already built under.

Unlike `postJournal` (no tRPC procedure — "posting occurs only at approved business events," never a direct user action), reversal is a human-triggered correction workflow per docs/04's "request a reason" language — the first slice to expose a real posting-adjacent mutation to end users at all. The closest shipped precedent is `SecurityInstrumentsService.recordReturn` (ADR-066): create a brand-new, opposite-signed journal entry via `postJournal` rather than editing anything in place. This slice follows the same "create the opposite, never mutate financial content" shape, but since `reverseJournal` lives in the *same* class as `postJournal` (not a different module across a service boundary), it achieves genuine single-transaction atomicity that ADR-066's cross-service call couldn't — appropriate given docs/09 calls reversal a "high-impact" action.

**Confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion**: no maker-checker restriction — any actor holding `journal.reverse` may reverse any posted entry, including one they posted themselves. This is the first slice where a human directly triggers a posting-adjacent action at all; the existing maker-checker precedent in this app (accounting-period lock/reopen) guards a far larger blast radius than one fully-audited, reason-required entry reversal.
Decision:
(1) **Scope**: reversing a single `POSTED` `JournalEntry` — creates a new, opposite-signed entry referencing the original, and flips the original's status to `REVERSED` with a reason/actor/timestamp, atomically, plus a UI action on the Journal Entries page. Out of scope, disclosed: reversing a *source business record* itself (reversing an EMD lodge's journal does not revert the `SecurityInstrument` row — it has no "undo lodge" concept; correcting the source record, if ever needed, is that module's own concern); credit notes/debit notes/replacement documents (docs/01's other three correction mechanisms — Phase 5 income-module territory); partial/amount-adjusted reversal (a reversal always fully undoes the original's exact lines, matching "create an *opposite* journal," not a corrected one).
(2) **`JournalEntry` gains four nullable columns and one self-relation**: `reversalOfJournalEntryId` (`@unique`, single-column FK to `JournalEntry.id` — matching `SecurityInstrument.lodgeJournalEntryId`'s own precedent of a single-column, non-composite FK into `JournalEntry`, since `JournalEntry` has no `(id, organizationId)` composite unique for anything to key off), `reversalReason`, `reversedBy`, `reversedAt` (mirroring the existing `postedBy`/`postedAt` shape). `reversalOfJournalEntryId` is set only on the new reversal entry, pointing back at the original; the original is found via Prisma's automatic back-relation (`reversedByEntry`), not a second physical column.
(3) **No new `version` column on `JournalEntry`** — the POSTED→REVERSED transition is inherently one-time-terminal; `updateMany({ where: { id, status: 'POSTED' }, ... })` with a zero-count check is sufficient optimistic-concurrency protection.
(4) **`resolveAccountingPeriod` extracted into a private method**, shared identically by `postJournal` and `reverseJournal` — `postJournal`'s public behavior and all its existing tests are unaffected (re-run unchanged as part of verification). **Ledger-account active/`allowManualPosting` validation is deliberately not reused for reversal** — the accounts were already validated when the original posted, and a reversal of a legitimately-posted entry must remain possible even if an account was deactivated afterward; re-validating would make some corrections permanently impossible. A disclosed, intentional asymmetry from `postJournal`'s own new-posting rules.
(5) **`reverseJournal`**: generic-`IdempotencyKey`-based idempotency check (`commandType: 'journal.reverse'`, full request-hash conflict detection) → load the original + lines → reject if `status !== 'POSTED'` (`INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`, covering both "already reversed" and unreachable `DRAFT`) → reject an empty/whitespace reason (`VALIDATION_FAILED`) → resolve the accounting period **for the reversal's own supplied `accountingDate`**, not the original's (the original's period may since be `LOCKED` — expected; you reverse *into* a fresh, open period, you don't reopen the old one) → build swapped lines (every original line's debit/credit exchanged, same ledger account/dimensions/narrative preserved) → one `$transaction`: create the new entry+lines (`sourceType: 'journal_entry_reversal'`, `sourceId: <original.id>`, `postingEvent: 'journal.reversed'`, `reversalOfJournalEntryId` set) + `updateMany` the original to `REVERSED` guarded by `status: 'POSTED'` (zero-count ⇒ `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`, catching a concurrent double-reversal race) + two audit events (`JOURNAL_POSTED` on the new entry, reusing `postJournal`'s own action string; `JOURNAL_REVERSED` on the original) + the `journal.reverse` `IdempotencyKey` row. `sourceType: 'journal_entry_reversal'` + `sourceId: original.id` also means the existing `uq_journal_entries_org_source` constraint doubles as "at most one reversal per original entry" for free, the same belt-and-braces reuse ADR-065 already relied on.
(6) **Reversing a reversal is allowed, not specially blocked** — the new entry created by a reversal is itself an ordinary `POSTED` entry, so a mistaken reversal can itself be corrected by reversing it again. Standard accounting practice, not a gap.
(7) **One new permission, `journal.reverse`**, following the `<resource>.<verb>` convention already used by `milestone.cancel` and matching docs/04's `receipt.reverse`/docs/09's `reverse` vocabulary (deliberately not `journal.void` — docs/09 names "void" and "reverse" as distinct concepts). Granted to Accountant and Admin only, per docs/09's "Manual journal posting is restricted to Accountant/Admin," extended to its natural counterpart. Not auto-granted to CEO (non-`.read` key), not granted to PM/Admin Staff.
(8) **`journal.ts` router gains its first mutation**, `reverseJournal`, gated `journal.reverse` — the router's "read-only" code comment is updated to note reversal as the one deliberate exception, a direct human-triggered correction action per docs/04, not a system-only event.
(9) **UI on the already-shipped `/journal-entries` page** (no new route): a "Reverse" row action visible only when `status === 'POSTED'` and the actor holds `journal.reverse`, opening a modal (reason — required; accounting date — defaults to today, editable). The entry-detail drawer shows the reversal linkage in both directions (a reversed original links to its reversal entry and shows the reason/date; a reversal entry links back to its original) via mutual navigation inside the same drawer. `StatusTag` gets an explicit `tone="neg"` override for `REVERSED` (not in the shared component's default tone map).
Consequences: `JournalEntry` gains `reversalOfJournalEntryId`/`reversalReason`/`reversedBy`/`reversedAt` plus the `JournalEntryReversal` self-relation (migration `20260812110000_phase4_06_reversal_mechanism`, purely additive, no backfill — generated via `prisma migrate diff` after `prisma migrate dev --create-only` hit the recurring `phase3_02_leads` drift, applied via `psql` + `prisma migrate resolve --applied` per the established procedure). `apps/api/src/accounting/posting.service.ts` gains the `resolveAccountingPeriod` private helper (extracted, behavior-preserving) and `reverseJournal`. `packages/contracts/src/ports/journal.port.ts` gains `reverseJournal` plus five new `JournalEntrySummary`/`JournalEntryDetail` fields. `packages/contracts/src/routers/journal.ts` gains the `reverseJournal` mutation. One new permission key; permission catalog count corrected 57 → 58. `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/journal-entries/page.tsx` gains the Reverse action, a `ReverseJournalModal`, and bidirectional reversal-link rendering in the existing detail drawer.

Verified with 9 new tests appended to the existing `posting.integration-spec.ts` (reversing a `POSTED` entry creates a correctly opposite-signed new entry with a full audit trail on both sides; reversing an already-`REVERSED` entry is rejected; an empty/whitespace reason is rejected; reversing into a `LOCKED` period for the reversal's own date is rejected; idempotent-repeat-returns-original and same-key-different-request-throws-`IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`; reversing an entry whose *original* period is now `LOCKED` still succeeds; a post→reverse round trip nets `getLedgerActivity`'s closing balance back to its pre-posting value; reversing a reversal is itself accepted) — all 25 tests in the file pass (16 prior, unchanged, confirming the `resolveAccountingPeriod` extraction didn't change `postJournal`'s behavior + 9 new). `roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog count updated 57 → 58. Full monorepo `lint`/`typecheck`/`test`/`test:integration`/production-`build` sweep green, forced fresh, across all workspaces (211 prior `apps/api` integration tests + 9 new = 220). A genuinely fresh database replayed the full 27-migration history via `prisma migrate deploy` and reseeded cleanly.

**This slice's status stays "UI verification pending," not "complete," until a real browser pass confirms**: the Reverse action's visibility rules and modal workflow; the detail drawer's bidirectional reversal-link rendering; responsive layout; keyboard/focus behavior; zero browser-console errors. No browser-automation tool was available this session.

**Phase 4 ("Milestones and accounting core") is now fully implementation-complete** — all six slices (4.1 Chart of Accounts, 4.2 Milestones, 4.3 Payment Alerts, 4.4 Journal Entries/Posting Engine, 4.5 EMD/SD, 4.6 Reversal Mechanism) have shipped their backend/decisions.
```
