UI verification remains outstanding on 4.2/4.3/4.4/4.5/4.6 — a real browser pass on all five is the only remaining work before Phase 4 as a whole can be called fully complete.
```text
Supersedes: none — additive; the sixth and final Phase 4 slice.
```

```text
ID: ADR-068
Date: 2026-08-13
Status: Accepted — complete, implementation and UI both verified
Context: Phase 5, Slice 5.1 — Invoice (Draft, Finalize, Void), the first slice of Phase 5 ("Finance income vertical slice"). `docs/04-screen-contracts.md` §11 names the full chain — `Milestone/Installment → Alert → Invoice → Receipt → Deductions → Journal → Bank Match` — as too large for one slice, matching how Phase 4 needed six slices to reach "Alert." This slice is Invoice only: draft, finalize, void, list, and PDF. It closes a gap ADR-064 (Payment Alerts, Slice 4.3) explicitly disclosed and left open: `PaymentAlertStatus` had no `CLOSED` value because "nothing in this slice can close an alert — that requires Phase 5's finalized-invoice event," and it unblocks the already-stubbed, disabled "Raise Invoice" button the Payment Alert tab shipped with a tooltip naming this exact slice.

The authoritative sources are `docs/04` §11.3 (`project-payment-alert.html`, wiring Raise Invoice), §11.4 (`project-invoice-add.html` — the core contract: `invoice.getDraftDefaults/get/previewPdf` queries, `invoice.createDraft/updateDraft/finalize/void` commands, the exact permission set, and the "Finalize: Dr Accounts Receivable; Cr Revenue and Output GST" journal effect), and §11.5 (`project-invoices.html`/`invoices-all.html`, the two list screens). `docs/06-accounting-posting-rules.md`'s "Finalize client invoice" table is the frozen posting rule. `docs/01-business-decisions.md`'s "Milestone, invoice, and receipt" section and `docs/03-domain-model.md`'s invoice invariants are the frozen domain rules: draft editable/finalized immutable, CAPEX billed through one-or-multiple invoices capped at its milestone's approved amount, OPEX has one active finalized invoice with a voided one replaceable, invoice number scoped to GST registration + financial year, seller/buyer snapshots immutable after finalization, "remove free ad-hoc invoice creation."

**Confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion**: build real PDF generation now, via `pdfkit` — docs/04 names PDF generation as this screen's own contract, not deferred scope the way "Raise Invoice" itself was.
Decision:
(1) **Scope**: `Invoice`/`InvoiceLine` models; `createDraftFromAlert`/`updateDraft`/`finalize`/`void`; GST computation (CGST+SGST vs IGST, mutually exclusive); seller/buyer snapshotting at finalize; server-generated invoice numbering; the finalize/void journal integration; closing/reopening the source `PaymentAlert`; a project-scoped Invoices tab and a global `/invoices` list; on-demand PDF generation. Out of scope, disclosed: Receipt/Deductions/Bank Match (later Phase 5 slices); Credit Note/Debit Note (docs/04 names these as their own separate deliverable); Vendor Bill; a real GST/HSN-SAC rate master (docs/12's own "Business details that can be configured later" names this explicitly — GST rate is hand-entered per line); free-standing/ad-hoc invoice creation — every invoice originates from a `PaymentAlert` via `createDraftFromAlert(paymentAlertId)`, no other creation path, and neither the project tab nor the global list carry an "Add Invoice" button (a deliberate divergence from both HTML prototypes, which do show one).
(2) **At most one active (non-void) invoice per `MilestoneEvent`/`MilestoneInstallment` at a time** — OPEX's explicit "one active finalized invoice; a voided invoice may be replaced" rule extended to CAPEX events too, enforced via partial unique indexes (`uq_invoices_source_event_active`/`uq_invoices_source_installment_active`, `WHERE status != 'VOID'`, hand-added in migration SQL — the same soft-delete-partial-unique-index pattern as `uq_legal_entities_org_pan`), with a defense-in-depth application-level check in `createDraftFromAlert` ahead of the DB constraint.
(3) **`Invoice.status: DRAFT | FINALIZED | VOID`**. `InstallmentStatus` gains `INVOICE_DRAFT`/`INVOICED` (its own doc comment already anticipated these) — not `ALERT_OPEN` (already represented by the `PaymentAlert` row's own existence) and not `REPLACEMENT_REQUIRED` (derivable from status + due date without a dedicated value). `PaymentAlertStatus` gains `CLOSED`, filling ADR-064's disclosed gap. Finalizing closes the source alert; voiding a finalized invoice reopens the *same* alert row (never a new one, since the source-id columns are hard-unique) and reverts the OPEX installment's status back to `PLANNED` — voiding a DRAFT also reverts the installment, freeing the source for a fresh draft either way.
(4) **`Invoice.paymentAlertId`** records which alert an invoice was *raised from* — set once at draft creation, immutable thereafter. This is deliberately a separate, distinct fact from `PaymentAlert.invoiceId`, which records which invoice currently *closes* the alert and is cleared on void-of-finalized (reopen). Modeled as two named relations between the same two tables (`InvoiceOriginAlert` and `PaymentAlertClosingInvoice`) rather than one shared column, since they answer different questions with different lifetimes — caught and fixed via a small follow-up migration after the original schema pass omitted the scalar column entirely and left only the inverse `invoiceId` back-relation, which a mid-implementation typecheck-and-service-review caught before any UI was built against it.
(5) **Numbering**: scoped to `(organizationId, gstRegistrationId, financialYearId)`, server-generated at finalize via the exact "compute next sequence inside the transaction, retry on unique-violation" pattern `MilestonesService`'s code generation already established (`MAX_NUMBER_ATTEMPTS = 5`, `isConstraintViolation` retry loop). The literal format is a simple zero-padded sequence (`0001`, `0002`, …) — `docs/12`'s "Business details that can be configured later" explicitly names "Invoice prefixes/number formats" as deferrable, so inventing a prefix scheme now isn't this slice's call.
(6) **Snapshots at finalize**: seller (legal entity name, PAN, GST registration GSTIN/state, registered address) and buyer (client name, GSTIN, state, address) are copied onto the `Invoice` header as plain columns, immutable afterward — matches `docs/03`'s invariants verbatim. Tax amounts (`taxableAmount`/`cgstAmount`/`sgstAmount`/`igstAmount`/`totalAmount`) are computed once at finalize and snapshotted, never recomputed live. `InvoiceTaxSummary` as a separate child table (named in `docs/03`'s domain-tree diagram) is simplified to these fixed summary columns directly on `Invoice` — disclosed as a simplification, since nothing in this slice needs multiple tax-summary rows per invoice.
(7) **Tax computation**: each `InvoiceLine` carries `hsnSac` (free text) and a hand-entered `gstRatePercent`. CGST+SGST vs IGST is determined by comparing the buyer's (client's) `stateId` to the selected `GstRegistration.stateId` — same state ⇒ CGST+SGST split of the rate (each half, remainder to CGST so the two always sum exactly); different state ⇒ IGST at the full rate — mutually exclusive, `Prisma.Decimal` arithmetic throughout, never floats. The frontend line-item editor deliberately shows no computed taxable/tax/total preview at all — CLAUDE.md: "Do not calculate accounting totals independently in React components" — the authoritative figures only ever come back from the server, on the resulting invoice after submit.
(8) **Finalize calls the existing `PostingService.postJournal`** — Dr `ACCOUNTS_RECEIVABLE`, Cr `SOFTWARE_PROJECT_REVENUE` (taxable value) + Cr the three **new** `OUTPUT_CGST_PAYABLE`/`OUTPUT_SGST_PAYABLE`/`OUTPUT_IGST_PAYABLE` ledger accounts (new rows under the existing `TAX_PAYABLE` group; the pre-existing generic `GST_PAYABLE` account stays untouched, a purely additive change), per docs/06's frozen table. Rejects with `VALIDATION_FAILED` if any required account for the invoice's actual tax mix is missing/unconfigured, rather than posting a wrong or partial journal. **Sequential, not single-transaction, across `postJournal` and the invoice's own row updates** — the now three-times-established ADR-066/067 pattern: `postJournal` always opens its own top-level transaction and cannot be nested inside a caller's, so `finalize` calls it first (using a best-effort preview invoice number in the journal's human-readable description), then performs the real numbering/status/snapshot writes in a separate transaction, with the journal's own idempotency key deterministically derived from `finalize`'s (`` `invoice.finalize:${idempotencyKey}` ``) so a retried call converges on one journal entry rather than an orphaned duplicate — the same disclosed, accepted tradeoff as EMD/SD's lodge/return.
(9) **CAPEX cap enforcement**: finalize sums the taxable amount of every other `FINALIZED` invoice under the same `ProjectMilestone` (via `MilestoneEvent.milestoneId`) plus this invoice's own lines, and rejects with `VALIDATION_FAILED` if the total would exceed the milestone's `approvedAmount` — docs/01's exact wording, "Total finalized invoice value cannot exceed the approved milestone value," checked at the milestone level (aggregating across that milestone's events) since CAPEX may bill through multiple events/invoices.
(10) **Void**: a `DRAFT` invoice voids with a simple status flip (no journal exists yet). A `FINALIZED` invoice voids by calling the existing `PostingService.reverseJournal` (Slice 4.6, reused as-is, required reason, its own accounting date) against `journalEntryId`, storing the resulting `voidJournalEntryId`, then in a separate transaction flips `Invoice.status` to `VOID`, reopens the source `PaymentAlert`, and reverts the OPEX installment. This is `reverseJournal`'s first real caller outside its own tests.
(11) **PDF via `pdfkit`** (new dependency), rendered on demand from the invoice's current stored data — no PDF file is ever persisted. Since finalized fields are immutable snapshots, a regenerated PDF is byte-for-byte equivalent to a stored one on every call, so skipping file storage costs nothing. `invoice-pdf.util.ts` is a pure function (no DB access), unit-tested in isolation. The separate "inward acknowledgment" upload reuses the existing entity-agnostic Documents module (`ENTITY_TYPES` gains `INVOICE`).
(12) **Six permission keys** (docs/04 §6/§11.4/§11.5 exactly): `invoice.read/create/update/finalize/void/export`. Accountant + Admin get all six (matching "manual journal posting is restricted to Accountant/Admin," extended to invoicing); CEO gets `.read` automatically via the existing `.read`-suffix filter; Project Manager and Admin Staff get `.read` only — matching the established `milestone.read`/`payment_alert.read`/`security_instrument.read` pattern. Read-oriented methods (`list`/`getDetail`/`getPdf`/`getDraftDefaults`) reuse `project.read_all`-driven PM-scoping exactly like `PaymentAlertsService`/`MilestonesService` already do: `InvoicesService.list` filters to the actor's assigned projects via `ProjectsService.list`'s own scoping when `!canSeeAll`; `getDetail`/`getPdf`/`getDraftDefaults` piggyback on `ProjectsService.getDetail`'s existing access check (mirroring `PaymentAlertsService.assertProjectAccess`'s established pattern) rather than reimplementing scope-query logic.
(13) **UI**: the Payment Alert tab's disabled "Raise Invoice" button gets a real `onClick` (navigates to `/projects/[id]/invoices/new?paymentAlertId=...`) gated by `invoice.create`, mirroring the tab's own `canSnooze` pattern. Two new dedicated full-page routes (not a Drawer — the line-items/tax/PDF surface is too large, matching docs/16's "Financial document form" reference) at `/projects/[id]/invoices/new` and `/projects/[id]/invoices/[invoiceId]` (the latter branches between an editable draft form and a read-only finalized/void snapshot with Download PDF / Void actions). A new read-only "Invoices" project tab and a new global `/invoices` list (mirroring the EMD/SD global-list-plus-project-tab shape from Slice 4.5) — neither carries an "Add Invoice" button, per decision 1. New `InvoiceIcon` and "Invoices" nav item under "Finance."
Consequences: New `Invoice`/`InvoiceLine` models, `InvoiceStatus` enum, `InstallmentStatus`/`PaymentAlertStatus` enum extensions, `PaymentAlert.invoiceId` back-reference (migration `20260813120000_phase5_01_invoice`, hand-written with three partial unique indexes) plus a small additive follow-up (`20260813130000_phase5_01_invoice_payment_alert_origin`) restoring the `Invoice.paymentAlertId` scalar+relation caught missing during service implementation (decision 4). A **second, more consequential fix caught only by running this slice's own integration tests, not by typecheck**: the pre-existing `chk_payment_alerts_snoozed_fields_consistent` CHECK constraint (from ADR-064's original Slice 4.3 migration) only allowed `status IN ('OPEN', 'SNOOZED')`, predating `CLOSED` — so `InvoicesService.finalize`'s `status: 'CLOSED'` update would have failed in production exactly as it failed in the first test run. Fixed via `20260813140000_phase5_01_fix_payment_alert_closed_check`, widening the constraint to `status IN ('OPEN', 'CLOSED')` for the null-`snoozed_until` branch. `apps/api/src/invoices/{invoices.service.ts,invoices.module.ts,invoice-pdf.util.ts}` (new), `packages/contracts/src/{ports/invoice.port.ts,routers/invoices.ts}` (new), wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`/`app.module.ts`/`main.ts` exactly like every prior module. `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` gains six permission keys, three ledger accounts (`OUTPUT_CGST_PAYABLE`/`OUTPUT_SGST_PAYABLE`/`OUTPUT_IGST_PAYABLE`), one entity type (`INVOICE`); permission catalog count corrected 58 → 64. `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/invoices/{page.tsx,shared.tsx}`, `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/{invoice-tab.tsx,invoices/new/page.tsx,invoices/[invoiceId]/page.tsx}` (new), `payment-alert-tab.tsx` and the project detail page's tab strip updated. `pdfkit`/`@types/pdfkit` added to `apps/api`.

Verified with 9 new tests in `invoices.integration-spec.ts` (CAPEX-event draft creation and intra-state CGST+SGST finalize computing the exact docs/06 journal; inter-state IGST computation; OPEX-installment draft creation with installment-status flip, seller/buyer snapshot verification, and INVOICED transition; duplicate-active-source rejection; draft void freeing its source for a new draft; finalized-invoice void reversing the journal via `reverseJournal`, reopening the alert, and reverting the installment; CAPEX milestone-cap rejection; idempotent-repeat-returns-original; cross-org isolation) plus 3 new `invoice-pdf.util.spec.ts` unit tests (finalized rendering, draft rendering with null fields, inter-state rendering) — all passing against the real dev database. `roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog count updated 58 → 64; `payment-alerts.integration-spec.ts` and `milestones.integration-spec.ts` re-run unaffected by the CHECK-constraint fix. `@zitaware/database`/`@zitaware/contracts`/`@zitaware/ui` rebuild cleanly; `apps/api`/`apps/web` both typecheck and lint cleanly; `apps/web` production build succeeds with all four new routes registered.

**UI verified live by the user**: the Raise Invoice wiring from the Payment Alert tab; the invoice-add form workflow; the draft-edit/finalize/void workflow on the detail page; PDF download; the project tab and global list. This slice is now complete end-to-end.
Supersedes: none — additive; the first slice of Phase 5.
```

```text
ID: ADR-069
Date: 2026-08-14
Status: Accepted — complete, implementation and UI both verified
Context: Phase 5, Slice 5.2 — Client Receipt (multi-invoice allocation, typed deductions, hold/retention release), the second slice of Phase 5, continuing docs/04 §11's chain (`Milestone/Installment → Alert → Invoice → Receipt → Deductions → Journal → Bank Match`) from where Slice 5.1 (Invoice, ADR-068) left off. This slice makes finalized invoices actually collectible.

`docs/04` §11.6 (`project-payment-add.html`, `FUNCTIONAL_CHANGE`) is the core contract — queries `receipt.getAllocationCandidates/get/calculatePreview`, `deduction.getOutstanding`; commands `receipt.post/reverse`, `deduction.release`; permissions `receipt.read/post/reverse`, `deduction.release`. §11.7 names the two list screens. `docs/06`'s "Record client receipt with deductions" and "Hold/retention release" tables are the frozen posting rules. `docs/01`'s "Receipt" subsection and `docs/03`'s `client_receipts → receipt_allocations → allocation_deductions → deduction_releases` domain tree plus its "Receipt invariants" are the frozen domain rules. `docs/09` requires "Receipt reversal requires a reason and elevated permission."

**Confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion**: (1) build a real `BankAccount` master this slice (matching `references/ui/html/08-banking/master-bank-account.html`), not a `LedgerAccount`-reuse shortcut — legal-entities.tsx's own pre-existing copy already said "Bank accounts are managed separately," anticipating this; (2) bundle Hold/Retention Release into this same slice rather than deferring it, since docs/04's own acceptance criteria for the receipt-add screen names "no over-...release" as in-scope and a lodged hold/retention with no release path would be a dead end.
Decision:
(1) **Scope**: `BankAccount` master (folded into the existing `LegalEntityService`/`legal-entities` router, not a new module — a legal-entity sub-resource reusing `legal_entity.read`/`legal_entity.manage` exactly like GST Registrations already do, a cleaner realization of the plan's own stated reasoning than a separate module would have been); `ClientReceipt`/`ReceiptAllocation`/`AllocationDeduction`/`DeductionRelease` models; `receipt.post` (one atomic action, no draft/finalize split — docs/04 names only one creation permission, matching EMD/SD's `lodge` precedent); `receipt.reverse`; `deduction.release`; a project Payment tab + global Payment Integration list; `PenaltyCategory` gains optional accepted/disputed ledger-account overrides. Out of scope, disclosed: Bank Transaction Import/Reconciliation/Matching (docs/04 §18 names this its own separate `NEW_REQUIRED` screen — the global list this slice ships has no reconciliation-status column as a result); Credit/Debit Notes and TDS Certificate/26AS reconciliation (both separate `NEW_REQUIRED` items). **Unallocated/on-account receipt balance** — docs/03 names it as a domain possibility but docs/06's own frozen posting table has no line for it (every debit always sums to one combined "Total invoice amount settled" credit) — so v1 requires a receipt's cash to be fully allocated across its invoice rows (`sum(cashAllocated) == actualAmount`, rejected otherwise) rather than inventing a suspense/unapplied-cash account docs/06 doesn't define.
(2) **`ReceiptAllocation`** snapshots the allocated invoice's taxable/CGST/SGST/IGST/total at allocation time (docs/04: "read-only") and stores `settledAmount` once at post (`cashAllocated + sum(its deductions)`, docs/03's invariant literally) — never recomputed. An invoice may appear in allocations across multiple non-reversed receipts (docs/01: "one invoice may receive multiple receipts"), so no uniqueness constraint on `invoiceId` alone. "Invoice outstanding" is computed live (`invoice.totalAmount - sum(settledAmount from prior non-reversed allocations)`), matching `PaymentAlert`'s "always read live" precedent rather than a stored running balance that could drift.
(3) **`AllocationDeduction`** covers nine types (`TDS`/`CGST_TDS`/`SGST_TDS`/`IGST_TDS`/`HOLD`/`RETENTION`/`ACCEPTED_PENALTY`/`DISPUTED_PENALTY`/`OTHER`). `ratePercent` is display-only (docs/04's own prototype hint: "rate is dynamic") and never drives the amount. `ACCEPTED_PENALTY`/`DISPUTED_PENALTY` require a `penaltyCategoryId`; `OTHER` requires a directly-picked `otherLedgerAccountId` (no master exists for arbitrary other-deductions, same pattern as EMD/SD's own bank-account picker). Outstanding-for-release is computed live (`amount - sum(releases.releasedAmount)`), not stored.
(4) **Ledger account resolution per deduction type**: `TDS`→`TDS_RECEIVABLE` (pre-existing), `CGST_TDS`/`SGST_TDS`/`IGST_TDS`→three new `GST_TDS_RECEIVABLE_*` accounts (`TAX_RECOVERABLE` group), `HOLD`/`RETENTION`→two new accounts under a new `OTHER_RECEIVABLES` asset group (Hold/Retention/Disputed aren't tax-recoverable, so don't belong under `TAX_RECOVERABLE`), `DISPUTED_PENALTY`→`DISPUTED_DEDUCTION_RECEIVABLE` (new, same group) unless the category overrides it, `ACCEPTED_PENALTY`→new `PENALTY_REVENUE_DEDUCTION` (under `PROJECT_REVENUE` — docs/06 names it "Penalty Expense/Revenue Deduction," a debit against a revenue-classified account functioning as the intended contra-revenue) unless the category overrides it, `OTHER`→the directly-picked account. Seven new ledger accounts, one new account group, all additive.
(5) **`receipt.post`** validates every allocated invoice is `FINALIZED`, the bank account and every allocated invoice share one legal entity (docs/04 point 8), all allocated invoices share one client, `sum(cashAllocated) === actualAmount` exactly, and each allocation's `cashAllocated + deductions <= live-computed outstanding`. Posts one combined journal (not one line per allocation×account) — Dr Bank (bank account's own ledger account, = `actualAmount`); Dr each distinct resolved deduction account (summed across all allocations); Cr `ACCOUNTS_RECEIVABLE` (`partyId` = client, = total settled across all allocations) — matching docs/06's literal table shape exactly. Same sequential "postJournal first, then a separate transaction for the rest" pattern as Invoice's finalize (ADR-066/067/068's now five-times-established tradeoff), with the journal idempotency key deterministically derived (`` `receipt.post:${idempotencyKey}` ``).
(6) **`receipt.reverse`** calls the existing `reverseJournal` (Slice 4.6) against the receipt's journal, required reason, flips `ClientReceipt.status` to `REVERSED`. Does **not** revert `ReceiptAllocation`/`AllocationDeduction` rows — reversal undoes the accounting effect, not the historical record (the same principle ADR-067/068 already established: an invoice's void doesn't un-finalize a reversed invoice's journal either). A reversed receipt's allocations are simply excluded from future outstanding computations via a status filter — no data mutation needed, since outstanding was already computed live.
(7) **`deduction.release`** validates the target is `HOLD`/`RETENTION` and `releasedAmount <= live-computed outstanding`, posts Dr Bank (the release's own bank account, independent of the original receipt's); Cr the deduction's resolved receivable account — docs/06's "Hold/retention release" table exactly. Each release gets its own new `id` as the journal's `sourceId` (not the deduction's `id`), since a deduction may be released more than once partially (docs/04 point 7) and `postJournal`'s own duplicate-source-event guard would otherwise block every release after the first.
(8) **Four new permissions** (`receipt.read/post/reverse`, `deduction.release`, docs/04's exact names). Accountant + Admin get all four; CEO auto-gets `receipt.read`; PM/Admin Staff get `receipt.read` only — the same grant shape as `invoice.*`/`security_instrument.*`. Read methods (`list`/`getDetail`/`getAllocationCandidates`/`getOutstandingDeductions`) reuse `project.read_all`-driven PM-scoping via `ProjectsService`, exactly like `InvoicesService` already does — including a fix applied before this shipped: the first draft of `list()` skipped PM-scoping entirely whenever a `projectId` filter was present, which would have let a PM query any arbitrary project's receipts by filter alone; fixed to always resolve the actor's assigned-project set first and intersect with the requested filter, mirroring `InvoicesService.list`'s own already-correct pattern.
(9) **UI**: the invoice detail page's "Record Payment" action (gated `receipt.post`, `FINALIZED` invoices only) navigates to `/projects/[id]/receipts/new?invoiceId=...`, pre-seeding that invoice as the first allocation row. The add form is a dedicated full page (not a Drawer, per docs/16's "Financial document form" reference) with a repeatable invoice-allocation editor and a nested typed-deduction sub-editor per row; it shows **no client-computed settlement math at all** — CLAUDE.md: "Do not calculate accounting totals independently in React components" — a `calculatePreview` query (reusing `postReceipt`'s own validation) renders the server-computed breakdown on demand, and the actual Post action calls the real mutation directly rather than gating on a separate preview step, avoiding state-sync complexity between the two. A receipt detail page shows the full allocation/deduction breakdown with Reverse and per-deduction Release actions. A project "Payment" tab and a global `/payment-integration` list mirror the Invoice tab/list shape; neither carries an "Add Receipt" button, since creation is invoice-initiated only. A new `/bank-accounts` master screen and nav item under "Organization."
Consequences: New `BankAccount`, `ClientReceipt`, `ReceiptAllocation`, `AllocationDeduction`, `DeductionRelease` models, `ClientReceiptStatus`/`AllocationDeductionType` enums, `PenaltyCategory` gains two nullable ledger-account FKs (migration `20260814090000_phase5_02_receipt`, hand-written, purely additive). Two real bugs caught and fixed before this shipped, both by the integration test suite rather than by typecheck: `detailInclude`'s object-spread of `summaryInclude` silently discarded the nested `allocations.include` override needed for `clientName` resolution (`{...summaryInclude, allocations: {...}}` replaces the whole key, doesn't merge it) — every `getDetail`/`postReceipt`/`reverseReceipt` call crashed until fixed; and the initial `toSummary`'s `clientName` read `invoice.project.name` (the project's own name) instead of `invoice.project.client.name` — a real display bug that would have shown the project name where the client name belongs on every receipt list row. `apps/api/src/organization/legal-entity.service.ts` gains `listBankAccounts`/`createBankAccount`/`updateBankAccount`/`setBankAccountActive`. `apps/api/src/receipts/{receipts.service.ts,receipts.module.ts,receipts.integration-spec.ts}` (new). `packages/contracts/src/{ports/receipt.port.ts,routers/receipts.ts}` (new) plus `legal-entity.port.ts`/`legal-entities.ts` router extended, wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`/`app.module.ts`/`main.ts`. `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` gains four permissions, seven ledger accounts, one account group; permission catalog count corrected 64 → 68. `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/{bank-accounts,payment-integration}/{page.tsx,shared.tsx}`, `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/{payment-tab.tsx,receipts/new/page.tsx,receipts/[receiptId]/page.tsx}` (all new), the invoice detail page gains "Record Payment," the project tab strip gains "Payment," the Penalty Category master Drawer gains the two ledger-account fields. New `BankAccountIcon`/`ReceiptIcon` and two nav items.

Verified with 10 new tests in `receipts.integration-spec.ts` (single-invoice receipt with a TDS deduction posts the exact docs/06 journal; multi-invoice allocation sums deductions per ledger account across allocations in one combined journal; GST-TDS/hold/retention/accepted-penalty/disputed-penalty deductions all resolve to their correct accounts, including a penalty-category override vs the default fallback; over-allocation rejection; sum(cashAllocated) != actualAmount rejection; non-FINALIZED-invoice rejection; reversal reverses the journal and frees the invoice for a second receipt; hold release posts the mirror journal, reduces live-computed outstanding, and rejects an over-release; idempotent-repeat-returns-original; cross-org isolation) plus 3 new bank-account tests appended to `organization.integration-spec.ts` (create/list/update/deactivate; cross-org ledger-account rejection; two bank accounts under different ledger accounts for one legal entity) — all passing against the real dev database. `@zitaware/database`/`@zitaware/contracts`/`@zitaware/ui` rebuild cleanly; `apps/api`/`apps/web` both typecheck and lint cleanly; `apps/web` production build succeeds with all six new routes registered.

**UI verified live by the user**: the Record Payment wiring from the invoice detail page; the multi-row allocation editor and nested deduction sub-editor; the server-computed preview display; the Reverse and Release actions on the receipt detail page; the Bank Accounts master screen; the project Payment tab and global Payment Integration list. This slice is now complete end-to-end.
Supersedes: none — additive; the second slice of Phase 5.
```

```text
ID: ADR-070
Date: 2026-08-19
Status: Accepted — complete, implementation and UI both verified
Context: Phase 5, Slice 5.3 — Credit Note / Debit Note, the third slice of Phase 5. Today the only way to correct an already-`FINALIZED` `Invoice` is `void`, which kills the entire document and forces a full re-bill (reopens the source `PaymentAlert`, reverses the whole journal). There is no way to make a partial correction — e.g. a rate or quantity error found after finalization — without destroying and re-raising the whole invoice. `docs/04-screen-contracts.md` §18 names this gap as its own `NEW_REQUIRED` deliverable: "Credit Note and Debit Note — Draft/finalize against invoice with tax-period-aware posting and immutable references." `docs/06-accounting-posting-rules.md` freezes the Credit Note posting rule: "Reverse revenue/output tax/receivable according to the credited values. Reference the original invoice and preserve tax-period rules." Debit Note has no frozen rule of its own anywhere in the docs. ADR-067, ADR-068, and ADR-069 each explicitly deferred Credit/Debit Note as "Phase 5 income-module territory" — this is the acknowledged next domino, not a new idea.

Chosen over Bank Transaction Reconciliation (docs/11 names Ledger Opening Balances as its own prerequisite — "a prerequisite for real bank reconciliation" — which doesn't exist yet, so reconciliation built now wouldn't be real) and over Ledger Opening Balances itself (zero screen contract or prototype anywhere — the highest-ambiguity remaining option, unlike this slice which has both a docs/04 contract and a docs/06 posting rule already frozen).

**Confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion before implementation**: (1) build both Credit and Debit Note together as one model with a `type` discriminator, rather than Credit Note only — Debit Note posts the mirror direction of Credit Note (identical shape to `Invoice.finalize`'s own journal) since no frozen rule exists for it but this is the only sensible double-entry reading; (2) cumulative Credit Notes against one invoice are capped at that invoice's original taxable amount, mirroring the existing CAPEX-milestone-cap precedent — no cap on Debit Notes; (3) a note always posts into whatever accounting period is open **today**, never the original invoice's own period, matching the Reversal Mechanism precedent (ADR-067) exactly, so a since-`LOCKED` historical period never blocks a correction.
Decision:
(1) **Scope**: one `CreditDebitNote`/`CreditDebitNoteLine` model pair (`type: CREDIT | DEBIT`, `status: DRAFT | FINALIZED | VOID`); `createDraft`/`updateDraft`/`finalize`/`void`/`list`/`getDetail`; a project-scoped Credit/Debit Notes card on the invoice detail page and a global `/credit-debit-notes` list. Out of scope, disclosed: on-demand PDF/export (no `.export` permission seeded this slice — `invoice-pdf.util.ts`'s renderer could be adapted later without touching anything built here); Documents-tab attachment (no `ENTITY_TYPES` row seeded for it this slice, unlike Invoice/Receipt); line-item-level reconciliation against the original invoice's own lines (a note's lines are free-form, matching `InvoiceLine`'s own shape, not tied to specific original `InvoiceLine` ids — the "reference the original invoice" requirement is satisfied by the `invoiceId` FK and the note's own number, which is what real GST credit/debit notes actually require).
(2) **`invoiceId` is not unique** on `CreditDebitNote` — one invoice may receive many notes over time (unlike `Invoice`'s own singleton-per-milestone-event constraint), matching how corrections actually recur in practice.
(3) **Numbering**: its own independent series per `type`, scoped to `(organizationId, gstRegistrationId, financialYearId, type)` — `CN-0001`, `DN-0001`, … — via a hand-added partial unique index and the exact `count()+attempt/padStart(4,'0')`-with-retry-on-constraint-violation idiom `InvoicesService.generateInvoiceNumber` already established, copied verbatim into a new `generateNoteNumber`. `type` is part of the scope deliberately, so Credit and Debit series never share one counter — matching real GST practice, which requires distinct series per document type.
(4) **Finalize posting** — `CreditDebitNotesService.finalize` reuses the existing `PostingService.postJournal` directly (confirmed `reverseJournal` cannot be reused here: it only supports full-entry reversal, not a scaled/partial amount). **Credit Note**: `Dr Project Revenue (taxable) + Dr Output CGST/SGST/IGST (credited amounts) / Cr Accounts Receivable (total)` — the literal mirror of `Invoice.finalize`'s own journal, debit/credit swapped, per docs/06's frozen rule. **Debit Note**: the identical direction as `Invoice.finalize`'s own journal (`Dr Accounts Receivable / Cr Project Revenue + Output GST`) — the confirmed decision above, since no frozen rule exists for it. Both reuse the exact same `LEDGER_CODES` constant `InvoicesService` already defines (now exported for this purpose) rather than duplicating the five ledger-account codes. `sourceType: 'credit_note_issue' | 'debit_note_issue'`, `sourceId` = the note's own id (not the invoice's — the invoice already owns its own `invoice_finalize` slot in the `(orgId, sourceType, sourceId)` uniqueness constraint).
(5) **Credit cap enforcement**: on finalize, sums this note's taxable amount plus every other `FINALIZED` `CREDIT` note's taxable amount against the same invoice, and rejects with `VALIDATION_FAILED` if the total would exceed the invoice's own taxable amount — copies the exact aggregate-then-compare shape `Invoice.finalize`'s own CAPEX-cap check already uses. No equivalent cap on Debit Notes (confirmed decision above).
(6) **Always posts into today's own accounting period** — `finalize` resolves the financial year/accounting period from `new Date()`, never from the source invoice's own `invoiceDate`, so a correction is never blocked by a since-`LOCKED` historical period (matching ADR-067's reversal precedent exactly). Re-validates at finalize time that the source invoice is still `FINALIZED` (not since voided) — a defensive re-check against live state, the same discipline `Invoice.finalize`'s own CAPEX-cap check already applies against live milestone data rather than trusting a draft-time assumption.
(7) **Never touches the source `Invoice`** — its `status` and source `PaymentAlert` are left completely alone by a note's create/finalize/void lifecycle. A note is a correction document in its own right, not a void/replace mechanism (docs/06 frames it as its own separate posted document).
(8) **`InvoicesService.list()`'s `outstandingAmount`** (shipped the day before this slice, see the "Invoice list — live outstanding amount" progress entry) is extended to also net out finalized notes — `outstandingAmount = totalAmount - settledAmount(receipts) - creditTotal + debitTotal`, computed via a second batched `creditDebitNote.groupBy` alongside the existing `receiptAllocation.groupBy`, same live-computed-never-stored precedent as everything else in this codebase. This was a required change, not optional — without it, the figure shipped the day before would have silently gone wrong the moment any note was finalized.
(9) **Five new permissions** (`credit_debit_note.read/create/update/finalize/void` — no `.export`, matching decision 1's scope cut). Accountant gets all five; Project Manager and Admin Staff get `.read` only; CEO auto-gets `.read` via the existing `.endsWith('.read')` filter — the same grant shape as `invoice.*`/`receipt.*`. Permission catalog count corrected 68 → 73.
(10) **UI**: the invoice detail page's `FinalizedOrVoidView` action row gains "Issue Credit Note…"/"Issue Debit Note…" (gated `credit_debit_note.create`, `FINALIZED` invoices only), navigating to a dedicated add route (not a Drawer, matching Invoice's own precedent) — `/projects/[id]/invoices/[invoiceId]/credit-debit-notes/new?type=CREDIT|DEBIT`. A new "Credit / Debit Notes" card sits at the page bottom, sibling to the existing Documents card, listing notes against that invoice. A note detail route (`.../credit-debit-notes/[noteId]`) mirrors the Invoice detail page's Draft-editor-vs-Finalized-or-Void-view split, reusing `LineItemsEditor`/`gstRegistrationSelectOptions` from `invoices/shared.tsx` verbatim (the line shape is identical) — simpler than Invoice's own finalize modal since no invoice-date input is needed (finalize always posts today, decision 6). A global `/credit-debit-notes` list mirrors `/invoices`' own shape, with an `IssueCreditDebitNoteModal` (project→invoice→type picker) for its "Add" entry point — included now rather than deferred, since Invoice/Receipt's own missing-Add-button gap was flagged as a real usability miss during their own fix pass and this avoids repeating it. New `CreditDebitNoteIcon` and nav item under "Finance."
Consequences: New `CreditDebitNote`/`CreditDebitNoteLine` models, `CreditDebitNoteType`/`CreditDebitNoteStatus` enums (migration `20260819090000_phase5_03_credit_debit_note`, hand-written, purely additive — two new tables, two new enums, no backfill). `apps/api/src/invoices/invoices.service.ts` gains two exports (`LEDGER_CODES`, `computeLines`) for reuse and an extended `list()`/`toSummary()`. `apps/api/src/credit-debit-notes/{credit-debit-notes.service.ts,credit-debit-notes.module.ts,credit-debit-notes.integration-spec.ts}` (all new). `packages/contracts/src/{ports/credit-debit-note.port.ts,routers/credit-debit-notes.ts}` (new), wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`/`app.module.ts`/`main.ts` exactly like every prior module. `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` gains five permission keys and their role grants; `roles.integration-spec.ts`'s hardcoded catalog-count assertion corrected 68 → 73 (caught only because the seed script was re-run and the dev DB's actual `permissions` table count was checked directly — the assertion would have silently stayed "passing" against a stale local DB otherwise, since it reads live DB rows, not the `PERMISSION_KEYS` source array). `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/credit-debit-notes/{page.tsx,shared.tsx}` (new), `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/invoices/[invoiceId]/credit-debit-notes/{new/page.tsx,[noteId]/page.tsx}` (new), the invoice detail page gains two actions plus the notes card. New `CreditDebitNoteIcon`.

Verified with 12 new tests in `credit-debit-notes.integration-spec.ts` (draft creation rejected against a DRAFT invoice and against a VOIDed invoice; CGST+SGST vs IGST line computation matches `Invoice.finalize`'s own; CREDIT finalize posts the exact mirrored journal; DEBIT finalize posts the exact same-direction journal as `Invoice.finalize`; CN/DN numbering sequences proven independent of each other and of invoice numbering via a live before/after count rather than a hardcoded starting number, since the file's own earlier tests already consume the first few of each series; cumulative-credit-exceeds-invoice-taxable-amount rejection; a large DEBIT note is *not* capped; a note finalizes successfully even after the source invoice's own accounting period has since been `LOCKED`, and its journal's `accountingDate` is confirmed to equal today, not the invoice's date; void of a finalized note reverses its journal via the existing `reverseJournal`; idempotent-repeat-returns-original; cross-org isolation; PM-scoping) plus one new test appended to `receipts.integration-spec.ts` proving `InvoicesService.list().outstandingAmount` correctly nets out a finalized Credit Note (draft note has no effect; finalizing drops it by exactly the credited taxable+GST amount). Three real test-isolation bugs were caught and fixed while writing/extending these two suites, none a product bug: the accounting-period-lock test permanently locked the one shared accounting period every other test's `finalizeInvoice()` helper depends on, breaking any test that ran after it — fixed by reordering it to run last among the tests needing that helper; a first test run's `afterAll` aborted partway through (a missing `accountingPeriodTransition` cleanup step, now fixed) leaving orphaned fixture rows whose globally-unique GSTINs collided with a second run — cleaned up directly and the GSTIN values changed to ones not already used by any other spec file (also fixing a genuine cross-file collision with `receipts.integration-spec.ts`'s own fixture GSTIN, surfaced only when the full suite ran in parallel, not standalone); and `receipts.integration-spec.ts`'s own `afterAll` had no `creditDebitNote`/`creditDebitNoteLine` cleanup at all, so its new test's rows blocked the subsequent `invoice.deleteMany` under `fk_credit_debit_notes_invoice`'s `ON DELETE RESTRICT` — fixed by adding that cleanup ahead of the existing `invoiceLine`/`invoice` deletes. Full `apps/api` integration suite re-run clean (24 files, 261 tests) after the reseed. `@zitaware/contracts`/`@zitaware/ui` rebuild cleanly; `apps/api`/`apps/web` lint clean; `apps/web` production build succeeds with all three new routes registered. (`apps/web`'s standalone `npm run typecheck` fails on one pre-existing, unrelated, untracked file pair — `src/proxy.{ts,test.ts}`, a session-timeout redirect feature that predates this slice and was never touched by it; Next's own build-time type-check, which is the one that actually gates deployment, passes clean.)

**UI verified live by the user**: the Issue Credit Note/Debit Note actions and their add-form workflow; the note detail page's draft-edit/finalize/void flow; the Credit/Debit Notes card on the invoice detail page; the global list and its Add flow; the invoice list's outstanding-amount figure correctly reflecting notes. This slice is now complete end-to-end.
Supersedes: none — additive; the third slice of Phase 5.
```

```text
ID: ADR-071
Date: 2026-08-19
Status: Accepted — complete, implementation and UI both verified
Context: Phase 5, Slice 5.4 — Ledger Account Opening Balances, the fourth slice of Phase 5, picked next per the user's own instruction. The Ledger Viewer has shown a hard-coded disclaimer since Slice 4.1/4.4 ("no opening balance has been entered for this account, so they do not yet represent this account's real-world balance") — every balance shown has always started from a flat zero, because `PostingService.getLedgerActivity` only ever sums this system's own `JournalLine` rows, with no mechanism anywhere to record an account's pre-existing (pre-cutover) balance. `docs/11-development-roadmap.md`'s own Phase 5 deliverable list names this directly, calling it "a prerequisite for real bank reconciliation, and for the Ledger Viewer... to show a true running balance instead of one computed from a zero baseline."

This is the least-specified slice built this session: zero HTML prototype, zero `docs/04` screen contract, zero `docs/06` posting rule, zero domain-model entity, zero prior ADR scope line beyond ADR-062's generic exclusion. Research (this session, three parallel Explore passes covering the backend balance-computation code, every doc that could plausibly mention this feature, and the existing Chart of Accounts/Journal Entries frontend) confirmed a real, previously-undiscovered tension: `docs/11`'s own phrasing calls for an opening balance "per legal entity/ledger account," but `LedgerAccount`/`AccountGroup` are organization-wide, not legal-entity-scoped (ADR-062, deliberate) — and `PostingService.getLedgerActivity` had **no legal-entity filter at all**, silently summing every legal entity's activity into one shared number.

**Confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion before implementation**: scope opening balances per legal entity (not just per account), matching `docs/11`'s literal wording and real accounting practice — two legal entities' books must never be merged into one balance. This necessarily meant also adding a legal-entity filter to `getLedgerActivity` itself as part of this slice, fixing that latent gap rather than deferring it.
Decision:
(1) **Core mechanism — an opening balance is just a normal journal entry, dated on the "as of" date.** No second balance-computation code path was invented: setting one posts a real `Dr [account] / Cr OPENING_BALANCE_EQUITY` journal (or the mirrored direction) through the **existing, unmodified** `PostingService.postJournal`. Once posted it is just another `JournalLine` on that account — `getLedgerActivity`'s existing prior-lines-sum-before-`dateFrom` logic and its existing in-range query pick it up automatically, with zero change to the balance formula itself. Correcting one later is therefore just reversing that journal (`PostingService.reverseJournal`, its fifth reuse this session after Invoice/Receipt/Credit-Debit-Note) — no bespoke edit path.
(2) **New system ledger account**: `OPENING_BALANCE_EQUITY`, seeded as a leaf account directly under the pre-existing `EQUITY` root group (one of the five system-defined roots from ADR-062 — no new group needed).
(3) **Signed `amount` convention** matches the sign convention the Ledger Viewer already displays (`runningBalance`/`formatDrCr`): positive = the account's own normal-balance side, negative = the abnormal side (e.g. an overdrawn bank account). Posting logic mirrors `getLedgerActivity`'s own `isDebitNormal ? (+debit-credit) : (+credit-debit)` formula in reverse: the account itself is debited when a debit-normal account has a non-negative amount, or a credit-normal account has a negative (abnormal-side) amount; `OPENING_BALANCE_EQUITY` always takes the opposite side to keep the journal balanced. A zero amount is rejected outright (`VALIDATION_FAILED`) as pointless noise.
(4) **New `LedgerOpeningBalance` model** — `id, organizationId, legalEntityId, ledgerAccountId, asOfDate, amount, status (ACTIVE|VOID), journalEntryId, voidJournalEntryId, voidReason, voidedBy, voidedAt, createdAt, createdBy`. No `version`/update path at all — immutable once posted, mirroring EMD/SD's lodge-then-return shape rather than Invoice's draft/edit shape, since there is no draft concept here. A hand-added partial unique index (`UNIQUE (organization_id, legal_entity_id, ledger_account_id) WHERE status != 'VOID'`, the same `uq_invoices_source_event_active` precedent) enforces at most one ACTIVE opening balance per legal-entity+account at a time — a correction means voiding the existing one, then setting a fresh one.
(5) **`PostingService.getLedgerActivity` gains one new optional `legalEntityId` param** (backward-compatible — `undefined` keeps the exact original all-entities-summed behavior for any other caller), applied as a `journalEntry: { legalEntityId }` filter on both its prior-lines query and its in-range query. `JournalPort.getLedgerActivity` and the `journal.ts` router's input schema gain the matching optional field. This is the only change made to a pre-existing, already-shipped method this slice.
(6) **One new permission**: `ledger_opening_balance.set` (Accountant + Admin, matching `docs/09`'s "manual journal posting is restricted to Accountant/Admin" and the `chart_of_account.manage` precedent). No new `.read` permission — visibility is already gated by the existing `journal.read` (same as the rest of the Ledger Viewer); `void` reuses the same `ledger_opening_balance.set` grant rather than a second key, since setting and correcting are the same sensitivity level for this specific, narrow action. Permission catalog count corrected 73 → 74.
(7) **Backend service** (`LedgerOpeningBalancesService`) exposes exactly three methods — `set`, `void`, `getForAccount` — deliberately no paginated `list`. The only consumer is "does *this* account, for *this* legal entity, have one right now," shown inline in the Ledger Viewer drawer; the full audit trail (the posting and any reversal) is already visible for free on the existing Journal Entries page, like any other journal entry.
(8) **UI**: no new route, no new nav item — this is inherently a nested action within viewing one account's ledger, not a standalone screen. The existing `LedgerActivityDrawer` (Chart of Accounts page) gains a required "Legal entity" `Select` (defaulting to the first one) feeding the new `legalEntityId` param, and — where the stale disclaimer paragraph used to sit — either a "No opening balance has been entered..." note plus a **"Set Opening Balance…"** button (a `Modal` with an as-of-date input and a `MoneyInput` amount, gated `ledger_opening_balance.set`), or, once one exists, the balance shown plainly with a **"Void…"** action (a reason+accounting-date `Modal` identical in shape to the Journal Entries page's own `ReverseJournalModal`). No change to the activity table itself — the opening-balance journal simply appears as the first row like any other posting.
Consequences: New `LedgerOpeningBalance` model, `LedgerOpeningBalanceStatus` enum (migration `20260819120000_phase5_04_ledger_opening_balance`, hand-written, purely additive — one new table, one new enum, no backfill). `apps/api/src/accounting/posting.service.ts`'s `getLedgerActivity` gains the optional legal-entity filter (its only change this slice) — `apps/api/src/accounting/posting.integration-spec.ts`'s three existing call sites updated to pass `legalEntityId: undefined` explicitly, confirming the original all-entities behavior is unchanged when the param is omitted. `apps/api/src/ledger-opening-balances/{ledger-opening-balances.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}` (all new). `packages/contracts/src/{ports/ledger-opening-balance.port.ts, routers/ledger-opening-balances.ts}` (new) plus `ports/journal.port.ts`/`routers/journal.ts` extended, wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`/`app.module.ts`/`main.ts` exactly like every prior module. `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` gains the `OPENING_BALANCE_EQUITY` ledger account (under the existing `EQUITY` root) and one permission key + role grant; `roles.integration-spec.ts`'s hardcoded catalog-count assertion corrected 73 → 74 (again verified directly against the reseeded dev DB's actual `permissions` table count, not just inferred from the source array, per the process fix adopted during the previous slice). `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/chart-of-accounts/page.tsx`'s `LedgerActivityDrawer` gains the legal-entity picker and the opening-balance section/modals — no other file touched on the frontend.

Verified with 9 new tests in `ledger-opening-balances.integration-spec.ts` (`set` posts `Dr account/Cr equity` for a debit-normal account and the mirrored `Cr account/Dr equity` for a credit-normal account; a negative (abnormal-side) amount posts the flipped direction; a second `set` is rejected while one is already ACTIVE for the same legal-entity+account; `void` reverses the journal and then permits a fresh `set`; `getLedgerActivity`'s new `legalEntityId` filter is proven to keep two legal entities' opening balances on the very same ledger-account-code fully isolated from each other in both the running balance and the `dateFrom`-driven prior-lines sum, while omitting the filter reproduces the original summed-together behavior exactly; a zero amount is rejected; idempotent-repeat-returns-original; cross-org isolation) plus 3 existing `posting.integration-spec.ts` assertions re-verified unchanged. Full `apps/api` integration suite re-run clean (25 files, 270 tests) after the reseed. `@zitaware/contracts` rebuilds cleanly; `apps/api` lint/typecheck clean; `apps/web` lint clean and production build succeeds (no new routes — every change lives inside the pre-existing `/chart-of-accounts` route).

**UI verified live by the user**: the legal-entity picker; Set Opening Balance posting and reflecting in the Opening/Closing summary and the activity table; Void reversing it and re-enabling Set. This slice is now complete end-to-end.
Supersedes: none — additive; the fourth slice of Phase 5.
```

```text
ID: ADR-072
Date: 2026-08-19
Status: Accepted — implementation complete, UI verification pending
Context: Phase 5 polish — user request: "add feature to upload inward copy with invoice (same as general docs). If Inward copy uploaded then in invoices list, it shows inwarded for that record," referencing `project-invoice-add.html`/`project-invoices.html`. docs/04 §11.4 already disclosed this exact boundary ("primary tax invoice PDF is generated by CRM; inward acknowledgment remains uploadable") and §11.5 requires the list to "show document status and settlement status separately" — this was a pre-scoped, not invented, requirement. `project-invoices.html`'s own table has a dedicated "Inward Copy" column rendering plain colored text ("Attached"/"—"), deliberately lighter-weight than the adjacent "Payment" column's settlement pill.
Decision:
(1) **No new model or column.** `DocumentLink.linkType` (existing, previously always `undefined` in practice) now carries the value `INVOICE_INWARD_COPY_LINK_TYPE = 'INWARD_COPY'` when a document is tagged as an invoice's inward copy — "has an inward copy" is `EXISTS(DocumentLink WHERE entityType='INVOICE' AND entityId=<invoice> AND linkType='INWARD_COPY')`, the same live-computed-never-stored precedent as `outstandingAmount`/opening balances. The constant is exported as a value from `packages/contracts` for `apps/api` (matches how `LEDGER_CODES` is already shared, just contracts-level instead of `apps/api`-local) but is a **separately duplicated literal** in `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/invoices/shared.tsx`, not imported from `@zitaware/contracts` — every existing `apps/web` import from that package is `import type` only, because `@zitaware/contracts`'s `index.ts` barrel also re-exports the server-only tRPC init (`./trpc`, which calls `initTRPC.context().create()` at module-evaluation time); a runtime *value* import forces Next to bundle the whole package into the browser, which crashed with "You're trying to use @trpc/server in a non-server environment" — caught live by the user immediately after this shipped, fixed within the same change by duplicating the literal instead of importing it.
(2) **`documents.listLinksForEntity` gains an optional `linkType` filter** (undefined = every link, today's exact behavior) rather than a second endpoint — the existing entity-agnostic documents module already had the field, it just had no reader ever asking for a subset.
(3) **`DocumentsTab` (the shared component reused by Lead/Tender/Project/Invoice/Receipt/Security-Instrument) gains optional `linkType`/`uploadCardTitle`/`listCardTitle`/`emptyText` props**, all defaulting to today's exact generic behavior — this is the "same as general docs" the user asked for: one tagged instance of the same component (`linkType="INWARD_COPY"`, titled "Inward Copy") sits alongside the existing untagged instance ("Documents", for free-form supporting attachments) on the invoice detail page, rather than building a bespoke single-file upload widget. Both instances share the same `document.upload/manage/download` permissions — no new permission.
(4) **The Inward Copy card only renders once an invoice is `FINALIZED` or `VOID`, not while `DRAFT`** — a client acknowledgment can't logically exist before the invoice has been raised and sent; matches the existing gating already used for the Credit/Debit Notes card on the same page.
(5) **List indicator is plain colored text, not a `StatusTag` pill** (`InwardCopyIndicator` in `invoices/shared.tsx`) — deliberately visually distinct from the adjacent settlement-status pill, matching `project-invoices.html`'s own contract exactly rather than defaulting to the app's generic pill component.
(6) **Applied to both the global `/invoices` list and the project-scoped Invoices tab**, matching the precedent just set by the Outstanding-amount column (both surfaces share `InvoiceRow`/render logic; the user's own wording named only "invoices list" but every prior list-column addition this session went to both).
Consequences: `InvoiceSummary.hasInwardCopy: boolean` (new field, computed via a batched `documentLink.findMany({distinct:['entityId']})` in `list()` mirroring the existing `settledByInvoiceId`/`netCreditDebitByInvoiceId` pattern, and a single `findFirst` in `getDetail()`). As with `outstandingAmount`/`netCreditDebit`, the mutation-return-summary paths (`createDraftFromAlert`/`updateDraft`/`finalize`/`void`) do not recompute it and report `false` by default — consistent with those methods' existing behavior for the other two live-computed fields, not a new inconsistency. `packages/contracts`: `ports/invoice.port.ts` (new exported const + field), `ports/document.port.ts`/`routers/documents.ts` (`linkType` filter param). `apps/api`: `documents/documents.service.ts` (`listLinksForEntity` filter), `invoices/invoices.service.ts` (`toSummary`/`toDetail`/`list`/`getDetail`). `apps/web`: `components/documents-tab.tsx` (new optional props), `app/(platform)/invoices/shared.tsx` (`hasInwardCopy` field + `InwardCopyIndicator`), `app/(platform)/invoices/page.tsx` + `app/(platform)/projects/[id]/invoice-tab.tsx` (new column), `app/(platform)/projects/[id]/invoices/[invoiceId]/page.tsx` (new card).
(7) **`listLinksForEntity`'s `linkType` is a real three-state contract, not a binary filter-or-not**: `undefined` = every link regardless of tag (the original, pre-tagging behavior, unchanged); `null` = untagged links only; a string = only that exact tag. This was forced by a real bug caught live: the generic "Documents" `DocumentsTab` instance queried with `linkType: undefined` (its prop, when not passed, is `undefined`), which under the original binary contract meant "no filter" — so an invoice's `INWARD_COPY`-tagged upload also appeared, duplicated, in the plain "Documents" list below the dedicated "Inward Copy" card. Fixed by having `DocumentsTab` pass `linkType ?? null` for its list query/invalidation specifically (not for the upload/link mutation, which was already correct) — a generic, untagged instance now only ever sees untagged links, while a tag-scoped instance is unaffected.
Verified with 1 new test in `documents.integration-spec.ts` (three-way: a specific tag returns only that tag's link; `null` returns only the untagged link, proving the exact reported bug is fixed; `undefined` still returns all three) and 1 new test in `invoices.integration-spec.ts` (hasInwardCopy is false on a fresh finalized invoice in both `getDetail`/`list`; a `SUPPORTING`-tagged link does not flip it; an `INWARD_COPY`-tagged link does, in both `getDetail`/`list`). No schema migration — `DocumentLink.linkType` already existed. Full `apps/api` integration suite re-run clean (25 files, 272 tests) after each of the two live-caught fixes. `@zitaware/contracts` rebuilds cleanly; `apps/api`/`apps/web` lint and typecheck clean; `apps/web` production build succeeds with no new routes.
Known issues: none — the client-bundling bug from decision (1) was caught on the user's first live check and fixed in the same change (`apps/web` typecheck/lint/build all passed with the broken version, since Next's build-time type-check doesn't catch a barrel file pulling server-only code into a client bundle — only an actual browser load surfaced it). No further live browser pass performed yet beyond that fix — see the docs/13-progress.md entry for the specific checklist.
Supersedes: none — additive; a Phase 5 polish item, not a new numbered slice.
```

```text
ID: ADR-073
Date: 2026-08-20
Status: Accepted
Context: `docs/11-development-roadmap.md`'s Phase 5 deliverable list names "Bank transaction entry/import and reconciliation" as the last remaining unbuilt item, and Phase 5's own exit criteria include "Source documents, journals, and reports reconcile" — bank reconciliation is the mechanism that would satisfy that criterion. Slices 5.1–5.4 (Invoice, Client Receipt, Credit/Debit Note, Ledger Opening Balances) are all complete and UI-verified; Ledger Opening Balances (5.4) was explicitly built as this feature's named prerequisite, so it is now unblocked, not stuck. The user asked directly whether it could be skipped; asked to choose between deferring-and-moving-to-Phase-6, deferring-but-staying-in-Phase-5, or dropping it outright, the user chose to defer it and move to Phase 6.
Decision: Bank Transaction Import/Reconciliation is deliberately deferred, not dropped — it remains a named, disclosed gap in Phase 5, revisitable later (e.g. once real bank-statement feed integration becomes a priority), rather than silently abandoned. Phase 5 is treated as functionally closed without it: every other Phase 5 deliverable and every named exit criterion except live bank/journal/report reconciliation is met. Development moves to Phase 6 — Procurement and expense next. The richer Ledger Viewer redesign (Account/Journal view toggle, opening-balance banner, Export, expandable entry detail — raised during Slice 4.5 verification, viable since Ledger Opening Balances now exists) is also deferred alongside it, since it was named as this same follow-up's companion item, not because of any new constraint.
Consequences: `docs/11-development-roadmap.md`'s Phase 5 section gains a closing status line recording the deferral in place of "Bank transaction entry/import and reconciliation" as an open item; no code, schema, or test changes in this decision itself. Phase 6 planning begins fresh, per this codebase's own "identify aggregate, owner module, state machine, permissions, audit events, accounting effect, and required indexes before adding a model" and "one bounded vertical slice at a time" rules (`CLAUDE.md`) — the first Phase 6 slice has not yet been chosen as of this ADR.
Supersedes: none — narrows Phase 5's own exit criteria by explicit user decision; does not reopen or contradict any prior ADR.
```

```text
ID: ADR-074
Date: 2026-08-20
Status: Accepted — implementation complete, UI verification pending
Context: Phase 6, Slice 6.1 — Vendor Master + Purchase Request, the first Phase 6 slice, chosen (via `AskUserQuestion`) as the most bounded starting point since Purchase Request submission has zero accounting effect (docs/04: "Submission creates workflow only; no expense/journal"; "Approval creates no accounting entry"), mirroring how Milestones preceded Payment Alerts/Invoice in Phase 4/5. Grounded in `docs/01` §Procurement (canonical `Purchase Request → Approval → Optional PO → ... → Bank Reconciliation` chain; maker-checker; no amount slabs), `docs/03` §Parties/§Procurement, `docs/04` §12.1/§18, `docs/05` §Procurement/§Project Operational status, `docs/06` (confirms no posting rule exists for Purchase Request itself). An initial plan was rejected twice with detailed, itemized feedback (vendor duplicate handling, request numbering, removing duplicate amount entry, immutable approval history, maker-checker role-matrix correction, idempotency, referential integrity, the document-upload sequencing, frontend nav simplification, and — in a third, narrower round — replacing an absolute self-approval prohibition with a permission-controlled exception) before being approved; this ADR records the final, approved design.
Decision:
(1) **Vendor is `VendorProfile`, a second role layered on the existing `Party` foundation** — `ClientProfile`'s own doc comment had already anticipated this. A Party may now hold both an active Client role and an active Vendor role independently. GSTIN/PAN are normalized (uppercased, trimmed) before every write/comparison and validated by their own regex, independently of each other; an exact-GSTIN duplicate within the org is rejected in-service (field-specific error, never the raw DB constraint message) before insert, backed by a partial-unique index as defense-in-depth. A new `vendors.checkForDuplicates` query returns exact-GSTIN/PAN matches (against an existing Vendor *or* Client) and a soft normalized-name(+city) match; `vendors.create` accepts an optional `existingPartyId` to attach a new `VendorProfile` to an existing Party instead of creating a duplicate one. Payment terms is a controlled `VendorPaymentTerms` enum (`ADVANCE|NET_15|NET_30|NET_45`), not free text.
(2) **A latent bug in existing Phase 3 code, found and fixed because this slice introduced a second Party role**: `ClientsService.setActive` previously flipped both `ClientProfile.isActive` and `Party.isActive` together, and `ClientsService.list()` filtered on the Party-level flag alone — correct only when Party could have exactly one role. `Party.isActive` is now a true party-level master switch never touched as a side effect of any single role's `setActive`; `VendorsService.setActive`/`ClientsService.setActive` each flip only their own profile; selectability requires `party.isActive && profile.isActive`, checked consistently in every `list`/`getDetail`. Covered by a dedicated regression test (deactivating one role on a dual-role Party leaves the other, and the Party row itself, untouched).
(3) **`PurchaseRequest`/`PurchaseRequestLine`/`PurchaseRequestDecision`, zero accounting effect at any status** — no `postJournal` call exists anywhere in `PurchaseRequestsService`. `requestNumber` (`PR-000001`, org-scoped, the same concurrency-safe count-and-retry generation as Invoice numbers/Milestone codes) is assigned at **draft creation**, not deferred to submit, since a stable reference is needed the moment a quotation/document can be attached. `title` is required; `description` optional.
(4) **Lines are the sole source of the base amount** — each line is just `sequence/description/amount` (positive); `taxableAmount` (GST path) or `estimatedAmount` (no-GST path) is derived server-side as the sum of lines **at submit**, never entered by the user as a separate, potentially-disagreeing figure. Request-level `gstInvoiceAvailable`/`itcAvailable`/`supplyType`/`cgstAmount`/`sgstAmount`/`igstAmount` remain a requester-entered estimate (confirmed: no real posting happens here — that only becomes authoritative at a future Vendor Bill slice) but are now genuinely enforced: `gstInvoiceAvailable=false` clears ITC/supplyType/all three tax fields; `gstInvoiceAvailable=true` requires a `supplyType`; `INTRASTATE` zeroes/nulls IGST, `INTERSTATE` zeroes/nulls CGST+SGST; every amount is non-negative. Enforced at every write (not just submit) by one pure `computeTotals` helper — mirroring `ReceiptsService.computeReceiptPlan`'s own "one calculation, reused by preview and by the real command" shape — reused by both a new `purchaseRequests.calculatePreview` query (the frontend's live total, never client-computed) and `createDraft`/`updateDraft`/`submit`, plus a hand-written `chk_purchase_requests_gst_fields_consistent` CHECK constraint as defense-in-depth at the database layer.
(5) **Append-only maker-checker ledger, `PurchaseRequestDecision`** — `id, purchaseRequestId, decision, actorId, decidedAt, note, isSelfApproval`. No amount/version snapshot columns: since a request becomes fully immutable the moment it's `SUBMITTED` (decision 7), the FK to that unchangeable row already is the complete audit linkage. `note` is enforced non-empty in-service whenever `decision=REJECTED` or `isSelfApproval=true`.
(6) **Maker-checker with a controlled self-approval exception** — default: creator can never approve/reject their own request. Exception, added in a third planning round replacing an earlier absolute prohibition: a new permission `purchase_request.self_approve` (granted **only to Admin** by default — explicitly not Accountant/PM/Employee) lets its holder approve their own request, but only with a mandatory justification note; the resulting decision row is permanently flagged `isSelfApproval=true`, shown plainly in the UI's Approval History (a distinct "Self-approved" tag), never silently indistinguishable from an ordinary approval. Corrected role matrix (the initial plan had a contradiction here): Admin/Accountant approve globally; Project Manager approves only their assigned projects (reusing the existing `project.read_all`-driven scoping mechanism verbatim — no new scoping dimension); Employee has no approve capability at all, including on their own request; CEO is read-only (falls out of the existing `.endsWith('.read')` auto-grant, since `.create`/`.approve`/`.self_approve` don't end in `.read`). `purchase_request.export` was cut from this slice — no export endpoint/UI exists yet. `EMPLOYEE_PERMISSIONS` gains `project.read` for the first time — verified safe before granting: it only unlocks `projects.list/getDetail/listTeamMembers/listProgress`, not any Milestone/Invoice/Receipt/PaymentAlert/CreditDebitNote data, each gated by its own separate `.read` key Employee still lacks. Permission catalog count corrected 74 → 80.
(7) **Lifecycle**: `DRAFT → SUBMITTED → APPROVED|REJECTED`; `DRAFT|SUBMITTED → CANCELLED` (creator-only withdrawal). `REJECTED`/`CANCELLED` are terminal this slice — no edit-and-resubmit path; a genuine do-over is a new request. Once `SUBMITTED`, every field except status/cancellation fields is immutable — approve/reject only ever write a decision row + the status flip. Every transition requires `expectedVersion` (optimistic concurrency) and an `idempotencyKey` (the existing generic `IdempotencyKey`/`hashRequest` mechanism, unchanged, reused a sixth time this project).
(8) **Document workflow, corrected from the first draft**: create the `DRAFT` row (gets its `requestNumber`) → redirect to the detail page → upload the quotation/supporting documents via the existing `DocumentsTab` (`entityType='PURCHASE_REQUEST'`, one new `EntityType` seed row, no new `DocumentCategory`) → then Submit. Documents are optional; whether a quotation becomes mandatory above some amount threshold is an explicit, unresolved business decision, not invented here.
(9) **Project operational-status gating**: a new Purchase Request may be created/submitted against `ACTIVE`/`ON_HOLD`/`SUCCESSFULLY_COMPLETED` projects, rejected against `CANCELLED`/`CLOSED` ("terminal for normal operations," docs/05) — a reasoned default, explicitly flagged as open to correction, not asserted as an existing rule.
(10) **UI**: one **"Purchases & Expenses"** nav group (not two similarly-named "Finance"/"Finance — Expense" groups, correcting the first draft) holding `/vendors` and `/purchase-requests`, with room for the future Direct Expense/Claim/Advance screens to join later. `/vendors` matches `master-vendor.html`'s exact inline add-form + filter + table shape and color semantics (green `pos` Active pill, red `neg` Deactivate action, brass primary actions) rather than the app's more common Drawer-based add pattern — a deliberate visual-fidelity choice. `/purchase-requests` matches `purchase-request.html`'s three form sections and `purchase-list.html`'s column set, minus every Payment-related tile/column (Pending Payment/Paid/GST-Credit-Eligible tiles, Paid/Paid-From columns) since no payment tracking exists until a future Vendor Bill/Payment slice. No inline "+New" vendor pattern risking a stale, unrefreshed selector (the first draft's flaw) — a compact in-context Add Vendor modal is used instead, invalidating the vendor list and auto-selecting the new one.
Consequences: New `VendorProfile`, `PurchaseRequest`, `PurchaseRequestLine`, `PurchaseRequestDecision` models; `VendorPaymentTerms`, `PurchaseRequestStatus`, `PurchaseRequestPriority`, `PurchaseRequestSupplyType`, `PurchaseRequestDecisionType` enums (migration `20260820090000_phase6_01_vendor_purchase_request`, hand-curated from a `prisma migrate diff` run — three unrelated pre-existing hand-added constraints/indexes the diff also proposed dropping, since Prisma's schema DSL can't represent them, were deliberately excluded). `apps/api/src/clients/clients.service.ts` gets the narrow `setActive`/`list` fix from decision (2). `apps/api/src/vendors/{vendors.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}` and `apps/api/src/purchase-requests/{purchase-requests.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}` (all new), wired into `app.module.ts`/`main.ts`. `packages/contracts/src/{ports/vendor.port.ts, ports/purchase-request.port.ts, routers/vendors.ts, routers/purchase-requests.ts}` (new), wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts` like every prior module. `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` gains six permission keys (`vendor.read/manage`, `purchase_request.read/create/approve/self_approve`) + corrected role grants + one `EntityType` row; `apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts`'s catalog-count assertion corrected 74 → 80 (verified directly against the reseeded dev DB). `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/vendors/page.tsx` (new), `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/purchase-requests/{page.tsx, new/page.tsx, [id]/page.tsx, shared.tsx}` (new), `apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx` (`PURCHASE_REQUEST` added to its `entityType` union), `packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, index.ts, nav-items.ts}` (two new icons, one new nav group).
Verified with 7 new tests in `vendors.integration-spec.ts` (new-Party and existing-Party-attach creation; independent GSTIN/PAN format rejection; exact-GSTIN-duplicate rejection; Client-GSTIN cross-match found by `checkForDuplicates` and resolved via `existingPartyId`; soft NAME_CITY match never blocking; the dual-role Party independence regression; list `includeInactive`/cross-org isolation) and 23 new tests in `purchase-requests.integration-spec.ts` (sequential immutable request-number generation; empty-lines rejection; CANCELLED-project-blocks/ON_HOLD-allows; inactive-vendor/inactive-category rejection; GST-No derives `estimatedAmount` and clears every GST field regardless of what was sent; GST-Yes intrastate/interstate compute and clear the opposite tax component; missing-supplyType rejection; `calculatePreview` matches `submit`'s own persisted numbers exactly; idempotent create; `updateDraft` rejected post-submit; partial `updateDraft` preserves untouched fields; self-approval rejected without the permission and without a note, succeeds with both, flags `isSelfApproval=true`; a non-creator approves normally; reject requires a reason; `REJECTED` is terminal; idempotent approve creates no duplicate decision row; PM-equivalent scoped approve succeeds only for an assigned project; cancel is creator-only and works from `DRAFT`/`SUBMITTED`; stale-version rejection on `updateDraft`/`submit`; scoped `list`/`getDetail`; cross-org isolation). Full `apps/api` integration suite re-run clean (27 files, 302 tests). `@zitaware/contracts`/`@zitaware/ui` rebuild cleanly; `apps/api`/`apps/web` lint and typecheck clean; `apps/web` production build succeeds with all four new routes registered (`/vendors`, `/purchase-requests`, `/purchase-requests/new`, `/purchase-requests/[id]`).
Known issues: none — no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session), the same disclosed gap as every other slice.
Supersedes: none — additive; the first slice of Phase 6.
```

```text
ID: ADR-075
Date: 2026-08-20
Status: Accepted — implementation complete, UI verification pending
Context: Phase 6, Slice 6.2 — Vendor Bill, the second Phase 6 slice, chosen by the user ("move to next slice") from an `AskUserQuestion` menu as the natural follow-on to Slice 6.1: once a Purchase Request is `APPROVED`, nothing further exists in the app today, but docs/06 already names a real posting rule for this exact step (`Dr Expense/Project Cost + Input CGST/SGST/IGST Receivable — Cr Accounts Payable — Vendor`) — the first point on the *purchase* side where a real accounting entry posts, mirroring how Invoice is that first-posting point on the income side. docs/04 §18 marks Vendor Bill `NEW_REQUIRED` with no HTML prototype; `purchase-view.html`'s own disposition note allows it to be designed fresh in the app's existing shell/card/table language. Three design forks were resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before planning: (1) ITC eligibility is per line item, not a whole-bill toggle; (2) no cap against the originating Purchase Request's own (non-authoritative) estimate; (3) one Purchase Request may receive multiple Vendor Bills over time. All three were resolved by selecting the recommended option; the resulting plan was approved with zero revisions, a first for this session's Phase 6 work (Slice 6.1's own plan needed two rounds of itemized rejection). Also closes a gap surfaced while answering the user's own question about Chart-of-Accounts setup for vendor payments: `ExpenseCategory` had no link to a ledger account, so nothing could tell Vendor Bill which account to debit.
Decision:
(1) **`ExpenseCategory.ledgerAccountId`** — a new nullable FK to `LedgerAccount` (org-consistent composite FK), mirroring `PenaltyCategory.acceptedLedgerAccountId`'s exact nullable-with-fallback shape. 11 of 13 seeded expense categories are mapped to a real new leaf ledger account under a new `VENDOR_EXPENSES` expense group; a category left unmapped (or any bill line under one) falls back to one seeded default `OTHER_EXPENSES` account at finalize. `emd_paid`/`salaries_and_wages` are deliberately left unmapped — EMD has its own posting flow already, salaries is a Phase 7 payroll concern.
(2) **New `VendorBill`/`VendorBillLine` models**, structurally `InvoiceLine`'s own twin plus two fields: `expenseCategoryId` (resolves the debit account) and `itcEligible` (boolean, default true, decision below). `vendorBillNumber` is the vendor's own invoice/bill number — free text, never system-generated, unlike `invoiceNumber`. `purchaseRequestId` is **not unique** (decision 3) — a hand-added partial-unique index `(organization_id, vendor_id, vendor_bill_number) WHERE status != 'VOID'` prevents recording the same vendor invoice twice while still letting a voided entry's number be reused, the same precedent as `uq_invoices_source_event_active`. No hand-added CHECK constraint on any line amount column — verified the original `InvoiceLine` migration has none either; validation lives entirely in `computeLines()`, reused as-is (see decision 4), not duplicated at the database layer. No maker-checker table — `finalize` is a plain permission-gated recording action, matching `Invoice.finalize`'s own precedent exactly; the maker-checker gate for spending already happened at Purchase Request approval.
(3) **ITC eligibility is per line item** (`VendorBillLine.itcEligible`) — confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: docs/06's "ineligible GST is included in cost rather than Input GST Receivable" depends on *what* was bought, and one bill can mix eligible and blocked items (e.g. a client-entertainment line alongside eligible hardware). A whole-bill toggle would have been simpler but wrong.
(4) **No cap against the Purchase Request's own estimate** — confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: that estimate was never authoritative even at Slice 6.1 (a requester-entered figure, real GST only becomes authoritative here). The originating request's estimated grand total is shown on the create form and the Purchase Request's own Vendor Bills card purely for reference, never enforced as a maximum.
```
