```text
(5) **Multiple Vendor Bills per Purchase Request** — confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`, mirroring the existing "one OPEX milestone → many invoices" / "one invoice → many credit-debit notes" precedent (partial/phased delivery against one approved request).
(6) **`finalize` aggregates from already-stored line amounts, matching `Invoice.finalize`'s own actual behavior** — `computeLines` (reused, its third caller after `CreditDebitNotesService`) is called only in `createDraft`/`updateDraft`, where lines are freshly entered; `itcEligible`/`expenseCategoryId` aren't part of `computeLines`'s own input/output shape, so they're carried alongside by array index and combined with its result before persisting. `finalize` itself never recomputes GST — it reads the frozen `VendorBillLine` rows, exactly as `Invoice.finalize` reads `invoice.lines` rather than recalling `computeLines` a second time. Per line: `costDebit = taxableAmount + (itcEligible ? 0 : cgst+sgst+igst)`, resolved to that line's `expenseCategoryId → ExpenseCategory.ledgerAccountId` (or the `OTHER_EXPENSES` fallback) and **grouped/summed by resolved ledger account**, so several lines under one category collapse into a single journal debit line rather than one per line. Eligible CGST/SGST/IGST are summed separately into up to three `Input {C/S/I}GST Receivable` debit lines. One `Accounts Payable — Vendor` credit line for the bill's full total (taxable + all GST, regardless of eligibility — ineligible GST is still owed to the vendor, it just isn't reclaimable). `sameState = vendor.stateId === gstRegistration.stateId` (the purchase-side mirror of Invoice's own `sameState = project.client.stateId === gstRegistration.stateId`) is computed once, at line-entry time in `createDraft`/`updateDraft`, not re-derived at finalize. Financial-year resolution copies `Invoice.finalize`'s own direct-Prisma lookup (`financialYear` covering `vendorBillDate` for the legal entity) — no new resolution mechanism. Posted via the existing, unmodified `PostingService.postJournal` (`sourceType: 'vendor_bill_finalize'`).
(7) **`void`** mirrors `Invoice.void` exactly — a `DRAFT` bill voids with a plain status flip; a `FINALIZED` bill voids via the existing `reverseJournal`, requiring an accounting date and reason.
(8) **New permissions**: `vendor_bill.read/create/update/finalize/void` (no `.export` — this records someone else's document, it doesn't generate a PDF of the org's own). Accountant/Admin get all five; Project Manager/Admin Staff get `.read` only (PM scoped to assigned projects via the existing `ProjectsService`-backed mechanism, reused verbatim for every mutating method as well as `list`/`getDetail` — a stricter scoping posture than `Invoice`'s own write-path methods, chosen deliberately since this is a spend-authorization-adjacent document); CEO auto-gets `.read` via the existing `.endsWith('.read')` filter; Employee gets nothing. Permission catalog count 80 → 85.
(9) **UI**: `/vendor-bills` (global list), `/vendor-bills/new?purchaseRequestId=...`, `/vendor-bills/[id]` — full pages, not drawers, per the user's own explicit "drawer is not comfortable to CEO" feedback from earlier in this session (which had already prompted converting the Purchase Request creation flow to two plain buttons on one page) — Vendor Bill's UI adopts that same two-button "Save as Draft / Save & Finalize" pattern from the start, without needing a second round of user feedback to get there. A **Vendor Bills card** on the Purchase Request detail page appears once `status === 'APPROVED'`, listing bills raised against it plus an "Add Vendor Bill" action, mirroring the existing Credit/Debit Notes card on Invoice's own detail page. New `VendorBillIcon` (inbound document/tray glyph, distinct from Invoice's own outbound-page glyph) in a new "Vendor Bills" nav item under the existing "Purchases & Expenses" group.
Consequences: `ExpenseCategory.ledgerAccountId`; new `VendorBill`/`VendorBillLine` models; `VendorBillStatus` enum (migration `20260821090000_phase6_02_vendor_bill`, hand-curated from a `prisma migrate diff` run — the same three unrelated pre-existing hand-added constraints/indexes the diff always proposes dropping were excluded again). `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` gains an `ACCOUNTS_PAYABLE` liability group + `ACCOUNTS_PAYABLE_VENDOR` account, three `Input {C/S/I}GST Receivable` accounts under the existing `TAX_RECOVERABLE` group, a new `VENDOR_EXPENSES` expense group with 11 mapped leaf accounts + one `OTHER_EXPENSES` default, the `ExpenseCategory.ledgerAccountId` backfill (reusing the existing `SECURITY_INSTRUMENT_PURPOSES`-style `ledgerAccountCode` seed-resolution pattern), five permission keys + role grants, and one `VENDOR_BILL` `EntityType` row; `apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts`'s catalog-count assertion corrected 80 → 85 (verified directly against the reseeded dev DB). `packages/contracts/src/{ports/vendor-bill.port.ts, routers/vendor-bills.ts}` (new), wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts` like every prior module. `apps/api/src/vendor-bills/{vendor-bills.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}` (new), wired into `app.module.ts`/`main.ts`; `apps/api/src/invoices/invoices.service.ts` unchanged — `computeLines`/`ComputedLine` were already exported. `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/vendor-bills/{page.tsx, new/page.tsx, [id]/page.tsx, shared.tsx}` (new), `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/purchase-requests/[id]/page.tsx` (Vendor Bills card), `apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx` (`VENDOR_BILL` added to its `entityType` union), `packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts}` (one new icon, one new nav item).
Verified with 16 new tests in `vendor-bills.integration-spec.ts` (blocked until the source Purchase Request is `APPROVED`, both on `getDraftDefaults` and `createDraft`; vendor/project/legal-entity pre-filled from the request and uncapped against its estimate; duplicate `vendorBillNumber` per vendor rejected while active, reusable once voided; multiple bills against one request all succeed; inactive expense category rejected; idempotent `createDraft`; intra-state CGST+SGST computed and ITC-eligible vs ineligible correctly splits Input GST Receivable vs folded-into-cost, with same-category lines collapsing into one grouped journal debit; inter-state IGST; fallback to `OTHER_EXPENSES` for an unmapped category; idempotent `finalize` posts no second journal; finalize rejected on an already-finalized bill; void of a finalized bill reverses the journal; void requires a reason and, for a finalized bill, an accounting date; PM project-scoping on create; stale-`expectedVersion` rejection; cross-org isolation). Full `apps/api` integration suite shows contention-driven timeouts only under the complete 29-file parallel run (a pre-existing dev-Postgres connection-pool limitation, confirmed by re-running the exact four "failed" files — including this slice's own — together in isolation, all 50 tests passing cleanly); not a functional regression. `@zitaware/contracts`/`@zitaware/ui` rebuild cleanly; `apps/api`/`apps/web` lint and typecheck clean; `apps/web` production build succeeds with all three new routes registered (`/vendor-bills`, `/vendor-bills/new`, `/vendor-bills/[id]`).
Known issues: none functional — no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session), the same disclosed gap as every other slice; the pre-existing full-suite parallel-run contention noted above is an environment/test-harness characteristic, not scoped to this slice to fix.
Supersedes: none — additive; the second slice of Phase 6.
```

```text
ID: ADR-076
Date: 2026-08-21
Status: Accepted — implementation complete, UI verification pending
Context: A follow-up user review of the freshly-shipped "Add Vendor Bill" page (ADR-075) raised four gaps, discovered while the user was answering the ADR-075 slice's own design questions live in the running app: no live per-line computed summary as quantity/unit price are entered; GST enterable only as a rate %, never a raw amount; quantity has no unit of measure; nothing surfaces when a bill (or cumulative bills) against a Purchase Request runs past that request's own — deliberately uncapped (ADR-075 decision 4) — estimate. The user asked for the first two to be modeled on the existing Client Receipt "Record Payment" page (`/projects/[id]/receipts/new/page.tsx`), read in full this turn: it never computes accounting totals in the browser (CLAUDE.md) — a debounced `receipts.calculatePreview` query (reusing the same calculation `postReceipt` itself uses) fills a sticky right-side "Live Calculation" panel, and each deduction row's Rate %/Amount pair auto-fills each other via a plain client-side `rate/100 × base` conversion where `base` is always a real, already-server-computed figure. For units of measure, `AskUserQuestion` offered a hardcoded-list vs. admin-manageable-master fork; the user chose an admin-manageable master with an explicitly non-editable code/slug — which turned out to be exactly this project's existing generic Masters system (`MasterPort`/`MasterTypeKey`, 12 existing flat lookup tables behind one API, `code` already immutable by construction since `update()` never accepts it). Before touching schema, the actual `vendor_bill_lines` table was checked for existing rows — one real, already-VOID row was found (the user had exercised the live feature themselves while asking these very questions), forcing an adaptation to the originally-planned NOT NULL column (see decision 1).
Decision:
(1) **Units of Measure — a 13th generic MasterTypeKey**, not a bespoke model: new `UnitOfMeasure`, byte-for-byte `DocumentCategory`'s own flat shape, wired into `MASTER_TYPES`/`getDelegate()` (confirmed, by reading `masters.service.ts` in full, to be the *entire* backend surface — zero per-type special-casing exists anywhere in that service) and one new `apps/web/masters/catalog.ts` entry (the generic `/masters/[type]` page needed no new code at all). No new permission — reuses `master.read`/`master.manage`. Seeded with `qty`/Qty (sortOrder 0, so it lists and defaults first), `ltr`/Litre, `mtr`/Meter, `cm`/Centimeter, `inch`/Inch, `feet`/Feet. `VendorBillLine.unitOfMeasureId` is **nullable at the DB layer**, not the originally-planned NOT NULL — the one real pre-existing VOID row (discovered live, mid-planning, before any migration was written) has no UnitOfMeasure data to backfill it from, and it is dead/immutable so a null unit there is harmless; every new `createDraft`/`updateDraft` call still requires it via a required (non-optional) zod field, the same nullable-at-DB/required-in-practice shape `ExpenseCategory.ledgerAccountId` already established. The seed script also backfills that one legacy row to the default `qty` unit, now that it exists, as a harmless idempotent no-op once already done.
(2) **`vendorBills.calculatePreview`** — a new, pure, non-mutating port method reusing the exact same `computeLines()` call `createDraft`/`updateDraft` persist with (its now-fourth caller, after `CreditDebitNotesService` and `VendorBillsService`'s own `createDraft`/`updateDraft`), so the live preview and what actually saves can never disagree — mirrors `receipts.calculatePreview`'s own "one calculation, reused by preview and by the real command" shape exactly. Gated by `vendor_bill.create` (same permission as `createDraft`, matching how `purchaseRequests.calculatePreview` is gated by `.create` even though it's also used from the edit page). The New Vendor Bill page and the Draft Editor on the detail page are both reworked into the Record Payment page's own two-column sticky layout (`UiCard flush` + `grid-cols-[1fr_360px]`), with the identical debounced-`useEffect`-plus-`utils.X.calculatePreview.fetch()` pattern powering a per-line "Live Summary" panel (`VendorBillLineItemsEditor`/`VendorBillLivePreviewPanel`, both now shared between the two pages).
(3) **GST rate % ↔ amount, both directions** — a purely frontend data-entry convenience layered on the existing, unchanged `gstRatePercent` field; no schema or `computeLines` change, the DB still only ever stores computed amounts exactly as before. `VendorBillLineItemsEditor` is restructured from a flat `.map()` into a per-row `VendorBillLineRowEditor` component (required because each row needs its own `Form.useWatch`, which `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` forbids inside a bare `.map()` — the same extraction the Record Payment page's own `DeductionRow` already demonstrates). Each row's local-only `gstAmountEntry` field cross-writes `gstRatePercent` (and vice versa) via `form.setFieldValue`, using that row's own `calculatePreview`-supplied `taxableAmount` as the base — `gstAmountEntry` is dropped when building the mutation payload; only `gstRatePercent` is ever submitted.
(4) **Soft-friction finalize warning + color variance share one new pair of live-computed fields** on `VendorBillSummary` (inherited by `VendorBillDetail`): `purchaseRequestEstimatedAmount` (free — `purchaseRequest.grandTotal ?? .estimatedAmount`, already `include`d) and `cumulativeBilledAmount` (sum of `totalAmount` across every FINALIZED, non-void Vendor Bill sharing that `purchaseRequestId`, batched via one `groupBy` in `list()` mirroring `InvoicesService.list()`'s own `outstandingAmount` pattern, single-row `aggregate` elsewhere). While a bill is itself still `DRAFT` this naturally excludes itself; once `FINALIZED` it's naturally included from then on — no separate "excluding self" field needed. `VendorBillDraftDefaults` gains the same `cumulativeBilledAmount` (via the identical helper) so the New Vendor Bill page's own warning accounts for prior bills against the same request, not only the one being created. The Finalize confirmation modal on both editing pages compares `cumulativeBilledAmount + (live preview's own totalAmount)` against `purchaseRequestEstimatedAmount` and, if it would be exceeded, shows an additional warning line — `Ok` is never disabled, matching "soft friction" exactly as requested, since Vendor Bill remains deliberately uncapped (ADR-075). A shared `varianceTone()` helper (`text-negative` when exceeded, default otherwise) colors the amount on the Vendor Bill list page, a new "Cumulative billed vs. estimate" line on the finalized/void detail view, and the existing Purchase Request "Vendor Bills" card's own Total column — one helper, three call sites.
Consequences: New `UnitOfMeasure` model; `VendorBillLine.unitOfMeasureId` (nullable, decision 1) — migration `20260821100000_phase6_02b_vendor_bill_units_and_variance`, hand-curated from a `prisma migrate diff` run (the same three unrelated pre-existing constraints/indexes always excluded). `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` gains `UNITS_OF_MEASURE` + a one-time legacy-row backfill. `packages/contracts/src/ports/master.port.ts` (`'units_of_measure'` — also required a second, independent fix in `packages/contracts/src/routers/masters.ts`'s own hardcoded zod `masterTypeSchema` enum, a real gap caught only by `apps/web`'s typecheck, not by the port-level type alone), `packages/contracts/src/ports/vendor-bill.port.ts` (`VendorBillLinePreviewRow`/`VendorBillLinesPreview`, `unitOfMeasureId`/`unitOfMeasureName` on line types, `purchaseRequestEstimatedAmount`/`cumulativeBilledAmount` on `VendorBillSummary`, `cumulativeBilledAmount` on `VendorBillDraftDefaults`, `calculatePreview` on `VendorBillPort`), `packages/contracts/src/routers/vendor-bills.ts` (`calculatePreview` procedure, `unitOfMeasureId` in `lineInputSchema`). `apps/api/src/masters/masters.service.ts` (registry entries), `apps/api/src/vendor-bills/vendor-bills.service.ts` (`calculatePreview`, `assertUnitsOfMeasureActive`, `getCumulativeBilledAmount` threaded through every mutating method plus `list`/`getDetail`/`getDraftDefaults`), `apps/api/src/vendor-bills/vendor-bills.integration-spec.ts` (unit fixtures, 6 new tests), `apps/api/src/masters/masters.integration-spec.ts` (catalog-type-count assertion corrected 13 → 14). `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/masters/catalog.ts` (new entry), `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/vendor-bills/shared.tsx` (`VendorBillLineRowEditor`, `VendorBillLivePreviewPanel`, `varianceTone`), `.../new/page.tsx` and `.../[id]/page.tsx` (two-column live layout, finalize warning), `.../page.tsx` (list coloring), `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/purchase-requests/[id]/page.tsx` (Vendor Bills card coloring).
Verified with 6 new tests in `vendor-bills.integration-spec.ts` (`calculatePreview` matches what `createDraft`/`finalize` actually persist, intrastate and interstate; empty-lines and non-approved-request handling; inactive unit rejected same as inactive category; `cumulativeBilledAmount`/`purchaseRequestEstimatedAmount` correct across two bills against one request, before and after finalize, reported consistently by `list()`/`getDetail()`, and dropping a voided bill back out) plus the corrected `masters.integration-spec.ts` catalog-count assertion — all 20 vendor-bills tests and 6 masters tests passing. `@zitaware/contracts` rebuilds cleanly; `apps/api`/`apps/web` lint, typecheck, and production build all clean (`apps/web` registers the same three existing Vendor Bill routes, no new routes this pass since Units of Measure rides the existing generic `/masters/[type]` screen).
Known issues: none functional — no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session), the same disclosed gap as every other slice.
Supersedes: none — additive; a Phase 6.2 polish pass, not a new numbered slice (same framing as ADR-072's Invoice Inward Copy).
```

```text
ID: ADR-077
Date: 2026-08-21
Status: Accepted — Batch 1 implementation complete, UI verification pending
Context: The user's very first message this session asked how much effort it would take to convert every Drawer in the app to a separate page, since Drawers feel cramped/uncomfortable for the CEO — answered as an estimate only, not acted on, while the session instead worked through Phase 6 slices. Once those slices shipped (ADR-074/075/076), the user returned to the original question with an explicit instruction to plan and execute it app-wide, with the hard constraint that no functional/business-logic behavior may change — only chrome. A dedicated research pass cataloged every Drawer and every substantial Modal across `apps/web` and found the conversion pattern already proven three times over in the existing codebase: `/users/[id]` already replaces its own edit-drawer with a full page; `/vendors` already renders "Add Vendor" as an inline page card; `RaiseInvoiceModal`/`IssueCreditDebitNoteModal`/`RecordPaymentModal` already use a tiny picker-modal-launches-a-page pattern. `AskUserQuestion` confirmed two scoping calls before planning: start with the lowest-risk batch first (prove the pattern cheaply before the largest forms), and scope this round to Drawers only — Modals (Record Return, Release, Opening Balance Set/Void, Edit Event, Snooze Alert) are a disclosed follow-up candidate list, not touched. The resulting three-batch plan was approved with zero revisions.
Decision:
(1) **The conversion pattern, applied identically everywhere**: a create flow becomes `/module/new/page.tsx` (`PageHeader` + the module's existing form body, `router.push` to the list or the new record's own detail page on success); an edit/detail flow becomes the module's own `[id]/page.tsx` rendering the same form body either always-editable or behind a local `editing` boolean toggling between the read view and an inline `<UiCard title="Edit X">` — mirroring `VendorBill`'s own `DraftEditor`/`FinalizedOrVoidView` toggle-by-status shape; a list page's Add/Edit handlers become `router.push()` calls, and the Drawer JSX plus its local open/close state are deleted outright, not left dead. Read-only detail Drawers convert the same way minus the Save step. Zero business-logic change **by construction**: every tRPC query/mutation, every validation rule, every field stays byte-for-byte identical — only `<Drawer>` vs. `PageHeader` + `UiCard` page chrome changes. Where a list page previously showed both a "View" and a separate "Edit" button pointing at what becomes the identical URL post-conversion, the redundant "Edit" button is removed, leaving one "View" action — matching the single-button convention `Purchase Requests`/`Vendor Bills` already established earlier this session.
(2) **Batch 1 (this pass) — the lowest-risk quick wins**: Leads/Tenders/Projects (all three already had a full `[id]` detail page and an already-Drawer-chrome-decoupled `XFormBody` component in their own `shared.tsx`, making this the cheapest conversion in the whole catalog) — list-page Add/Edit Drawers removed in favor of `/module/new` + `router.push` to `[id]`, and each `[id]` page's own Edit-Drawer converted to the inline `editing`-toggle card described above. Vendors — the one remaining Edit Drawer converted to a second inline "Editing {vendor}" card on the list page, mirroring the already-inline "Add Vendor" card exactly (no new route). Three read-only detail Drawers converted to dedicated pages: Journal Entries' `EntryDetailDrawer` → `/journal-entries/[id]` (its in-drawer "view original/reversal entry" cross-navigation becomes real `router.push` page-to-page links, a genuine UX improvement beyond pure chrome); Audit Log's `AuditDetailDrawer` → `/audit-log/[id]`; Chart of Accounts' `LedgerActivityDrawer` → `/chart-of-accounts/ledger/[accountId]` (its two nested opening-balance Set/Void Modals stay Modals, explicitly out of scope this round). The Group/Account create-edit Drawers on Chart of Accounts are **not** touched this round — they belong to Batch 2 (no existing `[id]` detail route to land on yet), keeping this batch strictly to the plan's own lowest-risk scope.
(3) **A missing read endpoint was added, not routed around**: Audit Log's row data had always been passed straight from the already-fetched list into its Drawer, with no dedicated single-record query — the new `/audit-log/[id]` page needs its own fetch (a page reload, a shared link, or navigating in directly all bypass the list's cache). Added `audit.getById` (`AuditPort`/`AuditService`/`audit.ts` router), gated by the existing `audit.read` permission, org-scoped via `findFirst({ id, organizationId })`, throwing `NOT_FOUND` exactly like every other module's own `getDetail`. Chart of Accounts' ledger page, by contrast, needed no new endpoint — `chartOfAccounts.listAccounts` already returns every account unpaginated, so the page finds its own row client-side from the same query the parent list page already runs, avoiding an unnecessary new read surface for a case the existing one already covers.
(4) **Explicit scope boundaries carried over from the plan, not silently dropped**: no Modal conversions this round — Record Return, Release, Opening Balance Set/Void, Edit Event, and Snooze Alert stay Modals. The mobile off-canvas navigation Drawer (`AppShell.tsx`) was never in scope — not a form/detail pattern. Plain confirm-style Modals (a reason/date field, nothing else) are correct as Modals everywhere and were not touched, including Journal Entries' own `ReverseJournalModal`.
Consequences: New routes `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/{leads,tenders,projects}/new/page.tsx`, `.../journal-entries/[id]/page.tsx`, `.../audit-log/[id]/page.tsx`, `.../chart-of-accounts/ledger/[accountId]/page.tsx`. Modified: `{leads,tenders,projects}/page.tsx` (Drawer removed, single "View" action), `{leads,tenders,projects}/[id]/page.tsx` (Edit-Drawer → inline `editing` toggle), `vendors/page.tsx` (Edit-Drawer → inline card), `journal-entries/page.tsx` (`EntryDetailDrawer` deleted, "View Lines" navigates), `audit-log/page.tsx` (`AuditDetailDrawer`/`JsonBlock` deleted, "View Details" navigates), `chart-of-accounts/page.tsx` (`LedgerActivityDrawer`/`formatDrCr`/`newIdempotencyKey` deleted, "View Ledger" navigates; Group/Account Drawers untouched). `packages/contracts/src/ports/audit.port.ts` (`getById` on `AuditPort`), `packages/contracts/src/routers/audit.ts` (`getById` procedure), `apps/api/src/audit/audit.service.ts` (`getById` implementation). No schema/migration changes — this batch is UI-chrome and one read-only endpoint only.
Verified with 3 new tests in `audit.integration-spec.ts` (`getById` returns the event for the requesting organization; throws for an event belonging to a different organization; throws for a non-existent id) — 10/10 audit tests passing. Full `turbo run typecheck lint build` across all 9 workspaces clean (27/27 tasks), `apps/web` production build registers every new route. Full `apps/api` integration suite: first parallel run showed 24 failures across 8 unrelated spec files (none touching a module changed this batch) all ending in `Test timed out in 5000ms`; re-run immediately after showed 28/28 files, 328/328 tests passing — confirmed as the same pre-existing dev-Postgres connection-pool contention already disclosed in ADR-075/076's own Known issues, not a regression (no orphaned test-data rows found; `vendor-bills`/`invoices` re-run in isolation passed 33/33 independently of the full-suite run).
Known issues: none functional — no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session), the same disclosed gap as every other slice this session. Batch 2 (new-route buildout for Masters/Legal Entities/Bank Accounts/Financial Years/Chart of Accounts groups-accounts/Roles/Clients/Introducers/Employees) and Batch 3 (EMD/SD's `LodgeSecurityInstrumentDrawer` via the picker-modal-launches-a-page pattern; Milestones' Capex/Opex Add drawers) remain as documented, not-yet-started future work, each needing only a short confirmation before starting per the approved plan.
Supersedes: none — additive; UI chrome plus one new read-only endpoint, no business-logic or data-model change.
```

```text
ID: ADR-078
Date: 2026-08-21
Status: Accepted — implementation complete, UI verification pending
Context: The user asked what the next slice was per the roadmap; `AskUserQuestion` offered Payment Request/Vendor Payment, Direct Expense, and the two remaining Drawer-to-page batches — Payment Request/Vendor Payment was chosen, continuing docs/04's own procurement chain (`Purchase Request → Vendor Bill → Payment Request → Vendor Payment → Bank Reconciliation`) from Slices 6.1/6.2. `AskUserQuestion` confirmed two design forks before planning: batching multiple Vendor Bills into one Payment Request (chosen over one-bill-per-request), and allowing partial payment requests spread across multiple requests over time (chosen over requiring a bill to be requested in full). A first plan was rejected outright with an exceptionally detailed 13-point technical review largely specifying the correct architecture directly: gross-liability semantics for `requestedAmount` with TDS explicitly excluded from this slice; concurrency-safe outstanding enforcement via row-locking, not an application-level race-prone check; a DB-level `@@unique` on lines, not service-only validation; a new `REVOKED` status/lifecycle for withdrawing an approved request; a precise self-approval/permission matrix; strict "no inaccessible line exists" (not "at least one line matches") project-scoping for multi-project batches; a Legal Entity selection workflow; a simplified form dropping `title`/`description` for a `requestedPaymentDate` field; a server-authoritative non-mutating preview reusing one shared calculation function; extraction of a shared sequential-number-generator utility reused by Purchase Request too; rich audit/decision-history requirements; and an explicit rejection of "an immediate rerun passed" as an acceptable resolution for the full-suite connection-pool timeout pattern already disclosed in ADR-075/076/077's own Known issues sections — demanding a real config fix instead. The revised plan addressed every point by section number and was approved with no further revisions.
Decision:
(1) **`requestedAmount` is the gross vendor liability amount authorized for settlement, not a projected bank outflow** — no TDS field, computation, or mention anywhere in this slice's schema or forms; how a future settlement later splits into cash paid vs. TDS withheld is entirely a Slice 6.4 concern, flagged in this ADR's own "Looking ahead" note but not decided here.
(2) **Concurrency-safe reservation via row-locking, not an application-level check**: `availableForRequest = vendorBill.totalAmount − sum(requestedAmount)` across every `PaymentRequestLine` whose parent status is `SUBMITTED` or `APPROVED` — computed by one shared, bounded helper (`getAvailableForRequest`) reused by `createDraft`/`updateDraft` (soft, advisory), `submit` (hard, authoritative), `calculatePreview`, `getEligibleBills`, and `getBillAvailability`; never duplicated. Draft-time checks are advisory only — `submit` is the sole authoritative gate: inside one transaction it collects every distinct `vendorBillId` referenced by the request, sorts them ascending, and runs `SELECT id FROM vendor_bills WHERE id::text IN (...) ORDER BY id FOR UPDATE` (a `uuid = text` Postgres operator error on the first attempt was fixed by casting the column, not the parameter) — pessimistic locks acquired in deterministic ascending order so two concurrent submits touching overlapping-but-not-identical bill sets can never deadlock. Availability is recomputed under lock and revalidated per line before flipping `DRAFT → SUBMITTED`; any line that no longer fits throws `VALIDATION_FAILED` and rolls back the entire submission — no partial submit. Verified with a genuine concurrent-submission test using `Promise.allSettled` on two racing `submit` calls against an overlapping bill set: exactly one succeeds, the loser gets a clean validation error, not a corrupted double-reservation.
(3) **`REVOKED` is a new, first-class status**, not a repurposed `CANCELLED`: `APPROVED → REVOKED` requires the new `payment_request.revoke` permission and a mandatory reason, recorded as a `PaymentRequestDecision` row (`decision: REVOKED`) exactly like `APPROVED`/`REJECTED` — no separate header timestamp columns. Naturally releases the reservation, since `REVOKED` drops out of the `SUBMITTED`/`APPROVED` set the availability formula counts. `revoke()` carries its own code comment recording a forward constraint for Slice 6.4: once a `VendorPaymentAllocation` model exists, revoke must additionally reject if one already references this request's lines — nothing to enforce yet, so not enforced now, but not left for 6.4 to rediscover either.
(4) **The permission matrix is deliberately stricter than Purchase Request's own default**: five new permission keys (`payment_request.read/create/approve/revoke/self_approve`). Admin gets everything; Accountant gets read/create/approve/revoke but not `self_approve`; Project Manager gets read/create/approve (project-scoped) but not revoke or self-approve; Admin Staff and CEO get read only; **Employee gets no grant at all** — unlike Purchase Request, which gives Employee read+create, since raising a payment authorization is judged a stricter action than raising a purchase request. Self-approval mechanics mirror `PurchaseRequestsService.approve` exactly (`isSelfApproval` stored and shown as a visible tag, mandatory justification note when self-approving).
(5) **Project-scoping for multi-project batches is "no inaccessible line exists," not "at least one line matches"**: a scoped actor may see/act on a request only if every line's `vendorBill.projectId` is in their assigned set, implemented as a `NOT: { lines: { some: { vendorBill: { projectId: { notIn: assignedProjectIds } } } } }` query-level filter for `list`, and an `assertBillsProjectAccessible` helper (throws `NOT_FOUND`, never partial exposure) for single-record operations — matching this codebase's own "access-scoping errors masquerade as not-found" convention.
(6) **A new shared utility, extracted mechanically**: `apps/api/src/common/sequential-number.util.ts` (`generateSequentialNumber({count, prefix, padLength, attempt})`), with `PurchaseRequestsService.generateRequestNumber` refactored to a two-line wrapper delegating to it (`PR-` prefix, behavior-identical) — the one file this slice touches outside its own new module, a strictly mechanical extraction. `PaymentRequestsService.generateRequestNumber` uses the same helper with prefix `PMR-`. `Invoice.generateInvoiceNumber` was deliberately left untouched — its per-GST-registration/financial-year scoping key is materially different, out of this slice's bounded scope.
(7) **A new composite-FK support constraint**: `VendorBill` gained `@@unique([id, organizationId], map: "uq_vendor_bills_id_org")` — required for `PaymentRequestLine`'s own org-consistent FK into it, the first model needing one; mirrors the identical existing pattern already on `Party`/`LegalEntity`/`BankAccount`/`DocumentCategory`.
(8) **The vitest connection-pool timeout pattern (disclosed in ADR-075/076/077) was root-caused and fixed for real**, per the review's explicit rejection of "a rerun passed" as an acceptable resolution: `apps/api/vitest.integration.config.ts` had no `poolOptions` at all — 29+ spec files, each opening its own unconstrained-per-CPU Prisma connection pool, could exceed Postgres's connection limit under full parallelism. Fixed with `poolOptions.threads.{minThreads: 1, maxThreads: 4}`, bounding total concurrent connection pools to a safe multiple regardless of spec-file count.
(9) **Header fields dropped `title`/`description` in favor of `requestedPaymentDate`** (the planned execution date, no accounting effect, may differ from Slice 6.4's actual payment date) — a display title is computed (`Payment to {vendorName} — {requestNumber}`), never stored. A Legal Entity picker auto-selects when exactly one active entity exists, or offers a required searchable choice when more than one; a request seeded from a specific Vendor Bill (`?vendorBillId=`) resolves both Vendor and Legal Entity entirely server-side, ignoring any client-supplied value for either, closing a spoofing vector the original draft plan hadn't addressed.
Consequences: New `PaymentRequest`/`PaymentRequestLine`/`PaymentRequestDecision` models and `PaymentRequestStatus`/`PaymentRequestDecisionType` enums (migration `20260821110000_phase6_03_payment_request`, hand-curated from `prisma migrate diff`, same three pre-existing constraints excluded as every prior migration). New `apps/api/src/common/sequential-number.util.ts` (shared); `apps/api/src/purchase-requests/purchase-requests.service.ts` mechanically refactored to use it. New `apps/api/src/payment-requests/{payment-requests.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}`, wired into `app.module.ts`/`main.ts`. New `packages/contracts/src/{ports/payment-request.port.ts, routers/payment-requests.ts}`, wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`. `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` gains 5 permissions, their role grants, and a `PAYMENT_REQUEST` `EntityType` row (bare, no `DocumentCategoryEntityType` links, matching the established "absence = applies everywhere" pattern). `apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog count corrected 85 → 90. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/payment-requests/{shared.tsx, page.tsx, new/page.tsx, [id]/page.tsx}` (imports `PRIORITY_OPTIONS`/`newIdempotencyKey` from `purchase-requests/shared.tsx` rather than duplicating them, since Payment Request reuses the identical `PurchaseRequestPriority` enum); `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/vendor-bills/[id]/page.tsx` gains a "Payment Requests" card, its own "Raise Payment Request…" action shown only while a balance actually remains, not merely once `FINALIZED`; `documents-tab.tsx` gains the `PAYMENT_REQUEST` entity type; `packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts}` gain a new icon and nav item. `apps/api/vitest.integration.config.ts` gains bounded thread pooling (see point 8).
Verified with 26 new tests in `payment-requests.integration-spec.ts` covering the full matrix named in the review (multi-bill same-vendor/legal-entity batches; cross-vendor/cross-legal-entity/duplicate-bill/non-finalized/zero-amount/exceeds-available rejections; the genuine concurrent-submission race; reservation release after reject/cancel/revoke; self-approval permission + mandatory justification; PM access across every project in a batch and rejection of a mixed-project batch with no partial leakage; stale-version conflicts; idempotent replay; cross-org isolation; full decision/audit history) — all passing. `turbo run typecheck lint build` clean across all 9 workspaces (27/27 tasks). A fresh-database migration replay against a disposable scratch database (`zitaware_crm_scratch_verify`, dropped after use) confirmed the full migration history — including this slice's new migration — applies cleanly from zero, with `prisma migrate diff` against the resulting database showing only the same three pre-existing hand-added constraints every prior migration in this project has excluded. The full `apps/api` integration suite's first clean run surfaced one real (not flaky) fixture defect this slice introduced — `payment-requests.integration-spec.ts` reused the exact literal GSTIN `24DDDDD0000D1Z5` already hardcoded in `vendor-bills.integration-spec.ts`'s own cross-org fixture, which a real, nationally-unique GSTIN constraint correctly rejected as a duplicate once bounded thread concurrency let both files run at the same time; fixed by giving the new spec file its own unique literal — full suite then passed 29/29 files clean on the next run, satisfying the review's own "first run, not a diagnostic rerun" acceptance bar.
Known issues: none functional — no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session), the same disclosed gap as every other slice this session. Slice 6.4 (Vendor Payment) is explicitly not started: `VendorPayment`/`VendorPaymentAllocation` mirror `ClientReceipt`/`ReceiptAllocation`; each allocation references a `PaymentRequestLine`; whether TDS is always applicable and exactly when it is recognized is an open policy question to confirm during 6.4 planning, not an assumption carried over from this slice; no TDS rate/section master exists anywhere in this codebase yet and none should be invented without confirming the right shape first.
Supersedes: none — additive; a new Phase 6 slice, no prior ADR's decision is reopened or contradicted.
```

```text
ID: ADR-079
Date: 2026-08-22
Status: Accepted — implementation complete, UI verification pending
Context: The user reported the shipped procurement chain (Purchase Request → Vendor Bill → Payment Request → Vendor Payment) was too long for day-to-day use — an employee needed a pre-approved *estimate* before a real vendor invoice even existed, then a separate Vendor Bill had to be raised against that approval before payment could even be requested. Reading the three original HTML prototypes this flow was derived from (`purchase-request.html`/`purchase-view.html`/`purchase-payment.html`) confirmed they never actually specified a two-document request+bill split — they show one request (with the real invoice attached), one view/edit screen, and one "mark as paid" screen. The user proposed merging Purchase Request's fields directly into Vendor Bill, landing on a three-step chain: Vendor Bill (with all details and actual GST amounts) → Payment Request → Vendor Payment. Three directional questions were confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before planning: the approval gate moves from "before purchase" to "after the vendor bill exists" (no more separate pre-commitment estimate approval); the Purchase Request module is deleted entirely rather than left as unused dead code; "Approve" and "Finalize" (post to ledger) become one combined action. Research (two parallel Explore passes, full-file reads of both services/contracts/frontend modules) confirmed this is a clean merge: Payment Request (6.3) and the not-yet-built Vendor Payment never reach through Vendor Bill into Purchase Request for anything — every check already reads `vendorBill.vendorId`/`projectId`/`legalEntityId`/`status`/`totalAmount` directly.
Decision:
(1) **Vendor Bill's state machine expands to absorb Purchase Request's own maker-checker workflow**: `DRAFT → SUBMITTED → FINALIZED / REJECTED`; `DRAFT/SUBMITTED → CANCELLED` (creator-only, reason required); `FINALIZED → VOID` (unchanged). `approve()` (renamed from `finalize()`) runs the exact `isSelfApproval`/mandatory-justification-note check the deleted `PurchaseRequestsService.approve()` used, AND posts the identical journal entry `finalize()` always posted — both in the same transaction as a new `VendorBillDecision` row. `FINALIZED` itself is an **unchanged enum value** — this is precisely why Payment Request and the future Vendor Payment slice needed zero code changes; they only ever filter on `status === 'FINALIZED'`. Cancel replaces what used to be a "Void Draft" status flip for withdrawing an unposted draft/submission; Void is now reserved solely for reversing an already-posted (`FINALIZED`) bill's journal entry.
(2) **`vendorId`/`projectId`/`priority` are entered directly on Vendor Bill** instead of being copied from a linked Purchase Request (the first two already existed as columns on `VendorBill`, populated by copy — they simply become directly-entered fields). `legalEntityId` is derived from the selected project, shown read-only, never a separate picker. `purchaseRequestId` (column, FK, index) is dropped from `VendorBill` entirely, along with the `purchaseRequestEstimatedAmount`/`cumulativeBilledAmount` derived fields and the `varianceTone()` "exceeds estimate" soft-friction UI — none of these were ever persisted columns, so this is a pure deletion, not a migration concern. No header `title`/`description` field was added — `VendorBillLine.description` already serves that purpose in every existing view.
(3) **`PurchaseRequestPriority` is renamed, not dropped**, to `VendorBillPriority` via `ALTER TYPE ... RENAME TO` in the migration — `PaymentRequest.priority` already depended on this exact enum type and needed zero data loss or code change. The raw `prisma migrate diff` output modeled this as "drop the old enum, create a new one, then DROP COLUMN + ADD COLUMN `payment_requests.priority`," which would have silently destroyed every existing PaymentRequest row's own priority value — replaced by hand with the plain rename Postgres supports natively, per this project's own "hand-curate from the diff" migration convention.
(4) **A new `VendorBillDecision` table mirrors the deleted `PurchaseRequestDecision` exactly** (`id, organizationId, vendorBillId, decision[APPROVED|REJECTED], actorId, decidedAt, note, isSelfApproval`) — only approve/reject go here; cancel stays header columns (`cancelledAt/cancelledBy/cancelledReason`), matching how `PurchaseRequestDecision` itself never recorded `CANCELLED` either.
(5) **Permissions collapse from 9 combined keys to 5**: `vendor_bill.{read, create, approve, self_approve, void}` — `create` now gates create/update/submit/preview and `approve` gates both approve and reject, mirroring Purchase Request's own precedent of reusing one key across related transitions. Project Manager gains `vendor_bill.approve` and Employee gains `vendor_bill.{read,create}` — both **necessary parity fixes**, not new grants of convenience: both roles held the equivalent Purchase Request permission before, and would have silently lost that capability once Purchase Request was deleted. The seed script's permission-upsert loop was additive-only by design (never previously needed to remove a permission) — a stale-permission and stale-`EntityType`-row prune step was added ahead of the upsert loop, deleting `role_permissions`/`user_permission_overrides` referencing a no-longer-declared permission before deleting the permission itself, so the catalog always reflects exactly what `PERMISSION_KEYS` declares rather than accumulating orphaned rows forever.
(6) **A real vendor-bill-number-reuse gap was caught by writing the new test suite, not found separately**: the existing hand-added partial unique index (`uq_vendor_bills_vendor_number ... WHERE status != 'VOID'`) only ever freed a vendor's bill number for reuse once voided — a newly-introduced `CANCELLED` bill (which, like `VOID`, never posted anything) would have permanently blocked reuse of its own number. Fixed with a small follow-up migration correcting the WHERE clause to `NOT IN ('VOID', 'CANCELLED')`, and the matching service-layer duplicate-number checks in `createDraft`/`updateDraft`.
Consequences: `packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma` (all of the above), two migrations — `20260822090000_phase6_04_merge_purchase_request_into_vendor_bill` (the main merge: drops `purchase_requests`/`purchase_request_lines`/`purchase_request_decisions` and three of their four enums, renames `PurchaseRequestPriority`, adds the new `vendor_bills` columns and status values, creates `vendor_bill_decisions`) and `20260822093000_phase6_04b_vendor_bill_number_reuse_after_cancel` (the index fix from point 6). `apps/api/src/vendor-bills/vendor-bills.service.ts` fully rewritten (`submit`/`approve`/`reject`/`cancel` added, `assertProjectAccessible`/`assertVendorActive` ported from the deleted service, `getDraftDefaults`/`getCumulativeBilledAmount` removed). `apps/api/src/purchase-requests/` deleted entirely (service, module, integration-spec), unwired from `app.module.ts`/`main.ts`. `packages/contracts/src/{ports/vendor-bill.port.ts, routers/vendor-bills.ts}` rewritten; `packages/contracts/src/{ports/purchase-request.port.ts, routers/purchase-requests.ts}` deleted, unwired from `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`. `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` (permission/role-grant changes from point 5, plus the new prune steps). `apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog count corrected 90 → 86. `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/purchase-requests/` deleted entirely; `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/vendor-bills/{shared.tsx, page.tsx, new/page.tsx, [id]/page.tsx}` rewritten to absorb Purchase Request's own field set and maker-checker Approve/Reject/Cancel modal UI (ported from its own `DecidedOrSubmittedView`). `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/payment-requests/shared.tsx` (one-line import change: `PRIORITY_OPTIONS`/`newIdempotencyKey` now come from `vendor-bills/shared` instead of the deleted `purchase-requests/shared`). `documents-tab.tsx` (`PURCHASE_REQUEST` entity type removed). `packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts, index.ts}` (`PurchaseRequestIcon` and its nav entry removed).
Verified with a full rewrite of `vendor-bills.integration-spec.ts` (38 tests, covering both the pre-existing GST/ITC/ledger-grouping/void-reversal coverage and the newly-absorbed maker-checker matrix: self-approval permission + mandatory justification, creator-cannot-approve-without-permission, reject terminal, cancel creator-only + reason reachable from both DRAFT and SUBMITTED, approve not retroactively blocked by a since-cancelled project while create/submit are, idempotent replay on every mutating command, version conflicts) plus a fixed `payment-requests.integration-spec.ts` (its `createFinalizedBill` fixture helper rewritten to build directly through Vendor Bill's own draft→submit→approve flow — Payment Request's production code itself needed zero changes, confirmed) — 38/38 and 26/26 passing respectively. `roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog count assertion at 86, verified against the live seeded database directly (`Permission.count()`, per-role `rolePermissions` inspection) before the test suite itself confirmed it. `turbo run typecheck lint build` clean across all 9 workspaces. A fresh-database migration replay on a disposable scratch database confirmed the full migration history — including both new migrations — applies cleanly from zero. Full `apps/api` integration suite: 29/29 files clean.
Known issues: none functional — no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session), the same disclosed gap as every other slice this session. Vendor Payment is the next slice (renumbered 6.5, since this merge occupies 6.4) — still not started; the same open TDS-policy question flagged in ADR-078 carries forward unchanged.
Supersedes: ADR-074 (Purchase Request's own design — the module it created no longer exists). Amends ADR-075/076 (Vendor Bill's own design — its state machine, permission set, and header fields all changed; the accounting posting logic itself, `computeLines`, and the GST rate/amount two-way entry are untouched). Does not reopen ADR-078 (Payment Request) — confirmed zero code changes required there.
```

```text
ID: ADR-080
Date: 2026-08-23
Status: Accepted — implementation complete, UI verification pending
Context: Payment Request (6.3) authorizes settlement of a Vendor Bill but posts nothing — the schema's own doc comment named Vendor Payment as the still-missing step that actually moves money. The user asked for this slice directly (`AskUserQuestion`, "Start Vendor Payment (Slice 6.5)"). Research confirmed `ClientReceipt`/`ReceiptAllocation` (`apps/api/src/receipts/receipts.service.ts`) is a clean direct mirror with the debit/credit sides flipped (paying down a liability instead of collecting a receivable) and `PaymentRequestLine` standing in for `Invoice` as the thing being allocated against — a single atomic `post`, no draft/finalize split, multi-row allocation with typed deductions, a live non-persisting preview, and a separate `reverse` action, all reused verbatim. Three scope questions were confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before planning: (1) deduction typing is `TDS`/`CGST_TDS`/`SGST_TDS`/`IGST_TDS` only — no Hold/Retention/Penalty-on-payment (nothing in this codebase withholds those from an outbound vendor payment the way a client receipt withholds them from an inbound one); (2) TDS is a free-entered amount with an optional display-only rate % convenience, mirroring `AllocationDeduction.ratePercent` exactly — no TDS section/rate master invented this slice; (3) batching and partial payment are both allowed — one Vendor Payment may settle several `APPROVED` `PaymentRequestLine`s (same vendor + legal entity, validated, not restricted to one seed Payment Request), and one line's authorized amount may be paid across multiple Vendor Payments over time, mirroring both Client Receipt's own multi-invoice allocation and Payment Request's own already-established partial-request design.
Decision:
(1) **`VendorPayment`/`VendorPaymentAllocation`/`VendorPaymentDeduction` mirror `ClientReceipt`/`ReceiptAllocation`/`AllocationDeduction` field-for-field**, narrowed to the TDS/GST-TDS deduction subset and with no `vendorId` column on the header (implied by the allocations, all of which must share one vendor — app-validated at post time, exactly like `ClientReceipt` has no `clientId`). No maker-checker of its own and no generated payment number — the authorization gate already happened at Payment Request's own `approve()`, and `ClientReceipt` itself has neither.
(2) **A new `"available to pay"` formula** (`line.requestedAmount − sum(allocation.settledAmount)` across every `VendorPaymentAllocation` on that line whose parent `VendorPayment.status = POSTED`) is symmetric to Payment Request's own `getAvailableForRequest`, computed by one shared private helper reused by `getEligiblePaymentRequestLines`, `getPaymentRequestAvailability`, and `computeVendorPaymentPlan` alike — never duplicated.
(3) **Posting logic mirrors `computeReceiptPlan`/`postReceipt` with debit/credit sides flipped**: validates every allocation's line → parent Payment Request is `APPROVED`, that every allocation shares one vendor and one legal entity (matching the paying bank account's own), and that `cashPaid + deductions` never exceeds a line's live "available to pay." Journal: one `Dr Accounts Payable — Vendor` line for the batch's combined settled amount (`partyId` = the shared vendor); one `Cr` line to the bank account's own ledger account; one `Cr` line per distinct TDS/GST-TDS ledger account referenced, summed across allocations. Four new leaf ledger accounts (`TDS_PAYABLE`, `GST_TDS_PAYABLE_{CGST,SGST,IGST}`) seeded under the existing `TAX_PAYABLE` group, the flip of the existing `TDS_RECEIVABLE`/`GST_TDS_RECEIVABLE_*` accounts.
(4) **`PaymentRequestsService.revoke()` activates its own forward-flagged check** (its doc comment had named this since Slice 6.3): a direct `VendorPaymentAllocation` existence query, scoped to the request's own line ids, added immediately after the existing status/access checks — no new dependency between the two services, no circular module import.
(5) **Three new permissions (`vendor_payment.{read,post,reverse}`) mirror `receipt.{read,post,reverse}`'s exact role restriction** — Accountant gets all three, Project Manager/Admin Staff/CEO get `read` only (CEO via its own automatic `.read`-suffix filter; PM/Admin Staff need an explicit grant since their permission sets are hand-enumerated), Employee gets none — moving real money stays Accountant/Admin-only, the same boundary Client Receipt already draws.
Consequences: `packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma` — new `VendorPaymentStatus`/`VendorPaymentDeductionType` enums and the three new models, plus back-relations on `LegalEntity`/`BankAccount`/`PaymentMode`/`JournalEntry`/`PaymentRequestLine`. New migration `20260823090000_phase6_05_vendor_payment` (hand-curated from `prisma migrate diff`, the same three pre-existing phantom lines excluded as every prior migration). `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` — 3 permissions + role grants (point 5), 4 ledger accounts (point 3), a `VENDOR_PAYMENT` `EntityType` row (bare, no `DocumentCategoryEntityType` links or documents tab, matching `ClientReceipt`'s own precedent of no attachment field at all). `apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog count corrected 86 → 89. New `apps/api/src/vendor-payments/{vendor-payments.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}`, wired into `app.module.ts`/`main.ts`; `apps/api/src/payment-requests/payment-requests.service.ts` gains the `revoke()` check (point 4). New `packages/contracts/src/{ports/vendor-payment.port.ts, routers/vendor-payments.ts}` (its own `DeductionInput`/`AllocationInput` type names re-exported under a `VendorPayment`-prefixed alias from the package barrel to avoid colliding with `receipt.port.ts`'s own identically-named exports), wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/vendor-payments/{shared.tsx, page.tsx, new/page.tsx, [id]/page.tsx}` (`new/page.tsx` requires a `?paymentRequestId=` seed, mirroring Record Payment's own `?invoiceId=` seed; a checklist of eligible lines each expand into their own cash-paid + deductions editor when selected, plus a live-preview panel powered by `calculatePreview`). `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/payment-requests/[id]/page.tsx` — the placeholder "Payment fulfilment... is not yet available" note is replaced with a real remaining-balance readout, a "Record Payment…" action (shown only while `APPROVED` and a balance remains, mirroring Vendor Bill's own "Raise Payment Request…" visibility rule), and a "Vendor Payments" card listing payments recorded so far. `packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts}` — new `VendorPaymentIcon` and nav item under "Purchases & Expenses".
Verified with 16 new tests in `vendor-payments.integration-spec.ts` (plain-TDS and split CGST/SGST/IGST-TDS journal shape; sum(cashPaid) != actualAmount rejected; batching two lines from separate Payment Requests sharing one vendor+legal entity; cross-vendor and cross-legal-entity batch rejection; a non-`APPROVED` line rejected; a genuine partial-payment sequence — first payment, a since-updated "available to pay" balance, an over-allocation correctly rejected, the exact remainder paid off cleanly; reversal reverses the journal, flips status, restores availability, and leaves allocation/deduction rows untouched; a stale-version reversal rejected; idempotent replay on post; project scoping (`NOT_FOUND` via `getDetail` and exclusion from `list` for an out-of-scope actor, confirmed visible for an in-scope one); cross-org isolation; and — in the same file, since `revoke()` lives on the service under test's own dependency — 2 tests proving `PaymentRequestsService.revoke()` now rejects once a `VendorPaymentAllocation` exists and still succeeds when none does) — all 16 passing on a clean first run. `turbo run typecheck lint` clean across all 9 workspaces (23/23 tasks); `turbo run build` clean for every workspace except `apps/web`, whose production build hit the pre-existing, previously-disclosed non-deterministic upstream Next.js `/_global-error`/`/_not-found` prerender crash (ADR-043, corrected by ADR-052/053 — confirmed intermittent across otherwise-identical builds, not tied to any application code) on 5 consecutive attempts this session; `web`'s own `typecheck`/`lint` and TypeScript-compile/production-bundle steps inside that same build all passed clean every attempt, including every new vendor-payments route, so this is not a regression introduced by this slice. A fresh-database migration replay on a disposable scratch database confirmed the full migration history — including this slice's new migration — applies cleanly from zero, with `prisma migrate diff` against the result showing only the same three pre-existing hand-added constraints every prior migration has excluded. Full `apps/api` integration suite passed clean on its first run (29/29 files, 363/363 tests).
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session), the same disclosed gap as every other slice this session. `apps/web`'s production build is intermittently red on the pre-existing upstream `/_global-error`/`/_not-found` bug described above — not new to this slice, left open per the same standing user decision recorded in ADR-043/053. This closes docs/01's own canonical procurement chain (`Vendor Bill → Payment Request → Vendor Payment`) end to end; Hold/Retention/Penalty-on-payment and a TDS section/rate master remain explicitly out of scope until a real policy need is confirmed.
Supersedes: none — additive; a new Phase 6 slice, no prior ADR's decision is reopened or contradicted. Fulfills the forward-flagged check named in ADR-078's own `PaymentRequestsService.revoke()` doc comment.
```

```text
ID: ADR-081
Date: 2026-08-24
Status: Accepted — implementation complete, UI verification pending
Context: With the procurement chain (Vendor Bill → Payment Request → Vendor Payment) complete end to end, the next unbuilt Phase 6 deliverable is "Employee advance, claim, reimbursement" (docs/11). The user asked to plan this directly, naming four reference prototypes (`expense-add.html`, `expense-employee.html`, `expense-employee-statement.html`, `expense-give-advance.html`). Direct Expense (`expense-add.html`'s own "Direct Company Expense" branch) was confirmed out of scope — split into its own later slice, since it has zero dependency on the employee-wallet concept this slice builds. Four scope questions were confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before planning: (1) `approveClaim` posts the expense-recognition journal immediately, and a later `reimburseClaim` posts a separate cash-settlement journal — a deliberate departure from this phase's usual "approval posts nothing" pattern, since docs/06 names "Approved expense claim against advance" as its own real posting rule; (2) GST is one flat, user-entered credit amount, no CGST/SGST/IGST split; (3) Advance + Claim (submit/approve/reject/cancel/reimburse) + Statement ship together this slice, Direct Expense deferred; (4) a claim/advance links to a Project or nothing (company overhead) — no Lead link target this slice.
Decision:
(1) **`EMPLOYEE_ADVANCE_RECEIVABLE` is a single bidirectional control account per employee, not two separate receivable/payable accounts.** Working through the Employee Statement prototype's own worked numeric example (R. Shah: ₹10,000 advance, then two claims of ₹2,400 and ₹4,000, closing wallet ₹3,600) confirmed this: advance-given and reimbursement-paid are the *identical* posting shape (`Dr control account / Cr Bank`) — the only difference is which business event triggers it — and claim-approval (`Dr Expense / Cr control account`) can legitimately drive the account negative, which is simply "the company now owes the employee" in ledger terms, not a distinct payable account. This is why docs/06 says "reimbursement... is posted separately" without ever naming a second account.
(2) **`EmployeeExpenseClaim`'s state machine and maker-checker mirror `VendorBill`'s own shape verbatim** (`DRAFT → SUBMITTED → APPROVED/REJECTED`, `→ CANCELLED` creator-only with mandatory reason, self-approval gated by `employee_claim.self_approve` + a mandatory note), plus a fifth `→ REIMBURSED` state unique to this model. `approve()` posts the expense-recognition journal (activating `Employee.isReimbursable`, unused on the schema since Phase 2); `reimburse()` is a later, independent action posting the cash settlement for that one claim's own full amount only — no partial or batched reimbursement. `reverseReimbursement` moves `REIMBURSED` back to `APPROVED` (not a new terminal state), matching `ReceiptsService.reverseReceipt`/`VendorPaymentsService.reverseVendorPayment`'s own correction shape, so a mistaken payment can simply be corrected and re-run. Reversing an `APPROVED`-but-not-yet-reimbursed claim's own approve-journal is explicitly out of scope this slice (no cash has moved yet at that point).
(3) **No project-based scoping anywhere in this module** — a real, deliberate simplification versus Vendor Bill/Payment Request. A claim is about the *claimant*, not a project team, so there is no "no inaccessible line exists" machinery to build. A plain Employee's own self-service views (`getMyEmployeeStatus`, `getMyWalletStatement`, `getMyClaims`) resolve the acting employee from the session (`Employee.findFirst({ userId: actorId })`) and need no special permission beyond the new `employee_claim.create` — never a client-supplied employee id. `getClaimDetail` blends admin and self-service access in one procedure (mirrors this codebase's own `canSeeAll` pattern): without `employee_claim.read`, it only returns a claim belonging to the actor's own resolved employee, letting a plain Employee view their own claim's detail page after submitting it.
(4) **GST is a flat, user-entered credit amount** — no rate, no CGST/SGST/IGST split, posted to one new generic `INPUT_GST_RECEIVABLE_EXPENSE` account, deliberately simpler than Vendor Bill's own GST engine and matching the prototype's own single toggle+amount field exactly.
(5) **Nine new permissions** largely extend this codebase's own one-permission-per-verb convention beyond the four docs/04 names verbatim (`expense_claim.read/approve/reimburse`, `employee_advance.read/create`): `employee_claim.{read,create,approve,self_approve,reimburse,reverse}`, `employee_advance.{read,create,reverse}`. Accountant gets all non-`self_approve` grants (Admin-only by default, matching Vendor Bill/Payment Request's own precedent); Project Manager/Admin Staff/CEO get `read` only on both; Employee gets `employee_claim.create` only — the one genuinely new self-service capability this slice grants.
Consequences: `packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma` — new `EmployeeAdvanceStatus`/`EmployeeExpenseClaimStatus`/`EmployeeExpenseClaimDecisionType` enums and the three new models, plus back-relations on `Employee`/`Project`/`LegalEntity`/`BankAccount`/`PaymentMode`/`JournalEntry`/`ExpenseCategory`. New migration `20260824090000_phase6_06_employee_advance_expense_claim` (hand-curated from `prisma migrate diff`, the same three pre-existing phantom lines excluded as every prior migration). `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` — 9 permissions + role grants (point 5), 2 ledger accounts (point 1, point 4), an `EMPLOYEE_EXPENSE_CLAIM` `EntityType` row (documents tab — bill/receipt attachment; `EmployeeAdvance` gets none, matching `ClientReceipt`/`VendorPayment`'s own no-attachment precedent). `apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog count corrected 89 → 98. New `apps/api/src/employee-expenses/{employee-expenses.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}` (one service covering both models, since they share one posting helper shape and one wallet-statement read model), wired into `app.module.ts`/`main.ts`. New `packages/contracts/src/{ports/employee-expense.port.ts, routers/employee-expenses.ts}`, wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/employee-expenses/{shared.tsx, page.tsx, give-advance/page.tsx, my/page.tsx, my/new/page.tsx, claims/[id]/page.tsx}`. `apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx` gains the `EMPLOYEE_EXPENSE_CLAIM` entity type. `packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts}` — new `EmployeeWalletIcon` and two nav items ("My Expenses", always visible; "Employee Reimbursements", gated by `employee_claim.read`) under a new "Employee Expenses" group.
Verified with 17 new tests in `employee-expenses.integration-spec.ts` — advance-issue journal shape and its reversal, plus a stale-version-conflict test on reverse; claim create/submit/reject/cancel state transitions, including rejection of a non-reimbursable employee and of editing/submitting someone else's own draft; approve's journal shape (plain claim, and one with a non-zero flat GST credit split into its own line), self-approval permission + mandatory-note gating, idempotent replay; reimburse's journal shape for a claim with no prior advance, its reversal back to `APPROVED`, a successful re-reimbursement after that reversal, and a stale-version-conflict test; the full R.-Shah-style multi-event sequence (advance → two claims approved) reproducing the exact running control-account balances shown in the prototype (₹10,000 → ₹7,600 → ₹3,600) via `getMyWalletStatement`; `getClaimDetail` scoping (a claim's own employee can view it without `employee_claim.read`, a different employee cannot, an Accountant with the permission can); cross-org isolation — all 17 passing on a clean first run. A real bug was caught by this suite, not found separately: `reimburseClaim` originally reused the claim's own id as the journal's `sourceId`, which is safe for `approveClaim` (a claim is approved at most once ever) but collided with `JournalEntry`'s own `uq_journal_entries_org_source` constraint on a *second* reimbursement attempt after a `reverseReimbursement` — fixed by generating a fresh id per reimbursement attempt. `turbo run typecheck lint` clean across all 9 workspaces; `turbo run build` clean for every workspace except `apps/web`'s own pre-existing, previously-disclosed non-deterministic `/_global-error`/`/_not-found` prerender bug (ADR-043/052/053) — not tied to this slice's own code, whose own compile/typecheck/lint steps all passed clean. A fresh-database migration replay on a disposable scratch database confirmed the full migration history — including this slice's new migration — applies cleanly from zero. Full `apps/api` integration suite passed clean on its first run.
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session), the same disclosed gap as every other slice this session. `apps/web`'s production build remains intermittently red on the pre-existing upstream bug described above. Direct Expense, a Lead link target, partial/batched reimbursement, a sub-category master, and a computed CGST/SGST/IGST split are all explicitly deferred, not silently dropped.
Supersedes: none — additive; a new Phase 6 slice, no prior ADR's decision is reopened or contradicted.
```

```text
ID: ADR-082
Date: 2026-08-24
Status: Accepted — implemented and fully verified
Context: A user linked their own login to a newly-added Employee record (resolving an unrelated "No employee record is linked" support question) and then asked a real follow-up: how to log an expense claim for an employee who does **not** need a CRM login at all — e.g. field staff. `createClaimDraft` (ADR-081) was self-service only by design, always resolving the claiming employee from the acting user's own linked Employee record, with no way to raise a claim for anyone else — a faithful match to the `expense-add.html` prototype, which has no employee-picker field, but a real gap for any employee who will never hold a login. `Give Advance` already let an Accountant target any employee directly; Claims never got the equivalent admin-side path.
Decision:
(1) `createClaimDraft` gains an optional `employeeId` input. When supplied, the actor must hold `employee_claim.approve` (checked server-side) or the call is rejected `FORBIDDEN` — no new permission was introduced; the existing Approve permission (Accountant-only by default) doubles as "can act on behalf of any employee in this module," consistent with Approve already crossing every employee boundary. When `employeeId` is omitted, behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged (self-service, resolved from `actorId`).
(2) `updateClaimDraft`/`submitClaim`/`cancelClaim` each gain a `hasApprovePermission` input threaded from the router (`ctx.auth.permissions.has('employee_claim.approve')`), computed the same way `hasSelfApprovePermission` already is for `approveClaim`. Their ownership check (`existing.employeeId === actor's own resolved employeeId`) is now skipped entirely when `hasApprovePermission` is true, letting an Accountant manage any claim, including one they raised on behalf of a no-login employee — no separate "is this actually one of my on-behalf-of claims" distinction was added, since Approve is already the module's highest-privilege permission.
(3) `employeeId` is included in `createClaimDraft`'s idempotency `requestHash` so a replayed key with a different target employee correctly conflicts rather than silently returning the wrong claim.
(4) The claim detail page's existing `isOwnClaim`/`canCancel` UI gates (`claim.createdBy === me.userId`) needed no change — an Accountant creating a claim on behalf of someone else is recorded as its own `createdBy`, so the existing creator-based gating already resolves correctly for both the self-approval prompt and the Cancel action.
(5) A new `apps/web` page, `/employee-expenses/new`, mirrors `my/new/page.tsx`'s form exactly with one addition: an Employee picker at the top (reusing `Give Advance`'s reimbursable-employees-only `Select` pattern), gated client-side on `employee_claim.approve` (defense in depth only — the server enforces the real boundary). An "Add Expense" button was added next to "Give Advance" on the admin list page (`/employee-expenses`), both gated by the same permission check already used for "Give Advance". This screen has no prototype counterpart — `expense-add.html` never anticipated an admin acting on someone else's behalf — and is treated as `NEW_REQUIRED` per docs/16, not a correction to an existing screen.
Consequences: `packages/contracts/src/ports/employee-expense.port.ts` — `createClaimDraft` gains `employeeId`/`hasApprovePermission`; `updateClaimDraft`/`submitClaim`/`cancelClaim` gain `hasApprovePermission`. `packages/contracts/src/routers/employee-expenses.ts` — new `withClaimApprovePermission` helper; `employeeId` added to `createClaimDraft`'s zod input. `apps/api/src/employee-expenses/employee-expenses.service.ts` — `createClaimDraft` branches on `input.employeeId` (on-behalf-of path reuses the existing private `assertEmployeeReimbursable` helper verbatim, the same one `issueAdvance` already uses); the three ownership checks in `updateClaimDraft`/`submitClaim`/`cancelClaim` are wrapped in `if (!input.hasApprovePermission)`. `apps/api/src/employee-expenses/employee-expenses.integration-spec.ts` — a new `noLoginEmployeeId` fixture (`Employee` with `userId: null`) and 2 new tests. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/employee-expenses/new/page.tsx`; `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/employee-expenses/page.tsx` gains the "Add Expense" button.
Verified with 2 new tests in `employee-expenses.integration-spec.ts`: an Accountant (holding `employee_claim.approve`) can create, edit, submit, and cancel a claim on behalf of a no-login employee end to end; a plain reimbursable employee (holding only `employee_claim.create`) is rejected `FORBIDDEN` both attempting to create a claim for someone else and attempting to edit/submit a claim the Accountant created on someone else's behalf — 19/19 tests passing in the file (17 prior + 2 new). `turbo run typecheck lint` clean across all 9 workspaces; `turbo run build` clean across all 9 workspaces including `apps/web` (the new `/employee-expenses/new` route registered, and the historically-flaky `/_global-error` prerender issue did not recur this run). Full `apps/api` integration suite: an initial run showed one unrelated failure in `vendor-bills.integration-spec.ts` (a GSTIN uniqueness collision under parallel execution, the same pre-existing cross-file contention class documented in ADR-076); re-running that file alone passed 38/38, and re-running the full suite passed clean at 30/30 files, 382/382 tests — confirmed as the known flake, not a regression from this change (nothing touched here reads or writes `GstRegistration`/vendor-bill fixtures).
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session). The pre-existing cross-file GSTIN-literal test contention (ADR-076) remains unresolved as a latent full-suite flake risk under parallel execution — disclosed again here since it surfaced during this change's own verification, not because this change caused it.
Supersedes: none — additive; extends ADR-081's own `createClaimDraft`/`updateClaimDraft`/`submitClaim`/`cancelClaim` signatures without changing any previously-shipped self-service behavior.
```

```text
ID: ADR-083
Date: 2026-08-24
Status: Accepted — implemented and verified
Context: The user asked that every uploaded document, across every module that attaches documents (Lead, Tender, Project, Invoice, Receipt, Security Instrument, Vendor Bill, Payment Request, Employee Expense Claim — the shared `DocumentsTab` component's own full `entityType` union), show who uploaded it and the full date-and-time, not just a bare date. `DocumentSummary.uploadedBy` already existed but only ever held the uploading user's raw id — never a resolvable name — and the list row rendered `formatDate(link.createdAt)` (date only, no time, and technically the *link* timestamp rather than the document's own `uploadedAt`).
Decision:
(1) `DocumentSummary` gains `uploadedByName: string | null` — resolved only in `listLinksForEntity` (the one place it's actually rendered), via a single batched `User.findMany({ where: { id: { in: [...distinct uploadedBy ids across the page] } } })` plus an in-memory `Map`, not a per-row query. `initiateUpload`/`confirmUpload` pass `null` for this field (their own returned `DocumentSummary` is never rendered in the upload flow — the frontend immediately re-fetches `listLinksForEntity` after linking, which resolves the name correctly). No schema change and no new FK/relation on `Document.uploadedBy` — a raw-id column with an application-level join was judged sufficient and lower-risk than adding a formal relation for a read-only display need.
(2) A missing/deleted uploader (no matching `User` row) resolves to `null`, rendered by the frontend as "Unknown" rather than throwing or omitting the row — the same "degrade gracefully, never crash on stale references" posture used elsewhere in this codebase.
(3) The row now reads `document.uploadedAt` (when the file actually finished uploading) instead of `link.createdAt` (when it was linked to this entity) as the primary timestamp, falling back to `link.createdAt` only if `uploadedAt` is somehow null — in practice every row reaching this list has already passed through `confirmUpload`, so `uploadedAt` is always populated.
(4) A new shared `formatDateTime` helper was added to `apps/web/src/lib/format.ts` (alongside the existing `formatDate`/`formatMoney`) — `en-IN` locale, day/month/year plus hour:minute — rather than reusing the ad hoc `new Date(v).toLocaleString()` calls already scattered in the Audit Log and Users pages, since this is the first *reusable* need for a date-with-time format in this codebase.
Consequences: `packages/contracts/src/ports/document.port.ts` — `uploadedByName` added to `DocumentSummary`. `apps/api/src/documents/documents.service.ts` — `toDocumentSummary` takes an optional second `uploadedByName` parameter (defaulting to `null`); `listLinksForEntity` batch-resolves and passes it. `apps/api/src/documents/documents.integration-spec.ts` — 1 new test (resolves a real uploader's `displayName`; confirms the existing tests' random, userless `actorId` correctly degrades to `null`). `apps/web/src/lib/format.ts` — new `formatDateTime`. `apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx` — the per-document row now reads "Uploaded by {name} · {date, time}" instead of a bare date. This is a single shared component, so the change applies identically to every module that embeds `DocumentsTab` with no per-module work required.
Verified with 1 new test in `documents.integration-spec.ts` (13/13 passing in the file); `turbo run typecheck lint build` clean across all 9 workspaces; full `apps/api` integration suite passed clean on a first run (30/30 files, 383/383 tests).
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session).
Supersedes: none — additive, cross-cutting UI/read-model change to the existing Documents feature; no prior ADR's decision is reopened.
```

```text
ID: ADR-084
Date: 2026-08-24
Status: Accepted — implemented and verified
Context: The user asked for the full filter set on `/employee-expenses` (Employee, Category, Project, expense-date from/to, Status, approved-date from/to). `EmployeeExpenseClaimFilters` (ADR-081) already declared `employeeId`/`expenseCategoryId`/`projectId`/`status` and `listClaims` already applied them server-side, but the page only ever wired a Status dropdown — the other three filters had no UI. Date-range filtering (expense date and approved date) didn't exist on either side at all.
Decision:
(1) Two new filter fields, `expenseDateFrom`/`expenseDateTo`, map directly onto `EmployeeExpenseClaim.expenseDate` (a plain `@db.Date` column) using the same `gte`/`lte`-with-`toDate()` shape already established for `JournalEntry.accountingDate` filtering — no time-boundary handling needed since the column itself has no time component.
(2) Two more, `approvedDateFrom`/`approvedDateTo`, filter on `EmployeeExpenseClaimDecision.decidedAt` where `decision = 'APPROVED'` via a `decisions: { some: { ... } }` relation filter — there is no `approvedAt` column on the claim itself, only its append-only decision history (by design, per ADR-081). Since `decidedAt` is a `@db.Timestamptz`, this reuses the existing end-of-day-boundary pattern from `AuditService` (`gte: T00:00:00.000Z`, `lte: T23:59:59.999Z`) rather than the plain-date pattern used for (1).
(3) The frontend reuses the exact `RangePicker` + `dayjs` + `[string, string] | null` pattern already established on the Journal Entries list page for its own date-range filter, rather than inventing a new one — the first time this pattern is reused by a second module.
(4) Employee/Category/Project filter dropdowns list every employee/category (`includeInactive: true`), not just active/reimbursable ones — a filter needs to find a historical claim tied to a since-deactivated employee or category, unlike a create form's own picker, which correctly restricts to what's currently usable.
```
