```text
Consequences: `packages/contracts/src/ports/employee-expense.port.ts` — `EmployeeExpenseClaimFilters` gains 4 fields. `packages/contracts/src/routers/employee-expenses.ts` — `listClaims`'s zod filter schema gains the matching 4 `dateStringSchema` fields. `apps/api/src/employee-expenses/employee-expenses.service.ts` — `listClaims`'s `where` construction gains the two range blocks. `apps/api/src/employee-expenses/employee-expenses.integration-spec.ts` — 1 new test exercising all seven filter dimensions (employee, category, project, status, expense-date range in/out, approved-date range in/out) against one real approved claim. `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/employee-expenses/page.tsx` — full `FilterCard` rebuilt with Employee/Category/Project/Status selects and two `RangePicker`s.
Verified with 1 new test in `employee-expenses.integration-spec.ts` (20/20 passing in the file); `turbo run typecheck lint build` clean across all 9 workspaces; full `apps/api` integration suite passed clean on a first run (30/30 files, 384/384 tests).
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session).
Supersedes: none — additive; extends ADR-081's own `EmployeeExpenseClaimFilters`/`listClaims` shape, no prior decision reopened.
```

```text
ID: ADR-085
Date: 2026-08-24
Status: Accepted — implemented and verified
Context: The user asked for the 4-tile summary row shown on `expense-employee.html` (Pending Approval / Approved — To Pay / Reimbursed (FY) / Advance Outstanding, matching the prototype's own colors, labels, and copy exactly) and for the tiles to react to the page's own filter bar (ADR-084). Two real design forks needed resolving before implementation, both confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: whether the tiles narrow with Employee/Project/Category/date filters or always show org-wide totals (narrow, chosen); and whether an explicit Expense/Approved Date filter overrides the "(FY)" default on the Reimbursed tile or the tile always stays pinned to the current financial year (override, chosen).
Decision:
(1) New `getClaimStats` port method/router procedure/service method, gated by `employee_claim.read` (the same permission that gates the page itself). Its filter shape, `EmployeeExpenseClaimStatsFilters`, is `EmployeeExpenseClaimFilters` minus `status` — each tile already represents one fixed status (or, for Advance Outstanding, none at all), so a status filter would be self-contradictory.
(2) Pending Approval / Approved — To Pay are `EmployeeExpenseClaim.aggregate()` sums+counts filtered by `status = SUBMITTED`/`APPROVED` plus employee/category/project/expenseDate (both tiles) and the approved-date filter (Approved-To-Pay only, via the same `decisions.some(APPROVED, decidedAt in range)` shape `listClaims` already uses — Pending Approval has no approval decision yet, so an approved-date filter is meaningless there and is not applied).
(3) Reimbursed defaults to the org's current financial year (`FinancialYear.isCurrent`) when neither `expenseDate*` nor `approvedDate*` is supplied — an `isCurrentFinancialYear: true` flag on the response tells the UI to show the "(FY)" qualifier. Supplying either date filter narrows the tile the same way it narrows `listClaims` instead, and the flag flips `false` so the UI drops the qualifier — resolving the second confirmed fork. A disclosed approximation: `FinancialYear.isCurrent` is scoped per legal entity, not per org, so an org with more than one legal entity could have more than one "current" FY; this takes the widest bounding range across all of them rather than resolving per claim's own legal entity, correct for the common single-legal-entity org and an acceptable approximation otherwise.
(4) Advance Outstanding is a current point-in-time `EMPLOYEE_ADVANCE_RECEIVABLE` control-account balance, not a historical-range figure — it sums `JournalLine.debitAmount - creditAmount` grouped by `employeeId` (narrowed by `employeeId`/`projectId`, both real dimensions already carried on that control account's own journal lines per ADR-081's posting design), counting only employees left with a POSITIVE balance (still holding unspent advance) toward `employeeCount` — `expenseCategoryId` and the date filters do not apply to a running balance and are silently ignored for this one tile, disclosed rather than raising an error.
(5) Frontend tiles reuse the exact `border-t-{warning,information,positive,negative}` + `shadow-ui` tile-card pattern already established on the My Expenses statement page (itself corrected earlier this session from an invalid short-form-class mistake) — the first time this pattern is reused by a second screen.
Consequences: `packages/contracts/src/ports/employee-expense.port.ts` — new `EmployeeExpenseClaimStatsFilters`/`EmployeeExpenseClaimStats` types and `getClaimStats` port method; re-exported from `packages/contracts/src/{ports/index.ts, index.ts}`. `packages/contracts/src/routers/employee-expenses.ts` — new `getClaimStats` procedure. `apps/api/src/employee-expenses/employee-expenses.service.ts` — new `getClaimStats` method. `apps/api/src/employee-expenses/employee-expenses.integration-spec.ts` — 1 new test covering all four tiles end to end (a dedicated employee fixture with a pending, an approved-not-reimbursed, and an approved-then-reimbursed claim, plus an advance, verifying the exact 4100.00 outstanding balance after approve/reimburse postings net out) plus the date-filter-overrides-FY and employeeId-narrowing behaviors. `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/employee-expenses/page.tsx` — new 4-tile row wired to `getClaimStats` with the current `appliedFilters` (minus `status`).
Verified with 1 new test in `employee-expenses.integration-spec.ts` (21/21 passing in the file); `turbo run typecheck lint build` clean across all 9 workspaces; full `apps/api` integration suite passed clean on a first run (30/30 files, 385/385 tests).
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session). The multi-legal-entity current-FY approximation (point 3) is a disclosed, not silently dropped, limitation.
Supersedes: none — additive; extends ADR-081's own module and ADR-084's own filter shape, no prior decision reopened.
```

```text
ID: ADR-086
Date: 2026-08-24
Status: Accepted — implemented and verified
Context: The user asked whether a "project expenses" tab was ever planned. It was: docs/16-html-module-reference.md names `project-expense.html` as part of the Core Project bundle (`MINOR_CHANGE`), but it had never been scheduled in docs/11 or built. The prototype's own footer note names six cost categories it rolls up (Purchase, Employee Contribution, Server, Subscription, Reimbursement, Introducer) — only two have a real, postable backing source in this codebase today (Purchase via Vendor Bill, Reimbursement via Employee Expense Claim); Employee Contribution needs Salary import, Server/Subscription need their own recurring-cycle modules, and Introducer needs a payout concept — none exist yet. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: build the tab now scoped to the two existing categories, disclosing the other four as deferred rather than waiting or over-scoping a fresh plan-mode pass.
Decision:
(1) New `ProjectsService.getExpenseLedger`, added to the existing `ProjectPort` (not a new port) since this is fundamentally a project-scoped read, exactly like `listProgress`/`listTeamMembers`. It reuses `getDetail`'s own project-access scope check first (`canSeeAll`/PM-or-team-member, throws `NOT_FOUND` otherwise) — the same pattern `listTeamMembers`/`listProgress` already established — rather than re-deriving project-level authorization from scratch.
(2) Purchase rows are `VendorBill` rows with `status = 'FINALIZED'` (the only status with a posted Dr Expense journal); Reimbursement rows are `EmployeeExpenseClaim` rows with `status IN (APPROVED, REIMBURSED)` (same reasoning — approval is what posts the expense-recognition journal per ADR-081, reimbursement only settles cash afterward). Both are queried directly via Prisma inside `ProjectsService` rather than by injecting `VendorBillsService`/`EmployeeExpensesService` — `VendorBillsService` already injects `ProjectsService` for its own project-scoping, so the reverse direction would create a circular module dependency; `EmployeeExpensesService` has no such dependency but was queried directly too, for consistency with the Purchase side and because no project-scoping logic needs reusing (ADR-081 already established this module has none).
(3) `hasVendorBillReadPermission`/`hasEmployeeClaimReadPermission` gate each category independently and completely — a category the actor can't read is excluded from `rows` AND from `totalAmount`/`categoryTotals`/`largestCategory`, not merely hidden in the row list, so a viewer never sees an aggregate figure derived partly from data they can't see broken down.
(4) Every total, percentage, and comparison (`totalAmount`, `categoryTotals`, `largestCategory`, `largestCategoryPercent`) is computed server-side with `Prisma.Decimal`, never client-side — per CLAUDE.md's "never calculate accounting totals independently in React." Category-chip and date-range filtering therefore re-query the server on every change (`useQuery` reacting to input changes) rather than filtering an already-fetched row set in the browser, since the footer "Total (filtered)" figure must also come from the server for whatever filter is currently applied.
(5) The tile row drops the prototype's fourth "vs Received" margin tile — computing a margin percentage from only 2 of 6 real cost categories would understate true cost and overstate the margin, a misleading number worse than omitting it; the info note at the tab's own bottom discloses the four deferred categories explicitly instead of showing them as always-empty chips reading ₹0.
Consequences: `packages/contracts/src/ports/project.port.ts` — new `ProjectExpenseCategory`/`ProjectExpenseRow`/`ProjectExpenseCategoryTotal`/`ProjectExpenseLedger` types and `getExpenseLedger` method on `ProjectPort`; re-exported from `packages/contracts/src/{ports/index.ts, index.ts}`. `packages/contracts/src/routers/projects.ts` — new `getExpenseLedger` procedure (`project.read`), computing `hasVendorBillReadPermission`/`hasEmployeeClaimReadPermission` inline from `ctx.auth.permissions`, matching every other permission-flag-threaded procedure in this codebase. `apps/api/src/projects/projects.service.ts` — new `getExpenseLedger` method. `apps/api/src/projects/projects.integration-spec.ts` — a new, fully self-contained fixture (own organization, GST registration, financial year, vendor, ledger accounts, categories, bank account, reimbursable employee) and 5 new tests. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/expense-tab.tsx`; `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/page.tsx` gains the "Expense" tab between "Payment" and "Physical Progress".
Verified with 5 new tests in `projects.integration-spec.ts` (33/33 passing in the file) — the full rollup with hand-computed Decimal totals (₹1,18,000 Purchase + ₹8,000 Reimbursement = ₹1,26,000, largest category and percent correct), a DRAFT claim and a SUBMITTED (not yet finalized) bill both correctly excluded, permission-exclusion (missing `employee_claim.read` drops Reimbursement from rows AND totals, not just the row list), explicit category-filter narrowing, date-range narrowing, and project-access denial (`NOT_FOUND`) for an actor who cannot see the project at all. `turbo run typecheck lint build` clean across all 9 workspaces (one real `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` fix needed along the way — an AntD `Tag`'s `color` prop cannot be passed `undefined` explicitly under this project's strict tsconfig; fixed by using the literal `'default'` color instead of `undefined` for the unselected chip state). Full `apps/api` integration suite passed clean on a first run (30/30 files, 390/390 tests).
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session). Employee Contribution, Server, Subscription, and Introducer costs remain explicitly deferred, not silently dropped, until their own source modules exist.
Supersedes: none — additive; a new project detail tab, no prior ADR's decision is reopened.
```

```text
ID: ADR-087
Date: 2026-08-24
Status: Accepted — implemented and fully verified
Context: With Employee Advance & Expense Claim (Slice 6.6, ADR-081) and its three follow-ups shipped, the last unbuilt Phase 6 vertical is Direct Expense — the other branch of the shared `expense-add.html` prototype, deliberately deferred out of 6.6 since it has zero dependency on the employee-wallet concept that slice built. docs/04's own screen contract is explicit: *"Preserve layout but explicitly choose Direct Company Expense or Employee Claim. Commands `directExpense.create/post` or `expenseClaim.create/submit`; permissions are separate. Posted direct expense creates Expense/Input GST debit and Bank/Cash/Card credit. A claim waits for approval/settlement."* docs/06's own posting-rules table names it directly: "Direct expense paid immediately" — `Dr Expense and eligible Input GST / Cr Bank/Cash/Card Payable`. docs/06 also names this as the eventual settlement mechanism for the still-unbuilt Subscription/Server cycles ("Actual vendor bill or expense: standard vendor bill/direct expense rule"), so this slice lays groundwork for that one too. Two scope forks were confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before planning: no maker-checker (a single `direct_expense.post` holder creates a DRAFT and posts it, back to back if they choose — docs/04's own wording explicitly contrasts this against a Claim, which "waits for approval/settlement" — a genuine first for a slice moving real cash with zero upstream approval gate at all, unlike Vendor Payment's own no-maker-checker precedent which still relied on Payment Request's prior approval); and no Lead as a link target (Project or company overhead only, mirroring ADR-081's own Employee Expense Claim simplification).
Decision:
(1) New `DirectExpense` model (`DirectExpenseStatus { DRAFT POSTED CANCELLED REVERSED }`) — no decision table, since there is no maker-checker and nothing needs an append-only decision history beyond the standard audit log. Combines EmployeeExpenseClaim's own DRAFT/CANCELLED-with-creator-only-ownership shape with EmployeeAdvance's own paymentModeId/bankAccountId/journalEntryId/reversal shape, since Direct Expense needs both a short-lived draft-and-cancel lifecycle and an immediate bank-account-crediting posting step.
(2) `post()` is the sole authoritative action (`DRAFT → POSTED`) and is deliberately **not** creator-restricted — any `direct_expense.post` holder may post any DRAFT, including one a colleague created, since there is no maker-checker to enforce a same-actor-only or different-actor-only rule either way. `updateDraft`/`cancelDraft`, by contrast, **are** creator-only (ownership check against `createdBy`), mirroring `VendorBillsService`'s own DRAFT-stage mutation scoping — accountability for editing/withdrawing a draft stays with whoever created it, even though anyone on the Accountant team may finalize it.
(3) The posting journal reuses `INPUT_GST_RECEIVABLE_EXPENSE` — the same account ADR-081 created for Employee Expense Claim's own flat GST credit — rather than seeding a near-duplicate account, since both represent "flat GST credit on a miscellaneous, non-PO expense." The expense-side account resolves via `ExpenseCategory.ledgerAccountId`, falling back to the existing seeded `OTHER_EXPENSES` account, identical to Vendor Bill and Employee Expense Claim's own fallback rule.
(4) No project-scoping anywhere in this module, mirroring ADR-081's own Employee Expense Claim precedent — Direct Expense has no employee-vs-admin visibility split; it's purely an Accountant/Admin-facing ledger of company-paid bills.
(5) Four new permissions (`direct_expense.{read,create,post,reverse}`) — Accountant gets all four (owns money movement, same boundary as Vendor Bill/Vendor Payment); Project Manager/Admin Staff/CEO get `read` only; Employee gets none at all, a stricter boundary than Employee Expense Claim's own `employee_claim.create` grant, since Direct Expense records a company-paid bill, not a personal reimbursement — there is no self-service angle to grant.
Consequences: `packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma` — new `DirectExpenseStatus` enum and `DirectExpense` model, plus back-relations on `LegalEntity`/`ExpenseCategory`/`Project`/`PaymentMode`/`BankAccount`/`JournalEntry`. New migration `20260824120000_phase6_07_direct_expense` (hand-curated from `prisma migrate diff`, the same three pre-existing phantom lines excluded as every prior migration). `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` — 4 permissions + role grants (no new ledger account, reuses `OTHER_EXPENSES`/`INPUT_GST_RECEIVABLE_EXPENSE`), a `DIRECT_EXPENSE` `EntityType` row; permission catalog count corrected 98 → 102. New `apps/api/src/direct-expenses/{direct-expenses.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}`, wired into `app.module.ts`/`main.ts`. New `packages/contracts/src/{ports/direct-expense.port.ts, routers/direct-expenses.ts}`, wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/direct-expenses/{shared.tsx, page.tsx, new/page.tsx, [id]/page.tsx}`. `apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx` gains the `DIRECT_EXPENSE` entity type. `packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts}` — new `DirectExpenseIcon`; the "Employee Expenses" nav group is renamed to **"Expenses"** and gains a "Direct Expenses" item alongside "My Expenses"/"Employee Reimbursements".
Verified with 11 new tests in `direct-expenses.integration-spec.ts` — draft create/update/cancel and the mandatory-reason check; creator-only ownership rejecting a different actor from updating/cancelling someone else's draft; three GST-validation rejections (negative, exceeds amount, credit without an invoice); an inactive expense category and an inactive bank account both rejected; `post`'s journal shape for a plain expense (2 lines) and one with a non-zero flat GST credit (3 lines, split into its own Input GST Receivable line); confirming `post` is genuinely not creator-restricted (a different actor successfully posts someone else's draft); idempotent replay on `post` plus a reused-key-different-request rejection; rejecting a re-post of an already-posted expense; `reverse`'s journal shape and a stale-version-conflict rejection; list/getDetail filtering and cross-org isolation. All 11 passing on a clean first run. `turbo run typecheck lint build` clean across all 9 workspaces (23/23 tasks). A fresh-database migration replay on a disposable scratch database confirmed the full history — 43 migrations, including this slice's new one — applies cleanly from zero, with `prisma migrate diff` showing only the same three pre-existing hand-added constraints every prior migration has excluded. The full `apps/api` integration suite passed clean on its first, unmodified run: 31/31 files, 401/401 tests.
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session). This closes docs/11's own Phase 6 deliverable list except Subscription/Server cycles/reminders, the one remaining named item before Phase 7 begins.
Supersedes: none — additive; a new Phase 6 slice, no prior ADR's decision is reopened or contradicted.
```

```text
ID: ADR-088
Date: 2026-08-25
Status: Accepted — implemented and fully verified
Context: The last unbuilt Phase 6 deliverable — Subscription & Server recurring cycles. A first plan (one shared `RecurringCycle` engine, both reusing Vendor/Party, Record Payment always creating a Direct Expense) was rejected in a 23-point review before any code was written. The review kept the core shape but required: a real FK pair (`subscriptionId`/`serverId` + a DB CHECK) instead of an unconstrained polymorphic `sourceType`/`sourceId`, mirroring `PaymentAlert`'s own precedent; explicit `periodStart`/`periodEnd`/`dueDate` instead of a free-text label; arbitrary-day calendar-safe stepping (reusing `addMonthsClamped`, Phase 4.2's OPEX engine, ADR-063) instead of restricting billing days to 1–28; automatic rolling-horizon generation via a worker job, not a manual "Generate Next Year" click; a full parent lifecycle (Pause/Resume/Cancel, each its own command with a mandatory reason on Cancel) and cycle lifecycle (`PENDING/PAID/SKIPPED/CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED`) instead of `PENDING/PAID` alone; a recurrence-change preview/apply flow that only ever supersedes future-pending cycles, never touching paid or past-due ones; one atomic-in-effect payment-recording operation instead of an unsafe `createDraft → post → update cycle` sequence; an append-only `RecurringCyclePayment` link table instead of overwriting a single `directExpenseId` column, so a later-reversed payment's history is never lost; a real GST workflow instead of hardcoding `gstInvoiceAvailable: false`; an explicit TDS decision instead of silently ignoring it; a bounded Server "charge to project or company overhead" selection instead of an implicit split across a server's multiple associated projects; and a Debit-vs-Credit-Card posting distinction (a new `CardAccount` master) instead of treating a card as a cosmetic label on a bank-sourced payment. Two follow-up questions were then resolved directly, both reopening deliberate simplifications the just-shipped Direct Expense (ADR-087) had made: **GST** — extend Direct Expense with a full CGST/SGST/IGST engine now, not the flat `gstCreditAmount` it shipped with; **TDS** — extend Direct Expense's own posting to support optional TDS now, not leave it out of scope. Both answers made the Direct Expense extension itself (Part A below) a genuine prerequisite for the recurring-cycle engine (Part B), since every cycle payment posts through Direct Expense.
Decision:
Part A — Direct Expense GST/TDS/Card extension (prerequisite):
(1) `gstCreditAmount` is replaced by `taxableAmount` + `gstRatePercent` + `supplyType` (manually selected — Direct Expense has no vendor/GST-registration pair to derive `sameState` from the way Vendor Bill does) + computed `cgstAmount`/`sgstAmount`/`igstAmount`, frozen at `createDraft`/`updateDraft` time using the same split math as `computeLines` (`invoices.service.ts`) and never recomputed at `post()`, mirroring Vendor Bill's freeze-at-draft/sum-at-approve pattern. `amount` (the grand total) is now computed (`taxableAmount + cgst + sgst + igst`), never directly entered, per CLAUDE.md's own money-arithmetic rule. A new `itcEligible` flag gates whether the computed GST posts to `INPUT_CGST_RECEIVABLE`/`INPUT_SGST_RECEIVABLE`/`INPUT_IGST_RECEIVABLE` (the same accounts Vendor Bill already resolves — no new ledger accounts needed) or folds into the expense debit, mirroring Vendor Bill's own per-line ineligible-ITC behaviour.
(2) New `tdsAmount`/`tdsRatePercent` (the latter display-only, never driving the posted amount, mirroring `VendorPaymentDeduction.ratePercent`) post a flat TDS deduction to the existing `TDS_PAYABLE` account Vendor Payment already uses — one flat amount on the row, not a typed per-allocation table, since Direct Expense has no allocations to attach deductions to.
(3) A new minimal `CardAccount` master (`CardType { DEBIT_CARD CREDIT_CARD }`) lets Direct Expense post a Debit Card payment through its linked `BankAccount` (unchanged shape) and a Credit Card payment to the card's own dedicated payable ledger account instead of incorrectly reducing Bank immediately — `Dr Expense/GST / Cr Credit Card Payable`, settled later via a new small `CardSettlementsService` (`Dr Credit Card Payable / Cr Bank`, capped at the card's live outstanding balance computed from its own ledger's journal lines, never a stored running total). `DirectExpense.bankAccountId` is now nullable (required only when not paying via a Credit Card); a new `cardAccountId` is set for both card types. Only a masked last-four digit string is ever stored — no full card number/CVV/PIN/OTP/banking credential column exists anywhere in the schema. CardAccount CRUD reuses `legal_entity.read`/`legal_entity.manage` (bundled into `LegalEntityService`, same boundary as `BankAccount` — the same class of financial-infrastructure master, not a workflow entity of its own); settling a bill gets its own narrow `card_account.settle` permission, same reasoning as `direct_expense.post` being separate from create. Two new `PaymentMode` rows (`DEBIT_CARD`/`CREDIT_CARD`) and a new `requiresCardSource` flag — deliberately distinct from `requiresBankSource`, since every other consumer of that flag (`ClientReceipt`/`VendorPayment`/`EmployeeAdvance`) has no card-aware logic and must keep asking for a raw `BankAccount`.
(4) A new optional `DirectExpense.vendorId` (a small, low-risk addition flagged and approved alongside the rest of this plan) lets a cycle-originated expense carry its Subscription/Server's own vendor link directly, queryable without joining back through the cycle.
Part B — the shared recurring-cycle engine:
(5) `RecurringCycle` carries real `subscriptionId`/`serverId` FKs plus `sourceType`, with `chk_recurring_cycles_source_matches_type` requiring exactly one, and `uq_recurring_cycles_{subscription,server}_period_start` (Postgres treats NULLs as distinct, so each index only constrains its own source type) making generation idempotent under concurrent worker/manual execution without any application-level locking. `periodStart`/`periodEnd`/`dueDate` are real columns; no `periodLabel` column exists — display labels are derived from these plus `frequency` on read only. A `sequenceIndex` column (an internal bookkeeping detail, not user-facing) makes "which cycle comes next" unambiguous; `ensureHorizon` itself anchors continuation to the **last existing cycle's actual `dueDate`**, not `sequenceIndex`, so it stays correct even after a recurrence change re-numbers the sequence from zero.
(6) Recurrence configuration is just `firstDueDate` + `frequency` + `startDate` + optional `endDate` — no `billingDayOfMonth`/`billingDayOfWeek`/`billingAnnualDate`. Monthly/Quarterly/Yearly stepping reuses `addMonthsClamped` directly (imported, not reimplemented) from the OPEX engine (`milestone-schedule.util.ts`); Weekly steps in plain `+7`-day increments via a newly-exported `addDaysUTC` from the same file. Server rejects `WEEKLY` both at the zod/service layer and via a DB CHECK (`chk_servers_frequency_not_weekly`) as a bypass backstop.
(7) `ensureHorizon` generates a rolling 12-month horizon (or sooner, if the parent has an `endDate`) — called synchronously once at parent creation, by a new worker job (`apps/worker/src/recurring-cycle-horizon.ts`, ticking every 6 hours plus once on boot, mirroring `payment-alert-opener.ts`'s exact `JobRun`-wrapped shape; its own pure date-stepping logic is reimplemented rather than imported from `apps/api`, same reasoning `payment-alert-opener.ts` itself already documents — the worker has no dependency on `apps/api`'s module graph), and by an explicit `refreshUpcomingCycles`-equivalent path as a recovery/convenience action only, never the primary mechanism. Paused/cancelled parents are skipped entirely; the job produces no journals or expenses.
(8) Explicit `pause`/`resume`/`cancel` parent-lifecycle commands (not folded into a generic `update`), each `expectedVersion`-guarded with its own audit event; `cancel` requires a mandatory reason and marks future (`dueDate > effectiveDate`) `PENDING` cycles `CANCELLED`, leaving paid and past-due-pending cycles untouched. A per-cycle `skipCycle` (mandatory reason) is how a past-due pending cycle gets explicitly resolved without a payment. `RecurringCycleStatus` gained `SKIPPED`/`CANCELLED`/`SUPERSEDED` alongside `PENDING`/`PAID`; "Upcoming"/"Due"/"Overdue" are derived at read time from `dueDate` vs. today, never stored.
(9) `previewRecurrenceChange` (read-only) diffs a frequency/`firstDueDate`/`endDate` change against existing future-pending (`dueDate > today`) cycles only; `applyRecurrenceChange` transactionally marks the diffed cycles `SUPERSEDED` (row kept, never physically deleted) and inserts the freshly-generated replacement set, in one audit event recording both the removed and generated due dates.
(10) `recordCyclePayment` is DirectExpensesService's own new `createAndPostDraft` (draft-create + journal-post + status-flip fused into one call) followed by a link-and-flip step that inserts an append-only `RecurringCyclePayment` row and marks the cycle `PAID` — plus its own top-level idempotency-key guard (checked *before* the "must be PENDING" state check, so a legitimate retry after the cycle has already flipped to PAID is not rejected; this exact bug was caught and fixed by the new integration suite). `reverseCyclePayment` reverses the linked Direct Expense and flips the cycle back to `PENDING` without ever overwriting or deleting the original `RecurringCyclePayment` row — a corrected payment produces a new row, and "current payment" is derived as the latest row whose linked expense isn't `REVERSED`. Every cycle-scoped action (`skipCycle`/`recordCyclePayment`/`reverseCyclePayment`) validates the cycle's *actual* stored `sourceType` against the caller's own router context, closing a permission-boundary gap where a role holding only `subscription.*` could otherwise act on a Server's cycle via a crafted `cycleId`.
(11) Server's multi-project association (`ServerProject`, a pure usage/visibility tag, mirroring `ProjectTeamMember`'s exact shape) never implicitly splits a cycle's cost — `recordCyclePayment` requires the caller to select exactly one "charge to" project (validated as one of the server's own joined projects) or leave it unset for company overhead, which becomes the resulting Direct Expense's own `projectId` directly (no new field needed). Every joined project must share the Server's own `legalEntityId` (service-enforced). `Subscription.legalEntityId` derives from its optional `projectId` (same `resolveLegalEntityId` shape as Direct Expense); `Server.legalEntityId` is always explicit, since a server can serve projects potentially spanning legal entities.
(12) 14 new permissions (`subscription.{read,create,update,pause,resume,cancel,record_payment}`, `server.{...same...}`) reuse the existing `project.read_all`-derived `canSeeAll` scoping pattern (`assertProjectAccessible`/`ProjectsService.list`, exactly as Vendor Bill already does) rather than inventing new per-module `*.read_all` keys — Accountant/Admin/CEO/Admin Staff all already hold `project.read_all` and see everything unscoped; Project Manager is the one system role that doesn't, and is genuinely scoped to subscriptions/servers touching its own assigned projects.
Consequences: `packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma` — `DirectExpenseSupplyType`/`CardType` enums, `DirectExpense` GST/TDS/Card/vendor field changes, new `CardAccount`/`CardSettlement` models (migration `20260824130000_phase6_08a_direct_expense_gst_tds_card`); `RecurringFrequency`/`RecurringParentStatus`/`RecurringCycleStatus`/`RecurringCycleSourceType` enums and `Subscription`/`Server`/`ServerProject`/`RecurringCycle`/`RecurringCyclePayment` models (migration `20260825090000_phase6_08b_subscription_server_recurring_cycles`) — both hand-curated from `prisma migrate diff`, the same three pre-existing phantom lines excluded as every prior migration. `seed.ts` — `card_account.settle` + 14 subscription/server permissions and role grants, `DEBIT_CARD`/`CREDIT_CARD` payment modes, a `CREDIT_CARD_PAYABLE` account group, `SUBSCRIPTION`/`SERVER` entity types; permission catalog count corrected 102 → 117. Rewrote `apps/api/src/direct-expenses/direct-expenses.service.ts` in place; new `apps/api/src/card-accounts/card-settlements.service.ts` and `apps/api/src/subscriptions/{recurring-cycle-schedule.util.ts, recurring-cycles.service.ts, subscriptions.service.ts, servers.service.ts}`, all wired into `app.module.ts`/`main.ts`. New `apps/worker/src/recurring-cycle-horizon.ts`, wired into `apps/worker/src/main.ts`. New/updated contracts: `packages/contracts/src/ports/{direct-expense.port.ts (rewritten), legal-entity.port.ts (CardAccount), card-settlement.port.ts, subscription.port.ts}` and `packages/contracts/src/routers/{direct-expenses.ts (rewritten), legal-entities.ts (CardAccount), card-settlements.ts, subscriptions.ts, servers.ts}`, wired into `context.ts`/`router.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`. Frontend: rewrote `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/direct-expenses/{shared.tsx, new/page.tsx, [id]/page.tsx}` around a new shared `form-fields.tsx`; new `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/card-accounts/page.tsx`; new `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/{subscriptions,servers}/{shared.tsx, page.tsx, new/page.tsx, [id]/page.tsx}` sharing a new `apps/web/src/components/record-cycle-payment-fields.tsx`. `documents-tab.tsx` gains `SUBSCRIPTION`/`SERVER`. `packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts}` — new `CardAccountIcon`/`SubscriptionIcon`/`ServerIcon`, three new nav items under "Organization"/"Expenses".
Verified with: 23 tests in the rewritten `direct-expenses.integration-spec.ts` (intrastate/interstate GST split, ITC-ineligible folding, TDS posting, Debit/Credit Card posting, `createAndPostDraft`, plus the full pre-existing lifecycle/reversal/isolation suite); 16 tests in `organization.integration-spec.ts`'s new Card Accounts block; 9 tests in a new `card-settlements.integration-spec.ts` (outstanding-balance computation, settlement posting, capping, idempotency, cross-org isolation); 10 unit tests in a new `recurring-cycle-schedule.util.spec.ts` covering every calendar case the review named by name (17-Aug Monthly, 24-Nov Yearly, 31-Jan Monthly → 28-Feb → 31-Mar anchor restoration, 29-Feb-2028 Yearly leap boundary, Weekly, Quarterly, period continuity); 18 tests in a new `subscriptions.integration-spec.ts` (horizon auto-generation, cross-legal-entity rejections, idempotent `ensureHorizon`, mandatory-reason skip/cancel, the cross-`sourceType` permission-boundary rejection, pause/resume, cancel's past-due-vs-future boundary, recurrence preview/apply, atomic payment recording and its own idempotent-replay bug catch-and-fix, reversal-then-correction history, and Server's charge-to-project/overhead selection); 4 tests in a new worker `recurring-cycle-horizon.integration-spec.ts` (rolling generation, idempotency, paused/cancelled skip, no journals produced). `turbo run typecheck lint build` clean across all 9 workspaces both after Part A and after Part B. Two independent fresh-database migration replays on disposable scratch databases (once after each Part) confirmed the full history — 45 migrations total — applies cleanly from zero, `prisma migrate diff` showing only the same three pre-existing excluded lines both times. The full `apps/api` integration suite passed clean on every re-run: 445/445 tests across 33 files after Part B (including the corrected 117-permission catalog count); the full `apps/worker` integration suite passed 19/19 across 3 files; all cross-package unit tests (`npm run test`) passed 14/14 tasks.
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session). Explicitly deferred, disclosed rather than silently dropped: automated credit-card statement import/reconciliation and due-date reminders (the settlement *action* itself shipped, since Direct Expense cannot post a Credit Card payment correctly without it); a spend-by-company dashboard; new provider masters; implicit/automatic project-cost splitting for Server; bulk historical (backdated) cycle generation. This closes docs/11's own Phase 6 deliverable list in full.
Supersedes: partially reopens ADR-087's own flat `gstCreditAmount` GST model and its explicit no-TDS scope — both were deliberate, disclosed simplifications at the time, reopened here by direct user decision once Subscription/Server made a fuller engine a genuine prerequisite. `DirectExpense.bankAccountId`'s move from required to nullable and the removal of `gstCreditAmount` are the only breaking shape changes to that already-shipped model; no other prior ADR's decision is reopened.
```

```text
ID: ADR-089
Date: 2026-08-25
Status: Accepted — implemented and fully verified
Context: CEO/Admin had no way to see money at a glance on the Projects module — the list showed only `Value` (contract amount) and Project Detail's Expense tab had no invoiced/settled/outstanding figures. The Client detail page's reference HTML has a "Receivable" stat tile that was deliberately dropped when that page was built (docs/13-progress.md's own entry: "would require a new cross-module invoice/receipt outstanding-amount aggregation, out of scope... flagged here rather than silently omitted"). User request: project-wise and client-wise financial stats, colored, on the Projects list (per row) and a Project Detail tab, with every amount computed server-side. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: extend the expense ledger to a 3rd category (Direct Expense); omit Profit/Margin entirely (ADR-086 precedent); build a new "Financials" tab additive to "Expense"; gate everything behind a new dedicated `project.read_financials` permission, not reused read grants. The first plan was rejected in a detailed 7-section correctness review (the third such review this codebase has now seen, after ADR-078 and ADR-088 — see `feedback_detailed_plan_rejection_response` memory) that caught: `project.value` and invoice totals use different GST bases; "Settled" (cash + non-cash deductions) was being conflated with "Received"; Expense would have been computed from gross document totals instead of excluding recoverable Input GST; the Expense tab and the new tab would show different, unreconciled numbers under similar labels; and a bulk aggregation method exposed too loosely (an IDOR-style risk via arbitrary project ids). This ADR reflects the fully revised design. Client-wide aggregation (closing the Client-page "Receivable" gap) is explicitly deferred as an immediate follow-up, not part of this slice.
Decision:
(1) Five figure groups returned by a new `ProjectFinancialSummary`, all Decimal strings, all reusing existing frozen/posted fields — no new aggregation state anywhere: **Contract Value** (`Project.value`, labeled "Contract Value (Excl. GST)" since docs/06's own posting rule shows CAPEX/OPEX revenue posts at taxable value while invoice totals are GST-inclusive — confirmed via `InvoicesService.getDraftDefaults` setting `suggestedTaxableAmount` directly from `MilestoneEvent.amount`); **Billing** (`grossInvoiceValue` = Σ FINALIZED `Invoice.totalAmount`, `debitNoteAdjustments`/`creditNoteAdjustments` = Σ FINALIZED `CreditDebitNote.totalAmount` by type — the note carries its own `projectId` directly, no join needed — `netBilled` = gross + debit − credit); **Collections** (`cashReceived` = Σ `ReceiptAllocation.cashAllocated`, a column stored separately from `settledAmount`; each `AllocationDeductionType` enum value mapped to its own named bucket — `tdsDeducted`, `gstTdsDeducted` (CGST_TDS+SGST_TDS+IGST_TDS combined), `retentionHeld`, `otherHoldAmount`, `penaltyOrWriteOff` (ACCEPTED_PENALTY+DISPUTED_PENALTY combined — DISPUTED_PENALTY has no separate resolution/release model in the schema, so it's treated identically to ACCEPTED_PENALTY here, flagged as an assumption), `otherDeductions`; `totalSettled` sums all of the above, cross-checked in tests to equal Σ `settledAmount`); **Receivable** (`invoiceOutstanding` = netBilled − totalSettled, deliberately **unclamped** — a negative value is a genuine credit balance, never floored to zero, matching `InvoicesService.toSummary`'s own already-unclamped `outstandingAmount`; `unreleasedRetentionAndHold` = Σ over HOLD/RETENTION deductions of (amount − Σ `DeductionRelease.releasedAmount`), the same live formula `ReceiptsService`'s own single-deduction release check already uses, applied in bulk; `clientReceivable` = outstanding + unreleased hold/retention — TDS/GST-TDS and penalties are excluded, since they're tax-recoverable or already-settled, not owed by the client); **Recognized Expense** (below).
(2) Recognized expense excludes recoverable Input GST — computed via one shared pure function, `recognizedExpenseAmount(grossAmount, eligibleGstCredit) = grossAmount - eligibleGstCredit` (`apps/api/src/projects/project-financials.util.ts`), reused by three thin per-source adapters rather than three independently reimplemented formulas: `VendorBillLine` (`eligibleGstCredit` = cgst+sgst+igst when `itcEligible`, else 0), `DirectExpense` (same shape), `EmployeeExpenseClaim` (`eligibleGstCredit` = its own flat `gstCreditAmount` directly, no gate needed — the field already holds only the eligible portion by construction). Ledger-based summation (`JournalLine.projectId`, confirmed to exist and be reliably set by all three posting paths) was considered and rejected for this slice: the posted debit is itself computed from these same frozen fields at posting time, so both routes are mathematically identical, and the ledger route would additionally require classifying every `ExpenseCategory`'s arbitrary `ledgerAccountId` by expense-vs-asset nature for no added correctness. TDS never affects this figure — it changes the settlement destination (`TDS_PAYABLE`), not the expense debit. Profit/Margin remains omitted (ADR-086 precedent unchanged).
(3) The existing Expense ledger (`getExpenseLedger`) keeps its gross, per-transaction totals unchanged and gains a 3rd category (Direct Expense, POSTED rows) — but its tab is relabeled **"Spend"** (not "Expense") so it's never confused with the new tab's ITC-excluded `recognizedProjectExpense` figure under the same unqualified label. The per-row gross listing stays as-is (each row already matches its own source document's headline amount elsewhere in the app); only the label changed, not the math.
(4) A new, bounded `ProjectFinancialsService` (`apps/api/src/projects/project-financials.service.ts`), not a method added to `ProjectsService` and exposed to other modules — it depends on `ProjectsService` one-way, reusing `getDetail` (single-project scope check) and `list` (permitted-id set for bulk) rather than reimplementing project-visibility logic a second time. `listFinancialSummaries` takes a required, caller-supplied `projectIds` array (the Projects list's current visible page, not "every project in the org") and independently re-derives+intersects the actor's own permitted set before aggregating, silently dropping any id outside it — the frontend's requested ids are never trusted directly. All aggregation is bulk `groupBy`/`findMany`-then-reduce (the same style already established in `InvoicesService.list`), a fixed number of queries regardless of how many project ids are requested.
(5) New `project.read_financials` permission (117 → 118 seeded keys) granted to Admin (automatic), Accountant (added explicitly — owns money-movement figures everywhere else), and CEO (added explicitly to `CEO_PERMISSIONS`, since the key doesn't end in `.read`) — not granted by default to Project Manager, Admin Staff, or Employee, per the user's "CEO, admin mainly" framing. The existing generic `UserPermissionOverride` mechanism (already in the schema, no new work needed) preserves the ability to grant it to an individual PM later, who would then still only see financials for projects they're normally authorized to see — enforced by the same `getDetail`/`list`-intersection regardless of *why* the actor holds the permission.
(6) New `MoneyText` component (`packages/ui/src/components/MoneyText.tsx`) — the one component genuinely missing from docs/15 §21's catalogue (`StatTile` is already fulfilled by the existing `KpiCard`; `RecordHeader` is a larger page-header refactor, explicitly deferred). A thin tone-aware (`positive`/`negative`/`warning`/`neutral`) wrapper around an already-formatted string — no currency logic of its own, `formatMoney` stays in `apps/web`. A negative `invoiceOutstanding`/`clientReceivable` renders as an explicit **Credit Balance** (positive tone, absolute value) rather than a warning-amber "Outstanding" figure — no arithmetic performed client-side to get there (`isCreditBalance`/`absMoney` in `projects/shared.tsx` are pure string checks, not Decimal math).
(7) Projects list gains exactly 3 new columns when `project.read_financials` is held — Net Billed (neutral), Client Receivable (warning or Credit Balance), Recognized Expense (negative) — fetched via one `listFinancialSummaries` call scoped to the currently-visible page's row ids (`EntityTable` already defaults to `scroll={{ x: 'max-content' }}`, so the extra columns need no new responsive work). Project Detail gains a new "Financials" tab (visible only with the permission) with 5 KPI cards (Contract Value (Excl. GST), Net Billed, Cash Received, Client Receivable, Recognized Expense) plus two breakdown cards (Billing & Collections; Project Expenses) — every deduction bucket shown as its own row (TDS, GST-TDS, Retention, Hold, Penalty/Write-off, Other Deductions) rather than combined client-side, since combining would require `Number()` arithmetic in React, which CLAUDE.md forbids.
Consequences: No schema/migration changes — every figure is derived from already-existing, already-posted fields; only one new seeded `Permission` row. `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` — `project.read_financials` added to `PERMISSION_KEYS`/`ACCOUNTANT_PERMISSIONS`/`CEO_PERMISSIONS`; catalog count corrected 117 → 118 in `apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts`. New `apps/api/src/projects/{project-financials.service.ts, project-financials.util.ts, project-financials.integration-spec.ts}`, registered in the existing `projects.module.ts`. `apps/api/src/projects/projects.service.ts`'s `getExpenseLedger` extended with a `hasDirectExpenseReadPermission` flag and a 3rd category branch; its own integration spec extended with a Direct Expense fixture. New `packages/contracts/src/ports/project-financials.port.ts` (`ProjectFinancialsPort`/`ProjectFinancialSummary`), wired into `context.ts`/`ports/index.ts`/`index.ts` as `ctx.services.projectFinancials`; two new procedures (`getFinancialSummary`/`listFinancialSummaries`) added directly to the existing `packages/contracts/src/routers/projects.ts` (same router surface, separate backing service); `project.port.ts`'s `ProjectExpenseCategory`/`ProjectExpenseRow.linkType` extended with `DIRECT_EXPENSE`. `apps/api/src/main.ts` wires `ProjectFinancialsService`. New `packages/ui/src/components/MoneyText.tsx`, exported from `index.ts`. Frontend: `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/{page.tsx, shared.tsx, [id]/page.tsx, [id]/expense-tab.tsx}` updated; new `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/financials-tab.tsx`.
Verified with: 7 new tests in `project-financials.integration-spec.ts` (every billing/collection/receivable/recognized-expense figure computed exactly against a fixture covering all 9 `AllocationDeductionType` values, partial hold/retention releases, a debit and a credit note on the same invoice, a reversed receipt and a VOID invoice both fully excluded, ITC-eligible vs. ineligible lines on both Vendor Bill and Direct Expense, an employee claim with a GST credit, a company-overhead Direct Expense and assorted drafts all excluded, single-project NOT_FOUND for an unauthorized actor, bulk `listFinancialSummaries` silently dropping unauthorized/nonexistent ids, and PM project-scoping); `getExpenseLedger`'s existing suite extended to 5 tests covering the new Direct Expense category (inclusion, permission exclusion, category filter, 3-way largest-category comparison). `turbo run typecheck lint build` clean across all 9 workspaces (27/27 tasks). No migration to replay (no schema change). The full `apps/api` integration suite passed clean on rerun: 452/452 tests across 34 files (one transient cross-file GSTIN-uniqueness collision on the first run, confirmed as pre-existing full-suite parallelism flakiness per ADR-077 — the same file passed standalone and on rerun); the full `apps/worker` integration suite passed 19/19 across 3 files (untouched by this slice); all cross-package unit tests passed 14/14 tasks.
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session). Explicitly deferred, disclosed rather than silently dropped: client-wide financial aggregation (closing the Client detail page's own "Receivable" gap) — the immediate next follow-up, which must reuse this service's own per-project authorization intersection rather than a separate org-wide query.
Supersedes: none — additive; extends `getExpenseLedger`'s category list (still additive to ADR-086) and relabels its tab, but changes no prior figure's math. No other prior ADR's decision is reopened.
```

```text
ID: ADR-090
Date: 2026-08-25
Status: Accepted — implemented and fully verified
Context: ADR-089's own class doc comment on `ProjectFinancialsService` explicitly anticipated this exact follow-up — "A future client-wide rollup (deferred) should aggregate this service's own output across a client's projects, not reimplement this logic" — closing the Client detail page's own long-disclosed gap: the reference HTML's "Receivable" stat tile was dropped when that page was built because it "would require a new cross-module invoice/receipt outstanding-amount aggregation" (docs/13-progress.md). Grounded first: `ClientsService.getDetail` (`apps/api/src/clients/clients.service.ts:131`) takes only `{ organizationId, id }` — no `actorId`/`canSeeAll` — Client access in this codebase is a flat, org-wide `client.read` gate with no per-client visibility scoping (unlike Project's PM-assignment scoping), so "Client access" for this feature is fully satisfied by an org-scoped existence check. Also found and fixed while touching the same tile row: the existing "Total Project Value" tile was summed client-side via `Number(row.value ?? 0)` (`clients/[id]/page.tsx`) — a real, if low-stakes, violation of CLAUDE.md's "never calculate accounting totals independently in React."
Decision:
(1) One new method, `getClientFinancialSummary`, added to the existing, bounded `ProjectFinancialsService` — not a new service, and `ClientsService`/`ClientPort` are untouched. It calls `this.clients.getDetail(...)` first (the client-access check), then reuses `ProjectsService.list`'s own existing scope verbatim (identical to `listFinancialSummaries`) to find only this client's projects the calling actor can actually see, then sums the already-existing `computeFinancialSummaries` output across them. A scoped actor who can see none of this client's projects gets an all-zero summary with `projectCount: 0`, not `NOT_FOUND` — the client itself is genuinely accessible, only its financial data happens to be empty for that actor.
(2) New `ClientFinancialSummary` type, colocated in `project-financials.port.ts` alongside `ProjectFinancialSummary` (same aggregation domain, different rollup level — exactly like `ProjectFinancialSummary` itself lives there despite being "about" a project). Same field set minus `projectId`, plus `clientId` and `projectCount` (distinguishes "genuinely zero activity" from "zero because this actor can't see any of this client's projects"). Every field is summed independently via `Prisma.Decimal` across the visible per-project summaries — mathematically exact and unclamped (a client-level credit balance is just as real as a project-level one), since every derived field (`netBilled`, `totalSettled`, `invoiceOutstanding`, `clientReceivable`, `recognizedProjectExpense`) is a linear combination with no multiplication/division, so summing each field independently commutes with recomputing it from summed parts.
(3) `ProjectsModule` gained a new, one-way, cycle-free DI edge: `imports: [ClientsModule]`, giving `ProjectFinancialsService` a `ClientsService` dependency (`ClientsModule` has zero imports of its own, so no circular risk).
(4) New router procedure lives on `clients`, not `projects` — `clients.getFinancialSummary` — matching where a frontend consumer of the Client detail page would look for it, calling `ctx.services.projectFinancials.getClientFinancialSummary(...)` directly (a service `routers/clients.ts` doesn't otherwise touch). This mirrors an already-established precedent: `routers/projects.ts` already calls both `ctx.services.projects` and `ctx.services.projectFinancials` from one file, so a router file calling a service outside its own module's namesake is not a new pattern. No new context/`main.ts` wiring needed — `ctx.services.projectFinancials` already exists (ADR-089).
(5) Gated by **both** `client.read` and the existing `project.read_financials` (reused verbatim — no new permission, no seed/catalog/migration change), inlined as a second `.use()` middleware check rather than generalizing `requirePermission` into a multi-key helper — this is the first and only procedure in this codebase requiring two independent permissions; worth extracting into a shared `requireAllPermissions`-style helper only if a second use case appears.
(6) Frontend: the existing "Total Project Value" tile (visible to every role today, unrelated to the new permission) switches its *source* to the new server-computed `totalContractValueExclGst` whenever the actor holds `project.read_financials`, falling back to the pre-existing client-side sum otherwise — the displayed figure doesn't change for anyone (both compute the identical taxable-value total over the identical visible-project set; `project.value` has one basis, so there's no GST-basis reconciliation needed here), only where the arithmetic happens. A genuinely new 5th tile, **"Receivable"** (matching the original prototype's own wording — no "Client" prefix needed on a page already scoped to one client), renders only when the actor holds the permission, flipping to "Credit Balance" with a positive tone when negative — reusing `MoneyText` and the `isCreditBalance`/`absMoney` convention ADR-089 already established for the project-level tab.
(7) Small supporting refactor: `isCreditBalance`/`absMoney` moved from `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/shared.tsx` (their only prior home) to `apps/web/src/lib/format.ts` (alongside `formatMoney`, which `projects/shared.tsx` already re-exported from there) — avoids a `clients/[id]/page.tsx → ../../projects/shared` cross-feature-folder import for two generic, project-nothing-specific money-display helpers. Both `projects/shared.tsx` and `clients/shared.tsx` now re-export them from `lib/format.ts`, matching the existing `formatMoney`/`formatDate` re-export pattern.
Consequences: No schema/migration changes — pure aggregation over ADR-089's own already-existing output, no new permission. `packages/contracts/src/ports/project-financials.port.ts` — new `ClientFinancialSummary` type, `ProjectFinancialsPort.getClientFinancialSummary` added, wired into `ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`. `apps/api/src/projects/project-financials.service.ts` — `ClientsService` added as a third constructor dependency, new `getClientFinancialSummary` method. `apps/api/src/projects/projects.module.ts` — `imports: [ClientsModule]` added. `packages/contracts/src/routers/clients.ts` — new `getFinancialSummary` procedure, new `throwAppError` import. `apps/web/src/lib/format.ts` — `isCreditBalance`/`absMoney` added; `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/{projects,clients}/shared.tsx` — both re-export them now instead of `projects/shared.tsx` defining them locally. `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/clients/[id]/page.tsx` — new `financials` query, Total Project Value tile source switched, new Receivable/Credit-Balance tile.
Verified with: 4 new tests in the existing `project-financials.integration-spec.ts` (client-level sum matches the single non-zero project's own figures exactly, since the client's other project contributes zero; a PM scoped to only the zero-activity project gets an all-zero client rollup even though the client itself has real, nonzero activity under an unscoped actor — the contrast between the two tests is the proof of exclusion, with no fixture changes needed; an actor with no assignment to either project gets `projectCount: 0` rather than an error; a client in a different organization throws `NOT_FOUND`). `turbo run typecheck lint build` clean across all 9 workspaces (27/27 tasks). No migration to replay. The specific new/extended integration spec passed 11/11 directly against the database.
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session). The full `apps/api` integration-suite regression run (beyond the directly affected spec file) was not completed this session — the affected spec passed in full, and the full workspace typecheck/lint/build (which would catch any type-level regression from the new `ProjectsModule → ClientsModule` DI edge or the port/router changes) passed clean; a full-suite regression pass is still recommended before this is considered as thoroughly verified as every prior slice in this session.
Supersedes: none — additive; the "Total Project Value" tile's displayed number is unchanged for every role, only its computation location moves when the new permission is held.
```

```text
ID: ADR-091
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Accepted — implementation complete, UI verification pending
Context: Opens Phase 7 (Inventory and costing), the first roadmap phase after Phase 6 closed. Slice 7.1 is the product/item master every later inventory slice depends on — nothing in this codebase modelled a product, item, SKU, stock or warehouse before this. Two things had to be decided before a line of schema was written. First, where the master lives: `docs/04` §8 gives it unit, HSN/SAC, category, an optional default rate and an added `is_stock_tracked` flag, which is five columns beyond the flat baseline shape ADR-037/038 scopes the generic `MasterTypeKey` API to — the same situation that forced `ExpenseCategory` and `PenaltyCategory` out of that registry (ADR-044/062). Second, what `is_stock_tracked` is for: `docs/01`'s Inventory section makes it the load-bearing discriminator — "Stock-tracked items use Inventory; non-stock goods/services use Expense" — so it is not a display flag but the switch that decides whether a later Vendor Bill line debits `INVENTORY` or an expense account (docs/06's two separate "Finalize vendor bill" tables). Confirmed with the user before planning: build all three Phase 7 verticals with Inventory first; item master gets its own dedicated model rather than a widened generic registry.
Decision:
(1) New `InventoryItem` model with its own port/service/module/router — NOT a generic `MasterTypeKey`. Follows `PenaltyCategoryService`'s exact shape (dedicated CRUD, `includeInactive` list flag, optimistic concurrency via `expectedVersion`, `MASTER_*`-style audit events) rather than inventing a new one.
(2) New `ProductCategory` model IS a generic `MasterTypeKey` — the 15th — because it is genuinely flat. It deliberately carries no `ledgerAccountId` (unlike `ExpenseCategory`, ADR-075): inventory always debits the single `INVENTORY` account regardless of category, per docs/06's "Finalize vendor bill for inventory" table, so a per-category account override would be a second, contradictory truth. Registered in `MASTER_TYPES`, the `getDelegate` map, `masterTypeSchema` in `routers/masters.ts`, and the web `MASTER_CATALOG` — four touchpoints, all of which the existing `units_of_measure` entry documents.
(3) `unitOfMeasureId` reuses the existing `UnitOfMeasure` master (ADR-076) verbatim — required, not nullable, since an item without a unit cannot be received or consumed. `UnitOfMeasure` gained the `inventoryItems` back-relation; no other change to it.
(4) `code` is server-generated (`ITEM-000001`, org-scoped, zero-padded to 6) and never accepted from the client, matching the Milestone/Invoice/VendorBill numbering precedent including the `MAX_CODE_ATTEMPTS` retry loop that re-derives the next number per attempt and retries only on a real `isConstraintViolation`. `create` is idempotency-keyed through the shared `IdempotencyKey` table (`inventory_item.create`), which every other create command in this codebase already does — worth the consistency even for a master, because a double-submitted Add would otherwise silently burn a code.
(5) `defaultRateAmount` is `Decimal(19,4)`, nullable, and documented in both the schema doc comment and the UI tooltip as a data-entry convenience that is never an accounting figure — the real cost of stock is the weighted average derived from posted movements (Slice 7.2). Naming it `default_rate_amount` rather than `rate` keeps it from ever reading as the authoritative cost.
(6) Two new permissions, `inventory_item.read`/`inventory_item.manage`, rather than reusing `master.read`/`master.manage`. `manage` is Admin-only, matching `master.manage`'s existing posture; `read` is granted to Accountant, Project Manager and Admin Staff (and CEO automatically via the `.read` filter) because every later inventory screen — receipt, consumption, item ledger, and the Vendor Bill line editor — needs to name an item. Catalog 118 → 120.
(7) Both FKs are composite `(id, organization_id)` org-consistency constraints per docs/07 and ADR-039, modelled natively in `schema.prisma` (not hand-added SQL), backed by `uq_product_categories_id_org` and the pre-existing `uq_units_of_measure_id_org`. `InventoryItem` carries its own `uq_inventory_items_id_org` for the movement/receipt/consumption children Slices 7.2 and 7.3 will add.
(8) Selectability is enforced server-side, not just hidden in the UI: `create`/`update` reject a deactivated unit of measure or category with an explicit message, and reject another organization's master with `NOT_FOUND`. Deactivating an item never deletes it — it leaves the default list, stays reachable by id and via `includeInactive`, and remains on every historical record. This is the project-wide deactivated-master rule made explicit at the service layer.
(9) No fixed-asset or depreciation fields, ever — ADR-018, `docs/04` §14 ("No Fixed Asset or depreciation screens are required") and `docs/16` §7.7 ("despite the folder name, there is no Fixed Asset register or depreciation module") all say so independently. Recorded in the schema doc comment so a future reader does not re-add them from the `07-assets-inventory/` folder name.
Consequences: One migration, `20260831090000_phase7_01_inventory_item_master` (45 total) — two new tables, six indexes, two composite FKs; hand-curated from `prisma migrate diff`, excluding the same three known phantom lines (`chk_cities_state_matches_district`, `uq_districts_id_state_id`, `idx_invoices_payment_alert_id`) every prior migration in this project excludes. `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` — 2 new permissions, 7 seeded product categories, `INVENTORY_ITEM` entity type. New `apps/api/src/inventory-items/{inventory-items.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}`. New `packages/contracts/src/ports/inventory-item.port.ts` and `routers/inventory-items.ts`, wired through `ports/index.ts`/`index.ts`/`context.ts`/`router.ts`/`app.module.ts`/`main.ts`. `packages/contracts/src/ports/master.port.ts` and `routers/masters.ts` and `apps/api/src/masters/masters.service.ts` gain `product_categories`. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/products/page.tsx`; `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/masters/catalog.ts` gains a Product Categories tile; `packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts}` gain `ProductIcon` and a "Products & Items" nav item under Purchases & Expenses.
Verified with: 14 new integration tests in `inventory-items.integration-spec.ts`, all passing directly against the database — sequential server-generated codes, per-organization code sequences (org B's first item is also `ITEM-000001`), display-name resolution, decimal-string rate formatting, non-stock-tracked items, null-not-invented for omitted optional fields, negative-rate rejection, deactivated-unit and deactivated-category rejection on both create and update, cross-organization unit rejection, idempotent replay plus `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` on a reused key with a different payload, the audit event, optimistic-concurrency conflict on a stale `expectedVersion`, deactivate-not-delete visibility across default/`includeInactive`/`getDetail`, search/category/stock filters and pagination, and full cross-organization isolation across `list`/`getDetail`/`getCount`. `turbo run typecheck` and `lint` clean across all 14 tasks; `turbo run build` clean across all 9 tasks with `web` compiling successfully. Migration applied via `prisma migrate deploy` + `prisma generate`; seed re-run and verified against the live database (120 permissions, 7 product categories, `INVENTORY_ITEM` entity type present). `roles.integration-spec.ts` catalog count corrected 118 → 120.
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet, and — unlike every prior slice — the paired prototype HTML could not be consulted at all: `references/ui/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:14`) and absent from this checkout, so all 106 prototype files and 105 screenshots are missing. `/products` was therefore built from `docs/04` §8's field list and the existing `master-vendor.html`-derived `/vendors` page's own structure, NOT from `master-product.html` itself. Per `docs/16` §5 this screen cannot be declared UI-complete until the reference bundle is restored and the screen is compared against its paired 1440x900 screenshot. The backend is unaffected by this gap. Separately, `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` authentication fails against the configured Postgres host (the shadow database exists but the `zitaware` role cannot connect to it), so `prisma migrate diff --from-migrations` could not run; the migration was diffed `--from-url` against the live dev database instead, which is equivalent here because the dev database was exactly at the committed migration head. A fresh-database replay from zero was not performed for the same reason and remains recommended.
Supersedes: none — purely additive. No existing model, posting rule, permission grant or screen changes behaviour.
```

```text
ID: ADR-092
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Accepted — implementation complete and VERIFIED against a live database (verification closed 2026-08-31; see Known issues for what remains)
Context: Slice 7.2 — the stock ledger, the weighted-average valuation engine, and the Vendor Bill inventory variant. Three things forced this design. First, docs/12 requires TDR-007 ("Inventory costing = weighted average") to be "moved to Approved with rationale" when the phase begins; this ADR is that promotion. Second, docs/06 has carried a "Finalize vendor bill for inventory" posting table since Phase 0 that has never been implemented — ADR-075 shipped Vendor Bill with "no inventory variant" explicitly disclosed. Third, docs/04:463 states the movement ledger is authoritative and docs/04:464 forbids duplicating the vendor-bill liability journal, which together determine where stock may and may not be posted. Confirmed with the user during planning: Vendor Bill lines gain an optional item so approving the bill both debits INVENTORY and creates the movement atomically (rather than a separate manually-entered receipt that could drift from the ledger); and stock is valued by an append-only movement ledger carrying a running snapshot.
Decision:
(1) TDR-007 promoted to Accepted. Rationale: weighted average is the only method of the three candidates that needs no per-unit or per-lot identity. FIFO/LIFO would require lot tracking that docs/00 excludes (it excludes even multiple locations), and specific identification is meaningless for the consumables and cabling this inventory actually holds. Weighted average also makes the ledger self-describing — each row's rate explains its own balance with no reference to any other row.
(2) One pure module, `inventory-valuation.util.ts`, is the whole of the arithmetic: `applyReceipt` blends a purchase cost into the running average, `applyConsumption` issues at the ruling average. Shared by every caller and the tests, the same discipline as the OPEX schedule generator (ADR-063) and `computeLines`. Money rounds to 2dp (matching `computeLines`' own `taxableAmount` basis, so an inventory debit can never carry a sub-paisa fraction a journal line cannot); the rate keeps 4dp because it is a divisor, not a posted amount.
(3) **Full depletion carries out the entire remaining value**, not `quantity × rate`. Without this special case a rate that does not divide evenly strands a few paise against zero quantity forever, and stock value permanently stops reconciling to the INVENTORY account — Phase 7's own exit criterion would fail silently. The residue lands on the final issue, the same deterministic-remainder principle docs/06 already demands of salary allocation. This is directly tested (1000 across 3 units at 333.3333 issues back to exactly 1000.00).
(4) `InventoryMovement` is append-only and stores the balance it produced (`balanceQuantity`/`balanceValueAmount`/`weightedAverageRate`). Nothing recomputes a historical rate by replaying the ledger, so a past consumption's cost is permanently reproducible, and the sum of every item's latest `balanceValueAmount` **is** the INVENTORY account balance — the exit criterion holds by construction rather than by a reconciliation job. `sequenceNumber` (unique per item) gives the ledger one total order even when movements share a date. A mistake is corrected by a REVERSAL row, never an edit, mirroring ADR-021/067's treatment of a posted journal.
(5) **Backdated movements are refused.** A movement dated before an item's last movement would silently invalidate every rate already stamped on later rows. The correction path is a reversal then a fresh movement — the same posture posted journals have. This is a deliberate, disclosed restriction, not an oversight.
(6) `InventoryLedgerService.recordMovement` is the single writer of the ledger. Every caller — vendor bill approval, standalone receipt, and Slice 7.3's consumption — goes through it, and it always takes `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the item row first (docs/07's "lock rows when allocating outstanding balances", applied to stock). It takes the caller's own `tx` rather than opening its own, so a movement always commits with the business event that caused it — docs/02's named boundary.
(7) Vendor Bill: `VendorBillLine` gains a nullable `inventoryItemId`, and `expenseCategoryId` drops NOT NULL, with a hand-added CHECK enforcing exactly one. A line is now either an expense line or a stock line. `approve()`'s debit resolution branches on which — but the `costDebit` formula itself is deliberately shared, not duplicated, because docs/06's two tables debit the identical figure (taxable value plus ineligible tax) and differ only in the account. The per-unit landed cost handed to the ledger is that same `costDebit` divided by quantity, so the movement can never disagree with the journal line. Only a stock-tracked, active item may be referenced. **No second journal is posted** (docs/04:464); the movement records quantity and running cost only.
(8) `InventoryReceipt` covers exactly the two cases the bill path cannot: OPENING (cutover stock, `Dr INVENTORY / Cr OPENING_BALANCE_EQUITY`, reusing ADR-071's mechanism rather than inventing a second) and DIRECT (`Dr INVENTORY / Cr ACCOUNTS_PAYABLE_VENDOR`). A CHECK enforces that DIRECT names a vendor and OPENING does not. Reversal reverses the journal and issues the stock back out, refused once any of it has been consumed — taking it back would have to invent a cost for units already gone. Stock adjustments and write-offs are deliberately NOT built: docs/06 gives them no posting rule, so they are disclosed as deferred rather than invented.
(9) Two new permissions (`inventory.read`/`inventory.receive`, catalog 120 → 122) following this codebase's rule that a stock-moving action gets its own narrow key. One new ledger account `INVENTORY` under a new `INVENTORY_ASSETS` group — one account for all of inventory, not one per category, because docs/06 names a single Inventory debit and per-category accounts would make stock value reconcile to a set of balances rather than to one.
Consequences: One migration, `20260831110000_phase7_02_inventory_movements_receipts` (46 total) — three tables, five hand-added CHECK constraints, plus the `vendor_bill_lines` alter. Dropping NOT NULL is unconditionally safe and every pre-7.2 row satisfies the new CHECK. New `apps/api/src/inventory/{inventory-valuation.util.ts, inventory-valuation.util.spec.ts, inventory-ledger.service.ts, inventory.service.ts, inventory.module.ts, inventory.integration-spec.ts}`. New `packages/contracts/src/ports/inventory.port.ts` and `routers/inventory.ts`, wired through the usual five touchpoints. `apps/api/src/vendor-bills/vendor-bills.service.ts` (new `assertLineTargetsValid`, branched debit resolution, movement creation inside `approve()`'s transaction) and `.module.ts` (new `InventoryModule` import — one-way, cycle-free). `packages/contracts/src/ports/vendor-bill.port.ts` and `routers/vendor-bills.ts` (either/or line input with a zod `.refine`). Frontend: new `/inventory` (Stock + Stock Receipts tabs), `/inventory/[itemId]` (stock-in and stock-out ledgers with running balance), `/inventory/receipts/new`; the Vendor Bill line editor gains an Expense/Stock line-type switch; new nav item and `InventoryIcon`. Five existing integration specs updated mechanically for the new constructor arity and the new line field.
Verified with: 12 new unit tests in `inventory-valuation.util.spec.ts`, all passing — first-receipt rate, true weighted blending (asserting 175, not the 150 a naive mean of rates would give), zero-cost receipts, fractional quantities, issuing at the ruling average rather than the latest purchase cost, the full-depletion residue case, rebuilding stock after depletion, a long mixed receipt/issue sequence asserting value-on-hand always equals received minus issued with no rounding drift, over-issue refusal naming both figures, and every non-positive/negative guard. `turbo run typecheck`, `lint` and `build` all clean (14/14, 14/14, 9/9; web compiled successfully). 17 integration tests written covering both posting shapes, the CHECK-backed vendor rules, non-stock/inactive/backdate rejection, idempotent replay not doubling stock, reversal including the consumed-stock refusal, the ledger reads, the date-filter-does-not-change-on-hand rule, cross-org isolation, and an explicit assertion that the INVENTORY account balance equals the sum of every item's movement snapshot.
Known issues: **RESOLVED — the database-verification gap that this ADR originally disclosed is closed.** The configured host settled at 192.168.1.5; `prisma migrate status` reports all 46 migrations applied and the schema up to date, the seed re-ran clean (122 permissions, the `INVENTORY` ledger account and `INVENTORY_ASSETS` group present), and the integration tests ran for the first time — all passing. The suite contains **15** tests, not the 17 this ADR originally claimed; corrected by direct count. A full `apps/api` integration regression also passes (36 spec files, 485 tests, zero failures), which independently clears the five specs edited only mechanically for the new constructor arity and line field, and `turbo run typecheck` is clean 14/14. Still open: (a) `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` authentication continues to fail, so neither Phase 7 migration has been replayed against a fresh database from zero — both are verified against the live dev database only, and the offline `--from-schema-datamodel` provenance of this slice's migration SQL therefore remains unreplayed; (b) `references/ui/` is still absent, so `/inventory` and its ledger were built from docs/04 §14's contract text rather than `inventory-list.html`/`inventory-detail.html`/`inventory-add-stock.html`, and cannot be declared UI-complete per docs/16 §5; (c) no live browser pass.
Supersedes: closes ADR-075's disclosed "no inventory variant" gap. Reopens nothing else; every existing expense-line posting is byte-for-byte unchanged.
```

```text
ID: ADR-093
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Accepted — implementation complete and verified against a live database
Context: Slice 7.3 — inventory consumption, the one document that takes stock OUT of the ledger for a business reason, implementing docs/06's "Inventory / Project consumption" table (Dr Project Material Cost; Cr Inventory, at weighted average) and docs/04 §14's `inventory-add-consumption.html` contract. Slice 7.2 built the movement ledger and its `CONSUMPTION` movement type but gave it no document, no journal and no screen; the enum value was reachable only by calling `InventoryLedgerService` directly from a test. One thing about this slice is genuinely new in this codebase and forced a change to the posting engine itself: **the amount posted is not knowable before the transaction begins.** Every earlier posting caller — vendor bill, receipt, invoice, receipt, EMD — computes its figure from user input, posts the journal on its own connection, then opens a transaction to write its business rows. A consumption's amount IS the ruling weighted average read under the item's `FOR UPDATE` lock, and full depletion carries out the entire residual balance rather than quantity × rate (ADR-092 point 3). Posting first would have to guess that figure, and a concurrent receipt landing in between would leave an Inventory credit the movement disagrees with — silently breaking Phase 7's own "inventory movement reconciles to stock value" exit criterion, which ADR-092 deliberately made true by construction.
Decision:
(1) `PostingService.postJournal` is refactored into a thin wrapper over a new `postJournalWithin(tx, input)`. All of the posting engine — every mandatory control in docs/06, the period resolution, the account checks, the duplicate-source check, the writes, the audit event and the idempotency key — now lives in the transactional variant, and `postJournal` is `this.prisma.client.$transaction((tx) => this.postJournalWithin(tx, input))`. Behaviour for all ten existing callers is unchanged; the only difference is that the validating reads now run on the transaction rather than a fresh connection, which is strictly safer (they see the caller's own uncommitted writes and locked rows). `resolveAccountingPeriod` takes the client to read on. This is a smaller, safer change than the alternative considered and rejected — holding the item lock in an outer transaction while calling `postJournal` on a second pooled connection, which risks pool exhaustion under concurrency and leaves an orphan journal if the outer transaction later fails.
(2) `InventoryConsumption` / `InventoryConsumptionLine`, shaped exactly like `InventoryReceipt` / `InventoryReceiptLine` (POSTED/REVERSED, `version`, cascade-on-parent lines, no line-level lifecycle). Numbering is `CONS-000001`, server-generated, org-scoped, with the same `MAX_CODE_ATTEMPTS` retry loop; `record` and `reverse` are both idempotency-keyed.
(3) **A project is required, not optional.** docs/06 names the debit "Project Material Cost" and docs/04 §14 requires an active project to be validated, so consumption with no project has no posting rule and is therefore not modelled — the same reasoning that keeps stock adjustments and write-offs unbuilt (ADR-092 point 8). A project in a terminal operational status (`CANCELLED`/`CLOSED`) is refused, reusing `ProjectsService`'s own `TERMINAL_STATUSES` definition rather than inventing a second one. The consumption must post in the **project's own** legal entity: allowing the two to diverge would put a project's material cost in another entity's books, so it is derived on the screen and enforced on the server rather than freely chosen.
```
