```text
(4) Both journal lines carry the `projectId` dimension, not just the expense leg. That is what lets project cost reporting (Phase 8, and Slice 7.6's manpower/material cost) read material spend straight off the journal with no second source of truth.
(5) Lines naming the same item are **chained, not valued independently**, by one `computeConsumptionLines` helper shared by the preview and the posting path. Valued independently against the opening balance, two lines for the same item could each "succeed" and drive the balance negative — and, worse, neither would be recognised as the depleting one, so the residual-value carry-out would never fire. Chained, the second line is issued out of what the first left.
(6) A new `previewConsumption` query returns the server's own figures for the entry screen — CLAUDE.md's "do not calculate accounting totals independently in React components" applied to the weighted average, which the browser genuinely cannot reproduce because of the depletion rule. It is explicitly a quote and not a reservation: the authoritative figure is re-derived under the row lock at posting time. It returns every line even when one is short, with a per-line `shortfallMessage`, so the screen shows every problem at once rather than one per round-trip. Gated on `inventory.consume` rather than `inventory.read` because it is part of composing the document.
(7) `recordConsumption` locks its items in **sorted id order** before doing anything else, so two concurrent multi-item consumptions can never deadlock against each other. After writing the movements it asserts their total equals the total it posted; the movements re-derive their own value from the same locked balances, so any disagreement is a bug rather than a race, and asserting it is what keeps the INVENTORY account and the stock ledger provably equal rather than equal-by-inspection.
(8) Reversal returns each line's quantity at that line's **own stored `unitCostAmount`**, not at today's weighted average. A later receipt legitimately moves the average; returning at the new one would silently change the total value of stock on hand and stop the ledger reconciling. This is exactly what the stamped per-line cost is for. Reversal is refused on an already-reversed document and requires a reason, matching `reverseReceipt`.
(9) One new permission `inventory.consume` (catalog 122 → 123), granted to Accountant only — the same posture as `inventory.receive`, since this action posts to the ledger. Project Manager gets `inventory.read` and can see what was issued but cannot issue: a deliberate, disclosed boundary rather than an oversight, and the natural thing to revisit if site teams are to raise issues themselves. One new ledger account `PROJECT_MATERIAL_COST` under a new `PROJECT_COSTS` group (Expenses) — a group rather than reusing `VENDOR_EXPENSES`, which is specifically the per-ExpenseCategory leaves, and one Slices 7.5/7.6 will also populate.
Consequences: One migration, `20260831130000_phase7_03_inventory_consumption` (47 total) — two tables, one nullable `inventory_consumption_id` column on `inventory_movements` (the third and final movement source, kept as a real FK per source exactly as the other two are), six indexes, four composite org-consistency FKs, and two hand-added CHECKs (positive line quantity mirroring the receipt side; reversal fields settable only together with `REVERSED` status). Adding nullable columns and new tables is unconditionally safe on existing data. `seed.ts` — 1 permission, 1 account group, 1 ledger account, `INVENTORY_CONSUMPTION` entity type. `apps/api/src/accounting/posting.service.ts` (the `postJournalWithin` extraction). `apps/api/src/inventory/inventory-ledger.service.ts` (new `lockAndGetBalance`, extracted from `recordMovement` which now calls it; `inventoryConsumptionId` on the movement input). `apps/api/src/inventory/inventory.service.ts` (four new methods plus consumption sources on the item ledger read). `packages/contracts/src/ports/inventory.port.ts` and `routers/inventory.ts` plus the two index touchpoints. Frontend: a new "Project Issues" tab on `/inventory`, an "Issue to Project" action, and a new `/inventory/consumptions/new` entry screen whose every figure comes from `previewConsumption`.
Verified with: 10 new integration tests, all passing against the live database (25 in the file total, up from 15) — issuing at the blended 175 rather than the latest purchase cost with both journal legs carrying the project dimension; the full-depletion residue case proving 1000.00 received comes back out as exactly 1000.00 across two documents; two lines for the same item chaining into one running balance and the follow-up attempt being refused; over-issue refused with nothing partial written; closed project, mismatched legal entity, non-stock item, inactive item, backdated date and zero quantity all refused; idempotent replay not issuing the stock twice plus `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` on a reused key; reversal returning stock at 40.00 after a later receipt moved the average to something else, with double-reversal and empty-reason both refused; preview matching the posted figure exactly and flagging a shortfall without throwing; the item ledger naming the consumption and its project; cross-organization isolation. The suite's own INVENTORY-account-equals-stock-value assertion now runs over real posted consumptions (its exclusion narrowed to the document-less raw ledger movements the suite writes by hand). Full `apps/api` integration regression passes — 36 files, 495 tests, zero failures — including the 25 posting-engine tests that cover the refactor. `turbo run typecheck` and `lint` clean.
Known issues: `references/ui/` is still absent, so the new tab and `/inventory/consumptions/new` were built from docs/04 §14's contract text and the existing `/inventory/receipts/new`'s structure, NOT from `inventory-add-consumption.html`; per docs/16 §5 they cannot be declared UI-complete until the bundle is restored. No live browser pass. `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` still cannot authenticate, so no fresh-database replay from zero was performed; the migration was diffed `--from-url` against the live database, which sat exactly at the committed migration head.
Supersedes: closes ADR-092's disclosed "Slice 7.3's consumption" forward reference. Changes no existing posting: `postJournal`'s observable behaviour, and every journal any existing caller writes, is unchanged.
```

```text
ID: ADR-094
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Accepted — implementation complete and verified against a live database
Context: Slice 7.4 — monthly gross salary import, which opens Phase 7's employee-costing vertical. docs/04 §13 (`employee-salary-upload.html`): "Import monthly gross salary only. Validate month uniqueness, duplicates, unmatched employees and totals; preview before atomic commit. Preserve source file/checksum and audit batch." docs/06 ("Salary project costing"): "Salary import creates the gross salary cost/payable posting", and — decisively for the NEXT slice — "Allocation moves cost analytically to project/non-project dimensions without changing total salary expense", which is why allocation posts nothing at all. docs/16 §7.6 settles the authority question: "Imported monthly gross salary is the historical costing source", so `Employee.displayedSalaryAmount` (a current-state field editable at any time, ADR-047) is explicitly NOT what project manpower cost multiplies. The posting shape (Dr Salaries & Wages Expense / Cr Salary Payable) was confirmed with the user during Phase 7's planning and is recorded in ADR-091's own task note.
Decision:
(1) **`preview` and `commit` share one `resolveRows` method.** The preview is only worth having if it is literally the commit's own validation run without writing — otherwise a preview that says "committable" and a commit that refuses can disagree, which is the exact failure docs/04 §13's "preview before atomic commit" exists to prevent. Every row is classified rather than throwing on the first bad one, so the accountant fixes a whole sheet in one pass.
(2) Five row outcomes, all decided server-side: `MATCHED`, `UNMATCHED` (no employee with that code, or no code at all), `DUPLICATE` (the code appears twice in the same file), `INACTIVE`, `INVALID_AMOUNT`. Matching is by employee code, case- and whitespace-insensitively — the sheet is typed by a person, the master is not. Only `MATCHED` rows count toward the total.
(3) **The whole month posts or none of it does.** A single unresolved row blocks the batch. A partial import would silently understate a month of cost and, worse, would be indistinguishable afterwards from a month where those people genuinely earned nothing.
(4) One `POSTED` batch per legal entity per month, enforced by a hand-added **PARTIAL** unique index (`WHERE status = 'POSTED'`), not a plain composite unique. Reversing a batch must free the month for a corrected re-import; a total unique index would block that forever while the reversed row still exists. The month is normalised to the first of the month on write, so two uploads dated the 1st and the 31st cannot look like different months.
(5) **One debit and one credit line per employee**, both stamped with the `employeeId` dimension `JournalLine` has carried since ADR-065 with nothing populating it. A single summed pair would balance equally well, but then "what is still owed to this person" would be unanswerable from the ledger. The narrative names the employee and the month.
(6) `sourceChecksum` is a SHA-256 of the **parsed rows** (code + amount), not of the file bytes: the same sheet re-saved by a spreadsheet application has different bytes and identical content, and it is the content a duplicate import would duplicate. `sourceFileName` is kept alongside it, and cleared if the rows were typed rather than uploaded.
(7) Gross salary only. Deductions, net pay, statutory contributions and payslips are not modelled — docs/00 excludes payroll processing outright and docs/06 gives none of them a posting rule. A gross of **zero is allowed** (an employee on unpaid leave for a whole month is real); negative is refused by a CHECK. Active employees absent from the sheet are reported but never block the import — a mid-month joiner or leaver may legitimately be missing, and the accountant should see who rather than be stopped.
(8) Three permissions, `salary_import.read/create/commit` (catalog 123 → 126) — `create` and `commit` are the two docs/04 §13 names, kept separate because previewing a sheet is data entry and committing it posts a month of cost to the ledger. Two new ledger accounts under two new groups: `SALARIES_WAGES_EXPENSE` under `EMPLOYEE_COSTS`, `SALARY_PAYABLE` under a new `EMPLOYEE_PAYABLES` group — a salary payable is owed to employees, a different counterparty from `ACCOUNTS_PAYABLE` (vendors) and `CREDIT_CARD_PAYABLE` (an issuer), and collapsing them would make "what do we owe whom" unanswerable.
(9) Frontend parses **CSV only**, in the browser, sending parsed rows rather than a file — there is no file endpoint and no upload to clean up if the commit fails. `.xlsx` is a zip of XML and would need a parser dependency this project has not taken; exporting to CSV first is the documented path. The parser is deliberately dumb (split, trim, strip currency punctuation, skip an obvious header): every judgement about the sheet belongs to the server.
Consequences: One migration, `20260831150000_phase7_04_salary_import` (48 total) — two tables, one enum, one partial unique index and three hand-added CHECKs (positive head count and non-negative gross; reversal fields settable only with `REVERSED` status; non-negative line gross). Nothing existing is altered, so it is unconditionally safe on existing data. `seed.ts` — 3 permissions, 2 account groups, 2 ledger accounts, `SALARY_IMPORT` entity type. New `apps/api/src/salary-imports/{salary-imports.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}`, wired through `app.module.ts`/`main.ts`. New `packages/contracts/src/ports/salary-import.port.ts` and `routers/salary-imports.ts` plus the four wiring touchpoints. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/salary-imports/{shared.tsx, page.tsx, new/page.tsx}`; new `SalaryImportIcon` and a "Salary Imports" nav item under a new **"Employee Costing"** group — deliberately not folded into Administration alongside the Employee master, because these are accounting activities against a month rather than maintenance of a master, and Slices 7.5/7.6 add their screens to the same group.
Verified with: 12 new integration tests, all passing against the live database — case/whitespace-insensitive matching with the system's own name returned alongside the sheet's; any day of the month normalising to the first; all five row outcomes flagged in a single pass with only the good row counted toward the total; active employees missing from the sheet named without blocking; the four-line per-employee journal with both legs carrying the employee dimension and the two account balances asserted exactly; the second-import-same-month refusal, with the preview independently agreeing; one bad row blocking the whole batch and leaving nothing written; an accounting date before the month refused; idempotent replay not posting a second month plus `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` on a reused key; reversal freeing the month for a corrected re-import while keeping the original batch's lines; empty-reason and double-reversal refused; cross-organization isolation across `getDetail`/`list`/`preview`. `turbo run typecheck` and `lint` clean; full `apps/api` integration regression clean.
Known issues: `references/ui/` is still absent (the standing Phase 7 blocker), so both screens were built from docs/04 §13 and docs/16 §7.6's field lists rather than from `employee-salary-upload.html`, and cannot be declared UI-complete per docs/16 §5. No live browser pass. `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` still cannot authenticate, so no fresh-database replay from zero. `.xlsx` parsing is deliberately not implemented (see decision 9) — a disclosed boundary, not a gap.
Supersedes: none — purely additive. Settles, rather than changes, the relationship between `Employee.displayedSalaryAmount` (ADR-047) and costing: the former stays a display figure and is never used as a costing input.
```

```text
ID: ADR-095
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Accepted — implementation complete and verified against a live database
Context: Slice 7.5 — the unified monthly employee allocation, and the project manpower cost derived from it. Two roadmap deliverables in one slice because the second has no schema and no rules of its own: it is a read over the first times the salary import (ADR-094). docs/04 §13 MERGEs `employee-effort-entry.html` and `employee-contribution-entry.html` into one canonical screen and RETIREs the second ("no independent percentage truth"); `employee-effort-summary.html` becomes Allocation Summary; `employee-manpower-cost.html`'s "formula is imported gross salary × allocation percentage. Totals reconcile to salary import and allocation records." Two sentences of docs/06 decide the whole design: allocation "moves cost analytically to project/non-project dimensions **without changing total salary expense**", and "rounding remainder is assigned deterministically so allocations equal gross salary". This closes two of Phase 7's three exit criteria — "submitted employee allocations total 100%" and "project cost uses imported gross salary".
Decision:
(1) **Allocation posts no journal, ever.** The expense is the salary import's; these percentages only say how to attribute it. Every amount this slice returns is therefore computed on read — imported gross × percentage — never stored and never posted. The direct consequence, and the reason this is the right shape: a corrected salary import instantly corrects every project cost derived from it, with nothing to re-run, nothing to reconcile, and no second figure that can drift. It is also why `AllocationTarget` deliberately carries no ledger account (unlike `ExpenseCategory`, ADR-075): there is nothing an account here could ever be used for, and having one would imply a posting that must never happen.
(2) One record per employee per month, DB-unique — the single percentage truth docs/04 §13's MERGE requires. `employee-contribution-entry.html`'s useful content lands as a `contributionNote` on the row that already owns the percentage, not as a second record with a percentage of its own. The month is normalised to the first of the month on write, as `SalaryImport` already does, so two saves dated the 3rd and the 19th cannot become two records.
(3) A line targets EITHER a project OR a non-project `AllocationTarget`, never both and never neither — a DB CHECK plus an explicit service message, the same either/or shape `VendorBillLine` uses (ADR-092). The same project or target may not appear twice: two rows for one project are not two facts, they are one percentage entered twice, and would make the summary's per-project figure ambiguous.
(4) `AllocationTarget` is a generic `MasterTypeKey` — the 16th — because it is genuinely flat (docs/16 §7.6's "approved internal/bench targets"). Seeded with bench, internal project, pre-sales, training, leave and administration.
(5) **Exactly 100% to submit, meant literally** — 99.9999 is refused. Phase 7's own exit criterion. Any tolerance would have to be invented here with no rule behind it, and would make manpower cost silently under- or over-state the month. A DRAFT may hold any total including zero, so a part-filled month is saveable.
(6) **The rounding remainder is assigned to one deterministically chosen line** — the largest share, ties broken by the earliest sequence — in one pure `allocation-cost.util.ts` shared by every caller and the tests. Without it, three equal shares of 100.00 round to 33.33 each and the month is a paisa short; left alone, every project cost report is quietly light and the shortfall grows with head count and month count. Spreading the remainder instead was rejected: one line carrying one extra paisa is explainable, whereas a distribution that shifts paise as unrelated lines change would make the same month reconcile differently on two runs. The line that carries it is flagged in the API so a screen can say so rather than look wrong.
(7) Manpower cost splits the WHOLE month first and then keeps only the project lines. Splitting just the project lines would decide the remainder against a different denominator, so two projects' figures would depend on which report ran. Only SUBMITTED months contribute — a draft is not yet the employee's answer, and including one would make a project's cost change as somebody types — and the count of excluded drafts is returned alongside the total so a figure that looks low is explainable rather than merely doubted.
(8) Project scoping on the manpower report reuses `ProjectsService.list` verbatim, so a scoped Project Manager sees cost for exactly the projects they can already see (ADR-058) — never a second, divergent definition of scope.
(9) `reopen` returns a SUBMITTED month to DRAFT, requiring a reason that is audited. Nothing is reversed because nothing was posted — but the project cost derived from that month will move, so the change is recorded rather than left silent. Three permissions (catalog 126 → 129): `employee_allocation.read/manage` and `project_cost.read`, granted to Accountant and Project Manager both — unlike the posting-side keys, entering and reading an allocation is delivery work, not accounting.
Consequences: One migration, `20260831170000_phase7_05_employee_allocation` (49 total) — three tables, one enum, and three hand-added CHECKs (exactly one line target; per-line percentage in (0, 100]; submitted status and timestamp settable only together). Nothing existing is altered. `seed.ts` — 3 permissions, 6 allocation targets, `EMPLOYEE_ALLOCATION` entity type. New `apps/api/src/employee-allocations/{allocation-cost.util.ts, .util.spec.ts, employee-allocations.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}`. New `packages/contracts/src/ports/employee-allocation.port.ts` and `routers/employee-allocations.ts` plus the usual wiring; `allocation_targets` added to the four generic-master touchpoints. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/{employee-allocations,project-manpower-cost}/page.tsx` and two nav items under Employee Costing. `masters.integration-spec.ts`'s master-type count corrected 15 → 16.
Verified with: 10 new unit tests on the pure allocation util — exact 100 across awkward fractions, one-ten-thousandth-short refused in both directions, a clean split producing no remainder line, the three-way 100.00 split where the remainder genuinely fires, a tie broken identically regardless of array order, a seven-way 14.2857 split losing nothing across four different gross figures, a zero gross allocating to zero rather than erroring, and a part-filled draft previewing at what it says rather than scaling up. Plus 10 new integration tests, all passing — an unstarted month already carrying its imported gross; any day of the month collapsing to one record; per-line shares computed server-side with the project and target names resolved; both directions of not-exactly-100 refused; submit locking editing, double-submit and empty-reason reopen refused, and the reopen reason landing in the audit trail; both-targets/neither-target/repeated-target/inactive-target/zero-percentage all refused; the summary listing NOT_STARTED employees (the people the screen exists to find); manpower cost counting only submitted months with excluded drafts reported; an explicit reconciliation that project cost plus bench equals the imported total exactly; an inverted month range refused and full cross-organization isolation.
Known issues: `references/ui/` is still absent (the standing Phase 7 blocker), so both screens were built from docs/04 §13 and docs/16 §7.6's field lists rather than from `employee-effort-entry.html`, `employee-effort-summary.html` and `employee-manpower-cost.html`; per docs/16 §5 they cannot be declared UI-complete. No live browser pass. `contribution-dashboard.html`'s MERGE (a KPI/trend presentation over the same records) is deliberately not built — the Allocation Summary carries the same data in table form, and a second visual surface with no new facts is disclosed as deferred rather than invented. No export action is built, so `employee_allocation.export` / `project_cost.export` are not in the catalogue.
Supersedes: none — purely additive. Consumes ADR-094's salary import as its costing source and ADR-058's project scope verbatim; changes neither.
```

```text
ID: ADR-096
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Accepted — implementation complete and verified against a live database
Context: Slice 7.6 — introducer agreement, accrual, payment and allocation. The last of Phase 7's three verticals and the last named deliverable in the phase. `Introducer` has existed since Phase 3.2 as a deliberately minimal model (name and phone) with no money attached; `Project.introducerId` has pointed at it since Phase 3.4 and meant nothing financially. docs/04 §15 names three screens and docs/06 gives exactly two rules: accrual is Dr Introducer Commission Expense / Cr Introducer Payable, and payment "follows vendor-style payable settlement with TDS where applicable. Excess payment posts to Introducer Advance Receivable." The basis shape (PERCENT_OF_PROJECT_VALUE | FIXED_AMOUNT, with accrual as a separate manual post) was confirmed with the user during Phase 7 planning and recorded in ADR-091.
Decision:
(1) Three documents in one aggregate and one service, because they are one obligation seen at three moments: what is owed (agreement), when it became a liability (accrual), how it was settled (payment). Splitting them across services would put the payable balance — which needs all three — in no service at all.
(2) **The agreement posts nothing.** It is an entitlement, not an event. docs/06 gives accrual a journal and says nothing about an agreement, precisely because commission becomes a liability when it is accrued.
(3) **Accrual is a separate, deliberately manual act**, not an automatic consequence of the agreement. The moment commission is earned — work order, first receipt, completion — is a business judgement docs/00 does not fix, so a trigger here would be inventing an accounting policy. Several accruals per agreement are supported (staged commission is normal).
(4) Basis and figure must agree — a DB CHECK, a service check and a zod `.refine`, all three. A percentage agreement carries a rate and no fixed amount, and vice versa. Without it the two columns can hold contradictory terms and nothing in the data says which is the agreement.
(5) **An unknown entitlement stays null, never zero.** A percentage agreement on a project with no `value` has no derivable ceiling; returning `0.00` would silently cap every accrual at nothing — a validation that looks like a rule but is really a missing input. Cumulative accrual IS capped at the entitlement whenever it is derivable, and deliberately uncapped (and disclosed) when it is not.
(6) **An over-payment debits Introducer Advance Receivable, never a negative payable** — docs/04 §15 says so explicitly and docs/06 names the account. Paying ahead of accrual is ordinary; representing it by driving the payable negative would make "what do we owe introducers" wrong for every reader. A payment with no allocations at all is therefore valid and posts a pure advance. The journal is `Dr Introducer Payable (allocated) + Dr Introducer Advance Receivable (excess) / Cr TDS Payable (withheld) + Cr Bank (net)`, and a leg is simply omitted when its amount is zero rather than posted as a zero line the posting engine would reject.
(7) An allocation may only touch an accrual belonging to the SAME introducer and the same legal entity, may not exceed that accrual's outstanding balance, and may not name the same accrual twice. Without the ownership check a payment could quietly clear somebody else's liability.
(8) **Allocations are read through their payment's status, never deleted.** Reversing a payment therefore restores every accrual it settled without touching a single allocation row — the audit trail of what was allocated survives intact. Reversing an *accrual* is refused once any of it has been paid: the payment settled a liability that would no longer exist, and unwinding it here would silently rewrite a cash transaction. Reverse the payment first.
(9) Balances are five separate figures — accrued, settled, TDS, cash paid, advance — plus a payable, exactly as docs/04 §15 requires, and never one blended number, which would hide whether an introducer is owed money or holding ours. Three permissions (catalog 129 → 132): `introducer_fee.read/create/pay`, with `pay` separate because it moves money; `export` is not in the catalogue because no export action is built. Three ledger accounts: the expense under `PROJECT_COSTS` (commission is always earned on a project, and the debit carries the project dimension so project cost reporting reads it straight off the journal), the payable under the existing `ACCOUNTS_PAYABLE` group (it is exactly that, to a different counterparty), and the advance under `OTHER_RECEIVABLES` beside `EMPLOYEE_ADVANCE_RECEIVABLE` — the same shape for the same reason.
Consequences: One migration, `20260831190000_phase7_06_introducer_fee` (50 total) — four tables, two enums, and six hand-added CHECKs (basis matches its figure; positive accrual; positive allocation; the payment's own arithmetic `gross = tds + net` with advance never exceeding gross; and reversal fields settable only with `REVERSED` status on both accrual and payment). Nothing existing is altered. `seed.ts` — 3 permissions, 3 ledger accounts, 3 entity types. New `apps/api/src/introducer-fees/{introducer-fees.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}`. New `packages/contracts/src/ports/introducer-fee.port.ts` and `routers/introducer-fees.ts` plus the usual wiring. New `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/introducer-fees/{shared.tsx, page.tsx, payments/new/page.tsx}` — one page with Balances/Agreements/Accruals/Payments tabs plus a dedicated payment screen — and a nav item under a new "Introducer" group.
Verified with: 14 new integration tests, all passing against the live database — entitlement derived from both bases and a second agreement for the same introducer/project pair refused while a second introducer on the same project is allowed; a valueless percentage project leaving the entitlement null rather than zero; every basis/figure mismatch, an inactive introducer and an out-of-range rate refused; the accrual journal with the project dimension on the expense leg; cumulative accrual capped at the entitlement but uncapped when it is unknowable; idempotent accrual replay plus `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`; a TDS settlement with all four legs asserted by account and the payable balance moving by exactly the allocated amount; an 8,000 payment against a 5,000 accrual splitting into payable and advance legs; a pure advance posting only two lines with no payable leg at all; over-allocation, over-payment-of-an-accrual, a duplicate allocation line, a foreign introducer's accrual and TDS above gross all refused; payment reversal restoring the accrual while keeping the allocation row; accrual reversal refused while paid and permitted once the payment is reversed; and the five separate balance figures with the payable total reconciling to the per-row sum. Cross-organization isolation across agreements, balances and payment detail.
Known issues: `references/ui/` is still absent (the standing Phase 7 blocker), so all screens were built from docs/04 §15's contract text rather than `introducer-all.html`, `introducer-fee-add.html` and `introducer-fee-payment.html`; per docs/16 §5 they cannot be declared UI-complete. No live browser pass. `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` still cannot authenticate, so no fresh-database replay from zero. An agreement has no deactivate/edit action yet (created and then accrued against; `isActive` exists and is honoured but nothing flips it) — disclosed as deferred rather than half-built. No GST on introducer commission: docs/06 gives it no tax treatment, so none is invented.
Also fixed while verifying this phase, and worth recording because it removes a recurring blocker rather than a one-off failure: **three integration specs hardcoded GSTINs, and `uq_gst_registrations_gstin` is globally unique** — not organization-scoped and not filtered by `deleted_at`. So a hardcoded GSTIN in a fixture is a process-wide singleton: if a spec's `beforeAll` throws for any reason its `afterAll` never runs, the row survives, and **every subsequent run of that spec fails during setup** with "this GSTIN is already registered" — a failure that looks like a regression in whatever was changed last and has nothing to do with it. This is one concrete mechanism behind the flakiness class ADR-077 records. A new `apps/api/src/testing/gstin.test-util.ts` builds a run-unique GSTIN from a caller-supplied state code (the only part these fixtures assert on, since it is what makes a supply intra- or inter-state) plus a randomised remainder matching the database's own format CHECK; `vendor-payments`, `credit-debit-notes` and `project-financials` now use it. `organization.integration-spec.ts` deliberately keeps its literals — there the GSTIN values ARE the subject under test (duplicate rejection, format validation). Chosen over deleting the orphaned rows, which would have fixed the symptom once and left the mechanism in place.
Also fixed in this slice (found by its own full-suite regression, not by the slice's code): **`PostingService.getLedgerActivity` had no total order.** It sorted only by `journalEntry.accountingDate`, and several postings share a date routinely — so Postgres was free to return them in any order and the running balance the Chart of Accounts' "View Ledger" drawer (ADR-065) shows could differ between two reads of the same ledger. The spec's own running-balance test passed in isolation and failed under load, which is what surfaced it. Now ordered by `accountingDate`, then `journalEntry.createdAt`, then `id`; because ids are UUID v7 the tiebreak is chronological rather than arbitrary. A read-path correctness fix with no schema, posting or API change.
Supersedes: none — purely additive. Gives `Introducer` (Phase 3.2) and `Project.introducerId` (Phase 3.4) their first financial meaning; changes neither.
```

```text
ID: ADR-097
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Accepted — implementation complete and verified against a live database
Context: The user reviewed the finished Phase 7 and made a correct architectural criticism: the eight delivered features are siloes. Each slice was built against its own docs/04 section, verified in isolation and shipped green, but "does this connect to what already exists" was never asked — and no exit criterion asks it either. The evidence is measurable: **every Phase 7 route had zero inbound links from any non-Phase-7 page** (`/products`, `/inventory`, `/salary-imports`, `/employee-allocations`, `/project-manpower-cost`, `/introducer-fees` — 0 each, against 8 for `/projects`). Worse, one number on screen was wrong: `docs/04:229` requires the project cost view to roll up "posted expenses, vendor bills, salary allocation, inventory consumption and introducer accrual", Phase 7 added those last three, and none reached it — so a project could carry material, manpower and commission cost on its journal lines while its Spend and Financials tabs reported none of it. ADR-086 had deferred exactly those categories "until their own source modules exist"; they exist now, so this is a scheduled follow-up coming due rather than new scope. Sequencing and two design forks were confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion.
Decision:
(1) **`getExpenseLedger` gains MATERIAL, MANPOWER and INTRODUCER**, and `ProjectFinancialsService` gains the same three in recognized expense. Every prior deferral is now closed; Server and Subscription deliberately do NOT become their own categories, because a recurring cycle's payment posts through `DirectExpensesService` and already arrives as `DIRECT_EXPENSE` — labelling it twice would split one ledger source or double-count it.
(2) All three enter recognized expense at **face value**, passing `ZERO` to `recognizedExpenseAmount` rather than bypassing it, so the "one shared formula" invariant that util asserts still holds. Each is genuinely zero for its own reason: consumption value is already net of recoverable ITC (the INVENTORY debit carried taxable value plus *ineligible* tax only), manpower carries no GST, and introducer commission has no modelled tax treatment (ADR-096).
(3) **Queried directly through Prisma, not by injecting the services** — `EmployeeAllocationsService` already injects `ProjectsService` for project scoping, so the reverse edge would be circular. This is precisely the reasoning ADR-086(2) used for `VendorBillsService`, now load-bearing a second time.
(4) Manpower reuses `allocateSalary` (ADR-095) and splits the WHOLE month before keeping the project's lines — splitting only the project lines would decide the rounding remainder against a different denominator, and the Spend ledger, the Financials tab and the Manpower report would each report a different figure.
(5) **Manpower is one row per month, not per employee-month.** The Spend table renders `pagination={false}`; per-employee rows would put hundreds on one unpaginated table. The per-employee breakdown lives one click away on the new Manpower tab.
(6) `ProjectExpenseRow.linkId` becomes nullable and a `NONE` link type is introduced. Manpower has no document to open — allocation posts no journal — and pretending otherwise would mean a View button that lies. `detailPath` gains an exhaustive switch instead of its previous silent fallthrough, which would have routed every new link type to the employee-claim page.
(7) **Three new project tabs** (Material, Manpower, Introducer), each a thin project-scoped mount of an existing procedure that already accepted a `projectId` filter — the `security-instrument-tab.tsx` pattern. The Introducer tab pre-fills the introducer from `Project.introducerId` (populated since Phase 3.4 and previously read by nothing) and warns when an agreement names someone else; the global agreement form follows the project's introducer too.
(8) **Consolidation.** `/project-manpower-cost` is removed — 163 lines, one query on the *same tRPC router* as allocations, no `useRouter`, faked pagination, zero links in or out — and becomes a second tab on `/employee-allocations`. The one-item "Introducer" nav group is deleted and `/introducer-fees` moves into CRM beside the Introducers master it is about. `introducer-fees/page.tsx` (571 lines holding four tab components) is split into sibling `*-tab.tsx` files, matching `projects/[id]`. `/products` and `/inventory` stay separate (the user's call — two separately-documented prototype screens, §8 and §14, and the reference bundle is missing so the split cannot be verified); they gain cross-links instead.
(9) The hardcoded `Location: Central Store` tile — the only literal-valued `KpiCard` in the app — is replaced with a real count. `/salary-imports` gains a detail drawer: committed rows were previously unviewable, and `journalEntryId` was carried in the row type but rendered nowhere.
Consequences: No migration. `packages/contracts/src/ports/project.port.ts` (category union, `ProjectExpenseLinkType`, nullable `linkId`, port signature) and `project-financials.port.ts` (three fields on both summary types). `apps/api/src/projects/{projects.service.ts, project-financials.service.ts}` and `packages/contracts/src/routers/projects.ts`. `packages/database/prisma/seed.ts` (E1 below). Frontend: three new `projects/[id]/*-tab.tsx` files, a descriptor-driven refactor of `expense-tab.tsx`'s hand-written category chips, the introducer split, the allocations hub, the removed route, nav changes and six cross-links.
Also fixed — two defects found while planning, neither introduced by this work:
**E1. `introducer_fee.*` was granted to the wrong role.** All three keys landed on `PROJECT_MANAGER_PERMISSIONS` instead of `ACCOUNTANT_PERMISSIONS` (a seed-edit anchor matched the wrong block in ADR-096). A Project Manager could **disburse money** via `introducer_fee.pay` and the Accountant could not see introducer fees at all — out of line with every other money-moving key in the phase. Moved to Accountant; PM keeps `read` only, which the new Introducer tab needs.
**E2. Role grants were entirely unasserted, and the seed could not correct one.** `roles.integration-spec.ts` checked only the *size* of the permission catalog, never which role holds which key — a count assertion cannot catch a misplaced grant, because the count is unchanged. That is how E1 shipped. Worse, the seed's role-permission loop was **additive only**: re-running it after fixing the definition changed nothing, so a mis-grant was permanent in any database that had ever run the buggy seed. The loop now reconciles — deleting grants no longer in the definition — scoped to SYSTEM roles only, since a custom role's permissions belong to administrators. New per-role assertions cover every money-moving key and were confirmed to FAIL against the pre-fix seed before passing after it.
Verified with: 2 new tests in `projects.integration-spec.ts` (35/35 in file) — the three streams' row shapes, link types and the null manpower link; a REVERSED consumption, a REVERSED accrual and a DRAFT allocation each excluded; 60% of an 80,000 gross reaching the project while the bench 40% does not; and a permission-exclusion test proving an unreadable category leaves `totalAmount` and `categoryTotals`, not just `rows`. `project-financials.integration-spec.ts` (11/11) asserts the three new fields exactly and `recognizedProjectExpense` rising 198,720 → 229,720. 2 new role-grant tests in `roles.integration-spec.ts` (9/9). Full workspace typecheck and lint clean 14/14.
Known issues: `references/ui/` is still absent, so the three new tabs and every consolidation decision were made from docs/04's contract text rather than the prototypes — the standing Phase 7 UI blocker applies to this work too. No live browser pass. No Profit/Margin tile is added: cost is complete now, but docs/04's report rules forbid a blended profit without a named accounting basis, which belongs to Phase 8.
Supersedes: closes ADR-086's four deferred categories. Corrects ADR-096's permission grant. Does not change any posting rule — every figure added here was already on the journal, merely unread.
```

```text
ID: ADR-098
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Accepted — implementation complete and verified against a live database
Context: The user's second criticism of Phase 7, and the sharper one: "for salary why individual every month — it should be automatic with maintained history from employee's current 60000, then revise the from-to". Slice 7.4 required a full CSV every month for every employee, even the ones whose salary had not changed, and kept no history at all — `Employee.displayedSalaryAmount` was a single mutable figure the schema itself labels "explicitly non-authoritative". This is not a new idea: **ADR-047 recorded the user asking for exactly this and deferred it — "a dedicated compensation-history table remains deferred to when Salary Import is built."** Salary Import was built in ADR-094. This ADR is that deferral coming due, and it reverses ADR-094's own closing sentence, which said `displayedSalaryAmount` "stays a display figure and is never used as a costing input". Confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion: the worker generates the draft automatically rather than offering an on-demand button only.
Decision:
(1) **New `EmployeeSalaryRevision`** — `grossAmount`, `effectiveFrom`, `effectiveTo` (null = current), `reason`. Revising never edits a row: it closes the open one by setting `effectiveTo` to the day before the new period starts, and inserts a new open row, in one transaction. The two periods abut without overlapping, so "what did this person earn in March" has exactly one answer forever. This is `RecurringCycle`'s append-and-supersede posture (ADR-088) applied to a value rather than a schedule.
(2) **At most one open revision per employee**, via a hand-added PARTIAL unique index `WHERE effective_to IS NULL` — Prisma cannot express a filtered unique, the same reason `uq_financial_years_one_current` and `uq_salary_imports_entity_month_posted` are hand-written. It makes "the current salary" a single unambiguous row rather than a convention the service must maintain.
(3) `effectiveFrom`/`effectiveTo` rather than the house `startDate`/`endDate`, because "effective from" is the domain term for a compensation revision and the distinction from a plain date range is the point of the model. A deliberate, documented departure.
(4) **`Employee.displayedSalaryAmount` survives but changes owner.** It is backfilled into each employee's first revision (in the seed, not the migration, so ids stay application-generated UUID v7 per CLAUDE.md — and so it is idempotent), then becomes a read-cache of the current revision with the revision service as its **only** writer. The plain salary field is removed from the employee edit form; it remains on create, where it seeds the first revision. It stops being a second truth not because it is derived but because nothing else can write it — the same discipline `InventoryMovement` uses for its stamped running balance.
(5) **`SalaryImport` gains a DRAFT status** and `journalEntryId` becomes nullable, because a draft has posted nothing. Four constraints follow, each hand-added: a journal exists exactly when the status is not DRAFT; the reversal-fields CHECK is *replaced* (the old one predates DRAFT and would reject every draft); and a second partial unique gives at most one DRAFT per legal entity per month alongside the existing POSTED rule. The enum change ships in **its own migration** — Postgres will not let a new enum value be used in the transaction that adds it, and the CHECKs reference `'DRAFT'` directly.
(6) **The flow becomes generate → review → commit.** `commit` now promotes an existing DRAFT rather than creating from transient input, so the rows that were reviewed and the rows that post are the same rows by construction — a stronger guarantee than ADR-094's shared-`resolveRows` invariant, which relied on running the same validation twice over the same payload. CSV upload survives as `replaceDraftRows`, re-scoped to "apply corrections to this draft" (a joiner, unpaid leave, a bonus). `discardDraft` deletes a draft outright, which is safe precisely because nothing was posted — a posted month is reversed, never deleted.
(7) **A new worker job generates the previous month's draft hourly.** Only the month that has actually ENDED is drafted: a salary month is not knowable while it is still running, and drafting the current one would invite committing a month still changing. It skips any month that already has a batch (draft or posted), so every later tick is a cheap no-op the partial unique indexes make safe rather than merely likely. It refuses to create an EMPTY draft — no salaries on record is a setup problem to report, not a document to invent, and an empty batch would fail the head-count CHECK anyway. Recipients resolve to every user whose effective permissions include `salary_import.commit`, never a hardcoded role, exactly as ADR-064 established for ACCOUNT_TEAM.
(8) No new permission: a salary revision is employee master data, so it reuses `employee.read`/`employee.manage`. The catalog stays at 132.
Consequences: Two migrations (52 and 53) — the enum alone, then the table, the nullable column, four CHECK/index changes. `seed.ts` gains the backfill. New `apps/api/src/employees/employee-salary-revisions.service.ts` and `.integration-spec.ts`; `salary-imports.service.ts` gains `generateDraft`/`replaceDraftRows`/`discardDraft` and a rewritten `commit`; `SalaryImportsModule` gains an `EmployeesModule` edge. New `apps/worker/src/salary-draft-generator.ts` and `.integration-spec.ts`, registered on an hourly tick. `packages/contracts` — `EmployeeSalaryRevisionRow`/`Port`, two new employee procedures, four reshaped salary-import procedures. Frontend: a Salary history section with a dated Revise action in the employee drawer, and a rewritten `/salary-imports/new` built around Generate.
Verified with: 5 new revision tests — the first salary opening a period and caching onto the employee; a second closing the first at exactly the day before, with the old figure unchanged; same-day, earlier and pre-joining revisions all refused; month-boundary resolution across June/July plus "no salary yet" returning absent rather than zero; cross-organization isolation. 5 new worker tests — `previousMonthStart` across a year boundary and on the last instant of a month; the drafted month excluding an inactive employee who *does* have a salary; a second tick being a true no-op; an already-POSTED month skipped; and an organization with no salaries producing nothing. 9 rewritten salary-import tests covering the new draft lifecycle end to end. `employee-allocations` continues to pass unchanged, which is the point: it reads `SalaryImportLine`, so a generated batch is transparent to manpower cost.
Known issues: `references/ui/` still absent, so the Salary section and the rewritten import screen were built from the contract text, not the prototypes. No live browser pass. A revision's own `effectiveFrom` cannot be corrected after the fact — that is a different operation from a raise, needing its own rules about already-posted months, and is disclosed as deferred rather than half-built. The worker notifies through the outbox; whether those notifications are actually delivered depends on the dispatcher's configured channel, unchanged by this work.
Supersedes: discharges ADR-047's explicit deferral of a compensation-history table. Reverses ADR-094's closing statement that `displayedSalaryAmount` is never a costing input — it is now the cached head of the revision chain, and the revision chain IS the costing input. ADR-094's other decisions (per-employee journal lines, the one-POSTED-batch-per-month partial index, whole-month-or-nothing) are unchanged.
```

```text
ID: ADR-099
Date: 2026-09-01
Status: Accepted — implemented; production build blocked by an unrelated upstream bug (see Known issues)
Context: The user's third and sharpest criticism: "everything is unambiguous and separated — rethink again and connect all and simple flow." Phase 7 shipped five correct cost mechanisms that a person could not actually operate. The project detail screen had grown to 14 tabs, three of them cost, each with an invisible chain of prerequisites and no statement of where the user stood in it. The live database made the failure concrete: July 2026 salary was POSTED at 60,000, no allocation had ever been started, and the Manpower tab therefore read 0.00 with no link and no explanation — indistinguishable from "this project genuinely has no manpower cost". Three separate bugs the user reported that week (`?tab=introducer` dead, an empty item dropdown, `/journal-entries/null`) shared one cause: runtime-only mismatches invisible to TypeScript, lint, build and 540 backend tests.
Decision:
(1) **One `Costs` tab replaces three.** `material-tab.tsx` and `introducer-tab.tsx` fold into a new `costs-tab.tsx` as sections; each section still gates on its own read key (`inventory.read`, `introducer_fee.read`) and the tab appears only if at least one would.
(2) **`manpower-tab.tsx` is deleted outright** — the user's explicit call: manpower is paid through salary, and per-project salary allocation is not something this business does, so the project stops asking for it. The server-side rollup is deliberately KEPT: it is correct, costs nothing when empty, and if anyone ever does allocate, project cost stays right. What changes is that the UI stops presenting an unfilled step as though it were pending work — the Financials tab hides the Manpower row when it is zero, and the Spend ledger's Manpower chip appears only if allocations actually exist.
(3) **`material` / `manpower` / `introducer` survive as `?tab=` ALIASES resolving to `costs`.** A dead `?tab=` was one of the three bugs; fixing it by creating three more would be absurd. Old links and bookmarks keep working.
(4) **Every empty state names the missing step**, through a new shared `components/next-step.tsx` generalising the one place that already did this well. The governing principle, stated once so it can be applied elsewhere: *a zero meaning "nothing happened" and a zero meaning "you have not finished setting up" must never look the same.* Detection reuses procedures that already existed — `inventoryItems.getCount` (present since 7.1 and, until now, called by nothing) and `inventory.listStock({inStockOnly:true})` — so this adds no backend surface.
(5) **Actions carry their context.** `/inventory/consumptions/new` accepts `?projectId=`, and when seeded it REPLACES the project picker with read-only text plus a hidden `Form.Item` rather than disabling the picker — a disabled AntD `Select` does not submit its value. Its header and post-success redirect both return to the project. Unseeded behaviour is unchanged, so the standalone screen still works. This copies `payment-requests/new` and `vendor-payments/new` rather than inventing a fourth idiom.
(6) **Contract row types are aliased, not re-declared**, in the three Phase 7 shared files (`salary-imports`, `inventory`, `introducer-fees`). This is the actual fix for the `/journal-entries/null` bug: the local copy said `journalEntryId: string` while the contract said `string | null`, so both Journal buttons navigated unconditionally, and a two-state ternary rendered a DRAFT batch as "Reversed" in red. Aliasing turns the next such drift into a compile error. **43 web files still hand-redeclare their row types and only 6 import them from `@zitaware/contracts`** — the remaining 40 are deliberately left alone, since none is currently broken and a wholesale conversion is a separate change with its own risk.
Consequences: No migration and no permission change. Frontend only: `costs-tab.tsx` added; `material-tab.tsx` and `manpower-tab.tsx` deleted; `introducer-tab.tsx` exports `IntroducerSection`; `projects/[id]/page.tsx` gains `TAB_ALIASES` and drops two tab keys; `expense-tab.tsx` derives its populated-category list from an unfiltered read of the SAME query (identical cache key on mount, so no extra request) rather than from an effect; `financials-tab.tsx` conditionally hides one row; the consumption page gains a `Suspense` boundary and the seeded-project path; `components/next-step.tsx` is new.
Verified with: `turbo run typecheck lint` clean across all 14 workspace tasks, after removing a stray untracked scratch file that was failing `no-console`. Full API suite green. The `roles.integration-spec.ts` comment naming the three old tabs was corrected in the same change, so the test text does not outlive the tabs it describes.
Known issues: `references/ui/` is still absent, so this was designed from docs/04 and docs/15 text rather than the paired 1440x900 screenshots — the standing Phase 7 UI blocker. No live browser pass. **The three bugs that motivated (6) were all runtime-only, and aliasing closes only the type-drift class; the allowlist and schema-max classes still need a browser test that loads a screen and asserts its controls populate.** Playwright is already a dependency and only its browser binary is missing (~150 MB); that test is not in this change.
Supersedes: reorganises ADR-097's three-tab layout without changing any figure it computes. No posting rule is touched.
```

```text
ID: ADR-100
Date: 2026-09-01
Status: Accepted — implementation complete and verified against a live database
Context: A real hole found while planning ADR-099, not a feature request. `SALARY_PAYABLE` was only ever **credited** — by the salary import — and nothing in the codebase debited it. "What we owe staff" therefore grew every month with no way to record paying it, and the liability could only ever increase. docs/00 excludes *payroll processing* (attendance, leave, computing pay) and docs/06 is simply silent on settlement; on review this is an oversight rather than an exclusion, because settling a liability the system already recorded is a bank disbursement of exactly the same shape as the vendor and introducer payments that do exist. The user was asked and confirmed adding it.
Decision:
(1) **New `SalaryPayment`**, mirroring `IntroducerPayment` (ADR-096) closely enough that the shape is already proven: legal entity, the `salaryImportId` being settled, bank account, date, optional mode and reference, `grossAmount`/`tdsAmount`/`netAmount`, journal, `POSTED|REVERSED` with reversal fields, audit and `version`.
(2) **Journal: Dr Salary Payable (gross) / Cr TDS Payable (tds) / Cr Bank (net)**, omitting any leg whose amount is zero — the posting engine rejects a line that is neither debit nor credit. TDS is *withheld rather than paid*: it moves from one liability to another and does not leave the bank, which is why gross is not the cash figure.
(3) **Hand-added CHECKs** enforce the row's own arithmetic in the row rather than only in the service: `gross > 0`, `tds >= 0`, `net >= 0`, `gross = tds + net`, plus the reversal-fields pairing every other posted document carries.
(4) **Cumulative payments may not exceed the month's `totalGrossAmount`**, and only a POSTED import can be paid — a DRAFT has recognised no liability and a REVERSED month's has already been unwound, so paying either would debit a payable that was never credited. The paying bank account must belong to the same legal entity that recognised the cost.
(5) **Outstanding is DERIVED, never stored**: `totalGrossAmount - sum(POSTED payments)`, and '0.00' for any batch that is not POSTED. Reversing a payment therefore restores the balance without editing a single figure — the same discipline ADR-096 used for introducer accruals.
(6) **Deliberately per-month, not per-employee.** The import already posts one payable line PER employee, each stamped with `employeeId`, so the ledger can still answer "what is owed to this person" and per-employee settlement stays possible later. Doing it now would mean an allocation UI for something that leaves the bank as a single transfer. Disclosed as a boundary, not a gap.
(7) **New permission `salary_import.pay`** (catalog 132 → 133), Accountant only — its own narrow key because it moves money, the same rule that separates `introducer_fee.pay` from `introducer_fee.create`. Idempotency-keyed like every other money-moving command.
Consequences: One migration (54) — the `SalaryPaymentStatus` enum, the `salary_payments` table, four FKs, four indexes and two CHECKs. `seed.ts` gains the key in the catalogue and in `ACCOUNTANT_PERMISSIONS`. `salary-imports.service.ts` gains `pay`, `reversePayment` and `listPayments`, and `SalaryImportSummary` gains `paidAmount`/`outstandingAmount`. Three new procedures on the salary-imports router. Frontend: an Outstanding column and a Pay action on `/salary-imports`, a pay modal that sends gross and TDS and lets the SERVER derive the net and every ledger line, and a payments table with per-payment reversal in the detail drawer. No new nav item.
Verified with: 6 new integration tests — the three legs asserted BY ACCOUNT (105000.50 debit to Salary Payable, 5000.50 credit to TDS Payable, 100000.00 credit to Bank); a partial payment leaving exactly 55000.50 and the next rupee refused, then the exact remainder accepted; a zero-TDS payment posting two lines and no empty third; idempotent replay returning the same payment with the paid total unmoved, and a reused key with different amounts refused; reversal restoring the full outstanding balance with the payment/reversal pair netting to zero on the bank account; and refusals for a DRAFT, a reversed month, another entity's bank account, a date before the month, and TDS above gross. Whole API suite 546/546 across 40 files. Role assertions updated to 133 and to require `salary_import.pay` on Accountant and forbid it on Project Manager; that suite is 9/9 after re-seeding.
Known issues: no live browser pass, and `references/ui/` remains absent — there is no prototype screen for salary payment at all, so its modal follows docs/15 and the existing introducer-payment screen rather than a paired screenshot. The production `next build` could not be run to completion, for a reason unrelated to this work: see docs/14's 2026-09-01 update, where a clean `HEAD` checkout with no working changes reproduces the same upstream Next.js prerender crash 3/3.
Supersedes: closes the credit-only `SALARY_PAYABLE` left by ADR-094 and unchanged by ADR-098. Adds the first posting rule docs/06 did not already name; recorded here because docs/06 is silent rather than contrary.
```

```text
ID: ADR-101
Date: 2026-09-01
Status: Accepted — implementation complete and verified against a live database
Context: The user opened the Pay modal and asked the obvious question ADR-100 had talked itself out of: "here may be coming whole staff, so why one reference?" ADR-100 settled a month as ONE anonymous figure with ONE reference number, and defended per-month settlement as a deliberate boundary on the grounds that "salary leaves the bank as a single transfer". That premise was wrong, and the reference field is what exposed it — a payroll run is several bank credits with several UTRs, and there was nowhere to put them. Worse, the modal could not even show WHO the amount covered: it asked the user to type 60,000.00 against a month with no statement of which people that settled. Confirmed with the user, who chose per-employee lines over both a bulk-only form and one payment per person.
Decision:
(1) **New `SalaryPaymentLine`** — `employeeId`, `paidAmount`, `tdsAmount`, and a per-line `referenceNumber`, unique per (payment, employee). The header keeps its own optional `referenceNumber` for the genuine bulk-file case, so both ways of paying are expressible.
(2) **The journal now posts per employee**: Dr Salary Payable / Cr TDS Payable / Cr Bank, one set PER LINE, each stamped with `employeeId`. This is the exact mirror of the credit ADR-094 already posted per employee, and it is the point of the change — "what is still owed to this person" is now answerable from both sides of the ledger instead of only the credit side. Zero legs are still omitted.
(3) **Each employee's OWN outstanding is enforced, not merely the month's total.** This is not a refinement of the old cap; it catches a case the month-level cap could not. If A is owed 40,000 of a 105,000 month, paying A 40,000.01 must be refused even though the month has 85,000 of headroom — a month-level check would have allowed it and left A's payable negative. A test asserts exactly that scenario.
(4) **Header totals are DERIVED from the lines server-side and never sent by the client.** A client-supplied total that disagreed with its own lines would be a second, contradictory truth; the CHECK `gross = tds + net` still holds on the header, and now cannot be satisfied by a figure nobody's lines support.
(5) **A zeroed row means "not paying this person now", not an error** — it is filtered out client-side rather than rejected, because partial payroll runs are ordinary and refusing them would force the user to delete rows to express a normal intent.
(6) The modal is now the month's own roster: every employee with something outstanding, pre-filled at their full balance, with their own Paying / TDS / Reference. The drawer's payment table expands to show who was paid under which reference, and the month's employee rows gain a per-person "Still Owed" column.
Consequences: One migration (55) — the table, two FKs, three indexes and a CHECK (`paid_amount > 0`, `tds >= 0`, `tds <= paid_amount`, TDS being withheld FROM the amount rather than added to it). `SalaryImportLineRow` gains `paidAmount`/`outstandingAmount` per employee; `SalaryPaymentSummary` gains `lines`; `pay()` takes `lines` instead of `grossAmount`/`tdsAmount`. `salary-imports.service.ts` gains `paidByEmployee` and a rewritten `pay`. The web pay modal becomes a table. No permission change — `salary_import.pay` already covers it, catalog stays at 133.
Verified with: the 6 payment tests rewritten around lines, all passing — six journal legs for a two-employee payment with every line carrying its employee and each person's own reference on their own row; the per-employee cap asserted against the case a month-level cap would miss; a zero-TDS payment still omitting the empty leg; idempotent replay and key reuse; reversal restoring both the month AND each employee, with the payment/reversal pair netting to zero on the bank; and refusals for a DRAFT, a reversed month, an employee not in the month, the same employee twice in one transfer, a wrong-entity bank account, a date before the month, and TDS above the amount settled. Whole API suite 546/546 across 40 files; workspace typecheck and lint clean 23/23.
Known issues: the production build blocker of docs/14's 2026-09-01 update is unchanged and unrelated. No live browser pass; `references/ui/` remains absent and there is no prototype screen for salary payment at all.
Supersedes: **reverses ADR-100's decision (6)**, which chose per-month settlement and recorded per-employee as a deliberate boundary. The reasoning there — that per-employee entry would be "data entry with no decision in it" — was wrong on both counts: each row carries a decision (how much, how much withheld, under which reference), and the alternative could not represent a real payroll run at all. Every other ADR-100 decision stands.
```

```text
ID: ADR-102
Date: 2026-09-01
Status: Accepted — implemented; verified by typecheck, lint and the dev server
Context: Four UI defects reported by the user in one sitting, all of them app-wide rather than local to the screen where they were noticed. Every one was invisible to typecheck, lint and 546 backend tests; three were visible the moment a browser was opened. That is now the sixth through ninth defect this week found by use rather than by any automated check.
Decision:
(1) **React `key` must never be spread into JSX.** AntD's `Form.List` hands each row a `{ key, name, ... }` object, and eight files spread the whole thing into `Form.Item` while ALSO setting `key={field.key}` on the wrapper. React 19 warns on every render and reported "two children with the same key, `0`" — the keys were being consumed by the wrong element. Fixed by destructuring at the map (`fields.map(({ key, ...field }) => ...)`) in all eight files, and by dropping `key` from the three sub-components whose prop types demanded it. A whole-app sweep confirmed no other spread carries a key.
(2) **The sidebar had two nested scroll containers.** `<aside>` carried `h-screen overflow-y-auto` and the `<nav>` inside it carried `flex-1 overflow-y-auto`, so the shell could show two scrollbars and scrolling the outer one dragged the brand header out of view. The aside is now `overflow-hidden`: the nav owns the scroll, and the header stays pinned. The nav also gets a thin, low-contrast scrollbar — the default OS scrollbar rendered a light track against `--ui-ink`, a bright stripe down the edge of a dark panel. Scoped to that one element, so tables, drawers and the page keep the platform scrollbar users expect.
(3) **Forms use the full content width, overriding docs/15 §9.2.** The product owner's call, taken with the trade-off stated: an unconstrained input at 1500px is harder to scan, which is why the cap existed. Two things made the override reasonable — the caps were applied WITHOUT centring, so every form sat hard-left with several hundred pixels of dead space down one side, reading as a fault rather than a measure; and §9.3's two-column grid already halves most fields. 28 files lose their `max-w-*` container caps. Right-aligned totals panels keep `max-w-xs`: they are summaries, not input, and were never part of the rule. **docs/15 §9.2 is amended in the same change rather than left contradicting the code**, since a design system the code disagrees with is worse than none.
(4) **Pointer cursor restored globally.** Tailwind v4's Preflight stopped forcing `cursor: pointer` on buttons and defers to the browser default of `cursor: default`, so EVERY button in the app lost its pointer — the hamburger and avatar were simply where it was noticed. Only two places in the entire codebase had compensated with an explicit class. Fixed with one rule for `button`, `[role="button"]`, `label[for]` and `summary`, plus `not-allowed` on disabled to match what AntD already does. Deliberately global rather than a class repeated on every button, so a NEW button is correct by default instead of only when someone remembers. An audit found no clickable `div`/`span`/`tr` anywhere — every interactive element is a real button, so one rule covers the app.
Consequences: No migration, no backend change, no permission change. `packages/ui/src/components/{AppShell,SidebarNavigation}.tsx`; `apps/web/src/app/globals.css` (two scoped blocks); 28 form pages; 8 `Form.List` files; 3 sub-component prop types. `docs/15-ui-design-system.md` §9.2 amended.
Verified with: workspace typecheck and lint clean 23/23; the dev server compiles and serves every touched route. The React key warning is gone by construction — the property is no longer in the spread.
Known issues: this is a visual change that has had no screenshot comparison, because `references/ui/` is still absent — the same standing Phase 7 blocker. The full-width decision in particular departs from the paired prototypes, which is now recorded in docs/15 so a later prototype comparison does not silently "correct" it back.
Supersedes: amends docs/15 §9.2's width patterns table and removes its "Do not stretch narrow forms across the full data-page width" instruction.
```

```text
ID: ADR-103
Date: 2026-09-11
Status: Accepted — implemented; verified by the new money-flow scenario test and the existing project specs
Context: The user asked for every figure to be tested "by digits" across multiple clients and projects. A hand-computed scenario — 3 clients, 5 projects, CAPEX and OPEX billing, receipts carrying every deduction type, releases, a reversal, notes, purchases, stock, claims, direct expenses, two salary months and an introducer — matched the application on every figure but one: a vendor bill's STOCK lines were counted in project cost twice. Approval debits a stock line to INVENTORY, an asset (docs/06 "Finalize vendor bill for inventory"), yet `ProjectFinancialsService` added every line of a FINALIZED bill to `purchaseExpense`, and ADR-097 then added the same stock again as MATERIAL when it was issued. The Spend ledger did the same with the bill's whole `totalAmount` as PURCHASE and the consumption as MATERIAL beside it. In the scenario, 20 boxes bought on the project's own bill and 12 issued to it showed purchase 90,000 instead of 40,000 — the full 50,000 of stock charged at purchase, then 31,200 of it charged again. Fixing it rather than keeping the numbers was the user's call via AskUserQuestion.
Decision:
(1) **`purchaseExpense` skips stock lines** (`inventoryItemId` set). A stock line reaches project cost only as MATERIAL, at its weighted-average consumption value — which is already net of recoverable ITC, so nothing is lost.
(2) **The Spend ledger's PURCHASE row sums a bill's expense lines only** (their `lineTotal`, GST included, keeping the ledger's document basis). A bill made entirely of stock lines produces no PURCHASE row: it has no project cost of its own until issued.
(3) No posting rule changes — the journal was already right; only the two read models disagreed with it.
Consequences: No migration, no permission change. `apps/api/src/projects/project-financials.service.ts` and `apps/api/src/projects/projects.service.ts`. Any project whose finalized vendor bills carry stock lines now shows lower purchase and recognized expense on its Financials tab, its client rollup and its Spend tab — on live data too, the first time this build is deployed.
Verified with: `apps/api/src/testing/money-flow-scenario/money-flow.integration-spec.ts` 23/23 — it failed on exactly this figure (P1 purchase 90,000 vs 40,000; C1 likewise) before the fix and passes after; `projects.integration-spec.ts` 35/35 and `project-financials.integration-spec.ts` 11/11 unchanged, since neither had a stock line on a bill.
Known issues: the "all-stock bill gets no Spend row" branch has no dedicated test (the scenario's stock bill also carries an expense line). The Spend row still titles itself `Bill #…` while showing only the bill's expense portion; no UI hint explains the difference from the bill's total.
Supersedes: corrects ADR-097's recognized-expense rollup for vendor bills carrying stock lines, and narrows the Spend ledger's PURCHASE row from a bill's total to its expense lines.
```

```text
ID: ADR-104
Date: 2026-09-11
Status: Accepted — implemented; verified by unit and integration tests
Context: Found while extending the money-flow scenario to CAPEX + OPEX: a milestone's billing status was never derived. `MilestonesService.toMilestoneSummary` returned the literal `'NOT_INVOICED'` whatever had been invoiced, the milestone screen's CAPEX "Billing" column was hard-wired to "Not Invoiced", and OPEX showed none — although docs/03 calls billing status "derived", docs/05 defines NOT_INVOICED / PARTIALLY_INVOICED / FULLY_INVOICED with "Do not store billing state as independently editable data", and docs/04:262 requires it on screen, separate from achievement. The user asked for it to be fixed.
Decision:
(1) **Derived on read, never stored**, from FINALIZED invoices through their existing `sourceEventId` / `sourceInstallmentId`. A DRAFT has billed nothing yet and a VOID was undone, so neither counts. No migration.
(2) A milestone bills in units — a CAPEX pool's events, an OPEX schedule's installments. A closed unit (a cancelled event; a skipped or cancelled installment) no longer expects an invoice, so it cannot hold a milestone back from FULLY_INVOICED; one invoiced before it closed still counts. The pure `deriveBillingStatus` util holds the rule.
(3) **Counted by units, not by amount.** Each unit has at most one non-void invoice (the partial unique indexes on `invoices`), so the count is exact; an amount basis would compare free-form invoice lines to the schedule and invent a tolerance docs do not give.
(4) Contract: `MilestoneEventSummary` gains `billingStatus` and `invoiceNumber`; `MilestoneInstallmentSummary` gains `invoiceNumber`; `MilestoneSummary.billingStatus` becomes real and gains `invoicedCount` / `billableCount` — the "progress" docs/04 asks for on OPEX.
(5) UI (`projects/[id]/milestone-tab.tsx`): the CAPEX Billing column shows the derived tag and the invoice number; OPEX rows gain a Billing column ("Partly invoiced · 2 of 36 invoiced"); each installment shows its invoice number. "Invoiced", not "raised", per docs/04.
Consequences: `packages/contracts/src/ports/milestone.port.ts`; `apps/api/src/milestones/{milestones.service.ts, milestone-billing.util.ts(+spec)}`; `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/milestone-tab.tsx`. Every method that returns a milestone, event or installment summary now carries derived billing, not just the list.
Verified with: 6 unit tests on the rule (none/partial/full, a closed uninvoiced unit ignored, a unit invoiced before closing still counted, empty); the money-flow scenario asserts every CAPEX event of three projects — including P4's Installation, NOT invoiced because its invoice was VOIDED — and both OPEX schedules at 2 of 12 and 2 of 36 with the right invoice numbers. Full apps/api integration suite green.
Known issues: no browser pass, and `references/ui/` is still absent, so the new columns were placed from docs/04's text rather than the paired screenshot. A CAPEX pool's own pool-level status is computed but not shown, since the screen lists events, which carry their own.
Supersedes: none — replaces a placeholder the docs never allowed.
```

```text
ID: ADR-105
Date: 2026-09-11
Status: Accepted — implemented; verified by the full apps/api and apps/worker integration suites
Context: Integration specs ran against `DATABASE_URL` — the shared development database. A failed run's cleanup never runs, so its organization stays in the data people work with (the money-flow scenario left two before its cleanup was made failure-proof). Separately, three specs (receipts, project-financials, credit-debit-notes) created only a 2026 calendar-year financial year while finalizing credit notes, which post on today's date (ADR-070 decision 6): every one of them would fail from 1 January 2027. The user asked for both to be fixed.
Decision:
(1) **A dedicated `TEST_DATABASE_URL`** (`zitaware_crm_test` on the same server). `apps/api` and `apps/worker` `vitest.integration.config.ts` point `DATABASE_URL` at it and refuse to run if it is unset or names the `DATABASE_URL` / `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` database. The root `.env` is loaded automatically, so integration runs no longer need `set -a; source .env`.
(2) A vitest globalSetup (`packages/database/scripts/integration-test-database.mjs`) runs `prisma migrate deploy` and the idempotent seed first (about 5 s), under a Postgres advisory lock because turbo runs both apps at once.
(3) **Worker integration files run one at a time.** Worker jobs sweep every organization — the outbox dispatcher claims the oldest due events of any organization — so a job under test in one file claimed the rows another file was asserting on. The dispatcher spec also parks other organizations' due events before it starts, and the alert-opener and salary-draft specs now delete their notifications before their outbox events (their cleanup crashed when the dispatcher had already turned those events into notifications — how the residue that exposed this got there).
(4) `extendFinancialYearsThroughToday` (apps/api/src/testing) adds consecutive non-current years after a fixture's own until one covers today; the three specs call it.
(5) The scenario purge deletes tables in foreign-key order read from `pg_constraint`, so a normal purge raises no errors.
Consequences: every developer needs `TEST_DATABASE_URL` in `.env` (in `.env.example`; docs/14). The demo loader (`demo:money-flow`) still writes to `DATABASE_URL` by design — its purpose is data to click through. CI is unaffected: it has never run integration tests.
Verified with: apps/api integration 41 files / 580 tests green on `zitaware_crm_test`; apps/worker 4 files / 24 tests green three runs in a row with a leftover organization deliberately still present; 3 unit tests for the year helper with a faked clock (nothing added in 2026; 2027 and 2028 added in March 2028; an April–March year kept aligned across a leap day); the development database carries no test organizations afterwards.
Known issues: the test database is not reset between runs, so residue from an interrupted run accumulates there — harmless to real data, and the dispatcher spec now tolerates it. CI still runs no integration tests.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-106
Date: 2026-09-11
Status: Accepted — implemented; verified by a full production build and both page checks against it
Context: Phase 7 could not be closed while `next build` failed at `/_global-error` with "Cannot read properties of null (reading 'useContext')". ADR-043 (8), ADR-053 and docs/14 recorded it as an unfixed upstream Next.js 16.x bug, with a retry loop, an `experimental.staticGenerationMinPagesPerWorker` mitigation and, from 2026-09-01, "fails every attempt" on this machine. A production release on 2026-09-11 showed the real cause: the build log said `You are using a non-standard "NODE_ENV" value`, and printed React's development-only key warnings during a production build. `.env.example` sets `NODE_ENV=development`; any `.env` / `.env.production` copied from it and sourced with `set -a` exports that into `next build`. Reproduced both ways on the same code: `NODE_ENV=development next build` fails every time with exactly the reported crash, `NODE_ENV=production next build` passes. ADR-043's clean-checkout bisect could not see it because the variable lived in the shell, not the tree — and its `--debug-prerender` probe forces development mode itself. The same release exposed a second build fault: each package's incremental `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` sat beside `dist/`, so turbo cached and restored `dist/` without it; a `dist/` older than its tsbuildinfo made `tsc` emit nothing and turbo then cached that stale output, which is how a release built `apps/web` against contracts lacking a router it imported. The user asked for both to be fixed so Phase 7 can close.
Decision:
(1) `apps/web`'s `build` script is `NODE_ENV=production next build`. The build can no longer inherit the shell's value. `.env.production` is not changed to production instead, because it is sourced before `npm ci`, and `NODE_ENV=production` would make npm skip devDependencies (turbo, typescript, eslint).
(2) The retry loop, the `staticGenerationMinPagesPerWorker` mitigation and every "upstream bug" claim are removed from `next.config.ts`, docs/14 and docs/11; this ADR supersedes ADR-043 (8) and ADR-053's diagnosis. The custom `global-error.tsx` / `not-found.tsx` stay — they are ordinary pages.
(3) Every package's and apps/worker's `tsBuildInfoFile` is `dist/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo`, so turbo caches and restores it together with the output it describes, and deleting `dist/` resets both. apps/api already kept it there (nest `deleteOutDir`).
```
