```text
(4) The routine release runbook gains the seed step — the seed is the only thing that adds new permissions, which is why Salary Imports was missing from the live menu — and docs/14's "Seeding" section now says what the seed really does (resets system roles, re-creates starter roles and the bootstrap admin if absent, prunes undeclared permissions) instead of "only creates rows".
Consequences: `apps/web/{package.json, next.config.ts}`; `packages/{accounting,config,contracts,database,testing,ui}/tsconfig.json`; `apps/worker/tsconfig.json`; docs/11, docs/14. No application code, no migration, no permission change.
Verified with: `npx turbo run build --force` with `NODE_ENV=development` exported in the shell — 9/9 tasks, all 63 static pages generated, zero "non-standard NODE_ENV" warnings; every package's tsbuildinfo written under `dist/` and none at the package root. The resulting production build, started as PM2 would (`node dist/main`, `next start` with NODE_ENV=production), passed `check:routes`: 79 pages passed, 0 failed, 4 skipped for want of demo records.
Known issues: none for the build. A server whose `.env.production` still says `NODE_ENV=development` is now harmless for the build, and PM2's `env_production` already overrides it at runtime.
Supersedes: ADR-043 item (8) and ADR-053's correction, which attributed this crash to Next.js.
```

```text
ID: ADR-107
Date: 2026-09-11
Status: Accepted
Context: Every Phase 7 deliverable and exit criterion is met, and the close-out items are done (ADR-106: production build fixed; a live browser pass of every page, 79 passing with zero console errors; the shadow database reachable with a clean migration replay). The one item left is docs/16 §5's screenshot comparison, which cannot be done because `references/ui/` — gitignored and kept only on disk — is absent from this checkout. Offered the choice of restoring the bundle first or accepting the comparison as a gap, the user chose to continue to Phase 8.
Decision: Phase 7 is closed with one accepted gap: no Phase 7 screen has been compared against its 1440x900 prototype screenshot. Phase 8 begins, and its screens are built from docs/04 §16's contract text and docs/15 on the same terms. When the bundle is restored, every Phase 7 and Phase 8 screen is compared and any required correction is made then.
Consequences: UI sign-off for Phase 7 and Phase 8 screens remains outstanding until the bundle returns. Backend, accounting and reconciliation acceptance are unaffected.
Supersedes: none — records a user decision on the standing Phase 7 UI blocker.
```

```text
ID: ADR-108
Date: 2026-09-11
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.1)
Context: Phase 8 begins (ADR-107). The user asked for all of Phase 8, built one slice at a time, with read access granted per report category and every report downloadable as Excel and PDF. docs/04 §16 requires every financial report to show legal entity, as-of date or period, currency and accounting basis; filters and export to use the same backend query; drill-down to source documents; no report with its own formula; permissioned, audited export. docs/11's exit criteria: every report names its basis, and report totals reconcile to ledger control accounts. Slice 8.1 builds the shared foundation plus Receivable Aging and Outstanding Summary.
Decision:
(1) Module. `apps/api/src/reports` with one entry point, `ReportsService` (ReportPort), behind `reports.*` tRPC procedures. Each slice adds its own report service beside `ReceivablesReportService`. Reports read posted documents and journal lines only; the one thing they write is the export's audit event.
(2) Permissions. One read key per report category, `report.<category>.read` (8.1 adds `report.receivables.read`), and one download key, `report.export`. Exporting needs the report's own read key as well, so the export key alone opens nothing. `reports.getCatalog` lists only reports whose category key the caller holds. Grants: Admin all; Accountant receivables read and export; Project Manager receivables read only, scoped to their own projects; CEO every report read through its `*.read` rule, plus export.
(3) Export. The server renders Excel (exceljs) and PDF (pdfkit) from the same computed report object the screen receives. The renderer formats and never calculates; Excel money cells are numbers with a two-decimal format so they sum. Every export writes an audit event (`REPORT_EXPORTED`, entity type `REPORT`) carrying the report key, format, filename and filters.
(4) As-of date. A document counts from its own ledger date: an invoice from its invoice date, a receipt from its receipt date, a credit/debit note from its note date, a hold/retention release from its release date. A void or reversal counts from its own journal's accounting date, so one made after the as-of date had not happened yet. The ledger figure compared against is debit minus credit over journal lines dated on or before the as-of date (optionally one legal entity), so a reversed entry and its reversal cancel, exactly as the Ledger screen computes it.
(5) Reconciliation is shown on the unrestricted view only. With a client or project filter, or for a caller without `project.read_all`, the report says why it is not compared: the receivable control accounts carry no project dimension, so a partial total has no ledger counterpart.
(6) Receivables figures. Invoice outstanding = invoice total − settled (cash plus every deduction) + debit notes − credit notes. Aging by days past the due date: Not due (before the due date), 0–30, 31–60, 61–90, 90+. Hold and retention are aged separately, from the date of the receipt that withheld them. Outstanding Summary keeps each measure separate and compares each with its own control account: invoice outstanding with ACCOUNTS_RECEIVABLE; TDS, CGST-TDS, SGST-TDS and IGST-TDS with their receivables; hold and retention with theirs; disputed penalties with DISPUTED_DEDUCTION_RECEIVABLE plus any penalty category's own disputed account. Accepted penalties and "other" deductions settle the invoice for good and are not receivables.
(7) Navigation. `NavItem.permission` may be a list (any one suffices); the single Reports item lists every category read key.
Consequences: new `apps/api/src/reports/*`; `packages/contracts/src/{ports/report.port.ts, routers/reports.ts}` plus exports, `createCallerFactory` exported for router tests; `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/reports/*`; `packages/ui` nav items and sidebar filter; seed (135 permissions, was 133). No migration. The seed must be run on every environment for the Reports menu to appear.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-109
Date: 2026-09-14
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.2)
Context: docs/04 §16 requires an Accrual Project P&L "from posted revenue/cost journal dimensions" and a Cost to Company report "from posted/direct cost and analytical salary/inventory/introducer dimensions", whose summary equals its detail. Checking which postings carry a project on their journal lines showed two gaps: (a) receipt deduction lines (accepted penalty, "other" deductions) carried no project, though each deduction belongs to an invoice and so to a project — the receipt merged deduction lines by ledger account across every invoice it settled; (b) salary is posted company-wide by design, with project manpower cost derived from monthly allocation (ADR-095).
Decision:
(1) Receipt deduction debit lines are merged by ledger account AND project, and carry that project. A receipt settling invoices of two projects now posts one line per account per project; totals, accounts and the receipt preview (still one line per account) are unchanged. Receipts posted before this change keep untagged lines — posted journals are not edited — and show under "not assigned to a project".
(2) Accrual Project P&L reads income and expense journal lines dated in the period (a new optional period start, `dateFrom`, with `asOfDate` as the end) and groups them by the line's project. Income is credit − debit on income accounts (revenue net of notes, voids and revenue deductions); expense is debit − credit on expense accounts. Lines with no project form a "Not assigned to a project" row, so projects + unassigned = the ledger's income and expense movement, which the report reconciles. A by-account table splits each account between project and unassigned lines.
(3) Manpower is shown beside each project as analytical (allocation months starting inside the period), with "contribution after manpower", but never added into the totals: salary is already in the unassigned expense.
(4) Cost to Company groups each project's expense lines by the event that posted them — vendor bill → purchases, direct expense → direct (or recurring when paid through a server/subscription cycle), claim → reimbursements, consumption → material, introducer accrual → introducer, anything else → other — with a reversal classified by the entry it reverses, plus analytical manpower. Posted project cost + unassigned posted cost reconciles to the ledger's expense movement.
(5) New category key `report.profitability.read`: Accountant, Project Manager (own projects only), CEO (by its *.read rule), Admin. Catalogue 136 permissions.
(6) Restricted views (client/project filter, or no project.read_all) show no unassigned row and no ledger comparison, as in ADR-108.
Consequences: `receipts.service.ts` journal lines; new `reports/profitability-report.service.ts`, `LedgerControlService.periodMovementByType`; contracts (ProjectPl*, CostToCompany*, COST_CATEGORIES, ReportFilters.dateFrom, ReportScope.dateFrom); two web pages and a period mode on the shared filter bar; seed. No migration.
Known issues: an "other" receipt deduction posted to an expense account is cost on Cost to Company (ledger basis) but a settlement deduction on the project Financials tab (ADR-089) — P4 in the scenario differs by that 1,000. Recurring classification is implemented but the scenario has no recurring payment, so it is covered only by its code path. Receipts posted before this change leave their deduction lines unassigned.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-110
Date: 2026-09-14
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.3)
Context: docs/04 §16 renames the Earning report to Cash Profitability ("state formula and do not equate it with accrual P&L") and requires the Business Overview to label each card accrual, cash, contract/budget or bank, with no blended profit; docs/06 "Reports" lists contract/budget performance as its own basis. Bank journal lines carry no project, so cash cannot be grouped by project from the ledger alone, and Project.budget had no consumer.
Decision:
(1) Cash Profitability reads every journal line on the ledger accounts behind registered Bank Account records in the period, nets them per journal entry, and attributes each entry to projects through the document that posted it (a reversal through the entry it reverses): receipt → Σ allocation.cashAllocated per invoice project; vendor payment → Σ allocation.cashPaid per bill project; direct expense, claim reimbursement, employee advance, EMD/security instrument lodge/return → the document's project; hold/retention release → the released deduction's invoice project; introducer payment → its net (after TDS) cash shared across the accruals it settles in proportion to gross allocated, rounded to paise with the last share taking the rounding, any advance share left unassigned. Salary payments, card settlements, unallocated cash and anything else are "not assigned". Formula, stated on screen and in exports: net cash = cash in (client receipts, hold/retention released) − cash out (every other category). Projects + not assigned = the bank accounts' movement, which it reconciles to. Cash margin = net ÷ cash in, null when no cash came in.
(2) Contract & Budget Performance (as of a date) adds no formula of its own: billed to date is the Accrual Project P&L income, cost to date is the Cost to Company total (posted + analytical manpower). Per project: billed %, remaining to bill (unclamped), budget used %, budget remaining (negative when over), over-budget flag. Totals' percentages and remaining amounts cover only projects that have a value/budget; the counts without are shown. No ledger comparison — contract value and budget are agreements, not balances.
(3) Business Overview composes the P&L, Cost to Company, Cash Profitability and Contract Performance outputs plus bank opening/movement/closing (LedgerControlService.bankMovement), each card carrying its basis; no card adds across bases. Bank cards are null on restricted views. Its reconciliation lists the P&L and cash lines together. Catalogue basis MULTIPLE ("Several bases").
(4) All three use report.profitability.read (no new permission). The filters → scope step is extracted into ReportScopeService, now shared with the profitability reports; selecting a legal entity also narrows the project list to that entity's projects.
(5) Web pages may import runtime report constants only from the client-safe entry `@zitaware/contracts/reports`; the package root evaluates the tRPC server and crashes in the browser. This had broken the 8.2 Cost to Company page unnoticed, because a render error lands in the error boundary rather than as an uncaught page error — the browser pass now also fails on the error boundary.
Consequences: new reports/{performance-report.service.ts, report-scope.service.ts, cash-attribution.util.ts}; LedgerControlService.bankMovement; contracts (CashProfitability*, ContractPerformance*, BusinessOverview*, CASH_CATEGORIES, ReportBasis MULTIPLE, `./reports` export); three web pages; money-flow scenario projects now carry budgets (P4 set over budget). No migration.
Known issues: a direct expense paid by card leaves the bank only at the card settlement, which is company-wide, so its project shows no cash out; an EMD posted to a ledger account that is not a registered Bank Account is outside the report; employee advances, security deposits and card settlements have code paths but no scenario data.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-111
Date: 2026-09-14
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.4)
Context: docs/04 §16 requires GST Output (finalized invoices and notes by registration and tax period, reconciled to output GST ledgers), GST Input (vendor bills and eligible ITC, eligible/ineligible/pending separate), GST Net (output − eligible input ± adjustments, components equal to the net and the ledger) and a GST document register that is not a return-filing engine. Direct expenses and expense claims carry no GST registration, and a claim's GST credit is stored as one unsplit figure posted to INPUT_GST_RECEIVABLE_EXPENSE.
Decision:
(1) Tax period = the calendar month of each document's own journal date; a void or reversal counts, negated, in the month of its own journal (the same as-of rule as ADR-108). Credit notes are negative on Output.
(2) Output reads invoices and credit/debit notes; Input reads finalized vendor bills (per line: eligible when itcEligible, else ineligible), posted direct expenses with GST (eligible/ineligible by itcEligible) and approved claims with a GST credit (eligible, component NOT_SPLIT; taxable = amount − credit). Pending = bills and claims currently SUBMITTED, dated on or before the end date — today's status, not a historical one — shown separately and never in the eligible totals. Documents without a registration are grouped as "No registration on document".
(3) Reconciliation per component on the unrestricted view: output CGST/SGST/IGST to OUTPUT_*_PAYABLE (credit − debit); eligible input CGST/SGST/IGST to INPUT_*_RECEIVABLE and claim credit to INPUT_GST_RECEIVABLE_EXPENSE (debit − credit), each over the period.
(4) Net per component (CGST, SGST, IGST, NOT_SPLIT) = output − eligible input + adjustments, where adjustments are lines on those seven GST accounts posted by any event other than invoice/note/bill/expense/claim (or a reversal of one), in liability direction (credit − debit). Each component's net is reconciled to its accounts' credit − debit. GST-TDS credits and set-off rules are not applied; the page says so.
(5) The register lists every tax line of invoices, credit/debit notes and vendor bills dated in the period (document date) with status as of the end date (VOID only if the void is dated by then); totals per document type exclude void documents; amounts as issued (unsigned). No ledger comparison.
(6) New category key report.gst.read (catalogue 137): Accountant, CEO (by its *.read rule), Admin; not the Project Manager — GST is company tax.
Consequences: new reports/gst-report.service.ts, LedgerControlService.periodMovementByCode; contracts (Gst* types, GST_REGISTER_DOCUMENT_TYPES); four web pages and gst-links.tsx; seed; nav. No migration.
Known issues: pending uses current status; a claim's ineligible GST is not recorded anywhere, so only its credit appears; a direct expense or claim cannot be grouped by GST registration; no GSTR/2B matching (out of scope per docs/04).
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-112
Date: 2026-09-15
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.5a)
Context: docs/04 §18 requires a new TDS Certificate/26AS Reconciliation screen ("import/record certificates and match internal deductions; partial/unmatched exception queue"), and §16 a TDS Inward report with certificate status and receivable reconciliation. docs/05 defines the TDS/GST-TDS deduction lifecycle RECORDED → CERTIFICATE_PENDING → CERTIFICATE_RECEIVED → RECONCILED. Nothing stored certificates. The user chose manual entry plus a CSV template (not a TRACES file parser), and split TDS deposits into Slice 8.5b.
Decision:
(1) New TdsCertificate aggregate (tds module): legal entity, client (deductor), source CERTIFICATE (Form 16A, GSTR-7A) or STATEMENT (Form 26AS, GST portal credit), deduction type TDS/CGST_TDS/SGST_TDS/IGST_TDS (one record per component), reference, deductor TAN/GSTIN, period, credit date, amount, status ACTIVE/CANCELLED (cancel needs a reason and no standing matches). Unique per client + source + type + reference.
(2) New TdsCertificateMatch: an amount between one certificate and one client AllocationDeduction. Never edited or deleted; unmatching stamps unmatchedAt/by and a reason. Matching validates same type, same legal entity, same client, a posted (not reversed) receipt, the amount within the certificate's unmatched balance and within the deduction's balance for that source — a deduction may be covered once by certificates and once by statement credits, independently. Matching locks the certificate and the deductions (FOR UPDATE, id order). No accounting effect: the receivables stay on the ledger.
(3) The lifecycle is derived, never stored: RECONCILED when statement matches cover the whole deduction; CERTIFICATE_RECEIVED when certificate matches do; CERTIFICATE_PENDING when neither does and the reporting period is over (calendar quarter for TDS, month for GST-TDS); RECORDED before that. A partial match is flagged, not a state. As of a date, matches and cancellations count when they happened before the next day began (UTC); certificates count from when they were recorded.
(4) CSV import: the browser splits the template (source, type, client name or GSTIN, TAN, GSTIN, reference, period start/end, credit date, amount, remarks); the server parses spellings (16A, 26AS, CGST-TDS…), ISO or DD/MM/YYYY dates and Indian-grouped amounts, resolves the client, checks duplicates in the file and the database, previews row by row, and imports all or nothing.
(5) Reports under new category key report.tds.read: TDS Inward (every client deduction with matched amounts, references, period end and lifecycle; summary by type and state; reconciled per type to TDS_RECEIVABLE and GST_TDS_RECEIVABLE_* as of the date) and TDS Certificate & 26AS Reconciliation (deducted vs certificate vs statement credits by type; exception queue of overdue deductions, partial matches, credits with unmatched balances and matches standing on reversed receipts; no ledger comparison). A restricted view shows its clients' certificates.
(6) Permissions tds_certificate.read, tds_certificate.manage, report.tds.read (catalogue 140): Accountant, CEO (reads), Admin; not Project Manager. Audit events TDS_CERTIFICATE_RECORDED/CANCELLED/MATCHED/UNMATCHED. New nav item Finance → TDS Certificates.
Consequences: migration 20260915090000_phase8_tds_certificates (two tables, three enums, CHECKs: positive amounts, period order, cancellation and unmatch stamps consistent); tds module (service, lifecycle and import utils); contracts tds-certificate.port, tdsCertificates router, TdsInward*/TdsReconciliation* report types; ReportScopeService exposes clientId; /tds-certificates page; two report pages; the money-flow scenario now records and matches five certificates.
Known issues: certificates are not attached as documents; no TRACES/GST-portal file parser; the Outstanding Summary still shows TDS "pending reconciliation" from the ledger regardless of certificate status (its figure reconciles to the ledger, which matching does not change); outward TDS deposits and certificates come in 8.5b.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-113
Date: 2026-09-15
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.5b)
Context: docs/04 §16 requires TDS Outward — "vendor/introducer TDS payable, deposited and certificate/challan status" — and docs/06 defines "TDS deposited: Dr TDS Payable / Cr Bank", but nothing recorded a deposit: TDS_PAYABLE and GST_TDS_PAYABLE_* were only ever credited. The user chose to add deposit posting in this phase.
Decision:
(1) New TdsDeposit aggregate (tds module): legal entity, bank account, deduction type (TDS or one GST-TDS component per deposit), challan number (CIN/BSR or the GST payment reference, unique per type), deposit date, status POSTED/REVERSED. Its amount is not entered: it is the total of its TdsDepositAllocation rows, each paying part or all of one withheld amount. Posting and the rows commit in one transaction (postJournalWithin) under a per-organization/legal-entity/type advisory lock, with an idempotency key; the journal is Dr the type's payable account / Cr the bank account's ledger account. Reversal reverses the journal (dated, with a reason) and marks the deposit REVERSED; allocations of a reversed deposit stop counting. Audit events TDS_DEPOSIT_POSTED / TDS_DEPOSIT_REVERSED.
(2) Withheld amounts come from the four postings that credit the payables: vendor payment deduction lines (TDS, CGST/SGST/IGST-TDS), direct expense TDS, introducer payment TDS and salary payment lines (per employee — salary TDS is included because it sits in the same account, which the report must reconcile). Allocations are polymorphic (source kind + id, no foreign key); the service validates each against the legal entity, type, a payment not reversed, and the amount still undeposited.
(3) Due date, for display and the overdue flag only: TDS by the 7th of the following month, 30 April for March; GST-TDS by the 10th of the following month.
(4) TDS Outward (report.tds.read), as of a date: every withheld amount with deposited, payable, challans, due date and overdue; per type withheld, deposited (deposits standing that date at full amount), payable, overdue; payable reconciled to each payable account. Client/project filters limit to vendor payments and direct expenses of those projects and drop the comparison.
(5) Cash Profitability gains a TAX_DEPOSIT category for tds_deposit.post bank movement (not assigned to a project).
(6) Permissions tds_deposit.read and tds_deposit.post (catalogue 142): Accountant, CEO (read), Admin; not Project Manager. Nav: Finance → TDS Deposits.
Consequences: migration 20260915120000_phase8_tds_deposits (two tables, two enums, CHECKs on amounts and reversal consistency); TdsDepositsService, due-date util; contracts tds-deposit.port, tdsDeposits router, TdsOutward* types; /tds-deposits page, reports/tds-outward page. The money-flow scenario now posts three challans (one reversed), so HDFC's balance is 14,04,127.77 and TDS Payable 8,000; the 8.3 cash and bank expectations were updated accordingly.
Known issues: issued Form 16A/16 certificates to deductees are not recorded yet (challan status is covered); interest, late fee and penalty on a challan are not modelled — a deposit pays tax only; a vendor-payment deduction's project comes from its bill.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-114
Date: 2026-09-15
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.6a)
Context: docs/04 §18 requires "Bank Transaction Import and Reconciliation: import/manual statement lines, match/unmatch receipts/payments, exception handling and period lock"; docs/05 gives a receipt's reconciliation state UNMATCHED → PARTIALLY_MATCHED → MATCHED and the bank reconciliation period OPEN → IN_PROGRESS → RECONCILED → LOCKED; docs/09 asks that the preparer not perform the final lock. Bank reconciliation had been deferred since Phase 5. The user chose CSV import with the columns mapped once per bank account. Period reconciliation and lock are Slice 8.6b.
Decision:
(1) BankStatementProfile per bank account: the header names of the date (with one declared format: YYYY-MM-DD, DD/MM/YYYY, DD-MM-YYYY, DD-MMM-YYYY, MM/DD/YYYY — never guessed), description, optional reference and balance columns, and the amount layout: separate withdrawal/deposit columns, one signed amount, or an amount with a Dr/Cr column. Versioned and audited.
(2) Import: the browser only splits the CSV into header and cells; the server maps, parses and previews every row as READY, DUPLICATE, SKIPPED (blank rows and rows whose first cell is not date-shaped, such as "Closing Balance") or ERROR, and imports all or nothing under the account's advisory lock. BankStatementLine amounts are signed (positive money in). A fingerprint of date, amount, description, reference, balance and the occurrence among identical lines in the file makes re-importing an overlapping export safe (unique per account). Lines are never edited.
(3) Matching: BankReconciliationMatch links an amount of one statement line to one bank journal line (a line on the bank account's ledger account), many-to-many. "Match selected" requires every chosen line's open amount to share one sign and the two sides to total the same; amounts are then paired in date order. Auto-match pairs a statement line with the one book line of the same open amount within five days, only when that pairing is unique in both directions — it never guesses between equal candidates. Unmatching stamps the match with who, when and why; nothing is deleted. Line status UNMATCHED / PARTIALLY_MATCHED / MATCHED is derived from standing matches.
(4) Exceptions: an unmatched statement line nothing in the books will match is marked with a reason (bank_reconciliation.manage) and approved by someone else holding bank_reconciliation.approve (SELF_APPROVAL_NOT_ALLOWED, also a database CHECK). Clearing an approved exception needs the approve key.
(5) The workspace shows a bank account's month: statement and book lines dated in it plus older lines still open (carried forward), matches, and server-computed in/out and open totals. No accounting effect anywhere in this slice.
(6) Permissions bank_reconciliation.read, .manage, .approve (catalogue 145): Accountant, CEO (read), Admin; not Project Manager. Audit events BANK_STATEMENT_PROFILE_CREATED/UPDATED, BANK_STATEMENT_IMPORTED, BANK_STATEMENT_MATCHED / AUTO_MATCHED / UNMATCHED, BANK_STATEMENT_EXCEPTION_MARKED / APPROVED / CLEARED.
Consequences: migration 20260915150000_phase8_bank_statements (four tables, two enums, CHECKs); bank-reconciliation module (service, statement parse util); contracts bank-reconciliation.port (client-safe entry `@zitaware/contracts/bank-reconciliation`), router; /bank-reconciliation page with column mapping, import preview, auto-match, match selected and exception actions; shared browser CSV reader apps/web/src/lib/csv.ts (TDS certificate import now uses it).
Known issues: manual entry of statement lines is not offered (CSV only, per the user's choice); a receipt or payment screen does not yet show its reconciliation state; bank-specific formats (Excel, MT940) are not read.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-115
Date: 2026-09-15
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.6b)
Context: docs/05 defines the bank reconciliation period OPEN → IN_PROGRESS → RECONCILED → LOCKED ("Lock requires zero unexplained difference or explicit approved exception") and says unmatching a locked bank period is prohibited; docs/09 says the reconciliation preparer should not perform the final lock; docs/04 §16 requires Cash Flow ("bank/cash transactions and matched postings by period/account; opening + movement = closing"). Slice 8.6a delivered statements, matching and exceptions.
Decision:
(1) BankReconciliationPeriod per bank account per calendar month (CHECK: a whole month). OPEN is having no row; a row is written when the month is marked reconciled. docs/05 reopening is not defined for bank periods, so a LOCKED month cannot be reopened.
(2) The bank reconciliation statement is computed from the account's first reconciled month ("the start"): adjusted book balance = book closing − book opening at the start + statement opening at the start − uncleared book entries + bank-only items, where uncleared book entries are bank journal lines dated from the start to month end not matched to statement lines dated by month end, and bank-only items are statement lines dated from the start to month end not matched to journal lines dated by month end (approved exceptions included). Unexplained difference = the statement closing balance entered − adjusted book balance.
(3) Mark reconciled (bank_reconciliation.manage) requires: the statement opening balance for the account's first month (the previous month's closing after that); the statement closing balance; the previous month RECONCILED or LOCKED; every statement line in the month matched or an approved exception; no exception awaiting approval; zero unexplained difference. It stores the figures as they stood. Uncleared book entries do not block — they carry forward (September reconciles with its challans uncleared).
(4) Lock (bank_reconciliation.approve) requires a RECONCILED month, every earlier month locked, the month still reconciling to zero, and a locker other than the preparer (SELF_APPROVAL_NOT_ALLOWED; database CHECK). Audit BANK_RECONCILIATION_RECONCILED / LOCKED.
(5) Guards on 8.6a actions: any change to a statement line dated in a LOCKED month (match, auto-match, unmatch, exception mark/approve/clear) is refused, and a statement import rejects rows dated in a locked month. A change dated in a RECONCILED month returns that month and every later reconciled month to IN_PROGRESS (audit BANK_RECONCILIATION_REOPENED), since later statements build on earlier ones. Book lines dated in a locked month may still be matched to later statement lines — clearing an item carried forward does not change the locked month's figures.
(6) Cash Flow (report.cash.read, catalogue 146; category "Cash & Bank", basis BANK): per bank account per month from its ledger account — opening, money in (debits), money out (credits), closing, the part matched to the statement, and the month's reconciliation status; totals per account and overall; net movement by kind reusing the Cash Profitability categories (all projects); each account's closing reconciled to its ledger balance. Company-level: client and project filters do not apply; with no start date it begins at the first bank movement.
Consequences: migration 20260915180000_phase8_bank_reconciliation_periods; BankReconciliationService statement/reconcile/lock/list and guards; CashFlowReportService; contracts (BankReconciliationStatement, BankReconciliationPeriodRow, CashFlow*, ReportCategory CASH); Month Reconciliation card on /bank-reconciliation; /reports/cash-flow.
Known issues: no reopening of a locked month; a book line posted later into a locked month appears as uncleared in later months rather than in the locked month; no trend chart on Cash Flow (the monthly table is the trend).
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-116
Date: 2026-09-15
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.7)
Context: docs/11 Slice 8.7 lists Payables aging, Vendor Payment, Expense, Reimbursement and Employee Advance reports. docs/04 §16: Expense = "posted direct expenses, vendor-bill expense lines and approved claims; explicitly exclude unposted requests/forecasts"; Vendor Payment = "vendor payments and allocations/TDS; reconcile to payable settlement and bank transactions"; Reimbursement = "employee claims, approvals, advance adjustments, reimbursements and recoveries"; Employee Advance (renamed from wallet) = "opening + advances − approved adjustments ± settlements = closing". Vendor bills have no due date and no payment terms.
Decision:
(1) Category PAYABLES ("Payables & Expenses"), read key report.payables.read (catalogue 147): Accountant, CEO (read), Admin; not Project Manager — Employee Advances is company-level employee data. Export still needs report.export.
(2) Payables Aging (as of, ACCRUAL): finalized vendor bills whose approval journal is dated on or before the as-of date and not voided by then, plus DIRECT stock receipts from a named supplier (they credit Accounts Payable — Vendor; ADR-103) not reversed by then. Settled = cash + TDS/GST-TDS of vendor-payment allocations whose payment is dated by then and not reversed by then. Aged from the document date (no due date exists; nothing is "not due"), in the receivable buckets 0–30/31–60/61–90/90+. "Approved for payment" = requested − settled on payment-request lines whose request is APPROVED today (request status has no history). Total reconciled to −(Accounts Payable — Vendor) as of the date on the unfiltered view; a client/project filter or a restricted user drops stock receipts (no project) and the comparison.
(3) Vendor Payments (period, BANK): each payment in the period of its journal, and a reversal as a negative row in the period of the reversal journal; per allocation requested, cash, typed deductions, settled. Reconciled with the postings of vendor_payment_post and its reversals only (LedgerControlService.movementBySource): payable settled = AP debit, cash = bank credit, deductions = TDS/GST-TDS payable credit. Filtered views show only allocations to bills in scope and skip the comparison.
(4) Expenses (period, ACCRUAL): non-stock vendor-bill lines (gross = taxable + GST; recoverable = the GST when ITC-eligible), direct expenses (gross = amount; recoverable GST when eligible), approved claims (gross = amount; recoverable = GST credit). Recognized = gross − recoverable. Vendor-bill voids and direct-expense reversals are negative in their own period. Drafts, requests and stock lines never count. Reconciled with the EXPENSE-type account movement of vendor_bill_approve, direct_expense.post and employee_expense_claim.approve postings and their reversals. Salary, introducer and inventory consumption costs are not "expenses" here — they are in Project P&L and Cost to Company.
(5) Reimbursements (period, ACCRUAL): submitted (non-draft) claims with the status on the end date — REIMBURSED from the reimbursement journal date unless reversed by then, APPROVED from the approval journal date (the expense date), otherwise the current SUBMITTED/REJECTED/CANCELLED. Listed when the expense, approval or reimbursement date falls in the period, or the claim is still open. Approved-in-period and reimbursed-in-period totals reconciled to the Employee Advance Receivable movement of the approve and reimburse postings.
(6) Employee Advances (period, ACCRUAL): per employee from Employee Advance Receivable lines (employeeId dimension): opening (before the start), advances (employee_advance.issue), claims adjusted (credit of employee_expense_claim.approve), reimbursements (debit of employee_expense_claim.reimburse), other (anything else, including lines with no employee); reversals classified by the entry they reverse. Positive closing: the employee holds company money; negative: owed to the employee. Company-level (client/project filters ignored); closing total reconciled to the account balance.
Consequences: PayablesReportService; LedgerControlService.movementBySource; contracts PayablesAging*, VendorPayment*, Expense*, Reimbursement*, EmployeeAdvance*, TaxDeductionAmounts; export documents; /reports/payables-aging, vendor-payments, expenses, reimbursements, employee-advances.
Known issues: "recoveries" of an advance (salary deduction, cash returned) are not modelled in the product, so they can only appear under "other"; approved-for-payment uses today's request status, not the as-of date's; vendor-bill aging cannot be by due date until vendor payment terms exist; recurring (server/subscription) spend appears here only where it was posted as a direct expense or vendor bill.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-117
Date: 2026-09-15
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.8a)
Context: docs/11 Slice 8.8 lists eight operations reports; it is split into 8.8a (EMD/SD Recovery, Introducer Statement, Penalties, Payment Holds) and 8.8b (Pending Invoice, Client Payment, Recurring Spend, Manpower Utilization). docs/04 §16: EMD Recovery = "instrument lifecycle, lodged/returned/forfeited/outstanding and due dates; distinguish cash from BG exposure"; Introducer Statement = "accrual, TDS, payment allocation, unallocated advance and balance by introducer/project"; Penalty = "typed penalties with accepted/disputed/resolution state and accounting treatment"; Payment Hold = "hold/retention deduction and release ledger with original deduction link and outstanding balance". docs/05 defines hold/retention OUTSTANDING → PARTIALLY_RELEASED → RELEASED (→ FORFEITED) and penalty RECORDED → ACCEPTED | DISPUTED → RESOLVED_ACCEPTED / RESOLVED_REVERSED; the product records no forfeiture, no penalty resolution and no partial EMD/SD return.
Decision:
(1) Category OPERATIONS ("Operations"), read key report.operations.read (catalogue 148): Accountant, CEO (read), Admin; not Project Manager. Shared by 8.8b.
(2) EMD/SD Recovery (as of): instruments submitted by the as-of date; RETURNED from the return date, otherwise OVERDUE after the return due date, otherwise LODGED. Exposure CASH when the instrument type posts (requiresCashPosting) and GUARANTEE otherwise — never added together. Category from the purpose (EMD/SD, or unclassified). Cash still lodged reconciled to the purposes' recoverable ledger accounts on the unfiltered view; a filtered view keeps project instruments only (tender-only instruments have no project). No forfeited state: it is not recorded.
(3) Introducer Statement (period): per introducer agreement (introducer × project) — entitlement (rate × project value, or the fixed amount), accrued to date and not yet accrued; movements on the accrual and payment journal dates, reversals on theirs, one line per payment per agreement; opening + accrued − paid = closing with a running balance. Payments table with allocated, advance, gross, TDS and net; payment-level figures and the unallocated advance are withheld (null) on a filtered view because they span projects. Reconciled to Introducer Payable (closing) and Introducer Advance Receivable (advance standing at the end date).
(4) Penalties (period): ACCEPTED_PENALTY and DISPUTED_PENALTY receipt deductions on the receipt's journal date, a reversed receipt's penalty negative on the reversal date; state ACCEPTED or DISPUTED only; treatment = the ledger account the deduction posted to (category override or the Penalty — Revenue Deduction / Disputed Deduction Receivable defaults). Accepted and disputed totals reconciled to the receipt_post movement on those accounts.
(5) Payment Holds (as of): HOLD and RETENTION deductions on receipts dated by then and not reversed by then, releases dated by then, status OUTSTANDING / PARTIALLY_RELEASED / RELEASED, last release, release history. Outstanding reconciled to Hold Receivable and Retention Receivable.
(6) Reconciliation helpers (notComparable, reconciliationLine, reconciled) move to reports/reconciliation.util.ts, shared with the 8.7 payables service.
Consequences: OperationsReportService; contracts EmdSd*, IntroducerStatement*, Penalty*, PaymentHold*; export documents; /reports/emd-sd-recovery, introducer-statement, penalties, payment-holds.
Known issues: forfeiture (hold/retention, EMD/SD), penalty dispute resolution and partial EMD/SD returns have no workflow, so those states never appear; TDS on an introducer payment is shown per payment, not split across projects.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-118
Date: 2026-09-15
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.8b)
Context: The second half of docs/11 Slice 8.8. docs/04 §16: Pending Invoice = "finalized invoice outstanding only; alert/draft invoice backlog appears as a separate billing-pending measure"; Payment (client receipt) = "actual cash received, invoice settled, deductions and unallocated cash are separate"; Recurring Spend = "show Forecast Cycles and Actual Posted Spend as separate columns/filters"; Manpower Utilization = "submitted/locked monthly allocation percentages and salary cost; no independent contribution dataset". Payment alerts and recurring cycles keep only their current status, and a receipt must allocate all its cash.
Decision:
(1) Same category and key as 8.8a (OPERATIONS, report.operations.read).
(2) Pending Invoices (as of): the Receivable Aging invoice position unchanged (ReceivablesReportService.pendingInvoices), each marked unpaid or part paid, past-due total, reconciled to Accounts Receivable. Billing pending = today's OPEN/SNOOZED payment alerts in scope with the milestone amount before GST, due date and any DRAFT invoice raised from the alert — never added to the outstanding and not dated as of the filter.
(3) Client Payments (period, BANK): receipts on their journal date, a reversal negative on its own; actual cash, invoice settled, each of the nine deduction types and unallocated cash (actual − cash allocated, zero today) per receipt and per invoice; by client. Cash reconciled to the bank and settled to Accounts Receivable through receipt_post postings and their reversals. A filtered view shows allocations in scope only and skips the comparison.
(4) Recurring Spend (period, MULTIPLE): per subscription and server — forecast = cycles due in the period that are PENDING or PAID × the approximate cycle amount; the approximate amount per month and per year (× cycles per year: weekly 52, monthly 12, quarterly 4, yearly 1; ÷ 12 per month, 2 dp); actual = direct expenses linked to its cycles posted in the period, reversals negative; overdue = pending cycles due before the end date; next pending due date after it. Forecast and actual are separate columns and never netted. No ledger reconciliation: a forecast is not accounting, and the actual direct expenses are reconciled by the Expenses report. Totals of monthly/yearly run rate count ACTIVE items only. A filtered view keeps items tagged to the projects and actual spend charged to them.
(5) Manpower Utilization (period → every calendar month it touches, MULTIPLE/analytical): per employee per month — state SUBMITTED / DRAFT / NOT_STARTED, project %, non-project %, total %, variance against 100% capacity, imported gross, and cost = gross × share for SUBMITTED months only, split over the whole month before any line is kept (ADR-095 rounding). Employees counted when active and joined by the month end, or paid or allocated that month. By month: counts by state, gross, project cost, non-project cost and salary not yet attributed. Gross reconciled to the expense movement of salary_import postings dated within those months. A filtered view shows only project shares in scope, without gross or non-project figures.
Consequences: ReceivablesReportService.pendingInvoices; OperationsReportService.clientPayments; ResourceReportService (recurring spend, manpower utilization); contracts PendingInvoice*, BillingPendingRow, ClientPayment*, ReceiptDeductionType, RecurringSpend*, ManpowerUtilization*; export documents; /reports/pending-invoices, client-payments, recurring-spend, manpower-utilization.
Known issues: billing pending and cycle status are today's, not the as-of date's (neither keeps history); a salary import posted outside its own month's dates shows as a reconciliation difference; there is no department filter (department is shown per row).
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-119
Date: 2026-09-15
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.9a)
Context: docs/04 §1 (`01-platform/dashboard.html`): "Query dashboard.getOverview; permission dashboard.read. Filters select legal entity and business period. Every card links to its source report. Values are derived from posted journals and authoritative operational read models; no screen-local profit formula. Test card-to-report reconciliation and scoped access." docs/16 lists KPI cards, charts, summaries and attention lists. docs/11 Slice 8.9 also holds the audit and exception dashboards, which are split into 8.9b.
Decision:
(1) New permission dashboard.read (catalogue 149): Accountant, Project Manager, Admin; CEO through its *.read rule. A viewer without it gets a welcome card on / instead of figures.
(2) DashboardService.getOverview computes nothing of its own. Each card is one figure returned by one report (through ReportsService) for the dashboard's filters — balances as of the end date, flows over the business period — and carries that report's key, title, path and basis: invoice outstanding and hold/retention (Receivable Aging), past due and billing pending (Pending Invoices), income and contribution (Accrual Project P&L), bank balance (Cash Flow), net GST (GST Net), TDS to deposit (TDS Outward), TDS certificates pending (TDS Inward), owed to vendors (Payables Aging), expenses (Expenses), EMD/SD cash lodged (EMD/SD Recovery). A card, the trend and the attention items drawn from a report appear only when the viewer holds that report's category read key; project scoping (project.read_all) passes through unchanged.
(3) Cash trend: Cash Flow's months with all bank accounts added together, shown as grouped columns (money in / money out) with a legend, per-column tooltip and a table view; two-colour palette checked with the dataviz validator.
(4) Needs attention: reports whose ledger reconciliation shows a difference (first), up to five past-due invoices (most overdue first), billing pending due by the end date, overdue EMD/SD, and withheld TDS past its deposit due date — each links to where it is resolved.
(5) Filters: from/to dates (default: financial year to date) and legal entity; no client/project filter, since the dashboard is the business-wide view.
Consequences: contracts dashboard.port (client-safe entry `@zitaware/contracts/dashboard`), dashboard router and context service; DashboardService in the reports module; the / page replaced (it was the Phase 2 placeholder).
Known issues: the overview runs up to ten reports per load, so it takes a few seconds on the demo data (no caching yet); cards cover the reports built so far, and exception/audit views arrive in 8.9b.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-120
Date: 2026-09-15
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 8.9b)
Context: docs/11 Phase 8 lists "Audit and exception dashboards". docs/09 requires audit coverage of corrections, period locks and reopens, user/role/permission changes, bank matches and period locks, and exports of sensitive reports, and asks for alerts on repeated authentication failure, period reopen and high-risk exports. Phase 8's exit criterion is that report totals reconcile to the ledger (docs/11); the reports built in 8.1–8.8 each carry their own check, and the work queues (bank reconciliation, TDS matching, maker-checker approvals, billing alerts, allocations) each live on their own screen.
Decision:
(1) Category CONTROLS ("Controls"), basis label "Controls" (not an accounting basis), read key report.controls.read (catalogue 150): Accountant, Admin; CEO through *.read; not Project Manager. Both reports are company-level.
(2) Audit Activity (period, UTC days): audit events counted by control group — CORRECTION (actions ending _REVERSED, _VOIDED, _UNMATCHED), DELETION (_DELETED), PERIOD_CONTROL (LOCK, REOPEN, SOFT_CLOSE, bank reconciliation lock/reopen, allocation reopen), ACCESS (role, user-role and permission changes, user enable/disable/invite, session revocations), SIGN_IN (failed sign-in, lock-out, password reset), EXPORT (report exports, document downloads), EXCEPTION (bank exception mark/approve/clear), ROUTINE (the rest); by user and by record type; users with three or more failed sign-ins in the period; the 200 most recent non-routine events linking to the audit log. The grouping is a pure function with unit tests; an action added later falls into ROUTINE until named.
(3) Exceptions (as of): the ledger checks of ten balance reports (Receivable Aging, Outstanding Summary, Payables Aging, Employee Advances, Payment Holds, EMD/SD Recovery, Introducer Statement, TDS Inward, TDS Outward, Cash Flow) run unfiltered, with their lines; and open items — statement lines dated by then with an open amount and no exception, bank exceptions awaiting approval, ended months with statement lines and no reconciled/locked period, TDS certificate/26AS exceptions (TDS Reconciliation's count), withheld tax past its deposit due date, vendor bills / payment requests / expense claims SUBMITTED by then (a vendor bill's amount is its lines' taxable + GST, since its total is fixed at approval), billing alerts past due, and posted salary employee-months without a SUBMITTED allocation. Each item has a count, amount where meaningful, oldest date and a link to where it is resolved. Queue statuses are today's.
(4) Neither report is compared with the ledger itself (reconciliation not applicable, with the reason shown).
Consequences: ControlsReportService (depends on the receivables, payables, TDS, cash-flow and operations report services, not the facade); audit-group.util (+spec); contracts AuditActivity*, AuditActionGroup, AUDIT_ACTION_GROUP_LABELS, Exception*, LedgerCheckRow; export documents; /reports/audit-activity and /reports/exceptions. Phase 8 is complete.
Known issues: no alert delivery (notifications) for repeated sign-in failures or reopens — the report lists them; queue items are current-state; the Exceptions page runs ten reports per load; failed postings/unbalanced attempts are not audited events today, so they cannot appear.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-121
Date: 2026-09-16
Status: Accepted — implemented (Phase 9 preparation)
Context: The demo organization (DEMO-MONEY-FLOW) is the organization the browser pass, the route check and hand-checking all run against, and docs/11 Phase 9 lists "User acceptance testing using HTML screen contracts". It is built entirely by buildMoneyFlowScenario, which is also the integration spec's fixture: every figure it produces is asserted to the paisa, so it cannot grow a module just to make a screen non-empty. A row count per organization-scoped table showed 25 tables empty in the demo — Leads, Tenders, EMD/SD, Subscriptions, Servers, Card Accounts, Employee Advances, project team and progress, client and vendor contacts, eleven master lists, and the bank statement flow that ADR-116's demo rebuild dropped. Those screens could not be exercised or checked at all, and six reports (EMD/SD Recovery, Employee Advances, Recurring Spend and the dashboard cards drawn from them) had nothing but zeros.
Decision:
(1) The extras live in their own module, testing/money-flow-scenario/demo-extras.ts, called only by scripts/load-money-flow-demo.ts after the scenario is built. The integration spec's organization never receives them, so no asserted figure moves. Same folder as sheet.ts, the other demo-only file.
(2) They go through the same real services as the scenario, so every posting, audit event, maker-checker step and reconciliation is the real one. The only direct writes are rows with no service of their own — two ledger accounts, the security-instrument purpose/type masters (whose category, recoverable account and cash-posting flag the generic Masters port does not carry) and vendor contacts — the exception the scenario builder already makes.
(3) Every cash movement an extra makes is dated September 2026, after the 31 August as-of date the scenario's asserted figures use. Four ledger balances move (BANK_HDFC_CURRENT 14,04,127.77 → 12,88,507.77; OFFICE_EXPENSE 28,850 → 38,570; INPUT_IGST_RECEIVABLE 1,800 → 2,700; EMPLOYEE_ADVANCE_RECEIVABLE −2,450 → 22,550) and two accounts are new (EMD_SD_RECOVERABLE 80,000, CREDIT_CARD_PAYABLE 0). The other 27 scenario balances are unchanged, checked against expected.ts after every rebuild.
(4) DEMO_EXTRAS_FIGURES carries the hand-computed figures — what each screen holds, the six September bank movements netting −1,15,620, and the restated ledger balances — and sheet.ts renders them as a "Demo extras" section of money-flow-demo-sheet.md. The sheet's opening claim is scoped to the scenario sections, so it no longer over-promises.
(5) The demo also classifies what the scenario left unset: client type, project type, and each employee's department, designation and employment type, plus reimbursement eligibility for the employee who receives the advance.
Consequences: apps/api/src/testing/money-flow-scenario/demo-extras.ts; sheet.ts renders the extras section and gained a left-aligned table mode; scripts/load-money-flow-demo.ts calls buildDemoExtras. Empty demo tables fall from 25 to 10, and the ten that remain are legitimately empty (S3-backed documents and document links, opening balances, and session/invitation/reset/override rows that only a live session creates). No migration, no permission, no production code path.
Known issues: documents and document_links stay empty because an upload needs real S3; no ledger opening balance is set, since one would move every scenario balance; accounting_period_transitions stays empty (no close/reopen is performed). The extras are not asserted by any test — they are checked by the ledger-balance comparison and the Exceptions report's ten ledger checks after a rebuild, not by a spec of their own.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-122
Date: 2026-09-29
Status: Accepted
Context: Signed-in users were returned to the login screen when the access token expired. ADR-035 set that token to 15 minutes and the refresh token to 30 days, and auth.refresh already rotated the refresh token, but neither the browser client nor the Next.js proxy called it. An expired access token therefore ended the session even while the refresh token was still valid.
Decision: ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_MINUTES is 60 and REFRESH_TOKEN_TTL_DAYS is 1, in .env, .env.example, and the env-schema defaults. The access-token cookie expires with the JWT. When that token is missing or its exp has passed and the refresh cookie is still present, the proxy calls auth.refresh once and continues the request with the new cookies. In-page tRPC calls do the same and retry once; login, logout, invite, and password-reset calls do not. A failed refresh still sends the user to /login. Reuse of an already-rotated refresh token still revokes the token family.
Consequences: A session lasts until the refresh token expires (one day), or until logout, disablement, or reuse detection. Changing either lifetime is still an env change only. The running dev process keeps the previous values until .env is sourced again and npm run dev is restarted.
Supersedes: the 15-minute access-token and 30-day refresh-token lifetimes in ADR-035.
```

```text
ID: ADR-123
Date: 2026-09-29
Status: Accepted
Context: The Add Client form required Department (docs/04 client-add.html). Users need to save a client before a department name is known.
Decision: ClientProfile.department is nullable. A blank or whitespace-only value is stored as null on create and on update. A non-blank value is still trimmed and limited to 200 characters. Existing department values are unchanged.
Consequences: The client list and client detail show an em dash when department is empty. Search ignores a missing department.
Supersedes: the required-department validation on client-add.html in docs/04.
```

```text
ID: ADR-124
Date: 2026-09-29
Status: Accepted
Context: Employees were added one at a time. Staff lists arrive as Excel, and the screen had no import and no sample file.
Decision: Employees with `employee.manage` can download an .xlsx sample and import it. The columns are employee code, full name, department, designation, employment type, reimbursable, monthly salary, joining date and status. Department, designation and employment type must already exist and are matched by name or code; a blank cell stays unset. A monthly salary is stored as the opening salary revision effective on the joining date, and the displayed salary follows it. Status blank means ACTIVE; reimbursable blank means No. Preview checks every row and the import writes all rows in one transaction, or none if any row is invalid. Login email and roles are not columns.
Consequences: The Employees screen shows Download sample and Import Excel beside Add Employee. A second import of the same codes is rejected. This does not replace monthly salary import, which remains the costing source for a posted month.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-125
Date: 2026-09-29
Status: Accepted
Context: Employee Costing was two menu items, Salary Imports and Monthly Allocation, plus a manpower-cost tab on the second. They are one month's sequence: post the salary, split it, then read the cost.
Decision: One menu item, Employee Costing, opens one screen with Salary, Allocation and Cost tabs. A user sees only the tabs their permissions allow. `/salary-imports` and `/employee-allocations` redirect to the matching tab. Importing a month stays at `/employee-costing/new`. No posting rule changes.
Consequences: Bookmarks and report links to the old paths land on the same screen. A group with a single menu item renders as that item, without a second heading.
Supersedes: the separate Salary Imports and Monthly Allocation navigation entries. ADR-097's manpower-cost figures are unchanged; the tab now sits beside Salary and Allocation.
```

```text
ID: ADR-126
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: An invoice was raised from exactly one payment alert, so each CAPEX event needed its own invoice. Users bill several CAPEX events for the same project on one invoice. An OPEX installment is still one billing period and stays alone.
Decision: A CAPEX invoice may include other open CAPEX events from the same project. Each event is one line, and finalizing closes every selected alert. One event still belongs to only one non-void invoice. An OPEX invoice still covers one installment; adding another alert is rejected. The journal stays one entry for the invoice total. Each milestone's approved amount still caps the finalized taxable total of its events.
Consequences: The add-invoice form lists the other open CAPEX events when the starting alert is CAPEX. Voiding the invoice reopens every alert it closed.
Supersedes: the one-alert-per-invoice rule for CAPEX only. OPEX is unchanged.
```

```text
ID: ADR-127
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: On Record Payment the amount entered was the bank receipt. Adding TDS on top of the full outstanding made the settlement larger than the invoice. GST-TDS rates were applied to the GST component.
Decision: The amount entered is the amount applied to the invoice, and it starts as the outstanding. Deductions come out of it. The bank receives the remainder. Cash plus deductions still equals the amount settled, and that total cannot exceed the outstanding. TDS and GST-TDS rates both use the invoice taxable amount.
Consequences: The receipt journal is unchanged. The Record Payment form shows "Amount applied to invoice", and GST-TDS options are marked on base.
Supersedes: the Record Payment instruction to enter the bank amount as the allocation figure.
```

```text
ID: ADR-128
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: Users need to send a proforma before the tax invoice. A proforma is not a supply document for the books.
Decision: An invoice is either a regular tax invoice or a proforma. Issuing a proforma gives it a proforma number and freezes the document. It posts no journal, creates no receivable, and does not close the payment alert. Converting that proforma to a tax invoice posts the existing finalize entry (receivable, revenue, GST), assigns the tax invoice number, and closes the alert. The proforma number stays on the record.
Consequences: Receipts, outstanding, and billed totals ignore an issued proforma because it is not finalized. The add-invoice form offers the choice.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-129
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: The store had no role. Stock, servers and hardware purchases were only reachable by the Accountant, who also keeps the books.
Decision: Hardware Manager is a starter role for the store: the product catalog, stock in and out, servers (not paying or cancelling them), and hardware vendor bills and payment requests. Approval and payment stay with the Accountant, who also keeps stock posting so a wrong receipt can be corrected. The other roles are identified on the Roles screen and matched to the job: Admin has everything; the Accountant runs the books and can correct a client billing record; a Project Manager runs assigned projects, including the lead, the tender, creating the project, its contract figures, purchase approval and their own expense claim, and does not see other projects or post money; the CEO can see and export but not change; Admin Staff keeps the office lists (clients, leads, vendors, lookup values) and files documents; an Employee files their own claim and a purchase on an assigned project.
Consequences: Re-seeding adds the role and any new grants. It does not remove a permission an administrator later added to a starter role.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-130
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: A client's GSTIN is optional and is kept for later invoicing. The 15-character pattern blocked values that are not a statutory GSTIN.
Decision: A client GSTIN is stored as entered. The form, the API, and the database no longer check the 15-character pattern. The company's own GST registration and a vendor GSTIN still use that pattern.
Consequences: A client GSTIN can be any text up to 50 characters. Duplicate checking for the company's GST registration is unchanged.
Supersedes: the client-add screen contract that required GSTIN validation when captured.
```

```text
ID: ADR-131
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: Lead and tender were two records and two screens. Users treated them as one opportunity and lost the path from a win to a project.
Decision: A lead is the only pipeline record. Tender number, tender date and submission deadline are fields on that lead. The Tenders menu is hidden. Marking the lead Won asks for the project name, legal entity and contract amount, then creates the project. An existing tender row stays in the database so older projects and EMD records still point at it.
Consequences: New opportunities are entered on Leads. Opening an old tender address goes to its lead.
Supersedes: the separate Tender lifecycle in the screen contract, and the earlier choice to keep lead and tender as two records.
```

```text
ID: ADR-132
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: The project stage dropdown greyed out every stage except the next one, and Closed was unavailable until the stage was Successfully Completed.
Decision: Any later stage can be chosen directly. Closed can be chosen from any stage. Moving backward still asks for a reason. Cancelling still asks for a reason. A cancelled or closed project stays final.
Consequences: The stage and status dropdowns have no disabled choices while the project is open.
Supersedes: the rule that a project moves forward one stage at a time, and the rule that it can be closed only after Successfully Completed.
```

```text
ID: ADR-133
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: A project is created when a lead is won, and the work is already underway. Starting the stage at Won made people move it forward before they could use it.
Decision: A new project starts at Work Started. Existing projects keep the stage they already have.
Consequences: The stage dropdown on a new project shows Work Started.
Supersedes: the project default stage of Won.
```

```text
ID: ADR-134
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: A role's menu and a role's screens were not the same thing. Dashboard and My Expenses were visible to every login, a project contract amount showed for roles that cannot read project figures, and typing an address opened a form the role cannot save.
Decision: The menu, the address, and the buttons follow the permission. Dashboard needs dashboard.read. My Expenses needs employee_claim.create. Contract value, budget, and the project money columns need project.read_financials. Opening an add screen needs the create or post permission for that record. A role without the permission sees a short message instead of the form.
Consequences: Employee and Hardware Manager land on the first screen their role can open. A CEO sees the company and the downloads, and does not see add or approve buttons. An accountant does not see project stage controls.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-135
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: The Accountant was limited to the books and could not change a project stage, run users, or open several other jobs.
Decision: The Accountant holds every permission. The screens are the same as Admin.
Consequences: Signing in as Accountant opens every menu item and every action. Project Manager, CEO, Admin Staff, Employee and Hardware Manager stay limited to their own jobs.
Supersedes: the Accountant limit in ADR-129, and the sentence in ADR-134 that an accountant does not see project stage controls.
```

```text
ID: ADR-136
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: The CEO could see the company and download reports, and could not post, approve, or change records.
Decision: The CEO holds every permission. The screens are the same as Admin.
Consequences: Signing in as CEO opens every menu item and every action. Project Manager, Admin Staff, Employee and Hardware Manager stay limited to their own jobs.
Supersedes: the CEO limit in ADR-129 and ADR-135.
```

```text
ID: ADR-137
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: Assigning a project team and allocating an employee's month were Admin-only. A project manager runs the job, and a hardware manager issues people and stock to projects.
Decision: Project Manager and Hardware Manager can add and remove project team members, and can allocate employees for the month. They still cannot edit the project record itself.
Consequences: The Team tab shows Add and Remove for those two roles, on projects they can open. Employee Costing shows the allocation tab. A project manager still only opens assigned projects.
Supersedes: the part of ADR-058 that kept the project team Admin-only.
```

```text
ID: ADR-138
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: Lead and tender are one record. The labels still said either lead or tender, so an overseas client and a tender did not fit the same screen.
Decision: The screen is titled Leads & Tenders. Fields use words that fit both: title, type, source, opened on, and a reference number for a tender number or an overseas client reference. Stage labels Preparing and Open replace SOW Preparation and Live. Stored stage values are unchanged.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-139
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: A project manager is an employee, the same kind of job as a hardware manager. The two roles had different menus.
Decision: Project Manager and Hardware Manager hold the same permissions. Both open every project, work leads and tenders, change stages, assign the team, allocate employees, run stock and servers, and raise and approve purchase bills. Neither posts money or manages users.
Consequences: A project manager is no longer limited to assigned projects. A hardware manager can run a project the same way.
Supersedes: the Project Manager assigned-projects-only limit, and the part of ADR-129 that kept project figures and purchase approval off Hardware Manager.
```

```text
ID: ADR-140
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: Giving Project Manager and Hardware Manager the same access still left leads, invoices, reports, and the books on both roles.
Decision: The shared set is the employee store set only: projects, the team, stock, servers, products, and purchase bills. Leads, invoices, receipts, reports, the dashboard, and purchase approval are not included. Re-seeding removes those extra grants from Hardware Manager as well as Project Manager.
Consequences: Both roles see the same shorter menu. Admin, Accountant, and CEO still open every screen.
Supersedes: the wide shared set in ADR-139.
```

```text
ID: ADR-141
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: An invoice printed one shared logo. A legal entity needs its own logo, seal, and authorized sign.
Decision: Those three images are optional fields on the legal entity. Each is a PNG or JPEG of at most 400 KB. The list shows whether an image is present. The image bytes load only when the form or an invoice PDF needs them. Removing an image is allowed. The audit record says the image was set or cleared, and does not store the file.
Consequences: Add and Edit Legal Entity show Logo, Seal, and Authorized sign. An invoice PDF uses that entity's logo when one is saved, and still uses the shared logo when it is not. Seal and authorized sign print at the bottom when they are saved.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-142
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: A legal entity can have more than one bank account. The tax invoice prints one of them.
Decision: The account is chosen when the invoice is created. One active account is used without a question. More than one shows a required choice on Add Invoice. The invoice stores that bank account, and the PDF prints its bank, account name, masked account number, and IFSC.
Consequences: Bank Accounts stays a master with no invoice flag. Changing a bank account later changes the bank lines on invoices that already point at it.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-143
Date: 2026-09-30
Status: Accepted
Context: The invoice PDF was a plain list. The printed tax invoice has to follow the bordered GST layout used by the company.
Decision: The PDF is a bordered tax invoice: logo and title, legal name and address, invoice number and date, reverse charge N, seller state and code, bill-to party, a work line of project name and service period, a line grid with SAC, amount, discount, taxable value and CGST, SGST, IGST and cess, amount in words, the tax summary, the chosen bank account, and the seal and authorised sign. Status, due date and PAN are not printed. Place of supply and jurisdiction come from the legal entity. Discount and cess print as zero because those amounts are not stored on the invoice.
Consequences: Add and Edit Legal Entity include Place of supply and Jurisdiction. Restart the API to print the new layout.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-144
Date: 2026-10-01
Status: Accepted
Context: The client form, the client page, and the lead and project pages showed contact persons and an internal note.
Decision: Those fields are not part of the client. Add Client, Edit Client, the client page, the client list, and the lead and project pages do not ask for or show them. Vendors keep their own note.
Consequences: Existing contact rows and notes stay stored and are not edited from the client screens.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-145
Date: 2026-10-01
Status: Accepted
Context: An invoice GST rate of 9 was split into 4.5 percent CGST and 4.5 percent SGST.
Decision: The rate entered on an invoice, credit note, debit note, vendor bill, direct expense, subscription payment, or server payment is the percent charged as CGST and the percent charged as SGST when the supply is in the same state. A different state charges twice that percent as IGST. CGST+SGST and IGST stay mutually exclusive.
Consequences: Enter 9 for a supply that is 9 percent CGST and 9 percent SGST. Reports read the amounts already stored, so they do not recalculate a posted document.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-146
Date: 2026-10-01
Status: Accepted
Context: Save as draft and Save were the same button. Required fields still blocked an unfinished form.
Decision: Save as draft stores the unfinished form for the current user and does not run field checks. Save runs the existing checks and writes the real record. A saved record clears that user's unfinished copy. This store is not a new status on a legal entity, lead, invoice, or any other business record.
Consequences: An unfinished form can be left blank and opened again later. Save still refuses a record that is missing a required field.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-147
Date: 2026-10-01
Status: Accepted
Context: Converting a proforma rewrote that same document into the tax invoice. The accountant needs to change the tax invoice, and the proforma that was already sent has to remain.
Decision: Convert opens a separate tax-invoice draft copied from the issued proforma. The lines, GST, date, heading, and bank may be changed on that draft. Finalizing it posts the existing receivable, revenue, and GST entry for the tax invoice's amounts and closes the payment alert. The proforma becomes Converted: it keeps its number, amounts, and PDF, posts nothing, and no longer holds the billing event. Voiding the draft before it is posted puts the proforma back to issued. Voiding the posted tax invoice reverses the journal. The proforma stays.
Consequences: The project invoice list shows both documents. Receipts, outstanding, billed totals, and GST follow the tax invoice only. A proforma already rewritten by the previous conversion stays as that one tax invoice.
Supersedes: the conversion step of ADR-128. Issuing a proforma is unchanged.
```

```text
ID: ADR-148
Date: 2026-10-05
Status: Accepted
Context: A CAPEX event or OPEX installment that was already due waited for the payment-alert job before it appeared on the project.
Decision: Saving a CAPEX or OPEX milestone opens a payment alert immediately for each event or installment that is already billable. Marking a CAPEX event achieved does the same. The scheduled job still opens alerts that become due later, and it does not open a second alert for a source that already has one.
Consequences: The Payment Alert tab shows a due item as soon as the milestone is saved. The eligibility rule is unchanged: a CAPEX event alerts when it is achieved or its due date is inside the reminder window, and an OPEX installment alerts when its planned invoice date is inside that window.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-149
Date: 2026-10-26
Status: Accepted
Context: The seed loaded all 36 states but only seven sample districts (ADR-036), so the District and City dropdowns were empty for most of India.
Decision: The seed loads every district and every urban local body (municipal corporation, municipality, nagar panchayat, notified area and similar) from the Local Government Directory, taken from the daily LGD archive at github.com/ramSeraph/opendata (release lgd-latest). The urban local bodies are the cities. LGD does not record a district on an urban local body, so each one goes in the district that holds most of the villages and wards it covers. The data is committed as packages/database/prisma/data/india-geography.json. packages/database/scripts/build-india-geography.mjs builds it from the extracted LGD CSVs. The first load is the 2026-10-26 snapshot: 784 districts and 5,034 cities.
Consequences: Re-running the seed adds only missing rows. Rows that already exist, including ones an administrator added or renamed in Masters, are left alone, and nothing is deleted. Older databases keep the earlier sample rows that LGD names differently ("Mumbai City", "Bengaluru", "Ahmedabad" city); an administrator can deactivate them in Masters. 41 districts have no urban local body in LGD and no cities, among them Mumbai, because Greater Mumbai is filed under Mumbai Suburban. 12 newly notified bodies with no LGD coverage yet are left out. Administrators can add cities in Masters (ADR-044). LGD names are kept as published (for example "Ahmadabad"), apart from capitals turned to title case and spacing tidied.
Supersedes: the representative-sample clause of ADR-036.
```

```text
ID: ADR-150
Date: 2026-10-26
Status: Accepted
Context: Finalizing an invoice failed with "No financial year covers this invoice date" whenever the legal entity had no financial year for the chosen date. Accountants want to enter any invoice date. They also want to type an invoice number, for example to carry on an existing series, rather than always taking the generated one.
Decision: When finalizing a tax invoice or issuing a proforma, a missing financial year is created for the invoice date: the Indian 1 April – 31 March year, labelled FY2030-31, with its monthly accounting periods, not made current, and audited as FINANCIAL_YEAR_CREATED. If the legal entity already has a year that overlaps that span, the new year is trimmed to the gap between the neighbouring years and labelled with its dates, for example "FY2026-27 (2027-01-01 to 2027-03-31)". It gets one period per month it touches. The Finalize dialog takes an optional invoice number. Blank still generates the next number. A typed number must be 1–16 letters, digits, "-" or "/" (GST rule 46(b)) and unused, ignoring case, for that GST registration and financial year. It is checked before the journal posts.
Consequences: A locked accounting period still rejects the posting; this decision does not reopen closed books. Generated numbers still count the numbers already issued in the series, so after typed numbers the next generated one may need a retry and can repeat a pattern the accountant used. Accountants who type numbers should keep to one scheme per series. Proforma numbers stay generated.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-151
Date: 2026-10-26
Status: Accepted
Context: One invoice may bill several CAPEX events (ADR-126), but the client often pays each event separately. A receipt could only settle the invoice as a whole, so the TDS and GST-TDS rate on the receipt screen was applied to the taxable amount of the whole invoice, and nothing recorded which event was paid.
Decision: A receipt allocation on an invoice billing two or more CAPEX events may name the one event it pays (receipt_allocations.source_event_id); left empty, it pays the whole invoice as before. On the receipt screen, "Paying for" offers All events or each event. The TDS and GST-TDS rate applies to the taxable amount of the choice: that event's line, or the whole invoice. An allocation naming an event may not settle more than that event's line total minus earlier allocations naming the same event. The invoice outstanding check still applies. Earlier rows of the same receipt now count toward both checks, so two rows can no longer each settle the full balance.
Consequences: The journal is unchanged: one Accounts Receivable credit per receipt, with deductions per ledger account and project (ADR-109). An event's outstanding counts only allocations naming that event, so a whole-invoice payment lowers the invoice outstanding, not an event's.
```
