The receipt screen caps the suggested amount at the lower of the two. The receipt detail shows the event on each allocation. Existing allocations keep a null event. The migration adds a nullable column, an index and a foreign key, and is reversed by dropping the column.
```text
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-152
Date: 2026-10-26
Status: Accepted
Context: A project needs the sales manager who won it, in addition to the project manager who runs it.
Decision: projects.sales_manager_id is an optional employee of the same organization, enforced by a composite foreign key as for the project manager. It is set and changed on the project add and edit forms, shown in the project list and header, and audited on change. A project created from a won tender gets the tender's assigned employee as its sales manager, as it already did for the project manager.
Consequences: A user without "see all projects" also sees, and may act on, the projects where they are the sales manager, as they already did as project manager or team member (extends ADR-058). What they may do on such a project is still set by their role's permissions.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-153
Date: 2026-10-26
Status: Accepted
Context: Any employee could be picked as a project's project manager or sales manager (ADR-152). The business assigns these roles by designation.
Decision: A project manager must hold the Project Manager or Hardware Manager designation. A sales manager must hold the Business Development Manager or Marketing Manager designation. A designation qualifies when its name or code matches, ignoring case, spaces, hyphens and underscores. The project service enforces this on create, and on update only for a manager being set or changed, so a project saved earlier with another employee can still be edited. The project form lists only qualifying active employees and shows each one's designation. A new project pre-filled from a won tender drops a pre-filled manager who does not qualify. The seed adds the Hardware Manager, Business Development Manager and Marketing Manager designations.
Consequences: An employee with no designation, or another designation, cannot be chosen until their designation is set in Employees. The rule lives in PROJECT_MANAGER_DESIGNATIONS and SALES_MANAGER_DESIGNATIONS in the contracts package. The seed matches designations by code, so if an administrator already created one of these designations under a different code, the seed adds a second designation with the same name; deactivate one of the two in Masters.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-154
Date: 2026-10-26
Status: Accepted
Context: The organization runs several legal entities (companies), and each employee works for one of them. A project belongs to one legal entity, but its manager and team pickers offered every employee of the organization.
Decision: employees.legal_entity_id names the employee's legal entity, enforced by a composite foreign key within the organization. It is nullable: the migration assigns existing employees when their organization has exactly one legal entity; others are set in Employees, where the field is required on save and the list has a Legal Entity column and filter. Only an active legal entity may be assigned. The Excel import takes an optional Legal Entity column (legal name or PAN); older files without it still import. A project's sales manager and project manager must be employees of the project's legal entity (in addition to ADR-153), and the candidate lists offer only them. A project team member may not be an employee of another legal entity; an employee whose legal entity is not set yet is still accepted, so existing teams and data keep working while employees are being assigned.
Employee costing follows the same rule. A legal entity's salary draft, whether generated from salaries on record (by a user or by the hourly worker) or uploaded, covers only its own employees, and committing a draft that carries another legal entity's employee is refused. An employee of another legal entity is refused as unmatched. An employee whose legal entity is not set counts only when the organization has a single legal entity, so a salary is never charged to two entities and never silently dropped where there is no doubt; otherwise the draft names how many are unassigned. An employee's monthly allocation may name only projects of the employee's legal entity (an unassigned employee is not held to it). The allocation list and the manpower cost report filter by legal entity; the report's total follows that filter.
Consequences: Until an employee's legal entity is set, they cannot be picked as a project's manager, and in an organization with several legal entities their salary is in no draft. Employee expense claims and advances, direct expenses paid by an employee, card holders, subscription and server owners and recurring-cycle payments still offer every employee of the organization; restricting them changes who can be paid by which company and is left for a separate decision. Rollback: drop the column, its index and its foreign key.
Supersedes: none.
```

```text
ID: ADR-155
Date: 2026-10-27
Status: Accepted — implemented; slice 2 is ADR-156
Context: Users need to see, per milestone, how much was invoiced and how much is still to invoice, and on each invoice and each milestone what was received, deducted as TDS/GST-TDS, held, retained and released. The invoice lists showed only total and outstanding although docs/04 §11.5 requires cash, TDS/GST-TDS, recoverable deductions, penalties and true outstanding as separate columns. The invoice detail had no payment view, and its outstandingAmount ignored receipts. Milestone billing (ADR-104) read only an invoice's header event, so an event billed on another event's invoice (ADR-126) showed Not invoiced.
Decision:
(1) One derived read model, owned by the invoices module and never stored: an InvoiceCollection on every FINALIZED invoice (invoice total, credit/debit note net, cash received, TDS, GST-TDS, hold, retention, penalty, other deductions, released, hold/retention still to release, outstanding = total + notes - cash - every deduction). Only POSTED receipts and FINALIZED notes count. It is on invoice.list and invoice.get under invoice.read, as docs/04 §11.5 places it; invoice.get also lists each receipt allocation with its deductions, so hold and retention can be released from the invoice.
(2) invoice.listBySource gives, per CAPEX event and OPEX installment of a project, the finalized invoices that billed it, the taxable billed and the collection on its share. Shares: the taxable billed is the event's own lines (the whole invoice for a single-event invoice, whose lines are untagged), matching the milestone cap at finalize. The invoice total and credit/debit notes are split by the events' line totals. An allocation naming an event (ADR-151) goes to that event alone. A whole-invoice allocation is split by what each event still owes after its named allocations, so paying the rest of an invoice never pushes an already-paid event into credit; when nothing is owed, by line totals. Splits round to paise with the last share taking the rounding (the ADR-110 apportion, moved to common/), so shares add back to the invoice exactly.
(3) Milestone summaries gain invoicedAmount and remainingAmount (amount - invoiced) on each event, installment and OPEX milestone, and the overview gains CAPEX/OPEX invoiced totals. Billing counts an event on another event's invoice through invoice_capex_sources. Billing status is still counted by units (ADR-104) in this slice.
(4) UI: invoice lists show the §11.5 settlement columns; the invoice page has "Payments against this invoice" with the figures, every receipt and a Release action; the milestone tab shows Invoiced and To Invoice, and a row expands to the invoices that billed it with their collection — payment by drilling into invoices, not as milestone state (docs/04 §11.1).
Consequences: No migration, no journal, no new permission. Receivable Aging, Pending Invoices and the receipt screen's event outstanding (ADR-151, which counts only allocations naming the event) are unchanged; the milestone drill-down is a reporting attribution, not a settlement rule.
Supersedes: none. ADR-104 decision 3 (units, not amount) is to be revisited in slice 2, when an event or installment can be billed across several invoices.
```

```text
ID: ADR-156
Date: 2026-10-27
Status: Accepted — implemented; verified by unit and integration tests
Context: Users bill a milestone event in parts (e.g. 70 of an event worth 100 now, the other 30 later), and an OPEX installment likewise. Each event and installment could have only one non-void invoice, and finalizing closed its payment alert, so the rest could never be invoiced. Slice 2 of ADR-155.
Decision:
(1) A CAPEX event or OPEX installment may be billed across several finalized invoices. Only one invoice per event or installment may be open (a draft or an issued proforma) at a time. Database: the partial unique indexes on invoices.source_event_id / source_installment_id now cover status IN ('DRAFT','ISSUED'); invoice_capex_sources gains finalized_at, set at finalize, and its open-row index excludes finalized rows. A finalized row still counts as billing the event.
(2) Billing status is by amount, superseding ADR-104 decision 3: per event/installment, nothing finalized is NOT_INVOICED, part of its amount PARTIALLY_INVOICED, all of it FULLY_INVOICED. A milestone is FULLY_INVOICED when every unit still expecting an invoice is fully invoiced; a closed (cancelled/skipped) unit billed in part counts as done, since nothing more will be billed on it. invoicedCount counts fully invoiced units.
(3) Alerts follow the amount: at finalize and void, each billed source's alert is CLOSED when fully invoiced (pointing at the invoice that finished it) and OPEN otherwise, so a partly billed source keeps its alert open and a void reopens it. An installment is INVOICED when fully invoiced, INVOICE_DRAFT while an invoice is open, else PLANNED. A new draft is refused only for a source already fully invoiced, whatever its alert status, so an older invoice that billed less than the amount can still be followed up.
(4) No new cap: the milestone's approved amount stays the only hard limit at finalize (ADR-126). Billing above one event's amount is still allowed, as before, and shows as "Over by" on the milestone tab; a hard per-event cap would have blocked events that cannot be edited after they are achieved. The invoice form shows what is already invoiced and suggests what is left.
(5) An installment billed in part cannot be skipped; its invoices are voided first. Cancelling an event is unchanged.
(6) Screens: the payment alert shows invoiced and left to invoice, and the project's pending amount sums what is left; Pending Invoices' billing-pending rows (and the dashboard card built on them) show what is left; the milestone tab shows billing by amount on events and installments and a Raise Invoice action wherever something is left to invoice.
Consequences: Migration 20261027120000_invoice_partial_billing; existing finalized capex source rows get finalized_at. Rollback recreates the previous indexes (fails while any source has two non-void invoices) and drops finalized_at. Receipts are unchanged: an allocation still pays one invoice or one event of it (ADR-151).
Supersedes: ADR-104 decision 3 (count by units); the one-non-void-invoice-per-source rule of Phase 5.1 and ADR-126.
```

```text
ID: ADR-157
Date: 2026-10-27
Status: Accepted — confirmed by the product owner
Context: Commit 460daa1 (2026-09-30) reduced the Project Manager and Hardware Manager to one shared staff permission set (ADR-139) and dropped project_cost.read and introducer_fee.read, which ADR-097/099 had given the Project Manager for the project Costs tab. A roles test still expected them, and ADR-139's text reads as if project managers see project figures.
Decision: Keep the reduced set. Project Manager and Hardware Manager do not see project manpower or introducer costs; those stay with finance roles.
Consequences: The roles test now asserts both keys are absent. No seed or data change.
Supersedes: the project_cost.read and introducer_fee.read grants of ADR-097 B / ADR-099 for the Project Manager.
```

```text
ID: ADR-158
Date: 2026-10-27
Status: Accepted — implemented; verified by unit and integration tests
Context: Users were logged out on their own. Once the 60-minute access token expires, the session is renewed by rotating the refresh token, and more than one caller can do that at the same moment: the Next.js proxy on a page navigation, the browser on its next API call, and other open tabs. Whichever came second presented a token that had just been rotated, which the API treated as theft and answered by revoking the whole login. The previous access token also stopped verifying the instant its session rotated, so a page loaded in that moment was sent to /login. Separately, the web server's in-flight refresh was one promise shared by every request on the server, so two users refreshing at once could have the second answered with the first user's cookies.
Decision:
(1) Reuse grace window, REFRESH_REUSE_GRACE_SECONDS (default 60): a refresh token rotated that recently, in a login that still has a live session, gets its own successor in the same family instead of revoking it. Older reuse is still theft and revokes the family. A logged-out or revoked login is never reopened.
(2) An access token whose session was rotated within the window still verifies while its login has a live session; logout, password reset and reuse revocation still end it at once.
(3) Logout revokes every live session of the login (family), since a grace refresh can leave two.
(4) The web server shares an in-flight refresh only between requests carrying the same refresh token.
Consequences: uq_sessions_rotated_from_session_id still records one successor per session; a grace successor is linked by family only, and its SESSION_REFRESHED audit event says so. The refresh token still expires one day after the last refresh (REFRESH_TOKEN_TTL_DAYS), so a user idle for a day is still logged out, by design. No migration.
Supersedes: the "any reuse of a rotated token revokes the family" rule of the Phase 2 identity design, for reuse within the grace window only.
Addendum 2026-10-28: renewal had in fact never succeeded — the client sent the body `null`, which the strict JSON parser rejected with a CORS-less 400 — so users were logged out every hour regardless. The client now sends `{}`; verified in a real browser.
```

```text
ID: ADR-159
Date: 2026-10-28
Status: Accepted — implemented; verified by unit and integration tests and a browser render
Context: The product owner asked for a financial summary on the Dashboard: payment received, total expense, net profit with margin, invoiced but not received; money to recover (EMD, SD, unpaid invoices, hold, penalty, inward TDS); expense by category and by project type; GST position; and what is locked in receivables — with quick periods (this/last month, this quarter, this/last financial year, all time).
Decision:
(1) Net profit is accrual, by the product owner's choice: Project P&L's contribution, i.e. income invoiced in the period less every posted cost, salaries included; margin is contribution over income. Payment received is Client Payments' cash received (net of deductions), shown as its own figure and not netted against expense.
(2) Every figure is a report's own for the same filters, shown only with that report's permission, and links to it: Client Payments, Project P&L, Receivable Aging, Pending Invoices, EMD/SD Recovery, Payment Holds, Penalties, TDS Inward, GST Net, Cost to Company. Expense by category is P&L's own expense accounts (largest six, the rest as Other), so it adds up exactly to total expense; the mock's headings (purchase, server, subscription…) are not ledger categories and are not invented. Expense by project type groups Cost to Company's project rows by the project's type — the one grouping done for the dashboard — and its total is Cost to Company's total (project costs, salaries by allocation), labelled so. Shares are percentages of the shown total.
(3) The new sections sit above the existing cards and charts, which stay.
Consequences: DashboardOverview gains `finance` (headline, recover, breakdowns). The dashboard reads four more reports (client payments, cost to company, penalties, payment holds) and TDS inward for the period. No migration, no new permission.
Supersedes: none; amends ADR-119's "no figure is computed for the dashboard alone" for the project-type grouping and percentages only.
```

```text
ID: ADR-160
Date: 2026-10-28
Status: Accepted — implemented; verified by integration tests
Context: Adding a bank account asked the user to pick its ledger account from the whole Chart of Accounts, and adding a credit card asked for its payable ledger the same way. Users found this confusing: they had to know the ledger existed (or create it first in the Chart of Accounts) and could pick a wrong one, while the seed itself says an admin creates one ledger per bank account under Bank Accounts and one per credit card under Credit Card Payable.
Decision: The ledger is no longer chosen on the forms. Creating a bank account creates its own ledger under the Bank Accounts group (code BANK_<name>), and creating a credit card creates its payable ledger under Credit Card Payable (code CARD_<name>_<last four>), in the same transaction, named after the account, with a numbered code when the name is already taken, and a readable name told apart by the masked account number (or else a plain number: "HDFC Operating Account 2"), and a LEDGER_ACCOUNT_CREATED audit event. A debit card still uses its bank account's ledger. The API still accepts an existing ledger id (scripts, tests, the seed). The edit forms show the linked ledger read-only; it stays permanent.
Consequences: Each bank account and credit card always has a ledger of its own, so bank balances, reconciliation and card settlement read one account each. An organization whose chart has no active Bank Accounts / Credit Card Payable group gets a clear error. No migration.
Supersedes: the manual "create a ledger per bank account / credit card" step of ADR-088 and the Phase 5.2 bank account form.
```

```text
ID: ADR-161
Date: 2026-10-28
Status: Accepted — implemented (screens)
Context: A receipt's deductions offered both "Accepted penalty" and "Disputed penalty". The product owner wants one penalty.
Decision: The receipt form offers a single "Penalty", recorded as ACCEPTED_PENALTY with its existing posting (to the penalty category's accepted account, or Penalty Revenue Deduction): the penalty is treated as final and reduces income. "Disputed penalty" is no longer offered. Receipts that already carry a disputed penalty keep it and still show it, and the Penalties, Client Payments and outstanding-deduction reports keep their disputed figures. The API still accepts DISPUTED_PENALTY (existing data, scripts and the money-flow scenario).
Consequences: A penalty the company contests is no longer recorded separately as recoverable; if it is later recovered, that is recorded as a separate receipt. No migration.
Supersedes: the accepted/disputed choice of the receipt form (docs/04 §11.6 deduction and penalty sections).
```

```text
ID: ADR-162
Date: 2026-10-28
Status: Accepted — implemented (screens)
Context: The financial summary of ADR-159 was placed above the older report cards of ADR-119. The two parts used different tiles, headings and chart frames, and several figures appeared twice (unpaid invoices, hold and retention, income and contribution, net GST, EMD/SD cash, expenses). The product owner found the page inconsistent.
Decision: The Dashboard is one layout. The four headline tiles come first, then every other figure in a single tile style under one heading style: "Money to Recover & Watch" (past due, billing pending, EMD, SD, hold, penalty) and "Cash, Tax & Payables" (bank balance, TDS to deposit, TDS certificates pending, TDS deducted by clients, owed to vendors). Then "Breakdown" (the four breakdown cards, framed like every other chart card) and "Trends & Attention" (the existing charts). An older card is hidden while the summary figure that covers it is on screen; a viewer without that summary report still sees the card. The unpaid-invoice figure is shown once, as a headline tile. The API's cards and figures are unchanged.
Consequences: No figure is shown twice. The Expenses card (expense register) is hidden while Total Expense (Project P&L, salaries included) is shown, so the page states one expense figure.
Supersedes: the separate card sections (Receivables, Performance, Cash & Tax, Payables & Operations) of the ADR-119 Dashboard layout.
```

```text
ID: ADR-163
Date: 2026-10-28
Status: Accepted — implemented; integration test written, not yet run (test database unreachable)
Context: Voiding a finalized invoice kept its number taken for good: the unique index and the Finalize check covered every numbered invoice, voided ones included. The product owner wants a voided invoice's number to be usable again.
Decision: A voided invoice keeps its number, its reversal and its audit trail unchanged. Only invoices that are not voided hold a number exclusively in their GST registration and financial year: the partial unique index uq_invoices_number_scope now also has "status != 'VOID'", and the Finalize number check ignores voided invoices. The generated number counts distinct numbers in the series, so a reused number counts once and the next generated number does not skip. Reuse is a choice, never automatic: a blank number still takes the next one in the series. The Finalize dialog lists the voided numbers that are free for the chosen invoice date (invoices.reusableNumbers, invoice.finalize permission) as one-click choices.
Consequences: The GST register can show one number twice, once VOID and once FINALIZED. Rule 46(b) GST asks for a unique, consecutive series. Reusing a number is safe only if the voided invoice was never sent to the client or reported in GSTR-1; once reported, it is a cancelled document (GSTR-1 Table 13) and its number should not be issued again. The dialog says so. The system does not track GSTR-1 filing, so it does not block the reuse. Rollback of the migration fails if a number has been reused meanwhile.
Supersedes: "invoice number unique once assigned" of ADR-068 and the "unused" rule of ADR-150, for voided invoices only.
```

```text
ID: ADR-164
Date: 2026-10-28
Status: Accepted — implemented; integration test written, not yet run (test database unreachable)
Context: Voiding a finalized invoice reversed only the invoice's own journal. Its posted receipts stayed, leaving the client's receivable in credit and the void invoice showing payments. The product owner wants the receipts reversed with the invoice.
Decision: Voiding a finalized invoice first reverses each posted receipt allocated to it, through the existing receipt reversal (its own reversal journal, status REVERSED, audit event), dated on the void's accounting date, with the reason "Invoice <number> voided: <void reason>". All checks run before anything is reversed. The void is refused when: the user lacks receipt.reverse; a receipt also pays another invoice (reversing it would unsettle that invoice too, so the user reverses it and records the other payment again); or a hold or retention on a receipt has already been released. Each receipt reversal's idempotency key derives from the void's key, so a retry neither double-reverses nor stops at a receipt already reversed. A draft or proforma void is unchanged; neither can have receipts.
Consequences: The money stays in the bank in reality; after a void, the user records the receipt again against the corrected invoice. The bank reconciliation shows the original and reversal entries for the receipt. The Void dialog lists the payments that will be reversed.
Supersedes: nothing; extends the invoice void of ADR-068.
```

```text
ID: ADR-165
Date: 2026-10-28
Status: Accepted — implemented; integration test updated, not yet run (test database unreachable)
Context: Finalizing a CAPEX invoice was refused when the milestone's finalized invoices would exceed its approved amount (docs/01; ADR-068). OPEX had no such check. The product owner says amounts are not always fixed in practice and neither should be refused.
Decision: No cap at finalize, for CAPEX or OPEX. A draft's detail carries overBilledBy: its taxable total less what is left (amount not yet billed by finalized invoices) on its event(s) or installment, when positive. The Finalize dialog shows it as a warning and still allows finalizing. Remaining amounts on events, installments and milestones are shown as zero, never negative; the invoiced amount shows the full figure.
Consequences: Over-billing is visible, not prevented. An event or installment billed in excess reads as fully invoiced. The approved amount stays as the record of what was approved; an amendment is not required.
Supersedes: the CAPEX approved-amount cap of docs/01 "Milestone, invoice, and receipt" and ADR-068.
```

```text
ID: ADR-166
Date: 2026-10-29
Status: Accepted — implemented; verified by the money-flow integration scenario
Context: The product owner wants the project page to show at a glance what the project is worth, billed, received, spent and made, with server and subscription costs visible, EMD/SD out, and physical progress, following a supplied mock. The mock stated profit as "received − expense", which mixes GST-inclusive bank receipts with GST-exclusive costs.
Decision: The project financial summary adds, on the GST-exclusive basis of the contract value: billedExclGst (taxable value of finalized invoices, plus debit notes, less credit notes) and billedPercent of the contract; profit = billedExclGst − recognized project expense (accrual basis, GST out of both sides) and its margin on billed; serverExpense and subscriptionExpense split out of directExpense by the cycle payment that posted them, with otherDirectExpense the rest; emdLodged and sdLodged still lodged against the project or, when no project is set on the instrument, against the tender the project came from. The project page shows a figures strip above the tabs (contract value, billed with a progress bar, received, expense, profit with margin, EMD, SD, physical progress) and the Detail tab's right column shows the money summary, the expense breakdown by head, and EMD/SD/hold. The Financials tab shows the server/subscription split and the profit line with its basis.
Consequences: Profit appears with its basis named. Received stays a cash figure (GST included) and is not used in profit. Unbilled work in progress is not counted as income, so profit early in a project can be negative until it is billed.
Supersedes: the "no Profit/Margin" note of ADR-086/ADR-089 for the project summary.
```

```text
ID: ADR-167
Date: 2026-10-29
Status: Accepted — implemented; verified by integration tests
Context: Since partial billing (ADR-156), an event or installment counts as fully invoiced only when finalized invoices reach its amount. A monthly server bill varies a little, so an invoice for slightly less than the installment left the alert open for a few hundred rupees, and the next month's bill was then raised from the wrong, still-open alert.
Decision: When a tax invoice draft bills less than is left on its event(s) or installment (taxable, before GST), Finalize asks whether it is the full bill. The answer is stored on the invoice (invoices.completes_source). A finalized invoice so marked makes its sources fully invoiced: remaining is zero, the installment becomes INVOICED, the alert closes, and the milestone's remaining is the sum of what each unit still expects. Voiding that invoice undoes it, since a voided invoice no longer counts. When the draft bills the whole remainder or more, nothing is asked. Proformas are not asked.
Consequences: The amount not billed is not written off anywhere; it was never posted. Milestone remaining now sums each unit's own remaining, so over-billing one unit no longer offsets another unit's shortfall.
Supersedes: nothing; refines ADR-156.
```

```text
ID: ADR-168
Date: 2026-10-29
Status: Accepted — implemented
Context: The card form asked for "masked last four digits", which people found awkward; they wanted to type the full card number and have it masked.
Decision: The Add/Edit Card form takes the full card number (12–19 digits, Luhn-checked, shown in groups of four). Only its last four digits are sent to the API and stored, as before (the CardAccount decision: no full card number column). The full number never leaves the browser, and Save as draft leaves it out because drafts are stored on the server. On edit the field is optional: blank keeps the stored last four.
Consequences: The full number cannot be shown later; it was never stored. Keeping it would bring the CRM under PCI DSS card-data rules, which this system is not built for.
Supersedes: nothing.
```

```text
ID: ADR-169
Date: 2026-10-29
Status: Accepted — implemented; unit-tested; integration spec written, not yet run (test database was down)
Context: The Subscription and Server modules (ADR-088: plans with a frequency, generated cycles, a payment per cycle) were not used: production had no subscription, server or cycle. The accounts team keeps one Excel sheet of bills actually paid (employee, name, basic, GST, total, date, INR rate, project, server or subscription, payment mode, card or account number, legal entity) and wants to bring that in instead.
Decision: The Subscription, Server, ServerProject, RecurringCycle and RecurringCyclePayment models, their pages, their subscription.* and server.* permissions and the Recurring Spend report (ADR-118) are removed. A server or subscription bill is a direct expense tagged spend_type SERVER or SUBSCRIPTION; a bill paid in another currency also keeps foreign_amount and exchange_rate, every other amount being in rupees. "Servers & Subscriptions" lists them and imports the sheet: each row is checked against the masters (legal entity by name or PAN, project of that legal entity, active employee by name or code, active payment mode, a card of that legal entity and type by its last four digits, or a bank account by its last four digits or name, expense category or the default Hosting / Cloud / Infra for a server and Software Licenses for a subscription). A row that matches nothing gets its own error, with the closest name offered for a typo; several active employees with one name are chosen by code in the preview. Currency signs are ignored; a blank INR rate is 1 and basic, GST and total are multiplied by it; Total Paid must equal Basic + GST. The sheet has the GST amount only, so it is booked as input GST as stated, IGST unless the sheet's optional GST Type column or the preview says CGST+SGST (split in half). A bill already posted (same legal entity, date, name, rupee amount) is refused. Nothing is posted until every row is clear; then each row posts as a direct expense (createAndPostDraft), keyed per row so a retried import does not post twice. Importing needs direct_expense.post. The Direct Expense form can tag a bill by hand too. NEFT/RTGS is deactivated as a payment mode, being the same as Bank Transfer; the sheet's NEFT, RTGS and IMPS read as Bank Transfer.
Consequences: Project Manager and Hardware Manager no longer see servers (they had server.read/create/update/pause/resume and no direct expense rights). No forecast of upcoming server or subscription bills remains. The project page's server/subscription split and Cost to Company's "Servers & subscriptions" head read the tag. The migration tags any expense already paid through a cycle before dropping the cycle tables.
Addendum (same day): Bills can also be added one at a time on the same page, checked as an imported row is. A posted bill is never edited or deleted. "Correct" reverses it (on a date the user picks, its own date by default, with a reason recorded as "Corrected: …") and posts the corrected bill, after checking the correction first so a bad one leaves the bill as it was; the bill's documents are linked to the corrected bill as well, and a DIRECT_EXPENSE_CORRECTED audit event links the two. "Reverse" posts the opposite entry. Every direct expense, not only servers and subscriptions, can carry a bill link (bill_url, a web address); the sheet's optional Bill Link column fills it; a bill file is uploaded as a document from the list's Files button or the expense's Documents. A bill in another currency also keeps its basic amount in that currency (foreign_taxable_amount), so a correction starts from the bill as entered.
Supersedes: the Subscription and Server parts of ADR-088; the Recurring Spend report of ADR-118.
```

```text
ID: ADR-170
Date: 2026-10-29
Status: Accepted — implemented; unit-tested; integration spec updated, not yet run (test database was down)
Context: The employee Excel import required a joining date, which the team does not always have, and refused any employee code already in the CRM, so a sheet could not be used again to bring salaries up to date.
Decision: employees.joining_date is nullable. The import's Joining Date column is optional, and a new Salary Effective From column is read. A new code creates the employee; its salary starts from Salary Effective From, or else the joining date, and a salary with neither is a row error. A code already here updates that employee: a filled cell that differs changes the field, a blank cell keeps it; a recorded joining date is not changed by the import (a different one is a row error), a missing one is filled. A salary equal to the current one is kept as it is; a different one becomes a salary revision from Salary Effective From, which must be after the current salary started and not before joining, through the same revision writer as a manual raise (applySalaryRevision: the open revision closes the day before). The preview marks each row New, Update (with every change in words) or No change; the import is one transaction. A manually added employee still needs a joining date; the edit form shows it read-only.
Consequences: Reports that start an employee from the joining date (Manpower Utilization) count one without a joining date from the start. A salary revision for an employee without a joining date has no lower bound but the previous revision.
Supersedes: nothing; refines ADR-098 (salary revisions) for the import.
```

```text
ID: ADR-171
Date: 2026-10-29
Status: Accepted — implemented; integration test written, not yet run (test database was down)
Context: The product owner wants to see who approves an employee expense claim, set per legal entity (Accountant, Admin or CEO), and who reimbursed it. Approval was by permission alone (employee_claim.approve), the same for every legal entity, and the claim screens showed decisions without names and nothing of who reimbursed.
Decision: A legal entity can list its expense claim approvers (legal_entity_claim_approvers), set from Legal Entities by a legal_entity.manage holder; only active users holding employee_claim.approve can be listed. When a legal entity lists approvers, only they approve or reject its claims (checked by the API; self-approval still also needs employee_claim.self_approve and a note). With none listed, anyone holding the permission may, as before. Claim lists and the claim page show, while submitted, "Waiting for: <listed names>" or "Waiting for any approver: <everyone with the permission>"; afterwards who approved or rejected it and when, and who reimbursed it and when. The Approve and Reject buttons show only to someone allowed to use them. Setting the list is audited (LEGAL_ENTITY_CLAIM_APPROVERS_SET).
Consequences: Permissions stay role-based; the list narrows who of the permission holders may decide for one legal entity. Removing someone's approve permission does not remove them from a list, but they can no longer reach the approve action.
Supersedes: nothing; refines ADR-081 (claims approval).
```

```text
ID: ADR-171
Date: 2026-10-29
Status: Accepted — implemented; integration test written, not yet run (test database was down)
Context: The product owner wants employee expense claims approved per legal entity by named people (Accountant, Admin, CEO), the claim to show who will approve it, and who approved and who reimbursed it. employee_claim.approve was a single organization-wide permission, and approvals and reimbursements were stored only as user ids.
Decision: A new legal_entity_claim_approvers list (legal entity → users), set from Legal Entities ("Approvers", legal_entity.manage). Only an active user holding employee_claim.approve can be listed. When a legal entity lists approvers, only they may approve or reject its claims (checked in the service; the Approve and Reject buttons hide for others); an empty list means anyone holding the permission, as before. A claim carries approval (LISTED with the names, or ANY_APPROVER with everyone holding the permission), its latest decision with the decider's name and date, and reimbursedByName; each decision in the history carries its actor's name. The claims list, the claim page and My Claims show "Waiting for: …", "Approved/Rejected by … · date" and "Reimbursed by … · date". Setting the list is audited (LEGAL_ENTITY_CLAIM_APPROVERS_SET).
Consequences: Self-approval still needs employee_claim.self_approve, and for a listed entity the claimant must also be on the list. Reimbursing is not limited by the list.
Revision (same day, product owner): approvers are not chosen by hand. A legal entity's approvers are worked out: users holding employee_claim.approve whose own employee record is in that legal entity, plus those whose employee record has no legal entity or who have none (an Admin or CEO). Only they approve or reject its claims. When that is nobody, anyone holding the permission may, as before. The hand-set list (legal_entity_claim_approvers) is dropped (migration 20261029170000); Legal Entities shows the worked-out approvers read-only. To change who approves an entity, set the approver's legal entity in Employees.
Supersedes: nothing; refines ADR-081 (employee expense claims).
```

```text
ID: ADR-172
Date: 2026-10-29
Status: Accepted — implemented; unit-tested (scope rules); not built or integration-tested here
Context: The product owner wants each user to see only their own legal entity's data across the whole CRM, except Admin. Until now every user with a permission saw every legal entity.
Decision: A new permission legal_entity.access_all, held by the Admin role only (Accountant and CEO keep every other permission). Every signed-in request runs in an entity scope (AsyncLocalStorage, set by the tRPC protected middleware from the request context): with access_all, none; otherwise the legal entity on the user's own employee record, or no legal entity at all when it is not set (the screens then say "No legal entity is set on your employee record"). A Prisma query extension narrows every find, count, aggregate, groupBy, update and delete inside a scope: models with their own legal entity directly (an unassigned employee or an organization-level audit event stays visible), their children through the parent (invoice lines, receipt allocations, journal lines, milestones and installments, alerts, bank statement lines, salary lines …), and the legal entity list itself. Records without a legal entity stay shared: clients, vendors, leads, tenders, introducers, products, the chart of accounts, masters, users and roles. A create is checked by the service's own lookup of the legal entity or project it names, which the scope narrows. Document numbers counted across the organization (stock receipts and issues, payment requests, introducer accruals and payments, salary payments) and the organization's legal-entity count used by payroll are read outside the scope (unscoped()). The worker, scripts and seed run with no scope.
Consequences: A non-Admin user sees nothing until their employee record has a legal entity; set Employees' legal entity for every login before going live. A user works for one legal entity at a time. Reports, the dashboard and the ledger show only the user's legal entity, because journal lines are narrowed through their journal entry.
Supersedes: nothing.
```

```text
ID: ADR-173
Date: 2026-10-29
Status: Accepted — implemented (seed and migration); not integration-tested here
Context: A review of the staff roles: the project page shows progress, milestones and dues, which a Project Manager could not see or record, and an Employee could delete and re-file documents.
Decision: Project Manager and Hardware Manager stay one set (ADR-139, confirmed by the product owner) and gain project_progress.create, milestone.read and payment_alert.read. Employee loses document.manage; it keeps reading, uploading and downloading documents, seeing the projects it is on, raising a purchase bill and filing its own expense claims. Neither staff role sees invoices, receipts, payments, the books, reports, users or roles. Migration 20261029190000 applies this to existing roles by name; the seed matches it.
Consequences: Production's Project Manager (one user) gains the three keys on deploy.
Supersedes: nothing; refines ADR-139.
```

```text
ID: ADR-174
Date: 2026-10-29
Status: Accepted — implemented; not built or tested here
Context: The product owner wants an Admin to log in as another user from Users, to see what they see.
Decision: A new permission user.impersonate, held by the Admin role only. "Log in as" on Users opens a new session for that user, recording the Admin and the Admin's own session (sessions.impersonator_user_id, impersonator_session_id, carried over on every refresh); the Admin's session stays open. Every screen then shows "You are logged in as … by …" with "Return to my login", which ends the viewing-as login and signs the Admin back in through their own login, or to the sign-in page if that has ended meanwhile. The viewing-as session has exactly the user's permissions and legal-entity scope (ADR-172). Not allowed: logging in as yourself, as an inactive user, as another holder of user.impersonate, or from inside a viewing-as session. Audited: USER_IMPERSONATION_STARTED and USER_IMPERSONATION_ENDED, and every audit event written while viewing as someone carries commandPayload.impersonatedBy = the Admin.
Consequences: An Admin acts as the user while viewing as them; the audit trail names both.
Supersedes: nothing.
```

```text
ID: ADR-175
Date: 2026-10-29
Status: Accepted — implemented; not built or tested here
Context: Salary is confidential, but Project Manager and Hardware Manager, holding employee.read and employee_allocation.*, were sent every employee's salary (Employees), their salary history, and each employee-month's gross salary and the cost of each allocation line.
Decision: A new permission employee.read_salary, held by Admin, Accountant and CEO. Without it the API leaves salary out rather than the screen hiding it: employees.list and getDetail send displayedSalaryAmount as null; listSalaryRevisions needs the permission; the allocation month (getMonth, saveDraft, submit, reopen) and summary send no gross salary, allocated total or per-line amount. Managers keep allocating their team by percentage. The Employees and Employee Allocation screens drop the salary column, salary history and salary boxes for them. Salary imports, project manpower cost, project financials and reports were already behind permissions the staff roles do not hold.
Consequences: Admin viewing as a manager (ADR-174) sees no salary either, as the manager would.
Supersedes: the note in ADR-098 that salary history is gated on employee.read.
```

```text
ID: ADR-176
Date: 2026-10-30
Status: Accepted — implemented; sheet reader unit-tested; not built or integration-tested here
Context: The product owner found Vendor Bills, Payment Requests and Vendor Payments, three separate screens, confusing, wanted one screen showing a bill's whole life by stage, no separate payment-request approval, and existing bills brought in from Excel with vendors picked or created automatically.
Decision: A Purchases screen lists every vendor bill by a derived stage: Draft, Waiting approval (submitted), To pay (approved, nothing paid), Part paid, Paid (posted vendor payments settle the total, TDS included), Rejected / void. Approve, Reject and Pay are row actions; "Pay selected" pays several bills of one vendor and legal entity. Paying needs only vendor_payment.post: behind the scenes the payment request the books link a payment to is created, submitted and approved by the payer with the note "Paid directly from Purchases", after using up any approved request lines of those bills not yet paid, then the vendor payment posts with TDS per bill. Purchase bills can be imported from Excel (vendor_bill.approve): one row per line, rows with the same vendor and bill number make one bill; the vendor is matched by GSTIN, else by name, and created when not found (its state from the GSTIN or a Vendor State column); project, our GST registration (the legal entity's only one, or an "Our GSTIN" column), expense category or stock item, unit and GST % (whole rate) are checked, and a bill already recorded for the vendor is refused; each bill is created, submitted and approved by the importer ("Imported from Excel"), and when the row has a Paid Date, Bank Account and Payment Mode it is paid too. The menu shows Purchases instead of Vendor Bills, Payment Requests and Vendor Payments; their pages stay (a bill's lines and documents, a payment and its reversal) with their route rules.
Consequences: Payment requests still exist in the books, one per payment made from Purchases; nobody approves them separately. A card payment is not recorded here (direct expense).
Supersedes: the separate payment-request approval step of ADR-080 for payments made from Purchases.
```

```text
ID: ADR-177
Date: 2026-10-30
Status: Accepted — implemented; not built or tested here
Context: Not every purchase belongs to a project; the vendor bill form required one.
Decision: vendor_bills.project_id is nullable. A bill without a project is company overhead: its legal entity is chosen on the form (and its GST registration from that legal entity), its journal lines carry no project, it adds to no project's cost, and its payment stays unassigned in project cash reports. A user who sees every project sees it; otherwise only whoever entered it (bill, payment request and payment access checks alike). On a draft, clearing the project turns it into company overhead. Vendor pickers on the bill and stock-receipt forms add a vendor with F2 (name, GSTIN, PAN, state).
Consequences: Reports list such bills under "Company overhead" / unassigned.
Supersedes: the "every vendor bill has a project" rule of ADR-079.
```

```text
ID: ADR-178
Date: 2026-10-30
Status: Accepted — implemented; sheet reader unit-tested; not built or integration-tested here
Context: The team's purchase records are a register of bills already paid (Date, Details, Supplier, Company, Invoice Link, Category, Other Charges, Amount, Amount With GST, Project, Paid by, Payment Receipt, Material Inward, Reimbursement Status/Date, status). The product owner asked for the Purchases import to take that sheet as it is, plus Payment Mode and Card/Account no columns as in the Server & Subscription import, instead of the line-per-row format of ADR-176.
Decision: The Purchases import reads the register. Each row becomes one bill, approved, then paid on its Date from the card or account given (through Purchases' own payment, ADR-176). Supplier is matched by name, else added to Vendors in the Company's GST state; Company is the legal entity; Project is optional (ADR-177) and must belong to the Company; Category must be an expense category in Masters (blank: Office / Administrative; anything else not found is a row error with the closest name offered). Taxable value = Amount + Other Charges ("-" or blank is none); GST = Amount With GST − that, booked exactly as stated (vendor bills accept a stated GST amount, split CGST+SGST within the state, else IGST by the vendor's state). Payment Mode as in the master (NEFT, RTGS, IMPS read as Bank Transfer); Card/Account no is the bank account (last four digits or name) or a debit card (paid from its linked bank account); a credit card cannot settle a purchase bill and is a row error. The sheet has no bill number, so one is made from the row (REG-date-fingerprint of supplier, date, details and total): the same row imported again is caught as already recorded. Invoice Link and Payment Receipt are kept on the bill (new vendor_bills.bill_url, payment_receipt_url, shown on the bill); Paid by is added to the bill's details; Material Inward and the Reimbursement columns are not imported.
Consequences: Bills paid by an employee and reimbursed come in as paid from the company account given in Card/Account no.
Supersedes: the import format of ADR-176.
```

```text
ID: ADR-179
Date: 2026-10-30
Status: Accepted — implemented; not built or integration-tested here
Context: Gaps left after ADR-176–178: a credit card could not settle a purchase bill, the import gave no way to change a row's GST split, F2 for a new vendor was missing on two forms, and the Pay dialog asked for the account every time.
Decision: A vendor payment is made from exactly one of a bank account or a credit card (vendor_payments.card_account_id; bank_account_id nullable; chk_vendor_payments_one_source). Paid by credit card it posts Dr Accounts Payable — Vendor / Cr the card's Credit Card Payable ledger (TDS as before); the card bill is settled later from Card Accounts. A debit card still pays from its linked bank account. Purchases' Pay dialog and the register import accept a credit card. The import preview has a per-row GST switch (CGST+SGST / IGST) overriding the vendor's state; vendor bills take a stated GST split per line for this. The Pay dialog remembers, per legal entity and in that browser only, the last payment mode and account or card. The F2 vendor picker is also on the draft bill editor and the payment request form. Production had no payment requests or vendor bills, so no half-approved request needed handling.
Consequences: Payment lists show "Paid from" as the bank account or the credit card. Card payments are not bank movements, so they do not appear in cash or bank reconciliation until the card bill is settled.
Supersedes: nothing; extends ADR-176–178.
```

```text
ID: ADR-180
Date: 2026-10-30
Status: Accepted — implemented
Context: The product owner asked that a Category in the purchase register that is not in Masters be added rather than rejected, matched ignoring case and spaces, and that spaces inside a card or account number (e.g. "1234 55555 5555 555") be ignored.
Decision: The Purchases import matches Category to Masters ignoring case, spaces and punctuation (as for every other name); one not found is added to Expense Categories on import (code made from the name, audited as MASTER_CREATED) and the preview tags it "New category". A new category has no ledger, so it books to Other Expenses until a ledger is set on it in Masters. In both the Purchases and the Server & Subscription imports, a Card/Account no that is only digits, spaces and dashes loses the spaces and dashes; an account name keeps single spaces.
Consequences: A typo in Category creates a new category instead of an error; the preview tag is the check.
Supersedes: the "Category not found is a row error" part of ADR-178.
```

```text
ID: ADR-182
Date: 2026-10-30
Status: Accepted — implemented
Context: The Legal Entities list showed Zitaware's approvers as "Anyone who can approve" for Concentric, Garantir and Ninja Tech: when a legal entity had no approver of its own, ADR-171 let anyone holding employee_claim.approve approve its claims. The product owner pointed out that Zitaware's users must not approve another company's claims, and it contradicts ADR-172 (each user sees only their own legal entity's data).
Decision: A legal entity's claim approvers are the users holding employee_claim.approve whose own employee record is in that legal entity, or who hold legal_entity.access_all (Admin). Nobody from another legal entity, and no fallback: a legal entity with none shows "No approver yet" and its claims wait. The ANY_APPROVER rule is removed. A user with no legal entity and no access_all is not an approver (they see no data either).
Consequences: Today Concentric, Garantir and Ninja Tech claims are approved only by Admin until one of their own employees is given the approve permission. Specs that approved as a user with no role now use a real approver (testing/claim-approver.test-util.ts).
Supersedes: the "anyone may approve when the legal entity has none" fallback and the "approver tied to no legal entity" rule of ADR-171.
```

```text
ID: ADR-183
Date: 2026-10-30
Status: Accepted — implemented on the expense, advance, direct expense, server & subscription and purchase forms
Context: On "Add Expense for Employee" the employee list showed bare names from every company, so an Admin could not tell which company or which of two same-named people was meant, and a non-admin had to pick a company that could only be their own.
Decision: Entry forms ask for the legal entity first, through one shared field: someone who sees every legal entity (Admin, legal_entity.access_all) chooses it; everyone else has their own filled in and locked. The employee, project, bank account and card lists then show only that legal entity's, and employees read "Name · Code" (also on the lead, tender, project team, card holder and claim filter pickers). Changing the legal entity clears what depended on it. The API refuses a claim or advance on behalf of an employee of another legal entity, and a project of another legal entity than the one given (claims, advances, direct expenses).
Consequences: Forms that still pick a legal entity without this field (vendor bill, payment request, stock receipt, introducer payment, EMD/SD, TDS, new project, salary import, employee, bank account, financial year) show a non-admin only their own legal entity already (ADR-172) but do not fill it in yet.
Supersedes: the "legal entity only when no project" picker on these forms.
```

```text
ID: ADR-184
Date: 2026-11-02
Status: Accepted — production repaired and cleaned
Context: Production raised "The column invoices.status does not exist". On 2026-10-30 between 06:11 and 06:51 UTC, 69 migrations of a different project (SWM, trips, facilities, tickets; not in this repo) were applied to the CRM database, so _prisma_migrations holds 157 rows against this repo's 88. Those files are not on this machine, so which one did it and who ran it are unknown. Its tables were added (all empty) and its columns were added to users, roles, role_permissions, notifications and password_reset_tokens. Lost: the InvoiceStatus and SalaryPaymentStatus enums and so invoices.status and salary_payments.status, 3 partial unique indexes and 2 status indexes on them, chk_salary_payments_reversal_fields, and NOT NULL on users.email. The other project's required columns also blocked the CRM's insert into password_reset_tokens.
Decision: Repaired by adding back only what was lost, with no migration file, since this repo's migrations were never the cause and all are recorded as applied. Invoice status was rebuilt from each invoice's own fields (voided_at set: VOID; journal_entry_id set: FINALIZED) and agreed with its last audit action on all 22 invoices: 18 FINALIZED, 4 VOID. salary_payments had no rows. password_reset_tokens' expiresAt, otpHash and userId stopped being required. Audited as SCHEMA_REPAIRED. A CSV export of all 175 tables was taken first (pg_dump 16 refuses a version 17 server).
Consequences: prisma migrate deploy cannot repair this class of damage, because it only looks at what is recorded as applied. Until whatever ran those migrations no longer points at this DATABASE_URL, it can happen again; that source has not been found. users.email NOT NULL was restored separately on 2026-11-02, with no audit row of its own. Every rule, index and enum of this repo, and all 1,780 of its columns, are present and match a database built from its migrations alone, and prisma migrate status reports up to date. The other project's 53 tables (all empty), 49 columns on five of our tables (null or default values only), 23 enums, the btree_gist extension and 69 migration records were then removed by a script using no CASCADE, after a production dry run; the owner ran the commit by hand because the assistant's permission check declined it. Production now has 122 tables and 88 migration records, identical to this repo's migrations. Older differences between schema.prisma and the migrations (some foreign keys, one index name, form_drafts.updated_at default) appear on a clean database too and were left alone.
Supersedes: nothing.
```

```text
ID: ADR-185
Date: 2026-11-02
Status: Accepted — implemented
Context: The Server & Subscription sheet and form had an INR Rate (ADR-169): amounts were entered in the bill's currency and multiplied by it. The team does not use it; production has no bill with a rate.
Decision: INR Rate is removed from the sample sheet, the reader, the form, the import preview, the list and bill pages, the Direct Expense page and the contract. Basic, GST and Total Paid are rupees as entered. A sheet that still has an INR Rate (or exchange rate) column is refused with "INR Rate is no longer used. Remove that column and enter every amount in rupees.", so an old sheet's foreign amounts are never posted as rupees.
Consequences: direct_expenses.foreign_amount, foreign_taxable_amount and exchange_rate stay in the schema, unused and empty; dropping them would need a migration and gains nothing now. A bill paid in another currency is entered at its rupee amount.
Supersedes: the INR Rate part of ADR-169.
```

```text
ID: ADR-186
Date: 2026-11-02
Status: Accepted — implemented in the web app
Context: F2 to add a record from a dropdown existed only for vendors, introducers and the masters; on other forms a missing bank account, card, client, project and so on meant leaving the form.
Decision: Every entry-form dropdown of a record the user may create gets F2 (and a line under the list) that opens that record's full create form and selects the new record. One shell, QuickAddSelect, gives the behaviour; each record has a picker (EmployeeSelect, BankAccountSelect, CardAccountSelect, ClientSelect, ProjectSelect, ProductSelect, LedgerAccountSelect, GstRegistrationSelect, LegalEntitySelect) whose box renders the same fields component as the record's own page, so the two cannot differ. The form's legal entity (and card type) is passed in and locked in the box. F2 is offered only with the record's create permission (the API checks it again); F2 inside an open box reaches that box's own pickers. List filters, report filters, view selectors and pickers of documents (invoices, bills) keep plain dropdowns. Payment modes are a fixed system list and have no create. "Another company" pickers on EMD/SD use QuickAddSelect directly, not LegalEntitySelect, so they are not locked to the user's own entity.
Consequences: Pickers on vendor payment and receipt forms now read bank accounts directly (legal_entity.read) rather than the server's eligible list; that list was the entity's active accounts, and every role holding vendor_payment.post or receipt.post has legal_entity.read. LegalEntitySelect now locks non-admins to their own entity on the employee, vendor bill, TDS, EMD/SD, payment request, salary import and other forms too.
Supersedes: nothing.
```

```text
ID: ADR-187
Date: 2026-11-04
Status: Accepted — implemented
Context: The top bar had a "Search…" box from the shell prototype that was never connected; nothing defined what it searched.
Decision: It is a global search over projects, clients, leads, tenders, invoices (by number), vendor bills (by number or vendor), vendors and employees (by name or code). search.global searches only the kinds whose read permission the user holds, five matches each, at least two characters. Each kind is scoped as its own list is: projects, invoices and vendor bills follow ProjectsService.list for users without project.read_all (a vendor bill with no project is seen by whoever entered it), and the Prisma entity scope (ADR-172) narrows everything to the user's legal entities. A match opens its page; vendors and employees, which have no page of their own, open their list filtered by ?search=. Ctrl+K or / focuses the box. The UI package's Topbar takes the box as a slot so it stays free of app data.
Consequences: Matching uses case-insensitive contains (no index); fine at today's sizes, and a trigram index can be added when lists grow. Draft invoices have no number and are not found.
Supersedes: nothing.
```

```text
ID: ADR-188
Date: 2026-11-04
Status: Accepted — implemented
Context: The sidebar showed the organization's name and a fixed logo to everyone, while users work for different legal entities of the group (ADR-172).
Decision: The shell is white-labelled by the legal entity of the user's own employee record: its legal name and its logo (the one printed on its invoices) replace the organization's in the sidebar. auth.me returns brandName and brandHasLogo so the name renders with the first page; the logo is a separate auth.brandLogo read, cached for the session, to keep me small. With no employee record (e.g. a platform Admin) the organization and default logo stay. An Admin linked to an employee sees that employee's legal entity, not every entity.
Consequences: Changing a legal entity's logo or an employee's legal entity shows in the sidebar on the next page load.
Supersedes: nothing.
```

```text
ID: ADR-189
Date: 2026-11-04
Status: Accepted — implemented
Context: Employee sheets name departments and designations that are not in Masters yet ("Dot net", "Senior Programmer"), and type dates as 6/4/2025 or 6/4/25; both were rejected row by row.
Decision: (1) A Department or Designation the employee import does not find (matched by name or code without case and with runs of spaces as one) is no longer an error: the preview tags it New and lists the new names, and importing adds each once (first spelling in the sheet; code slugified and kept unique), audited MASTER_CREATED, in the same transaction as the employees. Employment Type and Legal Entity must still exist. (2) Every Excel import (employees, servers & subscriptions, purchases) reads a typed date with a one- or two-digit day and month and a two- or four-digit year, separated by / - or . (spaces ignored), or YYYY-MM-DD; a two-digit year is this century up to next year, else the last ("25" → 2025, "98" → 1998). (3) Bank account and card pickers label by number first ("****0595 · HDFC Operating", "•••• 7823 · Corporate Card").
Consequences: A misspelt department in a sheet becomes a new master; it can be merged or deactivated in Masters. A date cell Excel itself converted with a month-first locale is read as Excel stored it.
Supersedes: nothing.
```

```text
ID: ADR-190
Date: 2026-11-09
Status: Accepted — implemented
Context: Outbound email went through Postmark (POSTMARK_SERVER_TOKEN / POSTMARK_FROM_EMAIL). The business sends from its own Gmail account instead, stores documents in a bucket shared with other apps, and the seeded super admin must be the business's own address.
Decision: (1) The worker sends notification email over SMTP with nodemailer. Env: SMTP_HOST (default smtp.gmail.com), SMTP_PORT (default 465 = implicit TLS; other ports use STARTTLS), SMTP_USER, SMTP_PASS (a Gmail App Password), optional SMTP_FROM_EMAIL (defaults to SMTP_USER, since Gmail only sends as the account or a verified alias) and SMTP_FROM_NAME. An SMTP_PASS starting with replace-with- selects the fake provider, as the placeholder Postmark token did. Postmark and its env keys are removed. (2) Optional S3_PREFIX_FOLDER puts every new document key under that folder ("crm/<org>/<group>/<doc>-<file>"). The full key is stored on the document, so documents uploaded earlier keep their old key. (3) The seed's bootstrap admin is zitawaresolutions@gmail.com.
Consequences: Gmail limits sending (about 500 recipients a day for a free account); a send over the limit fails and the outbox retries it. A database seeded before this change keeps its old bootstrap admin, and re-seeding adds zitawaresolutions@gmail.com as another Admin; deactivate the old one under Users.
Supersedes: the Postmark choice in docs/02 and the POSTMARK_* names in ADR-specified .env.example defaults.
```

```text
ID: ADR-191
Date: 2026-11-09
Status: Accepted — implemented
Context: The CRM is for Zitaware Solutions. Its look (brass accent on slate, Calibri, the previous product name and logo) and its seed (another company's organization, legal entity, PAN and GSTIN) did not belong to Zitaware.
Decision: (1) Palette follows the Zitaware brand (website globals.css): ink #0a1b4d (navy), ink-2 #13296b, ink-3 #1d3a8a, paper #f5f7fa, line #e3e8f0, text #1e293b, muted #5b6a82, muted-2 #94a3bb. Primary buttons, selected chips, checkboxes and pagination use the sidebar navy (Ant Design colorPrimary #0a1b4d, hover #13296b), as the user asked on 2026-11-09. The brass token family is renamed brand: brand #0f5bff (links, focus, section labels), brand-hover #0b4de0, brand-dark #083db5, brand-soft #e8efff. New highlight #ffc700 / highlight-soft #fff6d1 / amber #ff8a00: yellow marks the active sidebar item (3px left bar), the active tab ink bar, text selection and the auth card edge, and is never used for text on white. Semantic colors are unchanged. StatusTag tone 'brass' becomes 'brand'. (2) Font is Plus Jakarta Sans for display and body, self-hosted through next/font. (3) Name is "Zitaware CRM"; logos are cut from the brand artwork: /logo-mark.png (Z, white and yellow on transparent) in the sidebar, /logo-lockup.png (navy wordmark, transparent) on the auth screens and as the invoice PDF fallback logo, /favicon.png (Z on navy), /apple-icon.png and /og.jpg. (4) The seed creates organization "Zitaware Solutions" (code ZITAWARE) and no legal entity, GST registration or financial year; an administrator adds Zitaware's own in the app, so no PAN or GSTIN is invented.
Consequences: The HTML prototypes keep their old colors; layout and fields still come from them, colors from docs/15. A database seeded before this change keeps its old organization, legal entity and financial year, and re-seeding adds a separate ZITAWARE organization; a fresh database is the clean path. Internal identifiers are renamed too: npm scope @zitaware/*, PM2 processes zitaware-crm-*, cookies zitaware_crm_access_token/zitaware_crm_refresh_token (existing sessions sign out once), form-draft storage keys zitaware-crm-form-draft*, audit-grant DB role zitaware_crm_app.
Supersedes: docs/15 sections 4, 5.1, 5.3, 6.1, 7.3 and 8.2 colors/fonts; the seeded legal entity of ADR-035+.
```

```text
ID: ADR-192
Date: 2026-11-09
Status: Accepted — implemented
Context: Zitaware CRM is for any kind of client, not government work. Tenders, EMD/SD and government master data had no use, the menu still said "Leads & Tenders", and the seed hard-coded a known admin password and an EMD ledger.
Decision: (1) No tenders. The menu and screens say Leads; the /tenders pages are deleted (old links redirect to /leads); the Tender Portals master, the TENDER search kind, the TENDER document entity type, the tender.* permissions and the tender-only seed rows (lead source, lead type, document category, Tender Fee expense category and ledger) are gone. Lead fields keep their columns; the reference section reads "client reference" and "proposal due". Stored stage codes (TENDER_LIVE…TENDER_LOST) are unchanged — users only see their labels. (2) No EMD/SD. The EMD & SD menu, pages, project tab, project-overview figures, the EMD/SD recovery report, its dashboard card, alerts and summary figures, and its controls-report check are removed; the security_instrument.* permissions, purposes, types, EMD Deposits ledger, EMD Paid expense category and SECURITY_INSTRUMENT entity type are no longer seeded (/emd-sd redirects to the dashboard). The generic Security Deposits group and Security Deposit Recoverable ledger stay. (3) Generic master data: client types Enterprise/Corporate, SMB/Startup, Individual, Non-profit/Institution; lead types New Business, Upsell/Cross-sell, AMC/Renewal, Change Request/Extension, RFP/RFQ Response, Partner/Sub-contract; lead sources without e-Procurement Portal or Govt Gazette, plus Social Media and Partner; document categories Proposal/Quotation, Purchase/Work Order, Agreement, Invoice, Completion/Sign-off, Statutory, Company Credentials, Correspondence; no Government Body Types; no Turnkey/EPC project type. (4) TDS, GST-TDS, holds, penalties and retention stay: they are optional and apply to any client. (5) The seeded admin's password comes from SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD in .env, never from code; unset, a random one is used. (6) The code base carries no e-mail address other than zitawaresolutions@gmail.com: form placeholders use @zitaware.in and test/demo fixtures @zitaware.test (an undeliverable domain).
Consequences: The tender and security-instrument tables, API routers and services remain, unreachable without their permissions; a later slice can drop them with a migration. The money-flow demo scenario (apps/api/src/testing) still builds its own sample tender and EMD/SD rows for its tests.
Supersedes: ADR-131/138 (Leads & Tenders); the EMD/SD scope in docs/00.
```

```text
ID: ADR-193
Date: 2026-11-09
Status: Accepted — implemented
Context: A legal entity's logo, seal and authorized signature were stored as bytes in legal_entities, capped at 400 KB each (ADR-141). Every other file already lives in S3, and 400 KB was too small for real artwork.
Decision: The three images go to object storage — the same S3_BUCKET and S3_PREFIX_FOLDER as documents — at `<prefix>/<organization>/legal-entities/<legal entity>/<logo|seal|authorized-sign>-<uuid>.<png|jpg>`, a fresh key per upload so a replaced image never overwrites an older one. New nullable columns logo_storage_key, seal_storage_key, authorized_sign_storage_key (migration 20261109120000_legal_entity_image_storage_keys) hold the key; the *_bytes columns are left null for new uploads. Uploads happen before the database transaction, so a row never points at a missing object. Reads (legal entity branding, the sidebar brand logo, the invoice PDF) take the key first and fall back to old bytes. The limit is 5 MB per image (PNG/JPEG, magic-checked as before); the API JSON body limit rises from 4 MB to 25 MB to carry three base64 images. apps/api/scripts/move-branding-images-to-s3.ts moves any existing bytes to S3 and empties the columns (idempotent). Integration tests use an in-memory store.
Consequences: No file is kept in the database once the move script has run; a later migration can drop the *_bytes columns. A missing S3 object shows/prints as no image rather than failing. nginx must allow request bodies of at least 25 MB on the API host.
Supersedes: the 400 KB cap and in-row storage of ADR-141.
```
